## XV Bids Farewell to a Scots Laird / 十五、别苏格兰领主

===Original===

The night was mild and dark, and the high road which the two men followed was defined only by the faint glimmer of the rain-pools that lay in every rut. The smell of wet earth was in their nostrils, and the noise of brimming streams in their ears, and to Alastair, with a sword at his side again, the world was transformed. All might yet be saved for the Cause, and in twelve hours he should see the Prince; the thought comforted him, but it was not the main tenant of his mind. For a woman’s face had lodged there like an obsession in sleep; he saw Claudia’s eyes change from laughter to tragedy and back again to laughter, he heard her tongue stumble musically among greetings, he fancied he saw﻿—nay, it was beyond doubt﻿—her face some day light up for him, as a girl’s lights up for her lover.﻿ ﻿… Across the pleasant dream passed the shadow of a high coat-collar and a long sharp nose. He shivered, remembering the ugly business before him.

“Where are the Spoonbills?” he asked.

“By now they will be close around Brightwell, ready to run to my whistle.”

===Chinese===

夜温和而浓黑，两人循行的大道，只靠一道道车辙里积存的雨水泛出的微光才辨认得出。湿润泥土的气息充溢鼻端，涨满的溪流声喧在耳畔；而在腰间重新佩上剑的阿拉斯泰尔看来，天地已焕然一新。大业或尚有可为，十二个钟头之后他便要见到王子殿下；这念头予他安慰，心中占着主位的却不是它。因为一张女人的脸早已栖在那里，像睡梦中的一个缠念；他看见克劳迪娅的眼睛从笑意转为悲戚、又从悲戚转回笑意，听见她的舌头在寒暄问候之间磕磕绊绊、如乐音般悦耳，他自以为看见——不，这是确凿无疑的了——她的脸有朝一日会为他点亮，像少女为恋人点亮一般。……一道高竖的衣领、一只尖长的鼻子的阴影，掠过这甜美的梦境。他打了个寒噤，想起面前这桩肮脏的营生。

“篦鹭会的人在哪里？”他问。

“这会儿他们该已密密围拢在布莱特韦尔四周，只等我一声呼哨便动。”

===Original===

“Are they armed?”

“With staves only. We are men of peace.”

“Suppose Norreys has a troop of Kingston’s Horse for garrison. Or even that he and Kyd and a servant or two have pistols. We are too evenly matched to administer justice in comfort.”

“Then we must use our wits,” was the answer. “But a file or two of your Highland muskets would not be unwelcome.”

===Chinese===

“他们带了家伙么？”

“只有棍棒。我们是爱好和平的人。”

“倘若诺里斯手底下有一队金斯顿的骑兵驻守呢？再不然，他同基德，加上一两个仆人，人人揣着手枪呢。双方势均力敌，想舒舒服服地执法可不容易。”

“那便只好斗智了，”对方答道，“不过，你那边的高地火枪手若能来上一两小队，倒也不嫌多。”

===Original===

The wish was fulfilled even as it was uttered. As they swung round a corner of road, half a mile from Brightwell gates, they had to rein in their horses hard to avoid a collision with a body of mounted men. These were halted in a cluster, while by the light of a lantern their leader made shift to examine a scrap of paper. The sudden irruption set all the beasts plunging, and the lantern went out in the confusion, but not before Alastair had caught sight of him who had held it.

“God’s mercy!” he cried. “Charles Hay! Is it Tinnis himself?”

“You have my name,” a voice answered, “and a tongue I have heard before.”

Alastair laughed happily. “Indeed you have heard it before, Mr. Charlie. In quarters and on parade, and at many a merry supper in the Rue Margot. Your superior officer has a claim upon you.”

The lantern, being now relit, revealed a tall young man with twenty troopers at his back, most of them large raw lads who were not long from the plough tail. The leader’s face was flushed with pleasure. “Where in God’s name have you been lurking, my dear sir?” he cried. “I have looked for you at every bivouac, for I longed to clap eyes again on a soldier of Lee’s, after so much undisciplined rabble.”

===Chinese===

话音未落，这心愿便得了偿。两人刚拐过一道路弯，离布莱特韦尔大门还有半英里，就不得不死死勒住马，免得同一队骑马的人撞个正着。那队人簇作一团停在那里，领头的正就着一盏提灯的光亮，凑合着辨看一片碎纸。这不速之客突如其来，惊得众马纷纷蹿跳，混乱中提灯灭了——却已在熄灭之前，被阿拉斯泰尔看清了执灯的那张脸。

“上帝慈悲！”他失声道，“查尔斯·海！这可是蒂尼斯（Tinnis）本人在此？”

“我的名字让你叫着了，”一个声音答道，“这嗓门我也听见过。”

阿拉斯泰尔欢喜地笑了。“您确实听见过，查理先生（Mr. Charlie）。在营房里，在操场上，在马戈街（Rue Margot）多少回痛快的晚宴上。您的长官如今有权向您提出请求了。”

提灯重新点亮，照出一位高个子青年，身后跟着二十名骑兵，多半是才离开犁把子不久的愣大个儿生手。队长的脸喜得通红。“老天在上，你究竟躲到哪儿去了，我亲爱的先生？”他喊道，“每一处宿营地我都要寻你，同这许多毫无军纪的乌合之众厮混了这么久，我太想再亲眼见一位李氏团的士兵了。”

===Original===

“The story will keep, Charles, and meantime I claim a service. You are on patrol?”

“A patrol of Elcho’s ordered to feel our way down this valley and report at Derby town by breakfast. ’Tis a cursed difficult affair riding these hills when there is no moon.”

“You have time and to spare before morn. Turn aside with me here for a matter of two hours. You shall have a good supper to cheer you, and will do your Prince a distinguished service. I pledge my word for it.”

“Lead on,” said Mr. Hay. “I am back in Lee’s again, and take my orders from Captain Maclean.”

He cried to his men, and the troop wheeled behind him, where he rode with Alastair and the Spainneach. “Now tell me the ploy,” he said. “It should be a high matter to keep you away from Derby this night, where they say the fountains are to run claret.”

===Chinese===

“这故事留着慢慢讲，查尔斯，眼下我有件事要征调你。你们是在巡逻？”

“埃尔乔（Elcho）部下的一支巡逻队，奉命沿这条山谷探路，早饭前须回德比城复命。没有月亮的夜里在这些山头骑马，真真是桩要命的难事。”

“离天亮还早，时间宽裕得很。随我在这里岔开一趟，前后约莫两个钟头。管保有一顿好晚饭给你提神，还要为王子殿下立一桩殊勋。我以人格担保。”

“头前带路，”海先生说，“我算又回了李氏团，一切听麦克莱恩上尉号令。”

他朝部下喝一声令，全队便跟在他身后转了向，随他同阿拉斯泰尔和西班牙人并辔而行。“现在说说这桩买卖吧，”他说，“能叫您今夜不进德比的事，必是了不得的大事——听说今晚德比城里，喷泉都要淌起红酒来呢。”

===Original===

“We go to do justice on a traitor,” said Alastair, and told him the main lines of the story. Mr. Hay whistled long and loud.

“You want us to escort the gentleman to Beelzebub’s bosom,” he asked.

“I want you to escort him to the Prince.”

“Not the slightest use, I do assure you. His Highness has a singular passion for gentry of that persuasion. Yesterday Lord George’s force brought in a black-hearted miscreant, by the name of Weir, caught red-handed no less, and a fellow we had been longing for months to get our irons on. Instead of a tow or a bullet he gets a handshake from His Highness, and is bowed out of the camp with ‘Erring brother, go and sin no more.’ Too much damned magnanimity, say I, and it’s not like we’ll get much of it back from Cumberland. Take my advice, and hang him from the nearest oak, and then apologise to His Highness for being in too much of a loyal hurry.”

===Chinese===

“我们去对一个叛徒执法，”阿拉斯泰尔说，随即把事情的大略讲给他听。海先生打了个悠长响亮的呼哨。

“您是要我们护送这位老爷去别西卜（Beelzebub）的怀抱，”他问。

“我是要你护送他去见王子殿下。”

“一点儿用也没有，我可以向您担保。殿下对那一门子的绅士，有一种特别的偏爱。昨天乔治勋爵的队伍押来一个黑心肠的恶棍，名叫韦尔（Weir），人赃并获不说，还是我们巴望了几个月、早想给他戴上铁镣的角色。结果绞索也罢、枪子儿也罢都没轮上，他倒得了殿下的一握之手，还被恭恭敬敬送出营门，捎一句‘迷途的兄弟，去吧，今后不要再犯罪了’。要我说，这种该死的宽宏大量实在太多了，而且别指望坎伯兰会照样还我们几多。听我一句劝：就近找棵橡树把他吊了，再去向殿下告罪，就说自己忠心太切，操办得太急。”

===Original===

The gates of Brightwell to Alastair’s surprise stood open, and in the faint light from a shuttered window of the lodge it seemed as if there had been much traffic.

“Where are your Spoonbills?” he asked the Spainneach.

“I do not know. In furze bush and broom bush and hazel thicket. But when I whistle, in ten seconds they will be at the door of Brightwell.”

The troopers were left in the dark of the paved court, with certain instructions. Accompanied by the Spainneach, Mr. Hay and Mr. Hay’s troop sergeant, Alastair rode forward to the great door, and pulled the massive bell-rope. A tinkle sounded inside at an immense distance, and almost at the same moment the door was opened. There was a light within which revealed the ancient butler.

===Chinese===

出乎阿拉斯泰尔意料，布莱特韦尔的大门竟敞开着，门房一扇上了窗板的小窗透出微光，照见门前车马往来频繁的痕迹。

“你的篦鹭会的人呢？”他问西班牙人。

“我不知道。荆豆丛里、金雀花丛里、榛树窠里，都有。可我一打呼哨，十秒钟之内他们就会站到布莱特韦尔门前。”

骑兵们领了几道号令，留在铺石庭院的暗处。阿拉斯泰尔由西班牙人、海先生和海先生的骑兵军士陪同，策马直抵正门，拉响了那根粗重的门铃索。铃声在宅子极深处叮然一响，几乎同一瞬间，门开了。门内一片灯光，照出那位年老的管家。

===Original===

“We have business with Sir John Norreys.”

“Sir John awaits you,” said the man. “But are there not others with you, sir?”

So the conspirators had summoned their friends, doubtless a troop of Kingston’s Horse from down the water. A thought struck him.

“We are also appointed to meet a Scotch gentleman, Mr. Kyd,” he said.

“Mr. Kyd arrived some minutes ago,” was the answer, “and is now repairing his toilet after his journey. Will you be pleased to enter?”

===Chinese===

“我们有事要见约翰·诺里斯爵士。”

“约翰爵士正恭候诸位，”那人说，“不过先生，您不还有同伴么？”

如此说来，这几个阴谋家已经召来了他们的朋友，八成是下游开来的金斯顿骑兵的一队人马。他心生一计。

“我们还约了一位苏格兰绅士，基德先生。”他说。

“基德先生几分钟前就到了，”对方答道，“正在盥洗尘劳。请诸位赏光进屋吧？”

===Original===

Alastair spoke in French to Mr. Hay, who gave an order to his troop sergeant, who took the horses and fell back; and the three men passed through the outer portals into the gaunt gloomy hall, in which Alastair had shivered on his first visit. Tonight there was a change. A huge fire of logs roared up the chimney, and from a door ajar came a glimpse of firelight in another room, and the corner of a laden table. Miserly Brightwell was holding revel that night.

Hay flung himself on a settle and toasted his boots.

“Comfort,” he cried, “after bleak and miry moors, and I have a glimpse of the supper you promised me. Sim Linton will hold the fort against any yokels on carthorses that try to interrupt us. But what has become of your swarthy friend?”

The Spainneach had disappeared, and the two were alone. Kyd has his papers here, thought Alastair, and it were well to make certain of them first. Evidence should be collected before the court sat. It would seem that the staging of the play was in other hands than his, and what had been proposed as a feast would by an irony of destiny be turned into mourning.﻿ ﻿… And then he realised with a shock that Claudia was beneath this roof, an unwitting, unsuspecting dove in a nest of ravens.﻿ ﻿… But in a little the Duchess Kitty would be with her and she would be safe in Oxfordshire, and some day he would journey there.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

阿拉斯泰尔用法语对海先生交代了一句，海先生向军士下了道命令，军士带马退了下去；三人穿过外门，走进那高敞阴森的大厅——阿拉斯泰尔初来时曾在这里打过寒噤。今夜却是另一番景象。一大炉木柴在烟囱里烧得轰轰作响，一扇虚掩的门里透出邻室的火光，还看得见一张摆满杯盘的餐桌的一角。吝啬的布莱特韦尔，这一夜竟摆开了盛宴。

海先生往高背长椅上一倒，烤起他的靴子来。

“舒服啊，”他叫道，“熬过了荒凉泥泞的荒原，这下可舒服了——我还瞅见您许我的那顿晚饭的影子了。西姆·林顿（Sim Linton）自会守住阵脚，任他什么乡巴佬骑着拉车的驽马来搅扰，也近不得身。可您那位黑肤色的朋友哪儿去了？”

西班牙人已经不见了，只剩他们两个。基德的文书就藏在这宅子里，阿拉斯泰尔想道，最好先把它们拿到手。开庭之前，证据总要先收齐。这出戏的排演，看来已不归他掌管；而一场原打算的盛宴，竟被命运的翻云覆雨变成了丧仪。……跟着他猛然惊觉：克劳迪娅就在这片屋顶之下，一只懵然无觉的白鸽，栖在鸦巢里。……不过再过片刻，基蒂公爵夫人就会到她身边，她将安然待在牛津郡，而有朝一日，他也要寻路去那里。……

===Original===

A figure was standing at the foot of the great staircase, a splendid figure, with a nobly laced coat and such ruffles as were rarely seen outside St. James’s. It wore a sword, but its carriage was not that of a soldier. It advanced into the circle of the firelight, and, seeing it was observed, it bowed and smiled graciously. Its face was that of a young man, with a long sharp nose.

“I bid you welcome, gentlemen,” it began, and then its eyes rested on Alastair. An instant and extreme terror flooded its face. It stopped abruptly, stumbled a step and then turned and ran.

Alastair was after the man like an arrow, but his feet slipped on the stone floor, and ere he had recovered himself Norreys had disappeared in the corridor which led to the back regions of the house. It was in gloom, but a lamp burned at the far end, and to this Alastair directed himself. But the place was a cul-de-sac, and he had to turn back and find a side-passage. The first led him into cellars, the second into the kitchen, where there seemed to be a strange to-do, but no sign of Norreys. At last he found the way to the backyard, and rushed through an open door into a storm of rain. Surely the Spoonbills must have prevented the man’s escape. But the Spoonbills had been nodding on that side of the house, for it was certain that Norreys had gone. No doubt he had kept a horse always ready saddled, and the sound of hooves could be heard growing faint on the turf of the park. Hatless and cloakless, Sir John had fled to his Whig friends in Nottinghamshire to claim reward and sanctuary.

Alastair’s first impulse was there and then to ride the man down, with Hay’s troopers and the Spoonbills alike on his trail. His hatred of him had flared up furiously, when the mean face in the firelight had broken in on his thoughts of Claudia. The fellow must be brought to justice, or the castle of fancy he had been building would tumble. But it was clear that Kyd must first be dealt with, and, bitterly unwilling, he allowed his inclinations to give place to his duty.

===Chinese===

大楼梯脚下立着一个身影，好一派雍容气象：花边名贵的上衣，那样考究的皱领袖口，出了圣詹姆斯宫便难得一见。它佩着剑，那副举止却不是武人的举止。那身影走进火光的圈子里，见有人注视自己，便躬身嫣然一笑。那是一张年轻的脸，配着一只又长又尖的鼻子。

“欢迎诸位，绅士们，”它开口道，随即目光落在了阿拉斯泰尔身上。霎时间，极度的惊惶漫过那张脸。它猛地收住话头，踉跄一步，转身便跑。

阿拉斯泰尔箭一般追了上去，却在那石地上滑了脚，待他稳住身子，诺里斯已消失在通往宅子后部的走廊里。走廊里一片昏黑，唯有尽头点着一盏灯，阿拉斯泰尔便直奔那盏灯去。谁知那是条死路，他只好折回来另寻侧廊。第一条通向地窖，第二条通向厨房——厨房里似乎正闹得人仰马翻，却不见诺里斯的影子。他终于寻到通往后院的路，冲出一道敞开的门，一头扎进瓢泼大雨里。想来篦鹭会的人总该截住了此人的去路吧。可篦鹭会的人在宅子那一面偏偏正打着盹，因为诺里斯确乎是走脱了。毫无疑问，他随时备着一匹鞍鞯齐整的马，蹄声已在园圃的草地上渐渐远去、微不可闻。约翰爵士连帽子斗篷都没顾上戴，逃往诺丁汉郡的辉格党朋友那里讨赏钱、讨庇护去了。

阿拉斯泰尔的第一个冲动，便是当场纵骑去追，让海先生的骑兵同篦鹭会一齐循迹穷追。当那张猥琐的脸在火光里闯进他对克劳迪娅的思念时，他对这人的憎恨陡然熊熊烧起。这厮必须伏法，否则他心中正营筑的空中楼阁便会坍塌。但眼下显然须先对付基德，他万般不情愿，只得让职责压过自己的意愿。

===Original===

Kyd’s papers! The thought struck him that Norreys might have carried them off, and sent him hurrying along the passages to the hall, where Mr. Hay was still basking like a cat in the warmth. There, too, stood the Spainneach, looking like a panther in his lean dark shadowy grace.

“Mr. Kyd is in his chamber, cleansing himself of the stains of travel and humming merrily. I mistrust the servants, Sir Sandy, so I have replaced them by our own folk. Where are the said servants, you ask? Shut up in various corners, very scared and docile. Likewise I have discovered Mr. Kyd’s travelling-bag. It is in strange wardenship. Come and see.”

The man, stepping lightly, led the way up a broad shallow staircase, to a room of which he noiselessly opened the door. The hospitable warmth downstairs had not penetrated to that cold chamber, for the air of it was like a tomb. On a table stood a saddlebag from which the contents had been spilled, and over these contents hung the two grey women whom Alastair had seen on his earlier visit. They caressed the papers as if they were misers fumbling banknotes, one lean and hawk-beaked, the other of a dropsical fatness.

===Chinese===

基德的文书！他忽然想到诺里斯或许已把文书携走，急忙沿走廊奔回大厅。海先生还在炉火边取暖，像一只晒太阳的猫。西班牙人也立在那里，瘦削黝黑、幽雅如影，活像一头黑豹。

“基德先生正在房里，一面洗去旅途的尘污，一面快活地哼着小曲。我信不过这里的仆人，桑迪爵士，已把他们换成我们自己的人。您要问那些仆人哪儿去了？关在各处角落里，吓得服服帖帖。我还发现了基德先生的旅行袋，看管它的人可有些古怪。请随我来看。”

那人脚步轻悄地在前面引路，登上一道宽而坡缓的楼梯，来到一个房间门口，无声无息地推开了门。楼下那殷勤的暖意并未渗入这间冷屋，屋里的空气如坟墓一般。桌上一只鞍囊，里面的文书倾撒在外，两张灰扑扑的老妇人正俯在其上——正是阿拉斯泰尔前番来时见过的那两个。她们抚弄着那些文书，像守财奴摩挲钞票；一个精瘦，长着鹰钩鼻，另一个虚胖得发肿。

===Original===

“Sir Robert Leatham﻿—fifty men and five hundred pounds﻿—good pickings in that, sister. That makes the roll of Hereford complete. The fines will not be less than half a million pounds, and at two pounds per centum that is a sum of ten thousand﻿—half to cousin John and half to him we know of.﻿ ﻿…”

The other was fingering the rings on a tally-stick.

“He favours you, Caroline, and between you there will be a rare fortune. Cousin Johnnie has promised me Brightwell, when our father leaves us, and I look to you to assist the conveyance. That is my price, remember. If you play me false, I will scratch your eyes out and curse him till he rots. Ay, and I will tell on him to that puling miss in the Green Chamber.﻿ ﻿… Does Johnnie sup tonight?”

===Chinese===

“罗伯特·莱瑟姆爵士——五十个人、五百英镑——妹妹，这里头油水不小。赫里福德的名册这就齐全了。罚金不会少于五十万英镑，按每百抽二算，便是一万英镑——半数归约翰表亲，半数归咱们知道的那位。……”

另一个正捻弄着一根符木（tally-stick）上的圈环。

“他偏疼你，卡罗琳，你们俩凑在一处，便是天大的一份家业。约翰尼表亲答应过我，等咱们父亲一咽气，布莱特韦尔就归我——这桩交割，我指望着你成全。记住，这是我的价钱。你若敢耍我，我就挖掉你的眼睛，咒得他烂到骨头缝里。对，我还要到绿室（Green Chamber）里那个哭哭啼啼的小娘儿面前，告他一状。……约翰尼今晚下来用晚饭么？”

===Original===

“Ay, and departs early, for he is bound for the Duke of Richmond, but he we know of stays till the Duke comes hither. He’s the great man, sister, and Johnnie but a boy. A clever dutiful boy, to be sure, with an old head on his young shoulders. I’ll wager that when they both come to die there will be little difference between the fortunes of Sir John Norreys of Weston and Sir Robert Grosvenor of Eaton. The pity of it that he has set his heart on that baby-faced wench.”

“She brought him a fine estate, Caroline.”

“Pish! He thinks less of the good acres than her pink cheeks. I could scratch them till the bones were bare.﻿ ﻿… Read the Shropshire roll again, sister. How deep is Henry Talbot?”

===Chinese===

“下来，而且一早动身，他要赶去里士满公爵那里。可咱们知道的那位，要住到公爵大人驾临为止。那位才是大人物，姐姐，约翰尼不过是个孩子。倒是个机灵听话的孩子，千真万确，小小的肩膀上顶着一颗老成的脑袋。我敢打赌，到两人都咽气那天，韦斯顿的约翰·诺里斯爵士和伊顿的罗伯特·格罗夫纳爵士，家业上也未必差得了多少。可惜呀，他偏把一颗心拴在那个娃娃脸的丫头身上。”

“她给他带来了一份好家业呢，卡罗琳。”

“呸！良田千顷他不放心上，倒把心放在她那两片粉腮上。我恨不得挠得她露出骨头碴子。……把什罗普郡的名册再念一遍，姐姐。亨利·塔尔博特（Henry Talbot）陷得有多深？”

===Original===

The two witches, obscene, malevolent, furtive, bent over the papers as over a bubbling cauldron. Alastair stepped forward, choking down a strong disgust.

“I must beg your permission to remove these papers, mesdames. They are required for the conference to which Mr. Kyd will presently descend.”

The women huddled together, stretching each an arm over the papers.

“Mr. Kyd gave them into our charge,” they said in one voice.

===Chinese===

两个巫婆，淫猥、歹毒、鬼鬼祟祟，朝文书俯下身去，如同俯着一口滚沸的大锅。阿拉斯泰尔强压下一阵剧烈的憎恶，跨步上前。

“二位夫人，恕我恳请你们允准，移走这些文书。基德先生即刻便要下楼赴会，会上正用得着它们。”

两个妇人挤作一团，各伸出一条胳膊护住文书。

“基德先生把它们交给我们看管的。”两人异口同声。

===Original===

“He releases you from that charge,” said Alastair. “Permit me, madam,” and he laid a hand on the saddlebag and began to refill it.

The women would have resisted had not the Spainneach stepped behind them and murmured something into the lean one’s ear. Whatever it was, it caused her to draw back her protecting arm and bid her sister do likewise. Alastair bundled the papers into the bag, and left the room followed by two pairs of wolfish eyes. The Spainneach locked the door, and left the key on the outside. “Best keep these wild cats fast in their cave,” he observed. “There might have been a tussle over that treasure-trove, had I not remembered something I had heard of those grey ones long ago. Now I go to find the servant Edom.”

“When Kyd leaves his room see that the hall is empty. I will await him in the dining-room. When I ring, do you and Hay enter and join us. Make Edom wait at the meal with the servants you have provided.”

===Chinese===

“这项看管之责，他已经卸除了，”阿拉斯泰尔说，“夫人，请容我。”说着他一手按住鞍囊，动手把文书装回去。

若不是西班牙人绕到她们身后，对那精瘦的一个咬了几句耳朵，这两个妇人原是要抗拒的。也不知他说了什么，那瘦子竟缩回了护袋的胳膊，还叫她妹妹照样办。阿拉斯泰尔把文书胡乱塞进袋里，离了房间，身后两双狼一样的眼睛直盯着他的背影。西班牙人锁上门，把钥匙留在外头。“这些野猫，还是紧关在洞里为好，”他说，“若不是我想起多年前听来的那两个灰婆子的事，这份无主之财怕免不了一场争夺。这会儿我去找仆人以东。”

“基德一出房门，你务必让大厅里空无一人。我在饭厅等他。铃一响，你便同海先生进来与我们会合。让以东同你备下的那些人手一道，侍立在这顿饭上。”

===Original===

“It is a noble meal which is now cooking,” said the Spainneach. “Even the miserly will spend themselves on a high occasion. It is the habit of Madame Norreys to sup in her room, and that room is at the far end of the house from us. She will not be disturbed if we grow merry.”

Alastair sat himself by the fire in the great vaulted dining-room and tore open the saddlebag. He ran hastily through the papers, for he was looking for what he knew to be there, and it did not take him long to discard the irrelevant. Once or twice, as he found what he hoped and yet feared to find, an exclamation was wrung from him. He selected several documents and placed them in his breast, and reread others with set lips and a knotted forehead. Then he looked into the fire and mused.﻿ ﻿…

Through the open door came the sound of a step on the paved floor of the hall, a heavy, assured, leisurely step. The young man kicked the saddlebag under the table and stood erect by the hearth with an odd smile on his face. Grimness had left it, and a wry courtesy remained.

The laird of Greyhouses was a gallant sight. Gone were the splashed boots and muddy breeches, and all that might recall the wintry roads. He was dressed as on that night at Cornbury when he had kept Sir Christopher Lacy company﻿—in flowered waistcoat, and plum-coloured coat, and canary stockings, and buckled shoes that shone like well-water. He was humming a little tune as he entered, his eye bright and content, his heavy figure tautened and refined by hard travelling, his shapely face rosy as a winter’s eve. It was the entrance of a great man to a company where he expects to be acclaimed, for there was self-consciousness in the primness of his mouth. He lifted his genial eyes and saw Alastair.

===Chinese===

“眼下炉上正烧着一席盛馔，”西班牙人说，“守财奴逢上大日子，也肯倾囊挥霍一回。诺里斯夫人惯在自家房里用晚饭，那间房离我们这头隔着整座宅子。便是我等热闹些，也搅扰不着她。”

阿拉斯泰尔在那间拱顶大饭厅的炉火旁坐下，扯开鞍囊。他匆匆翻检那些文书——他要找的东西，他知道必在其中——不多时便把无关的都剔到了一边。有一两回，他寻见了既盼着找到、又怕找到的东西，不由得失声惊呼。他拣出几份文件揣进怀里，又咬紧嘴唇、锁着眉心重读另几份。随后他望着炉火，出了神。……

开着的门外传来大厅石地上的脚步声——沉重、笃定、不慌不忙。年轻人一脚把鞍囊踢到桌子底下，在壁炉边站直了身子，脸上挂着一丝古怪的笑。笑里的杀气已敛，只剩一点揶揄的客气。

格雷豪斯的领主好一派威仪。溅泥的马靴和污渍斑斑的马裤都不见了，一切能令人想起冬日旅途的东西都不见了。他的穿戴，与科恩伯里那一夜陪伴克里斯托弗·莱西爵士时一模一样——绣花马甲，梅子色的外套，鹅黄的长袜，带扣鞋擦得像井水一般亮。他进门时哼着一支小曲，目光明快而满足，壮硕的身躯经一路鞍马劳顿，反倒紧实清减了几分，那张端正的脸红润得像冬日黄昏的霞色。这是一位大人物走进一班他料想必将为他喝彩的宾客中间的做派——瞧他嘴角的板正里，还带着几分顾影自许。他抬起那双和蔼的眼睛，看见了阿拉斯泰尔。

===Original===

The man was a superb actor, for though Alastair was watching him like a hawk he could see no start of surprise, no flicker of disappointment or fear.

“Captain Maclean, upon my soul!” he cried. “And who would have expected it? Man, I did not know you were acquaint here. But ’tis a joyful meeting, my dear sir, and I’m *felix opportunitate coenae* the day.” He held out a cordial hand, which the young man left unnoticed.

“I am happy to repay hospitality,” he said. “You welcomed me some weeks back at a wayside inn, and it is my turn now to provide the entertainment. Let us sit down to supper, Mr. Kyd. There are other guests,” and he stretched a hand to the bell-rope.

“I confess I was expecting a wheen more,” said Mr. Kyd, and there was just the faintest quiver of his eyelids.

“Sir John Norreys begged to be excused. He was summoned into Nottinghamshire somewhat suddenly﻿—so suddenly that I fear he will take a catarrh, for he has forgotten his hat and cloak. The ladies of the house are detained in their chamber, and the master, as we know, has been bedridden these many years. But there are others to take their place.” Again he stretched out his hand, but Kyd interrupted him.

===Chinese===

此人是位一流的戏子。尽管阿拉斯泰尔鹰隼一般盯着他，却看不出一丝惊愕，寻不出半点失望或惧怕的闪烁。

“麦克莱恩上尉，我的老天！”他叫道，“谁能想得到呢？老兄，我可不知道您与此地相熟。不过这是一场可喜的重逢，亲爱的先生——今日我当真是幸逢盛宴（felix opportunitate coenae）了。”他热络地伸出一只手，年轻人却只当没有看见。

“我很高兴能回报一番款待，”他说，“几个星期前，您在一家路边客栈迎接了我，如今轮到我来做东了。请入席吧，基德先生。还有别的客人。”说着他伸手去拉门铃索。

“恕我直言，我原指望来的还不止这几位。”基德先生说，眼皮几乎难以察觉地颤了一颤。

“约翰·诺里斯爵士告了假。他被突然召往诺丁汉郡——急得连帽子和斗篷都忘了带，我怕他难免要染一场伤风。宅里的女眷们有事绊在房中出不来，而老爷呢，咱们知道，卧床已有多年。不过另有几位，来替补他们的位置。”他又伸手去拉铃索，基德却打断了他。

===Original===

“What is the meaning of it?” he asked in a low voice. “What does this pleasantry betoken, Captain Maclean?”

“It betokens that ‘Menelaus’ has come to Phaeacia to see his old crony ‘Alcinous.’ The two will have much to say to each other, but they will regret that ‘Achilles’ is not here to make it a three-handed crack.”

The mention of “Achilles” seemed to perturb the other. He narrowed his eyes, and into them came the shadow of that look which Alastair had surprised on the evening at the inn. Then he stepped to the table, filled a glass of claret and drank it off, while Alastair rang the bell.

The Spainneach entered with Hay on his heels. Kyd regarded them with puzzled eyes, as if striving to recapture a memory.

===Chinese===

“这是什么意思？”他低声问，“麦克莱恩上尉，这场玩笑究竟作何解？”

“它意味着：‘墨涅拉俄斯’来到法伊阿基亚，拜会他的老相知‘阿尔基努斯’。两位要谈的话多着呢，只可惜‘阿喀琉斯’不在，凑不成一场三人长谈。”

“阿喀琉斯”三字一出，对方似乎心绪被扰。他眯起眼睛，眼底掠过客栈那晚阿拉斯泰尔窥见的那种神色的阴影。随后他走到桌边，斟满一杯红酒一饮而尽，阿拉斯泰尔也在此刻摇了铃。

西班牙人走了进来，海先生紧跟在后。基德用困惑的目光打量他们，仿佛在竭力追回一段记忆。

===Original===

“I present to you Mr. Charles Hay of Tinnis,” said Alastair, “who commands a troop in His Highness’s Lowland Horse. The other gentleman is of the Nameless Clan. Sit you down, sirs.”

Kyd obeyed, but his eyes were not on the food and wine, for he was thinking hard. He had a stout heart and had often faced peril, so he forced his mind to consider the situation’s possibilities, when a weaker man would have been aflutter. Would the horsemen he had asked for from Kingston arrive in time?﻿—that was the main point. Beyond doubt they would, and meantime he would confuse this Highland jackanapes, who seemed to have stumbled on some damaging truths. But the appearance of Alastair, whom he had utterly written off from his list of obstacles, worried him in spite of all his robust philosophy. He made pretence to eat, but he only crumbled his bread and toyed with his meat, though he drank wine thirstily. The servants who moved about the room, too, perturbed him. There was his own man Edom acting as butler, but the others were strange folk, outlandishly dressed and with dark secret faces, and one, a trooper of Hay’s, had a belt with pistols round his middle and that at his shoulder which might be a white cockade.

Alastair read his thoughts.

===Chinese===

“我为您引见，这位是蒂尼斯的查尔斯·海先生，”阿拉斯泰尔说，“在殿下的低地骑兵团统领一队人马。这一位先生，出身无名氏族。二位请坐。”

基德依言落座，目光却不在酒菜上——他正急速盘算。他有副硬心肠，闯过不止一回风险，故此虽换了旁人早已方寸大乱，他还能强逼自己条分缕析眼下的局面。他向金斯顿公爵讨要的骑兵能及时赶到么？——这是头一件要紧事。必能赶到，毋庸置疑；趁这工夫，他要搅浑这高地小丑的心思，此人似乎瞎猫撞上死耗子，撞破了几桩要命的实情。可是阿拉斯泰尔的出现却叫他心头发紧——此人他早已从障碍名单上一笔勾销——任凭他有一身结实的达观功夫，也压不下去。他装出用饭的样子，其实只把面包捻成碎屑，把肉食拨来拨去，酒倒是渴极了似的灌。满屋走动的仆人也叫他心神不宁。领头管家是他的仆人以东，其余却是些生面孔，衣着古怪，一张张阴沉沉莫测高深，还有一个海先生的骑兵，腰间缠着插满手枪的皮带，肩头那物件，瞧着像是一枚白帽徽。

阿拉斯泰尔看透了他的心思。

===Original===

“I fear, sir, that your entertainment is not what you hoped, but I have done my best to provide a recompense. Since his Grace of Kingston could not send a garrison, I have brought Mr. Hay’s Scots. Since Sir John Norreys is summoned elsewhere, I have provided Mr. Hay in his stead. And since the ladies upstairs cannot honour us, I have bidden another lady, who will shortly arrive.”

The news seemed to move Kyd to action. Hope from Kingston’s horse was over, and the only chance lay in carrying matters with a high hand, and bluffing his opponent who must be largely in the dark. His plans had been too deep-laid to be discovered by a casual moss-trooper.

“Most considerate, I’m sure,” he said. “But let’s have an end of these riddles. I come here to a well-kenned house, expecting to meet an old friend, and find him mysteriously departed, and you in his place talking like an oracle. I venture to observe that it’s strange conduct between gentlemen of the same nation. What’s the meaning of it, sir?” He pushed back his chair, and looked squarely at the young man.

“The meaning of it is that Judas has come to judgment.”

===Chinese===

“先生，恕我直言，今晚的款待怕不合您的指望，但我已尽力备下补偿。金斯顿公爵大人既派不出守军，我便带来了海先生的苏格兰人。约翰·诺里斯爵士既被召往他处，我便请海先生代他作陪。楼上的女眷们既不能赏光，我又请了另一位夫人，即刻便到。”

这消息似乎催基德动了起来。指望金斯顿的骑兵是没指望了，唯一的生机便是拿出高压手段，唬住这个多半还蒙在鼓里的对手。他筹划的机关埋得那么深，岂是一个寻常的沼地马匪（moss-trooper）探得了底的。

“真是体贴入微，承情之至。”他说，“不过，这些哑谜也就打住吧。我到这户相熟的人家来，指望会一位老友，却见他不明不白地走了，由您占了他的位子，说话像神谕一般。恕我冒昧说一句：同一国的绅士之间，这样的行径未免离奇。先生，这究竟是什么意思？”他把椅子往后一推，直勾勾瞪着年轻人。

“这意思是——犹大落到审判跟前了。”

===Original===

Kyd laughed, with an excellent semblance of mirth, and indeed he felt relieved. This was a mere random general charge, for which he could readily invent a defence. “Oh, sits the wind that airt? It’s most extraordinary the way we of the honest party harbour suspicions. I’ve done it myself many’s the time. Weel-a-weel, if I’ve to thole my assize, so be it. I’ve a quiet conscience and a good answer to any charge. But who is to sit in judgment?”

The man’s composure was restored. He filled himself a glass of claret, held it to the light, and savoured its bouquet before he sipped.

As if in answer to his question the door opened to admit two newcomers. One was a small lady, with a black silk mask from her brow to her lips, so that no part of her face was visible. A velvet hood covered her hair, and her dress was hidden from sight by a long travelling-robe of fur. Behind her shambled a tall man, whose big hands strayed nervously to his dusty cravat and the threadbare lapels of his coat.

“Here is your judge,” said Alastair. “Madam, will you sit in the seat of justice?”

===Chinese===

基德笑了，一副欢愉的模样装得惟妙惟肖，心里也确实松了口气。这不过是一句不着边际的泛泛罪名，他随口便能编出一套辩词。“哦，原来风是从那处角落吹来的？咱们忠义一党爱起疑心，也真是天下奇谈。我自己就不止干过一回。罢了罢了，若轮到我领这场审判，那就审吧。我问心无愧，任凭什么罪名都有话回答。只是，坐堂问案的是哪一位？”

此人的镇定又回了体。他给自己斟上一杯红酒，举到灯前照了照，先品了品香气，方才啜饮。

仿佛在回答他的问话，门开了，进来两位不速之客。一位是身材娇小的夫人，一方黑丝面具从额际遮到唇边，脸上不见一寸肌肤。天鹅绒兜帽罩着头发，长长一袭皮裘旅袍，把裙衫也遮得严严实实。她身后拖沓着一个高个子男人，一双大手不安地摸向落满尘土的领结和磨旧的衣襟。

“您的审判官到了。”阿拉斯泰尔说，“夫人，可愿屈就公堂之上的席位？”

===Original===

He pulled forward a high-backed Restoration chair, and placed before it a footstool. Solemnly like a cardinal in conclave the little lady seated herself.

“Who is the prisoner?” she asked. “And what bill does the Prince’s attorney present against him?”

The servants had moved to the back of the room, and stood in the shadow like guards at attention. By a strange chance the place seemed to have borrowed the similitude of a court﻿—Kyd at one end of a table with the guards behind him, Mr. Johnson like a justice’s clerk sprawling beside the lady’s chair.

“His name, madam,” said Alastair, “is Nicholas Kyd of Greyhouses in the Merse, the principle doer of his Grace of Queensberry, and likewise a noted Jacobite and a member of His Highness’s Council.”

===Chinese===

他拖过来一把王政复辟时代的高背椅，椅子跟前安了一只脚凳。小个子夫人庄严落座，俨然列席教皇选举密议的红衣主教。

“犯人是谁？”她问，“王子的检察官对他呈递的是一份什么诉状？”

仆人们已退到屋子后部，立在暗影里，像一队肃立的卫兵。说来也怪，这地方竟俨然借来了一座公堂的形制——基德坐在长桌一端，卫兵立于身后，约翰逊先生像个治安官的书记官，摊手摊脚坐在夫人的椅子旁边。

“夫人，此人名叫尼古拉斯·基德，”阿拉斯泰尔说，“默斯人格雷豪斯的领主，昆斯伯里公爵大人的首要代理人，同时又是一位鼎鼎大名的雅各布党、王子殿下枢密会议的一员。”

===Original===

“And the charge?”

“That this Nicholas Kyd has for many months betrayed the secrets of his master, and while professing to work for the Cause has striven to defeat it by withholding vital information. Further, that the same Nicholas Kyd has sought for his own gain to bring about the ruin of divers honest gentlemen, by inducing them to pledge their support to His Highness and then handing such pledges to King George’s Government.”

“Heard you ever such havers?” said Kyd boisterously. This was what he had hoped for, a wild general accusation, the same he had heard brought against Balhaldy and Traquair and a dozen others, but never substantiated. “You’ll have a difficulty in proving your case, Mr. Attorney.”

===Chinese===

“罪名呢？”

“罪状是：这名尼古拉斯·基德多月以来，一直出卖其主人的机密；口称效力大业，实则扣压紧要消息，处处败坏大业。再者，同一尼古拉斯·基德为一己之私，诱使若干忠厚绅士立誓效忠王子殿下，再将这些誓书转呈乔治王的政府，图谋倾陷他们，毁其身家。”

“诸位可曾听过这等胡话？”基德大吵大嚷道。这正是他巴望的：一通狂放而空泛的指控，同当年落在巴哈尔迪（Balhaldy）、特雷夸尔（Traquair）等十几个人头上的如出一辙，从没有坐实过。“检察官先生，你这案子怕不容易证成呢。”

===Original===

Then Alastair told his tale from that hour when in the alehouse he met Kyd. He told of Kyd’s advice to go by Flambury and his troubles there, of the message given him in error, of Edom and his mission, of Sir John Norreys and his suspected doings, of his own kidnapping and imprisonment and the confession of Ben the Gypsy in the moorland farm.

“Your proofs, sir,” said the judge.

“They are here,” he replied, and drew from his breast a sheaf of papers. “There, madam, is the full account of the Duke of Beaufort’s purpose in Wales, written out and inscribed to the Duke of Kingston, for transmission to Mr. Pelham. There you have another document narrating conversations with the trusting Jacobites of the Marches. There you have a letter from Beaufort to his Prince, which would appear from its superscription to be directed afresh to the Duke of Cumberland.”

The lady looked at the papers shown her, knitted her brows and returned them. She glanced at Kyd, whose face was set in a mask which he strove to make impassive.

===Chinese===

接着，阿拉斯泰尔从头讲起他的经历：从他在那家酒馆里遇见基德的那一刻讲起。他讲到基德劝他取道弗兰伯里以及他在那里的麻烦，讲到误送到他手里的那封密信，讲到以东和他的差事，讲到约翰·诺里斯爵士和他形迹可疑的种种作为，讲到他自己如何被绑架囚禁，讲到吉普赛本在荒原农舍里的招供。

“拿出你的证据来，先生。”审判官说。

“都在这里。”他答道，从怀里抽出一叠文书。“夫人请看：这一份，是博福特公爵威尔士之谋的全文，誊录在案，题呈金斯顿公爵启，以转递佩勒姆先生。这一份，记着与边区那些轻信的雅各布党的多次谈话。这一封，是博福特致王子的书信——瞧那信封上的字样，分明已改投坎伯兰公爵了。”

夫人看了看呈上的文书，皱起眉头，交还回去。她瞥了基德一眼，那人的脸绷成一副面具，正竭力装出不动声色。

===Original===

“Proceed with your second and graver charge, sir,” she said.

Alastair told of his conversation with General Oglethorpe and of Kyd’s visit to the General’s room at midnight. He told of the two hags upstairs who were in partnership. “And for proof,” he cried, “here are the rolls of three counties taken from the man’s saddlebags, giving a list of the gentlemen who are liable to fines for their political action, and noting the shares which will come to each of the conspirators. Do you require further evidence, madam?”

The room had grown very still, and no one of the company stirred, till Kyd brought his fist down on the table. His face had whitened.

“What says the prisoner?” the lady asked.

===Chinese===

“继续你的第二项、也是更重的罪名吧，先生。”她说。

阿拉斯泰尔讲了与奥格尔索普将军的一席谈，讲了基德半夜造访将军房间的事。他讲了楼上那两个同谋的巫婆。“至于证据，”他扬声道，“这里是从此人鞍囊里起出的三郡名册，列着因政治行止而待罚的绅士名单，还注明了各同谋人应得的份子。夫人，还要更多的证据么？”

屋子里静极了，满座没有一个人动一动，直到基德一拳砸在桌上。他的脸已经白了。

“犯人有何话说？”夫人问。

===Original===

“Lies, madam, devilish lies﻿—and these papers a common forgery. Some enemy﻿—and God knows I have many﻿—has put them in my baggage.”

“You are acquainted with the handwriting, madam?” Alastair asked.

She studied the papers again. “I have seen it a thousand times. It is a well-formed and capable style, clerkly and yet gentlemanlike. Nay, there can be no doubt. His hand wrote these lists and superscriptions.”

Kyd’s face from pallor flushed scarlet. “God’s curse, but am I to have my fame ruined by a playacting wench! What daftness is this? What knows this hussy of my hand of write?”

===Chinese===

“谎言，夫人，恶魔般的谎言——这些文书不过是寻常的伪造。哪个仇家——上帝知道我的仇家多的是——把它们塞进了我的行囊。”

“夫人识得这笔迹么？”阿拉斯泰尔问。

她又把文书细看了一遍。“这笔迹我见过千百回。一笔好字，干净利落，有书记员的工整，又不失绅士的气派。不，断无可疑。这些名册和信封上的字，正是出自他手。”

基德的脸由白转红，红得发紫。“上帝咒罚！难道我的名声，要断送在一个跑江湖的戏子丫头手里！这是什么昏话？这贱婢又懂得什么我的手笔？”

===Original===

“Do you deny the authorship, sir?” Alastair asked.

The man had lost his temper. “I deny and affirm nothing before a court that has no sort of competence. I will answer to the Prince, when he calls for an answer, and I can promise a certain gentleman his kail through the reek on that day.”

“I should be happy to be proved in error. But if the papers should happen to be genuine you will admit, sir, that they bear an ugly complexion.”

“I’ll admit nothing except that you’re a bonny friend to lippen so readily to a clumsy fabrication. Ay, and you’ve the damned insolence to bring in a baggage from the roads to testify to my hand of write. You’ll have to answer to me for that, my man.”

===Chinese===

“先生，你否认这些是你所写么？”阿拉斯泰尔问。

这人动了真气。“在这样一个毫无权限的公堂之前，我什么也不否认，什么也不承认。等王子召我答话时，我自会答话——到那一天，我管保叫某位老爷的菜汤，隔着烟气送进肚里，有他受的。”

“我很乐意被证明是弄错了。可这些文书万一竟是真的，先生，您也得承认，它们的脸色可不大好看。”

“我什么也不承认，只承认你这位漂亮朋友，竟能这般轻信一份笨拙的伪造文书。是啊，你还胆大包天，弄来一个路上拾来的贱货，来指认我的手笔。就为这一条，你就得对我有个交代，我的好人。”

===Original===

There was a low laugh from the mask. He had not recognised her, partly because of his discomposure and fear and partly because he had never dreamed of her presence in that countryside. When, therefore, she plucked the silk from her face and looked sternly down on him, he seemed suddenly to collapse like a pricked bladder. His stiff jaw dropped, his eyes stared, he made as if to speak and only stammered.

“Your face condemns you, sir,” she said gravely. “I have seen your writing too often to mistake it, and I have lived long enough in the world to recognise the sudden confusion of crime in a man’s eyes. I condemn you, sir, as guilty on both charges, and fouler and shamefuller were never proven.”

Kyd’s defence was broken; but there was a resolute impudence in the man which made him still show fight. He looked obstinately at the others, and attempted a laugh; then at the Duchess, with an effrontery as of a fellow-conspirator.

“It seems we’re both in an ugly place,” he said. “You ken my secret, madam, which I had meant to impart to you when an occasion offered. Here’s the two of us honest folks at the mercy of the wild Jacobites and wishing sore that the Duke of Kingston would make better speed up the water.”

===Chinese===

面具后传来一声低笑。他方才没有认出她来，一半是因为心慌意乱，一半是因为做梦也想不到她会置身这片乡野。于是，当她扯下脸上那方丝巾、居高临下严正地注视他时，他仿佛忽然瘪了气，像一只被戳破的尿泡。僵硬的下颌垮了下来，两眼直瞪，张口欲言，只落得结结巴巴。

“先生，你这张脸就定了你的罪。”她庄严地说，“你的笔迹我见得太多，不会认错；我在这世上也活得够久，认得出人眼底骤然浮现的、犯罪时的慌乱。先生，我判你两项罪名俱各成立——古往今来被查实的罪案，再没有比这更污浊、更无耻的了。”

基德的辩局已破，可这人有一股死硬的无赖劲，还要负隅顽抗。他执拗地扫视旁人，试着笑了一笑；又看向公爵夫人，那副厚颜，倒像是同案共谋的一般。

“看来你我两个都落在了不妙的境地，”他说，“夫人，您知道了我的秘密——这本是我打算寻个时机，亲自奉告您的。咱们两个老实人，如今都捏在这帮野蛮的雅各布党手里，只巴望金斯顿公爵沿河上来，脚程再快一些才好。”

===Original===

“That is not my wish,” she said, with stony eyes.

It was those eyes which finally unnerved him.

“But, madam,” he cried, “your Grace﻿—you are of the Government party, the party I have served﻿—I have letters from Mr. Pelham﻿ ﻿… you winna suffer the rebels to take vengeance on me for loyalty to King George.”

“I am a Whig,” said she, “and will not condemn you for political conduct, base though I must judge it. The Prince’s Attorney must hale you to another court. You will take him to your master﻿—” this to Alastair﻿—“and leave him to that tribunal.”

===Chinese===

“那可不是我的巴望。”她说，目光冷如顽石。

最后叫他胆寒的，正是那双眼睛。

“可是，夫人，”他叫道，“公爵夫人殿下——您是政府一党，是我效力的那一党啊——我有佩勒姆先生的亲笔信……您总不会任凭叛党向我报仇，罚我一片对乔治王的忠心吧。”

“我是辉格党，”她说，“不会因你的政治行止定你的罪——尽管我必须说，那行止卑劣。王子殿下的检察官，须得把你解往另一座公堂。”——这句是对阿拉斯泰尔说的——“把他带到你主上跟前，交给那座法庭发落。”

===Original===

“With your assent, madam, I do not ask for judgment on the first charge, and I do not propose that he should go to the Prince. The penalty for his treason is death, and I am unwilling to saddle His Highness before he has won his throne with the duty of putting an end to a rascal.”

She nodded. “I think you are wise, sir. But the second charge is the more heinous, for it offends not against the law of men’s honour, but the law of human kindness and the law of God. There I find him the chief of sinners. What penalty do you ask for?”

“I ask that your Grace pronounce sentence of perpetual exile.”

“But where﻿—and how?”

===Chinese===

“夫人，既蒙俯允，第一项罪名我并不请求判决，也无意送他去见王子殿下。叛国之罪，刑名是死，而殿下宝座未得之时，我不忍把这了结一个恶棍的担子，压上他的肩头。”

她颔首。“先生，我想你是明智的。只是第二项罪名更为凶恶，因为它触犯的不是人与人之间的荣誉法度，而是仁爱的法度与上帝的法度。在这一条上，我认他为罪人之首。你请求何种刑罚？”

“恳请夫人宣判：终身流放。”

“可是流去哪里——又如何放逐呢？”

===Original===

“It matters not, so long as it is forth of Britain.”

“But you cannot be eternally watching the ports.”

“Nay, but he will not come back. There is a brotherhood which has already aided me﻿—your Grace knows nothing of them, but they know everything of your Grace. It is the brotherhood of Old England, and is sure as the judgment of God. To that charge we will commit him. They will see him forth of England, and they will make certain that he does not return.”

===Chinese===

“去哪里都不要紧，只要在不列颠以外的地方。”

“可你们总不能世世代代守住各个港口啊。”

“不必。他回不来了。有一个兄弟会早已帮过我的忙——夫人对它一无所知，它却对夫人知根知底。那便是老英格兰的兄弟会，它如上帝的裁断一般确凿无疑。我们要把他托付给这个会。他们会送他出英格兰，也会笃定叫他回不来。”

===Original===

Kyd’s face had lightened, as if he saw a prospect of avoiding the full rigours of the sentence. The Duchess marked it and frowned, but he misread her mood, which he thought one of displeasure at Alastair’s plan. He adopted an air of humble candour.

“Hear me, your Grace,” he implored. “It’s a queer story mine, but a juster than you think. I’m not claiming to be a perfect character, and I’m not denying that I take a canny bit profit when I find it, like an eident body. The honest truth is that I don’t care a plack for politics one side or the other, and it’s nothing to me which king sits on the throne. My job’s to be a trusty servant of His Grace, and no man can say that I’m not zealous in that cause. Ay, and there’s another cause I’m sworn to, and that’s Scotland. I’m like auld Lockhart o’ Carnwath﻿—my heart can hold just the one land at a time. I call God Almighty to witness that I never did ill to a kindly Scot, and if I’ve laboured to put a spoke in the Chevalier’s coach-wheels, it’s because him and his wild caterans are like to play hell with my puir auld country. Show me what is best for Scotland, and Nicholas Kyd will spend his last bodle and shed his last drop of blood to compass it.”

There was an odd earnestness, even a note of honesty, in the man’s appeal, but it found no acceptance. The lady shivered.

===Chinese===

基德的脸色松快了些，仿佛看到一线生机，可以逃过这判决的全部严酷。公爵夫人看在眼里，皱起眉来；他却会错了意，只当她是不满阿拉斯泰尔的处置。他摆出一副谦卑坦率的姿态。

“听我说一句，夫人。”他恳求道，“我这段故事听着古怪，却比您想的要正派些。我不自命完人，也不否认，碰上机会总要像勤谨人那样，捞一点稳当的好处。老实话是，两边的政事，我一个普拉克铜子儿也不在乎，宝座上坐的是哪位君王，与我何干。我的本分，是做公爵大人的一名可靠仆人，这份差事的尽心尽力，没人能挑我的不是。是啊，我另有一桩效忠的大事，那就是苏格兰。我就像卡恩沃斯的老洛克哈特——我这颗心，一时只装得下一个国土。我请全能的上帝作证，我从没亏待过一个厚道的苏格兰人；我若是一心要在‘骑士’的马车轮里插上一根辐条，那是因为他和他那帮狂野的山地劫匪，眼看要把我们可怜的故国糟蹋个稀烂。只要指给我看什么对苏格兰最好，尼古拉斯·基德情愿花尽最后一个波德尔铜子、流尽最后一滴血去办成它。”

这人的申诉里有种古怪的恳切，甚至有一丝诚实的调子，却没有人领受。夫人打了个寒噤。

===Original===

“If you can get him abroad, sir,” she addressed Alastair, and her voice was hard as granite, “I think I can promise you that he will not return. My arm is a weak woman’s, but it strikes far. His services will be soon forgotten by Mr. Pelham, but Kitty of Queensberry does not forget his offences. Though I live for fifty years more, I will make it my constant business to keep the rogue in exile.”

The man seemed to meditate. Doubtless he reflected that even the malice of a great lady could not keep him forever out of the country. She might die, or her husband lose his power, and politics would be politics to a Whig Government. One of those who looked on divined his thoughts, for a soft voice spoke.

“I do not think that Greyhouses will ever again be a pleasant habitation for the gentleman. Has he forgotten the case of the laird of Champertoun?”

Kyd started violently.

===Chinese===

“先生，你们若能把他送出国去，”她转向阿拉斯泰尔，声音硬得像花岗岩，“我想我可以向你担保：他回不来。我这条胳膊是弱女子的胳膊，可它打得很远。佩勒姆先生很快便会忘掉他的那些效力，昆斯伯里的基蒂却忘不掉他的罪愆。哪怕我再活五十年，看住这恶棍不叫他回国，都将是我一门不辍的常课。”

这人似乎沉吟起来。无疑他在盘算：纵然是一位贵妇的恶意，也未必困得住他一世。她兴许会死，她丈夫兴许会失势，而对一个辉格党政府来说，政治终究还是政治。旁观者中有一位猜透了他的心思，因为一个柔和的声音开口了。

“我看，格雷豪斯于这位先生，往后再不会是一处宜居的居所了。他忘了钱珀顿（Champertoun）领主那一桩案子么？”

基德猛然一惊。

===Original===

“Or the goodman of Heriotside?” The voice was gentle and soothing, but it seemed to wake acute terror in one hearer.

“Men die and their memories, but when all of us are dust the Bog-blitters will still cry on Lammermuir. I think that Mr. Kyd has heard them before at Greyhouses. He will not desire to hear them again.”

The Spainneach had risen and stood beside Kyd, and from the back of the room two of the Spoonbills advanced like guardian shadows. The big man in the rich clothes had shrunk to a shapeless bundle in the chair, his face grey and his eyes hot and tragic. “Not that,” he cried, “don’t banish me from my native land. I’ll go anywhere you please in the bounds o’ Scotland﻿—to St. Kilda, like Lady Grange, or to the wildest Hielands, but let me feel that I’m in my own country. I tell you my heart’s buried aneath Scots heather. I’ll die if you twine the Lammermuirs and me. Anything you like, my lady, but let me bide at home.”

He found only cold eyes and silence. Then he seemed to brace himself to self-command. His face was turned to the Duchess, and he sat up in his chair, settled his cravat, and with a shaking hand poured himself a glass of wine. His air was now ingratiating and sentimental, and he wiped a tear from his eye.

===Chinese===

“还有赫里奥特赛德（Heriotside）当家人的那一桩呢？”那声音温柔和婉，听在一人耳中，却似唤起了切肤的恐怖。

“人会死，人的记性也会死；可等到我们尽数化作尘土，沼怪帮还将在拉默缪尔上空哀号。我想基德先生从前在格雷豪斯就听见过他们。他不会再想听见第二回了。”

西班牙人已经起身，立在基德身旁，屋子后头又有两名篦鹭会的人，像两道护法的暗影走上前来。那个锦衣的大块头，已缩成椅上一团没了形状的肉裹，脸色灰败，两眼灼热而凄惶。“别这样，”他喊道，“别把我从故土上放逐出去。苏格兰地界之内，你们要我去哪儿都行——学格兰奇夫人那样去圣基尔达也好，去最荒凉的高地也好，只要让我觉出自己还在自己的国度。告诉你们，我这颗心就埋在苏格兰的石楠底下。你们若把拉默缪尔和我拆开，我就活不成了。夫人，什么都依你，只求让我留在家里。”

他得到的只有一双双冷眼和一片沉默。随后他似是强自振作，把脸转向公爵夫人，在椅子上坐直了身子，整了整领结，用一只发颤的手给自己斟了杯酒。他此刻的神态，又讨好又伤感，还抹去了眼角的一滴泪。

===Original===

“*Nos patriae fines et dulcia liquimus arva*,” he said. “I’ll have to comfort myself with philosophy, for man’s life is more howes than heights. Heigho, but I’ll miss Scotland. I’m like the old ballad:

> ‘Happy the craw
 

 That biggs i’ the Totten Shaw
 

 And drinks o’ the Water of Dye,
 

 For nae mair may I.’ ”

The words, the tone, the broken air gave to Alastair a moment of compunction. But in Mr. Johnson they roused another feeling. Half raising himself from his chair, he shook his fist at the speaker.

“Sir,” he cried, “you are worse than a rogue, you are a canting rogue. You would have driven twenty honest men into unmerited exile by your infamies and had no pity on them, but you crave pity for yourself when you are justly banished. I have sympathy with many kinds of rascal, but none with yours. Your crimes are the greater because you pretend to sensibility. With you, sir, patriotism is the last refuge of the scoundrel.”

===Chinese===

“Nos patriae fines et dulcia liquimus arva——别矣，故国的疆界，甜美的田畴。”他说道。“我只得拿哲学宽解自己了：人的一生，本是洼多过高。唉唉，我会想念苏格兰的。我正像那支古谣里唱的：”

> “快活的乌鸦啊，
> 筑巢在托滕树丛，
> 饮着戴伊河的水——
> 而我，再不能这般了。”

这词句，这腔调，这断断续续的调子，一时叫阿拉斯泰尔心里掠过一丝不忍。可在约翰逊先生胸中，它们激起的却是另一种情感。他从椅子里半撑起身子，朝说话的人挥起了拳头。

“先生，”他喝道，“你比恶棍更坏，你是个假仁假义的恶棍。你那桩桩无耻勾当，本要把二十个清白人逼上不白之冤的流放路，你对他们没有半分怜悯；轮到你自己罪有应得地遭放逐，你倒乞怜起来了。各色恶徒我多有几分同情，唯独你这一种，没有。你的罪孽更深重，因为你偏要假装一副多情善感。在你身上，先生，爱国心正是恶棍最后的藏身窟。”

===Original===

Alastair picked the saddlebag from below the table, and emptied its remaining contents in the fire.

“Except what I keep for His Highness’s eye, let ashes be the fate of this treason. There is your baggage, sir. You may want it on your long journey.”

The hand that lifted it was Edom’s.

“I’ll get the other pockmantie ready, sir,” he said to Kyd in the grave tone of a good servant. “Your horse is no just in the best fettle for the road, but I ride lichter nor you, and ye can take mine.”

“But you do not propose to continue in his service?” Alastair cried in astonishment. “See, man, you have saved my life, and I will take charge of your fortunes.”

Edom halted at the door. “I thank ye, sir, for your guidwill. But I was born at Greyhouses, and my faither and his faither afore him served the family. It’s no a sma’ thing like poalitics that’ll gar a Kyd and a Lowrie take different roads.”

===Chinese===

阿拉斯泰尔从桌子底下拎起鞍囊，把里头剩下的文书尽数倒进火里。

“留着呈殿下御览的除外——这份叛卖，就让灰烬去做它的下场吧。先生，这是您的行李。漫漫长路上，您或许用得着它。”

提起它的那只手，是以东的。

“我这就去把另一只行囊收拾好，老爷。”他用一个好仆人庄重的口吻对基德说，“您那匹马赶起长路来，气力不算顶好。不过我比您骑得轻，您就骑我这匹吧。”

“你难道不打算继续伺候他了？”阿拉斯泰尔惊呼道，“听着，伙计，你救过我的命，你的前程交给我来照管。”

以东在门口站住。“多谢您的好意，先生。可我生在格雷豪斯，我父亲，还有我父亲的父亲，都是伺候这一家的。政治这点儿小事，还拆散不了一个基德和一个洛里，叫他们各奔东西。”

## XVI Bids Farewell to an English Lady / 十六、别英格兰女士

===Original===
Duchess Kitty descended from her chair of justice and came to the fireside, where she let her furs slip from her and stood, a figure of white porcelain, warming her feet at the blaze.

“There was some word of a lady,” she said.

Johnson, too, had risen, and though the man’s cheeks were gaunt with hunger he had no eye for the food on the table. His mind seemed to be in travail with difficult thoughts.

“The lady, madam,” he groaned. “She is in her chamber, unsuspecting. Her husband should be here also. He may enter at any moment.”

===Chinese===
基蒂公爵夫人走下她那公案之椅，踱到炉火边，任一身裘衣从肩头滑落，立在火前烘着双足，俨然一尊白瓷的人像。

「方才似乎提到过一位女士，」她说。

约翰逊也已起身；这人两颊饿得深陷，却对桌上的食物看也不看一眼。他的心神仿佛正被艰难的思虑煎熬着。

「那位小姐，夫人，」他呻吟道。「她还在自己房里，浑然不觉。她丈夫本也该在这里。他随时都可能进来。」

===Original===
“He has fled,” said Alastair. “Fled, as I take it, to the Whig Dukes for his reward. The man is revealed at last, and his wife must disown him or be tainted by his guilt.”

The news seemed to affect Johnson painfully. He cast himself into a chair, which creaked under his weight, and covered his eyes with his hands.

“Why in God’s name did you suffer it?” he asked fiercely of Alastair. “I had another plan.﻿ ﻿… I would have brought the dog to repentance.”

“I will yet bring him to justice,” said Alastair grimly. “I have a forewarning of it, and tomorrow or next week or next year he will stand up before my sword.”

The words gave no comfort to Johnson. He rolled his melancholy eyes and groaned again. “ ’Twill break her heart,” he lamented. “She will know of his infamy﻿—it cannot be hid from her.﻿ ﻿… Oh, why, why!”

===Chinese===
「他已经逃了，」阿拉斯泰尔说。「依我看，是投辉格党诸公爵领赏去了。此人终于露了原形；他的妻子不与他决绝，便要受他罪愆的玷污。」

这消息似乎令约翰逊痛苦不堪。他一头跌坐进椅子里，椅身在他重量下咯吱作响；他双手掩住了眼睛。

「看在上帝份上，你为何竟容许此事？」他向阿拉斯泰尔厉声质问。「我原另有计较……我本要引那条狗悔改的。」

「我仍要叫他伏法，」阿拉斯泰尔冷峻地说。「对此我有一种预感——明日，或下星期，或来年，他总会站到我的剑前。」

这话没有给约翰逊半点安慰。他转着一双忧郁的眼睛，又呻吟起来。「这会叫她心碎的，」他悲叹道。「她终会知道他的丑行——那是瞒她不住的……唉，为什么，为什么啊！」

===Original===
Alastair spoke to the Duchess. “You will tell Lady Norreys that her husband has gone to the Prince. No more. I will make certain that he does not return to Weston, though I have to drag him with my own hands out of Cumberland’s closet.﻿ ﻿… Forgive me, madam, if I appear to command, but this is a tangled matter. Pray take her with you to Amesbury, and keep her out of Oxfordshire, till I send word that it is safe. She must not go to Weston or Chastlecote till she has the news of his death. I will contrive that he die, and ’tis for you to contrive that she thinks his death a hero’s.”

The Duchess mused. “You are a singular pair of gentlemen, and wondrous tender to the child’s feelings. I can see you are both in love with her. Prithee lead me at once to this enchainer of hearts.”

The Spainneach’s face appeared in the doorway, and his hand beckoned to Alastair.

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔向公爵夫人说道：「您只对诺里斯夫人说，她丈夫投王子殿下去了。不必多言。我自会担保他回不了韦斯顿——哪怕得我亲手把他从坎伯兰的私室里拖出来……夫人，恕我言语间像是发号施令，只是这桩事实在千缠百结。请您务必携她同赴埃姆斯伯里，在未得我平安的口信之前，务必使她远离牛津郡。在她闻悉他的死讯之前，万不可让她去韦斯顿，也不可去查斯特科特。教他死，是我的事；教她把他的死认作英雄之死，便有劳夫人安排了。」

公爵夫人沉吟片刻。「你们二位真是少见的一对君子，对这孩子的情意体贴入微。我瞧得出，你们两个都钟情于她。请即刻领我去见见这位缚人心魄的佳人罢。」

西班牙人的脸在门口一现，他的手向阿拉斯泰尔招了招。

===Original===
“My lady’s woman has descended and is distracted by the sight of strange servants. It seems her mistress desires Sir John’s company, which was promised for this hour, and the maid will not return without a clear answer.”

“Say that he is detained,” said Alastair, “and add that the Duchess of Queensberry begs the lady’s permission to wait upon her.”

He turned to the two at the fireplace. “Madam, ’tis time for your mission of charity.”

“Repeat me my lesson,” she said, standing before him as demure as a schoolgirl.

===Chinese===
「夫人的贴身女仆下来了，见了满宅生仆，正惊惶无措。看来她家小姐想要约翰·诺里斯爵士作陪——原约好这个时辰的——那女仆非要个明白答复才肯回去。」

「就说他被事情绊住了，」阿拉斯泰尔说，「再加一句：昆斯伯里公爵夫人恳请小姐俯允，容她前来拜望。」

他转身向着炉边的两人。「夫人，该去行您的慈善使命了。」

「把我的功课再背给我听，」她说，站在他面前，端庄得像个女学生。

===Original===
“You will inform the lady that Sir John Norreys has been summoned in great haste to join his Prince, and has left incontinent, trusting to her loyal heart to condone his seeming heartlessness. Say that he will find means to keep her informed of his welfare. Then press her to travel southward with you, pointing out to her that the war moves southward and she will be travelling the same way as Sir John.”

“ ’Tis a parcel of lies,” said the Duchess, “and I am a poor dissembler.”

Alastair shrugged his shoulders. “The cause is good and your Grace is a finished actress, when you please.”

===Chinese===
「您就这样禀告小姐：约翰·诺里斯爵士奉紧急之召，去投他王子殿下，即刻启程，惟望她一片忠心，宽宥他表面上的无情。再说，他必设法让她时时得知他的安好。随后便劝她与您同路南下，向她点明：战事正在南移，她这一路，恰与约翰爵士同一个方向。」

「这分明是一派谎言，」公爵夫人说，「偏偏我又是个不高明的骗子。」

阿拉斯泰尔耸了耸肩。「所事者正，而夫人您呢，只要愿意，便是位炉火纯青的戏子。」

===Original===
“But is it not cruel kindness?” she asked. “Were it not better that she should know the truth of her husband, that she might grieve the less when she has news of his end, which I see writ plain in your eyes, sir?”

Johnson broke in. “A thousand times no, madam. If she learns that her trust has been ill placed, her heart will break. She can bear sorrow but not shame. Believe me, I have studied that noble lady.”

“So be it. Have the goodness, Captain Maclean, to escort me to this paragon.”

Alastair gave her his arm, and, instructed by Johnson﻿—who followed in the wake﻿—conducted the Duchess up the first flight of the staircase to a broad gallery from which the main bedrooms opened. At the end, where were Claudia’s rooms, the maid, Mrs. Peckover, stood with a lighted candle to receive them.

===Chinese===
「可这不是残忍的仁慈么？」她问。「让她知道她丈夫的真相，岂不更好？将来她得到他的凶耗时——先生，这凶耗明明白白写在你眼里——她的悲痛也可轻些。」

约翰逊插了进来。「万万不可，夫人，万万不可。她若得知自己错付了信任，心便碎了。悲哀她受得住，耻辱她受不住。相信我，这位高贵的夫人，我已细细揣摩过了。」

「就这样罢。麦克莱恩上尉，有劳您护送我去见这位绝代佳人。」

阿拉斯泰尔递上手臂，在约翰逊——他随在后面——的指点下，引着公爵夫人登上楼梯的第一段，来到一条宽阔的长廊，正屋的几间卧房皆由此开门。长廊尽头是克劳迪娅的房间，女仆佩克欧弗太太正执一支点亮的蜡烛，候在那里迎他们。

===Original===
But suddenly they halted and stood motionless, listening. A voice was singing, the voice which had sung “Diana” at the Sleeping Deer. The door must have been ajar, for the song rose clear in the corridor, sung low but with such a tension of feeling that every word and bar seemed to vibrate in the air. The Duchess, clinging to Alastair’s arm, stood rigid as a statue. “O Love,” the voice sang﻿—

> “O Love, they wrong thee much
 

 That say thy sweet is bitter.
 

 When thy rich fruit is such
 

 As nothing can be sweeter.
 

 Fair house of joy and bliss,
 

 Where truest treasure is,
 

 I do adore thee.”

===Chinese===
可他们忽然停住，伫立不动，侧耳倾听。有个声音正在歌唱——正是那在睡鹿客栈唱过「狄安娜」的嗓子。房门想必虚掩着，歌声清清楚楚地浮上走廊，唱得低沉，情感却绷得那样紧，仿佛每一个字、每一小节都在空气中震颤。公爵夫人紧紧挽住阿拉斯泰尔的手臂，僵立如一尊雕像。「啊，爱情，」那声音唱道——

> 「啊，爱情，世人太冤屈了你，
 

 　竟说你的甘甜原是苦涩。
 

 　可你丰美的果实如此，
 

 　世间再无一物更甜。
 

 　欢乐与极乐的美宅，
 

 　至真宝藏之所在，
 

 　我崇敬你。」

===Original===
The voice hung on the lines for an instant in a tremor of passion. Then it continued to a falling close﻿—

> “I know thee what thou art,
 

 I serve thee with my heart,
 

 And fall before thee.”

“I think you do well to be tender of her,” the Duchess whispered. “Adieu! I will descend presently and report.”

===Chinese===
那声音在词句上萦留了一瞬，因激情而微颤。随后它接了下去，渐渐落成一个低回的收束——

> 「我识得你是什么，
 

 　我以全心事奉你，
 

 　俯伏在你面前。」

「我想你们这样怜惜她，是对的，」公爵夫人低声道。「再会！我稍候就下去回话。」

===Original===
The heavy hand of Johnson clutched his arm before he had reached the foot of the staircase.

“Did you hear that?” the tutor questioned savagely. “She sings of love like an angel of God, and her love is betrayed.” He forced Alastair before him, and shut the door of the dining-room behind them. The candles still burned brightly amid the remains of supper, but the logs on the hearth had smouldered low.

Johnson was become the strangest of figures, his sallow face flushed, his eyes rolling like a man in a fit, and a nervousness like palsy affecting his hands and shoulders. But Alastair saw none of these things, for his attention was held by something masterful and noble in the man’s face.

“Sit down, Alastair Maclean,” he said, “and listen to one who loves you as a brother. Sir, we are both servants of one lady and that is a bond stricter than consanguinity. I am poor and diseased and disconsidered, but I have a duty laid upon me which comes direct from Omnipotence. Sir, I command you to examine into your heart.”

===Chinese===
他还没走到楼梯脚下，约翰逊那只沉重的手已经攥住了他的手臂。

「你听见了么？」这位家庭教师声色俱厉地质问。「她唱起爱情来，像上帝的一位天使，而她的爱情正被人出卖。」他把阿拉斯泰尔推在自己前面，两人进了餐室，随手把门关上。烛火仍在晚餐的残席间明亮地燃着，炉床上的柴却已焖得将尽了。

约翰逊已变成一副再古怪不过的样子：灰黄的脸上泛着红，眼珠滴溜乱转，像发痫症的人，一阵瘫痉似的颤动袭着他的双手和肩头。可这些阿拉斯泰尔一概没有看见，他的注意力全被那人脸上一种威严而高贵的东西摄住了。

「坐下，阿拉斯泰尔·麦克莱恩，」他说，「听一个爱你如手足的人说句话。先生，我们两人是同一位小姐的仆人，这层纽带，比血亲还要严。我又穷、又有病、又受人轻贱，可我肩上负着一项职责，是全能者亲手交付于我的。先生，我命令你，检视你自己的心。」

===Original===
He laid a hand on the young man’s arm, a hand that trembled violently.

“What are your intentions toward Sir John Norreys?”

“I mean to find him, and, when found, to fight with him and kill him.”

“For what reason?”

“Because he is a traitor to my Prince.”

“And yet you did not press for the death of the man Kyd, who was the principal whereas Sir John was but the tool. Come, sir, be honest with me; why is the extreme penalty decreed to the less guilty?”

===Chinese===
他把一只手按在年轻人臂上，那只手抖得厉害。

「你对约翰·诺里斯爵士意欲何为？」

「我要找到他；找到了，便与他交手，杀死他。」

「为着什么缘由？」

「因为他背叛了我的王子殿下。」

「可是对基德其人，你倒不曾力主处死——他才是首恶，约翰爵士不过一件工具。来，先生，对我说实话；为何极刑反倒判给了罪轻的一个？」

===Original===
Alastair did not answer at first. Then he said﻿—

“Because Sir John Norreys is the husband of a lady to whom the knowledge of his true nature would be death.”

“That reply is nearer the truth, but still far from complete honesty.”

Alastair had a sudden flame of wrath. “Do you accuse me of lying?” he asked angrily.

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔起初没有作答。随后他说——

「因为约翰·诺里斯爵士是一位小姐的丈夫；她若知道了他的真面目，便无异于死。」

「这话近于真相了，可离十足的坦白还远。」

阿拉斯泰尔腾起一股怒火。「你是指控我说谎么？」他愠怒地问。

===Original===
Johnson’s face did not change. “Sir, all men are liars,” he said. “I strive to make you speak truth to your own soul. The death of Sir John is intended merely to save the lady from the pain of disgrace? On your honour, for no other purpose?”

Alastair did not reply. The other sank his harsh voice to a gentler and kindlier pitch, and the hand on the young man’s arm from a menace became a caress.

“I will answer for you. You love the lady. Nay, I do not blame you, for all the world must love her. I love her most deeply, but not as you, for you love with hope, and look some day to make her yours. Therefore you would slay Sir John, and to yourself you say that ’tis to save her from shame, but before God, you know that ’tis to rid yourself of a rival.”

The man’s eyes were compelling, and his utter honesty was like a fire that burned all shamefastness from the air. Alastair’s silence was assent.

===Chinese===
约翰逊面不改色。「先生，人人皆是说谎者，」他说。「我不过是要努力教你对自己的灵魂说真话。约翰爵士的死，只是为了使那小姐免遭耻辱之痛？凭你的名誉起誓，别无其他目的了么？」

阿拉斯泰尔没有作声。对方把粗厉的嗓音沉下来，放得又温和又慈爱；按在年轻人臂上的那只手，也由威胁变成了抚慰。

「我替你回答罢。你爱那位小姐。不，我并不怪你，因为全世界都必然爱她。我爱她爱得极深，只是与你不同——你是怀着希望去爱的，指望有朝一日使她归于你。所以你要杀约翰爵士；你对自己说，这是为使她免于耻辱，可在上帝面前，你知道，这是为除去你的情敌。」

那人的目光令人无从抗拒，他那彻底的坦诚像一团火，把空气里一切羞怯都烧尽了。阿拉斯泰尔的沉默，便是默认。

===Original===
“Sir, a lover seeks above all things the good of his mistress. If indeed you love her﻿—and it is honourable that you should﻿—I implore you to consider further in the matter. We are agreed that it is necessary to save her from the shame of the knowledge of her husband’s treason, for it is a proud lady who would feel disgrace sharper than death. If that were all, I would bid you godspeed, for Sir John’s death would serve that purpose, and you and she are fit mates, being alike young and highly born. After the natural period of mourning was over, you might fairly look to espouse her. But ah, sir, that is not all.”

He got to his feet in his eagerness and stood above the young man, one hand splayed on the table, as he had stood that afternoon at the Sleeping Deer.

“Listen, sir. I have watched that child in her going out and coming in, in her joys and melancholies, in her every mood of caprice and earnestness﻿—watched with the quick eye of one who is half lover, half parent. And I have formed most certain conclusions about that high nature. She trusts but once and that wholly; she will love but once, and that with a passion like a consuming fire. If she knew the truth about Sir John, she would never trust mankind again. On that we are agreed. But I go further, sir. If she lost him, she would never love another, but go inconsolable to her grave. It is the way of certain choice spirits.”

===Chinese===
「先生，一个钟情人首要寻求的，是他心上人的幸福。你若果真爱她——你爱她，也是光明正大的事——我求你把这事再掂量掂量。我们已然商定：必须使她免于知晓丈夫叛逆的耻辱，因为她是一位骄傲的小姐，耻辱在她，比死亡更为锋利。倘若仅止于此，我便要祝你一帆风顺了，因为约翰爵士一死，正可了结此事；你与她又是般配的一对，同样年轻，同样出身高贵。服满人之常情的丧期之后，你尽可指望娶她为妻。可是，唉，先生，事情不止于此。」

他急切之下站起身来，俯立在年轻人上方，一只手摊开按在桌上，一如那天下午他在睡鹿客栈时的模样。

「听着，先生。那孩子的一出一入、一喜一忧，她每一回的心血来潮与一本正经，我都在旁看着——用的是一双快眼，一半是钟情人，一半是父亲。对那份高贵的天性，我已得出千真万确的结论。她只信托一次，信托了便是全部；她只爱一次，那一爱便如焚身的烈火。她若知道约翰爵士的真相，便再也不会信托世人。这一点我们意见相同。可我还要进一步，先生。她若失去了他，便再不会爱上旁人，只有抱恨无穷，直至进了坟墓。某些精选的灵魂，天生便是这样的。」

===Original===
Alastair made a gesture of dissent.

“Sir, did you not hear her singing?” Johnson asked. “Answer me, heard you ever such a joy of surrender in a mortal voice?”

Alastair could not deny it, for the passionate trilling was still in his ear.

“But your reasoning is flawed,” he said. “Granted that my Lady Norreys has given her love once and for all; yet if Sir John remain alive she will presently discover his shame, and for the rest of her days be tormented with honour wounded through affection.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔做了个不以为然的手势。

「先生，你难道没有听见她唱歌么？」约翰逊问。「回答我：一个凡人的嗓音里，你可曾听过那样一种甘愿委身的欢悦？」

阿拉斯泰尔无法否认，那激情颤动的歌声仍在他耳中。

「可你的道理有破绽，」他说。「就算诺里斯夫人已把她的爱一劳永逸地付了出去；然而约翰爵士一日活着，她迟早要发现他的丑行，从此余生，都要受着那因情爱而伤残的名节的啮咬。」

===Original===
“It need not be,” said Johnson, and his voice had sunk to the level of argument from the heights of appeal. “I have studied both of them during the past weeks, and this is my conclusion. She has made a false image of him which she adores, but unless the falsity be proved to the world by some violent revelation she will not discover it. She is a happy self-deceiver, and to the end﻿—unless forcibly enlightened﻿—will take his common clay for gold. As for him﻿—well, he is clay and not gunpowder. He has been moulded into infamy by a stronger man and by his ancestral greed﻿—for, judging by the family here, his race is one of misers. But let him be sufficiently alarmed and shown where his interest lies, and he will relapse to the paths of decorum. Good he will never be, little he must always be, but he may also be respectable. He will not lose his halo in his lady’s eyes and they may live out their time happily, and if God wills some portion of the mother’s quality may descend to the children.”

The thought to Alastair was hideously repellent. To whitewash such a rogue and delude such a lady! Better surely a painful enlightenment than this deceit. He comforted himself with the reflection that it was impossible.

“But by this time Sir John Norreys is with his paymaster, and the mischief is done.”

===Chinese===
「未必至此，」约翰逊说，嗓音已从恳求的高处，落到了说理的平地上。「过去这几个星期，我把他们两个都揣摩透了，我的结论是这样。她亲手造了他一尊假像，奉之如神；可若没有一场雷霆万钧的揭发，把那虚假昭示于世，她便发现不了。她是个幸福的自欺者，除非被人强拉着睁开眼，到死都会把他寻常的黏土当作黄金。至于他——唉，他是黏土，不是火药。他是被一个更强的人、再加上他祖传的贪吝，捏进了这桩丑行里去的——瞧瞧这一家子便可知，他这一族人原是悭吝成性的。可只要吓他个够，再点明他的利益所在，他自会缩回体面的老路上去。好人他永远做不成，器量小他一辈子免不了，可他也未尝不能做个体面人。他在他小姐眼里不会失了光环，他们两口子可以和和美美把日子过完；天意若许，做母亲的一份品性，或者还能传给儿女几分。」

这念头在阿拉斯泰尔只觉可憎已极。给这样一个无赖粉饰，又把这样一位小姐蒙在鼓里！与其这般欺瞒，毋宁一场痛苦的醒悟。他自宽自解地想道：这断然是行不通的。

「可事到如今，约翰·诺里斯爵士早已到了他发赏钱的东家那里，祸事已经铸成了。」

===Original===
“Not so,” said Johnson. “Sir John does not ride to Kingston or to Richmond but to Cumberland himself, and he lies far in the south. He may yet be overtaken and dissuaded.”

“By whom?”

“By you, sir.”

Alastair laughed loud and bitterly.

===Chinese===
「不然，」约翰逊说。「约翰爵士这趟不是投金斯顿，也不是投里士满，而是直奔坎伯兰本人去的，此刻他还在南边老远处歇着呢。还追得上，也还劝得转。」

「由谁去劝？」

「由你去，先生。」

阿拉斯泰尔放声笑了，笑得又响又苦。

===Original===
“Are you mad, sir? I journey at once to the Prince’s camp, for I have news for him that may determine his future conduct. Already I am late in starting. I must order my horse, and bid farewell to the ladies.” He moved to the door, and cried instructions to the Spainneach, who smoked a cigarro by the hall fire.

Johnson seized him by the lapels of his coat. “I implore you, sir, by the mercy of God. Follow Sir John and persuade him, compel him, at the sword’s point, if need be. The happiness of my darling child depends on it. If you do not go, I must go myself. The Prince’s news can wait, for it will be only a few hours’ delay at the most. What does it matter whether or not he be in London a day earlier, compared to the well-being of an immortal soul? I beseech you, sir, for the love of Christ Who redeemed us﻿—”

“Tush, man, you are raving,” Alastair broke in, and moved to the half-open door. At that moment the Duchess’s voice sounded on the stairs.

“Come up, sir,” she said. “My lady will receive you before you go, and she bids you bring the other, the clumsy fellow whose name I know not.”

===Chinese===
「你疯了么，先生？我即刻便要赶赴王子殿下的大营，我带去的消息，说不定能定他日后的行止。我动身已经迟了。得叫人备马，还得去向两位女眷辞行。」他走向门口，向在大堂炉边抽着雪茄的西班牙人高声吩咐了几句。

约翰逊一把攥住他的衣襟。「我求你了，先生，看在上帝的慈悲份上。追上约翰爵士，去劝他，逼他，必要时不妨剑锋相向。我那心肝孩子的幸福，全系在此。你若不去，我便自己去。王子殿下的消息等得，至多不过误几个时辰。与一条不朽灵魂的福祉相比，殿下早一天晚一天进伦敦，又算得什么？我恳求你，先生，看在救赎了我们的基督之爱的份上——」

「呸，你这是在说胡话，」阿拉斯泰尔打断他，朝那扇半开的门走去。正在此时，楼梯上响起公爵夫人的声音。

「上来罢，先生，」她说。「小姐要在你行前见你一面，还嘱你把另一位也带上——就是那个连名字我也不知的笨手笨脚的家伙。」

===Original===
Duchess Kitty met him at the door of Claudia’s chamber.

“Oh, my dear, she is the very archangel of angels, and of an innocence to make one weep. She will come with me to Amesbury. She dotes on her Sir John and will weary me, I fear, with her rhapsodies, but I am nobly complaisant and flatter her passion. I fear you stand no chance, sir. Her heart is wholly in the rogue’s keeping. Enter, for she awaits you.”

In the dim panelled room lit by many candles and a leaping fire the figure of the girl sitting up in the great four-poster bed stood out with a startling brilliance. Madam Claudia was dressed to receive him, as she had been in the midnight colloquy at Flambury, in a furred bed-gown and a nightcap of lace and pink satin. But her brown eyes were no longer pools of dancing light. She held out a hand to Alastair with a little sigh.

“I rejoice that you are free from your t-troubles, sir,” she said. “ ’Twas a shameful charge, and I did not credit it, nor truly did Sir John. And justice, they tell me, has been done to the traitor! Sir John was deceived like the rest of you, and ’tis a cunning rogue that can hoodwink Sir John. You are at the end of your mission, sir, and can now engage in the honest business of war.”

===Chinese===
基蒂公爵夫人在克劳迪娅的房门口迎着他。

「哦，我的亲爱的，她真是天使中的天使长，那份天真，直叫人落泪。她肯随我到埃姆斯伯里去了。她一心恋着她那位约翰爵士，我怕要被她的痴话缠乏了；不过我大大方方地迁就她，还给她那股热情添柴呢。我怕你是没有指望了，先生。她那颗心，整个儿押在那个恶棍手里。进去罢，她正等着你呢。」

在许多支蜡烛与一炉跳动的炉火映照的幽暗板壁房间里，那姑娘端坐在四柱大床上的身影，焕发得惊人地明丽。克劳迪娅小姐为见他而穿戴齐整，一如弗兰伯里午夜密谈时的模样：一件毛皮晨衣，一顶蕾丝镶边的粉缎睡帽。可她那双棕色眼睛，已不复是两泓跃动的光。她轻轻叹了口气，向阿拉斯泰尔伸出一只手。

「您脱了那桩烦、烦难了，先生，我真欢喜，」她说。「那原是个不名誉的指控，我素来不信；说实在的，约翰爵士也不曾信。如今那奸人，他们说，已经伏法了！约翰爵士同你们大家一样，都是受了蒙蔽——能蒙得住约翰爵士的，可真是个狡猾的恶棍呢。您的差事到头了，先生，往后便可去从事战争这桩光明正大的正业了。」

===Original===
“And for yourself, my lady?”

“I, too, take the road,” she said. “You have heard of her G-grace’s kindness. I am fortunate to travel in such g-gentle company. So it is farewell, sir. You ride this night to the Prince, who is at Derby? My dear Sir John has preceded you there. Oh, would that I could be with him!” And with a morsel of cambric she dried a rising tear.

“And you, Puffin,” she asked, catching sight of Johnson. “Do you travel south with us?”

“Nay, madam, I go with Captain Maclean to the Prince’s camp.”

===Chinese===
「那您自己呢，小姐？」

「我也上路呢，」她说。「她公、公爵夫人的恩典，您已经听说了。我能有这般温、温雅的旅伴，真是好福气。那么就此别过了，先生。您今夜便骑马去王子殿下那里——他在德比，是么？我亲爱的约翰爵士已先您一步到了那边。唉，但愿我能同他在一处才好！」说着，她用一方细麻纱帕子拭去将溢的泪。

「还有您呢，老风箱，」她一眼望见约翰逊，问道。「您同我们一起南下么？」

「不，小姐，我随麦克莱恩上尉去王子殿下的大营。」

===Original===
“Bravo!” she cried. “You have declared yourself at last. God prosper you, my gallant gentlemen. I will be there to cheer when you ride behind the Prince into London.”

Alastair was scarcely conscious of her words. He saw only her wild wet eyes, compared to which those of the pretty Duchess were like pebbles to stars. It was the child in her that overwhelmed him, the appealing child, trusting utterly with no thought but that all the world was well-disposed to her and her love. He had known many women in his time, though none had touched his cold fancy, but he had never before seen woman’s face transfigured with so innocent an exaltation. The sadness in it was only the anxiety of a soul that trembled for the perpetuation of an unbelievable joy. He was nothing to her, nor was any man except the one; the virgin garden of her heart was enclosed with impenetrable defences. The truth moved him not to irritation, but to pity and a protecting care. He could not mar a thing so rare, and if its foundations were rotten he would be in league to strengthen them. For the moment he was not the lover, but the guardian, who would perjure his soul to keep alive a childish paradise.

He raised her hand and kissed it. “I am your very humble and devoted servant,” he said. And then she did a thing for which he was not prepared, for with a little cry she put her hands over her eyes and wept.

===Chinese===
「好啊！」她叫道。「你们总算表明了心迹。上帝保佑你们，我英勇的两位先生。待你们跟在王子殿下身后骑马进伦敦时，我必在那里为你们喝彩。」

阿拉斯泰尔几乎没听清她的话。他只看见她那双湿润放光的眼睛——比起它们，那位娇美公爵夫人的眼睛，便如顽石之于星辰。慑住他的，是她身上的那个孩子，那惹人怜爱的孩子，一片至诚地信托着，从不想世上会有一人对她和她的爱怀着恶意。他平生识得的女人不少，却没有一个扰动过他冷静的心旌；可他从未见过哪张女子的脸，被如此天真的一种狂喜照得通明。那脸庞上的哀愁，不过是一个灵魂的惴惴——它战栗着，惟恐那难以置信的欢乐不能长驻。于她，他什么也不是；任何一个男人也都不是，除了那一个。她心上那座处女的花园，围着攻不破的重垣。这真相不曾激恼他，反使他生出怜惜与呵护之心。这样一件稀世之物，他无从下手毁伤；纵然它的根基已经朽坏，他也甘愿与之合谋，去加固那根基。此刻他不是钟情人，而是守护者——便是把灵魂押上伪誓，也要护住那一座孩童的乐园。

他捧起她的手，吻了一下。「我是您最谦卑、最忠实的仆人，」他说。随后她做了一件他全然不备的事——她轻呼一声，双手掩住眼睛，哭了。

===Original===
He hurried from the room without looking back. He had made a decision which he found was like a dry patch of ground in the midst of rising floods, for gathering from every corner of his soul were dark and unplumbed tides.

===Chinese===
他匆匆走出房去，没有回头。他已做了一个决定；那决定在他觉来，恰似四面上涨的洪水中一块干地——因为他灵魂的每一隅，正涌集起一股股幽暗而深不可测的潮水。

---

===Original===
As he mounted, the Spainneach spoke: “He has gone by Milford and the Ernshawbank. Likely he will sleep an hour or two at the Pegtop. You might find him there if you haste.”

Johnson’s horse had also been brought, and its rider had some trouble in mounting.

“You will delay me, sir, if you insist on keeping me company,” said Alastair.

===Chinese===
他上马的时候，西班牙人开言道：「他打米尔福德和恩肖班克那条道走的。多半要在『陀螺』客栈睡上一两个钟头。您赶得紧些，兴许能在那儿寻着他。」

约翰逊的马也牵来了，这位骑手上鞍还有些费事。

「您若执意要与我同行，可要误我的事了，先生。」阿拉斯泰尔说。

===Original===
“I am a strong rider when I am once in the saddle,” said the other humbly. “But why this hurry? You will be in Derby long ere daybreak.”

“I do not ride to Derby, but down the vale to overtake a certain gentleman.”

He heard Johnson mutter a fervent “God be thanked” as he turned for a last look at the house. In an upper floor there was a glow of firelight and candlelight through the curtains of unshuttered windows. There lay Claudia, stammering her gentle confidences to Duchess Kitty, but with her thoughts ranging the hill-roads in the wake of her worthless lover. And from one of those dark windows two grey beldams were peering into the night and trembling for the riches that were the price of their souls.

===Chinese===
「只要一上得鞍子，我骑得并不含糊，」对方谦卑地说。「可何必这样赶？不到天亮，您早到德比了。」

「我不是去德比，是要沿谷地而下，去追一位先生。」

他回头最后望那宅子一眼时，听得约翰逊喃喃念了一声恳切的「感谢上帝」。楼上未上窗板的窗帘后面，透出炉火与烛光的暖明。克劳迪娅就卧在那里，一边结结巴巴向基蒂公爵夫人倾吐着温柔的体己话，一边心儿却沿着山间的道路，追着她那无用的爱人去了。而那些黑黢黢的窗子里，有一扇后面，两个灰发的老妪正窥望着黑夜，为那笔以灵魂作价换来的钱财瑟瑟发抖。

## VIII Broom at the Crossroads / 八、十字路口的金雀花
===Original===
The face before him had the tightened look of a sudden surprise: then it relaxed into recognition; but it showed no fear, though the young man’s visage was grim enough.

“You are Mr. Kyd’s servant?”

“Your honour has it. I’m Edom Lowrie at your honour’s service.”

“Your master started yesterday for Wiltshire. Why are you not with him?”

The man looked puzzled.

“Ye’re mista’en, sir. My master came here yestereen. I left him at skreigh o’day this morning.”

===Chinese===
面前那张脸先是猛吃一惊，绷得紧紧的，随即松弛下来，认出了来人；脸上却不见惧色，虽然年轻人的面容已够狰狞。

「你是基德先生的仆人？」

「老爷说得是。小人以东·洛里（Edom Lowrie），听候老爷差遣。」

「你家主人昨日已动身去威尔特郡了。你为何不随他同去？」

那人露出困惑的神色。

「您老弄岔了，老爷。俺主人是昨儿夜里到的这儿。今儿天刚麻麻亮，俺才同他分手。」

===Original===
It was Alastair’s turn to stare. Kyd had lied to him, thinking it necessary to deceive him about his road﻿—scurvy conduct, surely, between servants of the same cause. Or perhaps this fellow Edom was lying. He looked at him and saw no hint of double-dealing in the plain ugly face. His sandy eyebrows were indistinguishable from his freckled forehead and gave him an air of bald innocence, his pale eyes were candid and good-humoured, the eaves of his great teeth were comedy itself. The more Alastair gazed the harder he found it to believe that this rustic zany had betrayed him. But what on earth was Kyd about?

“Where is your master now?” he asked.

The other took off his hat and scratched his head. “I wadna like to say, sir. You see he telled me little, forbye sayin’ that he wadna see me again for the best pairt o’ a month. I jalouse mysel’ that he’s gone south, but he micht be for Wales.”

“Were you in Flambury last night?”

The man looked puzzled till Alastair explained. “Na, na, I was in nae village. I had a cauld damp bed in a bit public. My maister wasna there, but he appeared afore I was out o’ the blankets, a’ ticht and trim for the road, and gied me my marching-orders. I was to traivel the woods on foot, and no get mysel’ a horse till I won to a place they ca’ Camley.”

===Chinese===
这回轮到阿拉斯泰尔发怔了。基德对他撒了谎——认为有必要在行程上瞒他；同为一大业效命之人，行此手段，未免下作。再不然，便是这个名叫以东的家伙在撒谎。他端详着他，那张平庸丑陋的脸上瞧不出半点两面三刀的痕迹。他沙色的眉毛同雀斑点点的前额浑然难辨，倒给他添了一股光秃秃的天真气；浅色的眼睛坦率和善，那口大门牙的牙檐本身就是一出喜剧。阿拉斯泰尔越是打量，越难相信这个乡土活宝曾出卖过他。可基德究竟葫芦里卖的什么药？

「你家主人眼下在哪里？」他问。

对方摘下帽子，搔了搔头。「这个俺不好说，老爷。您瞧，他跟俺没多话，只除了说一句：往后大半个月里，怕是见不着俺了。俺自个儿寻思，他是往南去了，可保不齐又是奔威尔士。」

「昨夜你在弗兰伯里么？」

那人一脸茫然，直等阿拉斯泰尔说明缘由。「没的事，没的事，俺没住啥村子。俺在一家小酒肆里挨了一夜又冷又潮的床铺。俺主人本来不在那儿，可俺还没钻出被窝他就到了，一身行头收拾得利利索索，专待上路，随即给俺下了行进的令。俺得靠两条腿蹚林子，走到一个唤作坎利（Camley）的地界，才许自个儿弄马。」

===Original===
“Are you for Scotland?”

“Nae sic fortune. I’m for the Derbyshire muirs wi’ letters.” He hesitated. “Your honour’s no gaun that road yoursel’? I wad be blithe o’ company.”

The light in the hut was too dim to see clearly, for there was no window, the door was narrow and the day was sullen.

“Step outside, Mr. Lowrie, till I cast an eye over you,” said Alastair.

The man pocketed the remains of his bread and cheese and shambled into the open. He wore a long horseman’s coat and boots, a plain hat without cocks, and carried a stout hazel riding-switch. He looked less like a lackey than some small yeoman of the Borders, habited for a journey to Carlisle or St. Boswell’s Fair.

===Chinese===
「你要回苏格兰去？」

「没那个造化。俺带着信件，是往德比郡（Derbyshire）的荒原去的。」他迟疑了一下。「老爷莫非也要走那条道？有个伴儿，俺巴不得呢。」

棚屋里光线太暗，看不真切——屋里没有窗，门又窄，天色也阴沉着。

「到外头来，洛里先生，容我把你细细打量一番，」阿拉斯泰尔说。

那人把吃剩的面包干酪揣进兜里，趿拉着脚步走进空地。他穿一件长长的骑手外套，蹬一双靴子，戴一顶不翻檐的素帽，手里提一根结实的榛木马鞭。他瞧着不像个跟班仆役，倒像边境上哪户小自耕农，一身行装正要上卡莱尔去，或是赶圣博斯韦尔集市（St. Boswell's Fair）。

===Original===
“You know who I am,” said Alastair. “You are aware that like your master I am in a certain service, and that between him and me there are no secrets.”

“Aye, sir. I ken that ye’re Captain Maclean, and a gude Scot, though ower far north o’ Forth for my ain taste, if your honour will forgie me.”

“You carry papers? I must know more of your journey. What is your goal?”

“A bit the name o’ Brightwell near a hill they ca’ the Peak.”

Alastair had not been prepared for this, had had no glimmering of a suspicion of it, and the news decided him.

===Chinese===
「你知道我是谁，」阿拉斯泰尔说。「你也明白，我同你主人一样，也在办一件差事，他与我之间并无秘密。」

「是，老爷。俺知道您是麦克莱恩上尉，是位好苏格兰人——虽说老家在福斯河以北，比俺中意的远了不少，老爷恕俺直言。」

「你带着文书？你的行程我必须多知一二。你的目的地是哪里？」

「一个唤作布莱特韦尔（Brightwell）的地方，挨着一座他们叫皮克的山。」

这一点阿拉斯泰尔全然不曾料到，连一丝疑心的影子都不曾有过，而这消息令他拿定了主意。

===Original===
“It is of the utmost importance that I see your papers. Your master, if he were here now, would consent.”

The man’s face flushed. “I kenna how that can be. Your honour wadna have me false to my trust.”

“You will not be false. You travel on a matter of the Prince’s interest, as I do, and I must know your errand fully in order to shape my own course. Your master and I have equal rank in His Highness’s councils.”

The other shook his head, as if perplexed. “Nae doot﻿—nae doot. But, ye see, sir, I’ve my orders, and I maun abide by them. ‘Pit thae letters,’ my maister says, ‘intil the hand of him ye ken o’ and let naebody else get a glisk o’ them.’ ”

===Chinese===
「让我看看你的文书，此事干系重大。你主人此刻若在场，也会点头应允。」

那人脸红了。「这俺可没法子。老爷总不见得叫俺背弃所托吧。」

「这不叫背弃。你此行所办，关乎王子的利益，与我相同；我必须尽知你的差事，方能定夺自己的行程。你主人与我，在殿下的帷幄之中，位分相当。」

对方摇了摇头，像是犯了难。「那是——那是。可是您瞧，老爷，俺领了令，就得守令。『把这些信，』俺主人说，『送到你晓得的那个人手里，旁的什么人，连一眼也不许瞟见。』」

===Original===
“Then it is my duty to take them by force,” said Alastair, showing the hilt of his sword and the butt of a pistol under his coat.

Edom’s face cleared.

“That is a wiser-like way o’ speakin’. If ye compel me I maun e’en submit, for ye’re a gentleman wi’ a sword and I’m a landward body wi’ nocht but a hazel wand. It’s no that I mistrust your honour, but we maun a’ preserve the decencies.”

He unbuttoned his coat, foraged in the recesses of his person, and from some innermost receptacle extracted a packet tied with a dozen folds of cobbler’s twine. There was no seal to break, and Alastair slit the knots with his sword. Within was a bunch of papers of the same type as those he had received from Brother Gilly, and burned in the fire of the Dog and Gun. These he put in his pocket for further study. “I must read them carefully, for they contain that which must go straight to the Prince’s ear,” he told the perplexed messenger.

But there was a further missive, which seemed to be a short personal note from Mr. Kyd to the recipient of the papers.

===Chinese===
「那么，用强把它们取走，便是我的本分了，」阿拉斯泰尔说着，亮出剑柄，又掀开外衣，露出一把手枪的枪托。

以东的脸色豁然开朗。

「这才像句在理的话。您要是强来，俺也只好认了——谁叫您是佩剑的绅士，俺不过一个乡下的粗人，手里只一根榛木棍儿。倒不是俺信不过老爷，只是咱们大伙儿都得守个体面规矩。」

他解开外衣，在身上各处暗兜里掏摸一番，从最里层的一个藏袋里取出一个小包，鞋匠的线绳缠了十二道。没有要启的封印，阿拉斯泰尔用剑挑断了绳结。里面是一沓文书，同他先前从吉利兄弟手里收到、又在狗与枪客栈的火里烧掉的那些如出一辙。这些他都揣进衣袋，留待细读。「我必须仔细过目，因为其中所载，须得一字不差直达王子耳中，」他对这位惶惑的信使说。

此外另有一封书函，瞧着像基德先生写给文书收件人的一纸短简。

===Original===
> Dear “Achilles,”

it ran.

> Affairs march smoothly and the tide sets well to bring you to Troy town, where presently I design to crack a bottle and exchange tales. The Lady Briseis purposes to join you and will not be dissuaded by her kinsman. A friendly word: mix caution with your ardour her-ward, for she has got a political enthusiasm and is devilish strong-headed. The news of the Marches and the West will travel to you with all expedition, but I must linger behind to encourage my correspondents. “Menelaus” greets you﻿—a “Menelaus” that never owned a Helen.

The full sense of the document did not at first reach Alastair’s brain. But he caught the word “ ‘Achilles,’ ” and remembered a girl’s whispered confidence the night before. A second phrase arrested him﻿—“Briseis”﻿—he remembered enough of Father Dominic’s teaching to identify the reference. This Norreys, this husband of the russet lady, was far deeper in the secrets of the Cause than he had dreamed, if he were thus made the channel of vital intelligence. He was bidden act cautiously towards his new wife, and Mr. Kyd, who had heard Johnson’s accusations at Cornbury and said nothing, had all the time been in league with him. A sudden sense of a vast insecurity overcame the young man. The ground he trod on seemed shifting sand, and nowhere was there a firm and abiding landmark. And the girl too was walking in dark ways, and when she thought that she tripped over marble and cedar was in truth skimming the crust of quicksands. He grew hot with anger.

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的「阿喀琉斯」（Achilles）：

信上写道。

> 事势进展顺遂，潮头亦正顺当，必将送你直抵特洛伊城；届时我打算开上一瓶好酒，同你把盏话旧。布里塞伊斯夫人（Briseis）有意前来与你会合，她那位亲眷劝阻无门。进一友好之言：朝她那头去，热忱之中须掺几分谨慎，因为她染上了一股政治热狂，又固执得厉害。边区与西部的消息，自当以最快的脚程送达你处；我却须滞留一时，为我那班通信人打打气。「墨涅拉俄斯」向你致意——一个从未有过海伦的「墨涅拉俄斯」。

这封信的全部意涵，起初并未进入阿拉斯泰尔的脑海。但他捉住了「阿喀琉斯」这个字眼，想起前夜一位姑娘附耳说下的体己话。又一个词攫住了他——「布里塞伊斯」——多米尼克神父（Father Dominic）往日课业里灌进他脑壳的那点东西，还够他认出这个出典。这位诺里斯，这位栗衣女士的丈夫，若竟由此成了紧要情报的渠道，那么他卷入大业机密之深，便远非他所能梦见。信上叮嘱他对新妇谨慎行事；而基德先生——在科恩伯里亲耳听了约翰逊的指摘却不置一词的那位——竟自始至终与他串通一气。一阵天地无凭的不安全感攫住了这年轻人。他脚下所踏仿佛流沙，举目竟无一处坚实长存的界标。那姑娘也在暗路上行着——她自以为踏着的是大理石与雪松，实则不过在流沙的壳上滑掠。他怒火中烧。

===Original===
“Do you know the man to whom these are addressed?” he asked with stern brows.

Edom grinned.

“I ken how to find him. I’m to speir in certain quarters for ane Achilles, and I mind eneuch o’ what the Lauder dominie lickit intil me to ken that Achilles was a braw sodger.”

“You do not know his name? You never saw him?”

The man shook his head. “I wad like the letters back, sir,” he volunteered warily, for he was intimidated by Alastair’s dark forehead.

The latter handed back the “Achilles” letter, and began to read more carefully the other papers. Suddenly he raised his head and listened. The forest hitherto had been still with the strange dead quiet of a November noon. But now the noise of hounds was heard again, not half a mile off, as if they were hunting a line in the brushwood. He awoke with a start to the fact of his danger. What better sport for the patrons of the Flambury Hunt than to ride down a Jacobite horse-thief? A vague fury possessed him against that foolish squire with the cherubic face and the vacant blue eye.

===Chinese===
「你可知道这些信是写给谁的？」他浓眉紧锁地问。

以东咧嘴一笑。

「俺知道咋找他。俺得到几处地方，打听一个叫阿喀琉斯的人。劳德那位教书匠当年抽打进俺脑壳的东西，俺还记得不少，够俺明白阿喀琉斯是员好汉兵了。」

「你不知道他的名姓？也从未见过他？」

那人摇了摇头。「俺想把信要回来，老爷，」他怯生生地主动搭话，因为阿拉斯泰尔阴沉的眉宇镇住了他。

阿拉斯泰尔把那封「阿喀琉斯」的信交还，开始细读其余文书。忽然他抬起头，侧耳细听。方才林中还笼罩着十一月正午那种古怪的死寂，此刻猎犬的喧声却又起了，就在半英里之内，像是正沿着灌木丛里的某条嗅迹追赶。他悚然惊觉自己处境的危险。对弗兰伯里猎队的诸位主顾来说，还有什么消遣比纵马追翻一名雅各布党的盗马贼更快意？一股说不清的怒火在他胸中翻腾，直指那个蠢乡绅——那张小天使般无辜的脸，那双空茫茫的蓝眼睛。

===Original===
“The hunt is cried after me,” he told Edom, “and I take it you too have no desire to advertise your whereabouts. For God’s sake let’s get out of this place. Where does this road lead?”

Edom’s answer was drowned in a hubbub of hounds which seemed to be approaching down the ride from the east. Alastair led the way from the hut up a steepish hill, sparsely wooded with scrub oak, in the hope of finding a viewpoint. Unfortunately at the top the thicket was densest, so the young man swung himself into a tree and as quickly as riding-boots would permit sought a coign of vantage in its upper branches. There he had the prospect he wanted﻿—a great circle of rolling country, most of it woodland, but patched with large heaths where gorse-fires were smouldering. The piece of forest in which he sat stretched far to east and west, but to the north was replaced in less than a mile by pasture and small enclosures. As he looked he saw various things to disquiet him. The grassy road they had left was visible for half a mile, and down it came horsemen, while at the other end there seemed to be a picket placed.
