Worse still, to the north, which was the way of escape he had thought of, there were mounted men at intervals along the fringe of the trees. The hounds could be heard drawing near in the scrub east of the hut, and men’s voices accompanied them. He remembered that they would find the hut door open, see the crumbs of Edom’s bread and cheese, and no doubt discover the track which led up the hill.

He scrambled to the ground, his heart filled with forebodings and a deep disgust. He, who should long ago have been in the battlefield among the leaders, was befogged in this remote countryside, pursued by yokels, clogged and hampered at every step, and yet with the most desperate urgency of haste to goad him forward. His pride was outraged by such squalid ill-fortune. He must get his head from the net which was entangling and choking him. But for the moment there was nothing for it but to cower like a hare, and somewhere in the deep scrub find a hiding-place. Happily a foxhound was not a bloodhound.

Down the other side of the hill they went, Edom panting heavily and slipping every second yard. At the bottom they came on another road running parallel with the first, and were about to cross it when a sound from in front gave them pause. There were men there, keepers perhaps, beating the undergrowth and whistling. The two turned to the west and ran down the track, keeping as far as possible in the shadow of the adjacent coppice. A fine rain was beginning, which brought with it a mist that lowered the range of vision to a few hundred yards. In that lay Alastair’s one hope. Let the weather thicken and he would undertake to elude all the foresters and foxhunters in England. He cursed the unfamiliar land, which had no hills where fleetness of foot availed or crags where a bold man could laugh at pursuit.

===Chinese===
「他们放出猎犬喊追我了，」他对以东说，「我料想你也不愿张扬自己的行藏。看在上帝份上，咱们快离开此地。这条路通向哪里？」

以东的回答被一片犬噪声吞没了——那声浪似乎正沿着林间马道自东面压来。阿拉斯泰尔领路出了棚屋，攀上一座颇陡的小山，山上稀稀落落长着丛生的橡树，他指望寻一处瞭望之地。不巧山顶灌木最密，年轻人便翻身攀上一棵树，以马靴所容许的最快身手，在高处枝桠间觅得一个凭眺的所在。在那里，他得到了想要的视野——一大圈起伏的原野，多半为林地所覆，间杂着大片荒原，荆豆丛中的燎火兀自阴燃。他栖身的这片森林东西绵延甚远，北面却在不足一英里处，便让位给牧场与小块围田。他一面观望，一面看见好几桩令他心惊的事。他们方才离开的那条草路，半英里内尽收眼底，路上正有骑手驰来，而路的那一头似乎设了哨。更糟的是北面——那正是他盘算中的退路——林缘一带每隔一段，便有骑马的人。猎犬的动静在棚屋以东的灌木丛里越逼越近，还有人声相随。他想起他们将会发现棚屋的门敞着，瞧见以东吃剩的面包干酪屑，十有八九还会寻出那条通上山坡的小径。

他手忙脚乱地溜下树来，心中塞满凶兆与深深的厌恶。他本该早已身在战场、厕身统帅之列，如今却困死在这穷乡僻壤的迷雾里，被一帮乡巴佬追猎，步步牵绊掣肘，偏又有十万火急的军情鞭策他疾行。这般寒碜的厄运，狠狠羞辱了他的骄傲。他必须把脑袋从那张正缠拢来扼住他的罗网里挣脱出去。可眼下别无良策，只能学野兔蜷伏，在灌木深处觅一个藏身之所。所幸，猎狐犬不是寻血犬。

两人翻下山的另一面，以东喘得厉害，每隔一码便打一个滑。到得山脚，他们撞上又一条路，与先前那条恰成平行，正要穿过去，前面传来的声响却教他们止步。那边有人——多半是看守——正抽打着矮树丛，打着呼哨。两人折向西，顺着小路奔下，尽量走在毗邻矮林的阴影里。细雨初起，随之带来一场薄雾，把目力所及压到几百码之内。阿拉斯泰尔唯一的指望便落在这上头。且教天色再浓些，他便敢担保：全英格兰的林务与猎狐人尽数加起来，也捉他不住。他咒骂这陌生的土地——没有凭脚力取胜的山冈，也没有让胆壮之人笑傲追兵的峭壁。

===Original===
The place seemed terribly full of folk, as if whole parishes had emptied their population to beat the covers. Now he realised that the mist had its drawbacks as well as its merits, for he might stumble suddenly into a posse of searchers, and, though he himself might escape, the clumsier Edom would be taken. He bade the latter choose a line of his own and save himself, as he was not the object of the hunt, and owed his chief danger to his company, but this the man steadfastly refused to do. He ploughed stubbornly along in Alastair’s wake, wheezing like a bellows.

Then the noises seemed to die down, and the two continued in a dripping quiet. It was idle to think of leaving the forest, and the best that could be done was to find a hiding-place when they were certain that the pursuit was outdistanced. But this meant delay, and these slow rustics might keep up their watch for a week.﻿ ﻿…

Presently they came to a crossroads, where a broader path cut their ride, and in the centre stood an old rotting stake, where long ago some outlaw may have swung. They halted, for Edom had his breath to get. He flung himself on the ground, and at that moment Alastair caught sight of something tied to the post. Going nearer, he saw that it was a bunch of broom.

Had his wits not been sharpened by danger and disgust it might have had no meaning for him. But, as it was, Midwinter’s parting words on Otmoor came back to him, and with it the catch which he had almost forgotten. As Edom lay panting, he shaped his lips to whistle the air. In the quiet the tune rang out clear and shrill, and as he finished there was silence again. Then the bushes parted, and a man came out.

===Chinese===
这地方人多得可怕，仿佛一个个堂区都倾尽了人丁，来驱赶围场。此刻他才明白，雾有雾的利，也有雾的弊：他兴许会一头撞进搜索的人群怀里；他自己或许还能脱身，手脚笨拙的以东却要被拿住。他叫以东自择一路、各自逃命，因为猎的不是他，他的凶险多半是拜同伴所赐；可那人执意不肯。他顽固地跟在阿拉斯泰尔身后跋涉，喘得像一具风箱。

后来，那些声浪似乎渐渐平息，两人便在一片湿漉漉的寂静里继续赶路。想离开林子是妄想；顶好的打算，也不过是确知追兵已被甩远之后，寻个藏身之处。可这就意味着耽搁，而这些慢性子的乡民，没准能盯上整整一个礼拜……

不多时，他们来到一处十字路口，一条更宽的小径截断了脚下的马道，路口正中立着一根朽烂的老木桩，很久以前，说不定曾有哪个绿林亡命徒在上头吊死。两人停下歇脚，以东得缓过气来。他一头栽倒在地上；就在这当口，阿拉斯泰尔瞥见桩上系着一件东西。走近一看，原来是一束金雀花。

若不是危险与厌恶把他的心智磨得锋利，这东西在他眼里或许毫无意义。但此刻，仲冬在奥特沼泽上的临别之言回到了他心头，连同那支他几乎忘却的暗号码子。以东躺在地上喘气的工夫，他撮起嘴唇，把那调子吹了出来。寂静之中，曲声清越高亢；一曲既终，四下复归沉寂。随后，灌木分开，走出一个人来。

===Original===
He was a charcoal-burner, with a face like an Ethiopian, and red sore eyes curiously ringed about with clean white skin.

“Ye have the tune, master,” he said. “What be your commands for the Spoonbills? Folks be huntin’ these woods, and maybe it’s you as they’re seekin’.”

“The place is surrounded,” said Alastair, “and they are beating the covers between the rides. Get us out, or show us how we can be hid.”

The man did not hesitate. “Escape’s better’n hidin’,” he said. “Follow me, sirs, and I’ll do my best for ye.”

He led them at a great pace some two hundred yards into a tiny dell. There a glaze hung in the dull air from a charcoal-oven, which glowed under a mound of sods. Neat piles of oak and birch billets stood around, and the shafts of a cart stuck up out of the long bracken. On one side an outcrop of rock made a fine wind-shelter, and, pushing aside the creepers which veiled it, the charcoal-burner revealed a small cave.

===Chinese===
他是个烧炭人，一张脸黑得赛过埃塞俄比亚人，一双红肿的烂眼四周，怪模怪样地环着一圈干净的白皮肤。

「客官吹得这调子，」他说。「对篦鹭会的弟兄们，您有什么吩咐？这几片林子正有人搜捕呢，保不齐他们要拿的就是您。」

「此地四面被围，」阿拉斯泰尔说，「他们正在马道之间驱赶围场。带我们出去，或是指点一条藏身之路。」

那人毫不迟疑。「逃出去比藏起来强，」他说。「跟我来吧，二位，我必尽力周全你们。」

他领着他们大步流星约莫两百码，进到一个小小的谷地。阴沉的空气里悬着一层炭窑的釉烟，窑火在草皮堆下红红地闷烧。四下里码着一堆堆齐整的橡木桦木短柴，一辆大车的车辕从高深的蕨草中斜戳出来。一侧有岩层露头，正好挡风；烧炭人拨开掩覆岩面的藤蔓，露出一个小小的洞窟。

===Original===
“Off with your clothes, sirs,” he said. “They’ll be safe enough in that hidy-hole till I gets a chance to return ’em. Them rags is my mates’, and in this pickle are better’n fine silks.”

Two filthy old smocks were unearthed, and two pairs of wooden-soled clogs which replaced their boots. The change was effected swiftly under the constant urging of the charcoal-burner, who kept his ears cocked and his head extended like a dog. In five minutes Alastair was outwardly a figure differing only in complexion from the master of the dingle. Then the latter set to work, and with a handful of hot charcoal smeared hands and faces, rubbing the dirt into the eye-sockets so that the eyes smarted and watered. Hats and cravats were left in the cave, and Alastair’s trim hair was roughly clubbed, and dusted with soot for powder. There was no looking-glass to show him the result, but the charcoal-burner seemed satisfied. The transformation was simpler for Edom, who soon to Alastair’s eyes looked as if he had done nothing all his days but tend a smoky furnace.

“I’ll do the talking if we happen to meet inquiring folk,” the charcoal-burner admonished them. “Look sullen and keep your eyes on the ground, and spit﻿—above all, spit. Ours is a dry trade.”

He led them back to the main ride, and then boldly along the road which pointed north. The forest had woke up, and there were sounds of life on every side. The hounds had come out of covert and were being coaxed in again by a vociferous huntsman. Echoes of “Sweetlip,” “Rover,” “Trueman,” mingled with sundry oaths, came gustily down the wind. Someone far off blew a horn incessantly, and in a near thicket there was a clamour of voices like those of beaters after roebuck. The three men tramped stolidly along, the two novices imitating as best they could the angular gait, as of one who rarely stretched his legs, and the blindish carriage of the charcoal-burner.

===Chinese===
「把衣裳都脱了，二位，」他说。「搁在那个藏洞里保险得很，等寻着机会我再送还。这些破烂是我伙计们的，眼下这光景，比绫罗绸缎还金贵。」

挖出两件腌臜的旧罩衫，还有两双木底屐，替换了他们的靴子。改装是在烧炭人不住口的催促下飞快完成的——他支棱着耳朵，探伸着脑袋，活像一条狗。不出五分钟，阿拉斯泰尔在外表上已同这谷地的主人相差无几，只除了面色。接着烧炭人动手，抓一把热炭灰，把两人的手脸涂抹了个遍，还把黑灰揉进眼窝，揉得两眼又辣又淌泪。帽子领巾留在洞里；阿拉斯泰尔梳理齐整的头发被胡乱束成一条辫子，扑上烟灰权充发粉。没有镜子照看成果，烧炭人却似乎颇为满意。以东的改装省事得多，不多时，在阿拉斯泰尔眼里，他那副模样就像一辈子没干过别的、专司伺候一座烟熏火燎的炭窑。

「万一撞上盘问的人，话都由我来答，」烧炭人叮嘱道。「脸要阴沉，眼睛只管盯着地，还要啐唾沫——顶要紧的是啐唾沫。咱们这行当，燥得很。」

他领他们回到大马道上，随即大大方方，沿着指北的路走去。森林已经醒了，四面八方都有声气。猎犬窜出了隐蔽处，一个嗓门洪亮的猎手正连哄带喝，把它们唤回去。「甜唇」「罗弗」「特鲁曼」的呼喝混着种种咒骂，一阵一阵随风刮来。远处有人吹号，吹个不住；近处一片灌木林里人声鼎沸，像驱狍围猎的赶山人。三个汉子木然前行，两个生手竭力学着烧炭人那副僵直的步态——活似一个素来难得伸开腿脚的人——还有他那半瞎似的做派。

===Original===
A knot of riders swept down on them. Alastair ventured to lift his eyes for one second, and saw the scarlet and plum colour of Squire Thicknesse and noted the grey’s hocks. The legs finicking and waltzing near them he thought belonged to Moonbeam, and was glad that the horse had been duly caught and restored. The Squire asked a question of the charcoal-burner and was answered in a dialect of gutturals. Off surged the riders, and presently the three were at the edge of the trees where a forester’s cottage smoked in the rain. Beyond, wrapped in a white mist, stretched ploughland and pasture.

Alastair saw that his treetop survey had been right. This edge of the wood was all picketed, and as the three emerged a keeper in buckskin breeches came towards them, and a man on horseback turned at his cry and cantered back.

The keeper did not waste time on them, once he had a near view.

“Yah!” he said, “it’s them salvages o’ coalies. They ain’t got eyes to obsarve nothin’, pore souls! ’Ere, Billy,” he cried, “seen any strange gen’elmen a-wanderin’ the woods this morning?”

===Chinese===
一小队骑手朝他们席卷而来。阿拉斯泰尔壮着胆子抬眼一瞬，看见西克尼斯乡绅一身绯红配梅紫，又认出那匹灰马的飞节。近旁那两条正挑剔地踏着舞步的马腿，他想是月华（Moonbeam）的，不由庆幸那马总算被规规矩矩逮住，物归原主了。乡绅向烧炭人问了一句，得了一句喉音浓重的土话作答。骑手们蜂拥而去；不多时，三人已到林子边缘，一座林务小屋在雨中冒着烟。再往外，白雾包裹之中，铺展开耕地与牧场。

阿拉斯泰尔见自己树顶上的侦察果然不差。林子这一缘处处设了哨；三人刚一露头，一个穿鹿皮马裤的看守便迎上前来，一个骑马的人闻他一唤，拨转马头小跑而回。

看守走近瞧清了他们，便不在这三人身上耽搁工夫。

「嗐！」他说，「是那帮烧炭的野人。他们那双眼睛啥也瞧不见，可怜的货色！喂，比利（Billy），」他喊道，「今儿早上可见过什么面生的绅士，在林子里晃荡？」

===Original===
The charcoal-burner stopped, and the two others formed up sullenly behind him.

“There wor a fallow-buck a routin’ round my foorness,” he grumbled in a voice as thick as clay. “Happen it come to some ’urt, don’t blame me, gossip. Likewise there’s a badger as is makin’ an earth where my birch-faggots should lie. That’s all the strange gen’elmen I seen this marnin’, barrin’ a pack o’ redcoats a-gallopin’ ’orses and blowin’ ’orns.”

The rider had now arrived and was looking curiously at the three. The keeper in corduroy breeches turned laughing to him. “Them coalies is pure salvages, Mr. Gervase, sir. Brocks and bucks, indeed, when I’m inquirin’ for gen’elmen. Gawd A’mighty made their ’eads as weak as their eyes.”

What answer the rider gave is not known, for the charcoal-burners had already moved forward. They crossed a piece of plough and reached a shallow vale seamed by a narrow stagnant brook. Here they were in shelter, and to Alastair’s surprise their leader began to run. He took them at a good pace up the water till it was crossed by a high-road, then along a bypath, past a farm-steading, to a strip of woodland, which presently opened out into a wide heath. Here in deference to Edom’s heaving chest he slackened pace. The rain was changing from a drizzle to a heavy downpour and the faces of the two amateurs were becoming a ghastly piebald with the lashing of the weather.

===Chinese===
烧炭人站住脚，另外两个阴沉着脸，在他身后一字站定。

「倒是有头扁角鹿，在我炉子跟前打转来着，」他用黏土般厚浊的嗓音咕哝道。「它要是遭了什么殃，可别怪我，老哥。还有一只獾，想在我堆桦柴的地方打洞安家。今儿早上我见着的面生绅士，拢共就这些——除了一伙红衣兵，纵马飞跑，号角吹个不停。」

骑马的人此时已到近前，正好奇地打量这三人。穿灯芯绒马裤的看守笑着转向他。「这些烧炭的真是十足的野人，杰维斯先生（Mr. Gervase），长官。我打听的是绅士，他倒拿獾和鹿来搪塞。全能的上帝造他们的脑袋，就跟造他们的眼睛一样不管用。」

骑马的人回了句什么，无人知晓，因为三个烧炭人早已前行了。他们穿过一块耕田，到达一道浅谷，一条窄窄的死水溪在谷底蜿蜒。到了这里有了遮蔽，而令阿拉斯泰尔吃惊的是，领头的竟撒腿跑了起来。他带着他们顺着溪水一路疾行，直到一条大道横跨溪上；再沿一条小径，过一座农庄，进到一带林地，不多时林地敞开，展成一片辽阔的荒原。到了此处，看在以东起伏不定的胸膛份上，他放缓了脚步。雨正由蒙蒙细雨转为倾盆，两个外行人的脸被风雨抽打得黑一道白一道，花斑可怖。

===Original===
The charcoal-burner turned suddenly to Alastair and spoke in a voice which had no trace of dialect.

“You have escaped one danger, sir. I do not know who you may be or what your desires are, but I am bound to serve you as far as it may lie in my power. Do you wish me to take you to my master?”

“I could answer that better, if I knew who he was.”

“We do not speak his name at large, but in a month’s time the festival of his name-day will return.”

Alastair nodded. The thought of Midwinter came suddenly to him with an immense comfort. He, if anyone could, would help him out of this miasmic jungle in which his feet were entangled and set him again upon the highway. His head was still confused with the puzzle of Kyd’s behaviour.﻿—Edom’s errand, the exact part played by Sir John Norreys, above all the presence of a subtle treason. He remembered the deep eyes and the wise brow of the fiddler of Otmoor, and had he not that very day seen a proof of his power?

===Chinese===
烧炭人忽然转向阿拉斯泰尔，开口说话，嗓音里已无半点土腔。

「您已脱过一场险了，先生。我不知道您是何人，有何贵干，但力所能及之处，我义不容辞，必当效劳。您要我带您去见我的主人么？」

「先让我知道他是谁，这话才好作答。」

「他的名字，我们不当众乱讲；不过再过一个月，他命名日的节庆又要到了。」

阿拉斯泰尔点了点头。仲冬其人蓦地浮上心头，带来莫大的慰藉。若有人能救他，必是此人——把他从这片缠住双足的瘴气莽林里领出去，重新送上大道。他的脑子仍叫基德行事的谜团搅得混沌——以东的差事，约翰·诺里斯爵士所扮的确切角色，尤其是那桩隐微叛迹的存在。他想起奥特沼泽那位提琴手深沉的眼睛与睿智的额头；何况就在今日，他不是亲眼见过他神通的一证么？

===Original===
The heath billowed and sank into ridges and troughs, waterless and furze-clad, and in one of the latter they came suddenly upon a house. It was a small place, built with its back to a steep ridge all overgrown with blackberries and heather﻿—two stories high, and flanked by low thatched outbuildings, and a pretence at a walled garden. On the turf before the door, beside an ancient well, a sign on a pole proclaimed it the inn of The Merry Woman, but suns and frosts had long since obliterated all trace of the rejoicing lady, though below it and more freshly painted was something which might have resembled a human eye.

The three men lounged into the kitchen, which was an appanage to the main building, and called for ale. It was brought by a little old woman in a mutch, who to Alastair’s surprise curtseyed to the grimy figure of the charcoal-burner.

“He’s alone, sir,” she said, “and your own room’s waiting if you’re ready for it.”

“Will you go up to him?” the charcoal-burner asked, and Alastair followed the old woman. She led the way up a narrow staircase with a neat sheepskin rug on each tread, to a tiny corridor from which two rooms opened. The one on the left they entered and found an empty bedroom, cleanly and plainly furnished. A door in the wall at the other end, concealed by a hanging cupboard, gave access to a pitch-dark passage. The woman took Alastair’s hand and led him a yard or two till she found a door-handle. It opened and showed a large chamber with daylight coming through windows apparently half cloaked with creepers. Alastair realised that the room had been hollowed out of the steep behind the house, and that the windows opened in the briars and heath of the face.

===Chinese===
荒原波涛般起伏，又沉入一道道山脊与洼地，无水无溪，遍生荆豆。就在其中一道洼地里，他们蓦地撞见一座房子。那是座小小的宅子，背倚一道陡岭，岭上长满黑莓与石楠——两层高，两侧辅着低矮的茅顶厢屋，还煞有介事地围出一方园子。门前草皮上，一口古井旁，一根竿子挑着招牌，道此地为「快活女人客栈」（The Merry Woman）；只是日炙霜欺，那位快活夫人早被磨得踪影全无，倒是在她底下，有个漆得较新的图形，隐约像是一只人眼。

三人懒懒散散踱进厨房——那是正屋的附属——唤了麦酒。端酒的是个戴白布软帽的矮小老妇人；令阿拉斯泰尔吃惊的是，她竟向烧炭人那满身炭垢的身影行了个屈膝礼。

「就他独自一位，先生，」她说，「您那间屋子也候着呢，您预备好了便可过去。」

「您这就上去见他么？」烧炭人问，阿拉斯泰尔便随老妇人去了。她在前头引路，上一道窄窄的楼梯，每级踏板上都铺着一块齐整的羊皮小毯，通到一条小小的走廊，走廊上开着两扇门。两人进了左边那扇，是一间空卧室，陈设得洁净而朴素。尽头墙上另有一扇门，叫一具吊柜遮着，通向一条漆黑的过道。老妇人牵起阿拉斯泰尔的手，领他走了一两码，摸着了门把。门开处，露出一间宽敞的内室，天光从窗口透入，窗上似乎半掩着藤蔓。阿拉斯泰尔这才醒悟：这间屋子是在屋后陡坡的肚腹里掏出来的，那几扇窗，就开在坡面正前的荆棘与石楠丛中。

===Original===
A fire was burning and a man sat beside it reading in a book. He was the fiddler of Otmoor, and in the same garb, save that he had discarded his coat and wore instead a long robe de chambre. A keen eye scanned the visitor, and then followed a smile and an outstretched hand.

“Welcome, Alastair Maclean,” he said. “I heard of you in these parts and hoped for a meeting.”

“From whom?”

“One whom you call the Spainneach. He left me this morning to go into Derbyshire.”

===Chinese===
炉火正旺，一个人坐在炉边，就着一本书读。正是奥特沼泽的那位提琴手，还是那身装束，只是褪去了外衣，换穿一袭长长的家常晨袍。一双锐利的眼睛把来客扫视一过，随之而来的是一个微笑与一只伸出的手。

「欢迎，阿拉斯泰尔·麦克莱恩，」他说。「我听闻你到了这一带，正盼着一晤。」

「听谁说的？」

「一个被你们唤作西班牙人的。他今晨辞了我，往德比郡去了。」

===Original===
The name stirred a question.

“Had he news?” Alastair asked. “When I last saw you you prophesied failure. Are you still of that mind?”

“I do not prophesy, but this I say﻿—that since I saw you your chances and your perils have grown alike. Your Cause is on the razor-edge and you yourself may have the deciding.”

===Chinese===
这名字勾起一桩疑问。

「他捎来什么消息没有？」阿拉斯泰尔问。「上回见你，你预言的是败局。如今还持此见么？」

「我不作预言，但有一句话在此——自上次见你，你的机运与凶险已一同增长。你的大业正悬于剃刀之刃，而执其决断的，或许正是足下。」

## V Chance-Medley / 五、不期而战
===Original===
In his dreams Alastair was persistently conscious of Mr. Kyd’s face, which hung like a great sun in that dim landscape. Fresh-coloured and smiling at one moment, it would change suddenly to a thing peaked and hunted, with aversion and fear looking out of narrow eyes. And it mixed itself oddly with another face, a pale face framed in a high coat collar, and adorned with a very sharp nose. It may have been the supper or it may have been the exceeding hardness of the bed, but his sleep was troubled, and he woke with that sense of having toiled furiously which is the consequence of nightmare. He had forgotten the details of his dreams, but one legacy remained from them﻿—a picture of that sharp-nosed face, and the memory of Mr. Kyd’s open countenance as he had surprised it for one second the night before. As he dressed the recollection paled, and presently he laughed at it, for the Mr. Kyd who now presented himself to his memory was so honest and generous and steadfast that the other picture seemed too grotesque even for a caricature.

On descending to breakfast he found, though the day was yet early, that his companion had been up and gone a good hour before. Had he left a message? The landlady said no. What road had he taken? The answer was a reference to a dozen unknown place names, for countryfolk identify a road by the nearest villages it serves. Mr. Kyd’s energy roused his emulation. He breakfasted hastily, and twenty minutes later was on the road.

The mist had cleared, and a still November morn opened mild and grey over a flat landscape. The road ran through acres of unkempt woodlands where spindlewood and briars glowed above russet bracken, and then over long ridges of lea and fallow, where glimpses were to be had of many miles of smoky-brown forest, with now and then a slender wedge of church steeple cutting the low soft skies. Alastair hoped to get a fresh horse at Flambury which would carry him to Chester, and as his present beast had come far, he could not press it for all his impatience. So as he jogged through the morning his thoughts had leisure to wander, and to his surprise he found his mind enjoying an unexpected peace. He was very near the brink of the torrent; let him make the most of these last yards of solid land. The stormy October had hastened the coming of winter, and the autumn scents had in most places yielded to the strong clean fragrance of a bare world. It was the smell he loved, whether he met it in Morvern among the December mosses, or on the downs of Picardy, or in English fields. At other times one smelled herbage and flowers and trees; in winter one savoured the essential elements of water and earth.

===Chinese===
在梦里，阿拉斯泰尔始终摆脱不开基德先生的那张脸，它像一轮巨日，悬在梦境晦暗的风景上空。前一刻还面色红润、笑意盈盈，转眼便变作一副尖削仓皇的面相，窄窄的眼睛里透出憎恶与恐惧。这张脸又奇怪地与另一张脸纠缠在一处——一张苍白的脸，镶嵌在高高的衣领里，装点着一只极尖的鼻子。许是那顿晚饭作祟，许是床板硬得离谱，他睡得极不安稳，醒来时浑身带着一番苦役般的疲乏，正是噩梦留下的后果。梦中的细枝末节他已忘却，只剩一样遗产——那尖鼻子面容的一幅图像，还有对基德先生那张坦然面容的记忆：昨夜他曾出其不意地窥得它一秒钟。穿衣的当口，这段回忆渐渐淡了，不多时他便自笑起来，因为此刻浮上他记忆的基德先生，是那样忠厚、慷慨、坚毅，另一幅图像简直滑稽得连漫画都算不上了。

下楼用早餐时他发现，天色尚早，同伴却已起身走了足足一个钟头。可曾留下话？女店主说没有。走的哪条路？回答是一连串十来个没听过的地名——乡下人指认一条路，向来拿它沿途最近的村子作凭据。基德先生的劲头引得他也不甘落后。他匆匆用了早餐，二十分钟后便上了路。

雾已散尽，一个十一月的静谧清晨在平坦的原野上展开，温和而灰白。道路穿过大片无人料理的林地，卫矛与荆棘在黄褐的蕨草上灼灼发光，随后又翻过一道道草甸与休耕地的长岗，从那里可望见绵延数英里的烟棕色森林，不时有一线纤细如楔的教堂尖顶，划破低垂柔淡的天空。阿拉斯泰尔指望在弗兰伯里（Flambury）换一匹新马，好载他直抵切斯特（Chester）；眼下这匹牲口已走了远路，任他心急如焚，也催逼不得。于是他缓缓骑行，消磨晨光，心思得以闲游四散，而他惊觉自己的心境竟享受着一份意想不到的安宁。他已站在激流的边缘，且让他充分消受这最后几码坚实的岸土吧。风雨的十月催快了冬天的脚步，秋的气息在大多数地方已让位于一个草木凋尽的世界那强烈而洁净的芬芳。这正是他钟爱的气味——无论在莫尔文（Morvern）十二月的苔沼间，在皮卡第（Picardy）的旷野上，还是在英格兰的田野里。别的季节里，人闻到的是草卉花木；到了冬天，人品味的是水与土的本原。

===Original===
In this mood of content he came after midday to a large village on the borders of Stafford and Shropshire, where he halted for a crust and a jug of ale. The place was so crowded that he judged it was market day, and the one inn had a press about its door like the visiting hours at a debtors’ prison. He despaired of forcing an entrance, so commissioned an obliging loafer to fetch him a tankard, while he dismounted, hitched his bridle to the signpost, and seated himself at the end of a bench which ran along the inn’s frontage.

The ale was long in coming, and Alastair had leisure to observe his neighbours. They were a remarkable crowd. Not villagers clearly, for the orthodox inhabitants might be observed going about their avocations, with many curious glances at the strangers. They were all sizes and shapes, and in every variety of dress from fustian to camlet, but all were youngish and sturdily built, and most a trifle dilapidated. The four men who sat on the bench beside him seemed like gamekeepers out of employ, and were obviously a little drunk. In the throng at the door there were horse-boys and labourers and better-clad hobbledehoys who might have been the sons of yeomen. A raffish young gentleman with a greyhound and with a cock of his hat broken was engaged in an altercation with an elderly fellow who had a sheaf of papers and had mounted a pair of horn spectacles to read them. Through the open window of the taproom floated scraps of argument in a dozen varieties of dialect.

Alastair rubbed his eyes. Something in the sight was familiar. He had seen it in Morvern, in the Isles, in a dozen parts of France and Spain, when country fellows were recruited for foreign armies. But such things could not be in England, where the foreign recruiter was forbidden. Nor could it be enlistment for the English regiments, for where were the bright uniforms and the tuck of drums? The elderly man with the papers was beyond doubt a soldier, but he had the dress of an attorney’s clerk. There was some queer business afoot here, and Alastair set himself to probe it.

===Chinese===
怀着这份怡然，他在晌午过后来到一座大村，地处斯塔福德郡（Staffordshire）与什罗普郡（Shropshire）两郡交界，便停下来打算吃口干粮、喝罐麦酒。村里人头攒动，他估摸着必是集市之日，唯一一家客栈门口拥挤不堪，像债务人监狱会客的钟点。挤进去是休想了，他便托一个殷勤的闲汉替他打一大杯酒来，自己下了马，把缰绳拴在招牌柱上，在沿客栈门脸排开的一条长凳尽头坐了。

麦酒迟迟不来，阿拉斯泰尔得闲打量身旁众人。这一群人着实特别。显然不是本村人，因为正牌的村民尽可见各干各的营生，还不时好奇地瞟这些外乡客。这些人高矮胖瘦俱全，衣装从粗绒布到羽纱样样都有，却个个年轻力壮、筋骨结实，多数还带着几分潦倒。坐在他长凳上的四条汉子像是失了业的猎场看守，分明已有几分醉意。门口人群里有马童、有苦力，还有穿得体面些的半大小子，多半是自耕农人家的儿郎。一位帽檐翻塌、牵着条灵缇的浮浪少年绅士，正跟一个老汉争执不下；那老汉抱着一沓文书，架起一副角框眼镜要看。酒厅敞开的窗口里飘出争吵的只言片语，方言口音足有十来种。

阿拉斯泰尔揉了揉眼睛。这景象有些眼熟。他在莫尔文、在赫布里底的诸岛、在法兰西和西班牙的十几处地方都见过——那是乡间汉子应募外国军队的时候。可这种事断不该出现在英格兰，外国募兵官在此地是明令禁止的。也不会是应募英军的团队，因为鲜亮的军装呢？咚咚的鼓点呢？那个抱文书的老汉无疑是个行伍出身，穿的却是讼师文书的衣裳。这里头必有蹊跷，阿拉斯泰尔便存心要探它一探。
===Original===
His neighbour on the bench did not understand his question. But the raffish young man with the greyhound heard it, and turned sharply to the speaker. A glance at Alastair made his voice civil.

“Matter!” he exclaimed. “The matter, sir, is that I and some twoscore honest men have been grossly deceived. We are of Oglethorpe’s, enlisted to fight the Spaniard in the Americas. And now there is word that we are to be drafted to General Wade, as if we were not gentleman-venturers but so many ham-handed common soldiers. Hark, sir!”

From within the inn came a clatter of falling dishes and high voices.

“That will be Black Benjamin warming to work,” said the young man, proffering a pewter snuffbox in which there remained a few grains of rappee. “He is striving in there with the Quartermaster-Sergeant while I seek to convince Methody Sam here of the deceitfulness of his ways.”

The elderly man, referred to as Methody Sam, put his spectacles in his pocket, and revealed a mahogany face lit by two bloodshot blue eyes. At the sight of Alastair he held himself at attention, for some instinct in him discerned the soldier.

“I ain’t denyin’ it’s a melancholy business, sir,” he said, “and vexatious to them poor fellows. They was recruited by Gen’ral Oglethorpe under special permission from His Majesty, God bless ’im, for the dooty of keeping the Spaniards out of His Majesty’s territory of Georgia in Ameriky, for which purpose they ’as signed on for two years, journeys there and back included, at the pay of one shilling per lawful day, and all vittles and clothing provided ’andsome. But now ’Is Majesty thinks better on it, and is minded to let Georgia slip and send them lads to General Wade to fight the Scotch. It’s a ’ard pill to swallow, I ain’t denyin’ it, but orders is orders, and I ’ave them express this morning from Gen’ral Oglethorpe, who is a-breakin’ the news to the Shropshire Companies.”

===Chinese===
长凳上的邻座听不懂他的问话。倒是那牵灵缇的浮浪少年听见了，霍地转向发话人。朝阿拉斯泰尔望了一眼，他的口气便客气起来。

「事由！」他嚷道。「事由么，先生，就是我和四十来个老实汉子叫人狠狠地坑了。我们是奥格尔索普（Oglethorpe）麾下的人，应的是去美洲打西班牙人的募。如今却听说要把我们拨给韦德（Wade）将军，好像我们不是绅士冒险家，倒是一群手脚粗笨的寻常大兵似的。听，先生！」

客栈里面传来碟盏坠地的哗啦声和高声的叫嚷。

「那准是黑本杰明（Black Benjamin）来劲了，」年轻人说，一面递上一只白镴鼻烟盒，里头还剩几粒拉皮鼻烟。「他在里头正跟军需军士掰扯，我在这儿呢，正劝这位循道萨姆（Methody Sam）幡然醒悟，明白他自己那套营生多么坑人。」

那被唤作循道萨姆的老汉把眼镜揣进兜里，露出一张红木色的脸，两点充血的蓝眼睛亮着。一看见阿拉斯泰尔，他便挺身立正——他体内某种本能认出了军人。

「俺不否认，这档子事怪叫人心里发苦，先生，」他说，「对那帮可怜弟兄也实在窝囊。他们是奥格尔索普将军募来的，奉国王陛下特旨——上帝保佑他——去美洲守陛下的佐治亚（Georgia）地界，把西班牙人挡在外头。为这个他们画押应募两年，来回盘程一概算在内，饷银每法定日一先令，吃喝衣装体面供应。可如今陛下圣心改了主意，打算撒手佐治亚，把这帮小子改拨韦德将军，去打苏格兰人。这药丸子是苦，俺不否认，可军令就是军令，今儿早上俺刚从奥格尔索普将军手里接了专令，他这会儿正挨着什罗普郡的各连队，逐一开导呢。」

===Original===
One of the drunkards on the bench broke into a flood of oaths which caused Methody Sam to box his ears. “Ye was enlisted for a pious and honourable dooty, and though that dooty may be changed the terms of enlistment is the same. No foul mouth is permitted ’ere, my lad.”

The young gentleman with the greyhound was listening eagerly to what was going on indoors. “Benjamin’s getting his dander up,” he observed. “Soon there will be bloody combs going. Hi! Benjy!” he shouted. “Come out and let’s do the job fair and foursquare in the open. It’s a high and holy mutiny.”

There was no answer, but presently the throng at the door began to fan outward under pressure from within. A crowd of rough fellows tumbled out, and at their tail a gypsy-looking youth with a green bandana round his head, dragging a small man, who had the air of having once been in authority. Alastair recognised the second of the two noncommissioned officers, but while one had protested against oaths the other was filling the air with a lurid assortment. This other had his hands tied with a kerchief, and a cord fastening the joined palms to his knees, so that he presented a ridiculous appearance of a man at his prayers.

“Why hain’t ye trussed up Methody?” the gypsy shouted to the owner of the greyhound.

The sergeant cast an appealing eye on Alastair. There seemed to be no arms in the crowd, except a cudgel or two and the gypsy’s whinger. It was an appeal which the young man’s tradition could not refuse.

===Chinese===
长凳上一个醉鬼脱口涌出一串脏话，循道萨姆抬手便给了他一记耳光。「你应的是一桩虔诚体面的差事，差事兴许要变，应募的条款可没变。这儿不许满嘴喷粪，小子。」

牵灵缇的少年绅士正竖着耳朵听屋里的动静。「本杰明火气上来了，」他评点道。「眼看就要斗得鸡冠子见血了。喂！本杰！」他喊道。「出来！咱们到亮处公公平平、堂堂正正地干一场。这可是一场高尚神圣的哗变。」

没人应声，可不多时，门口的人群便被里头的压力挤得朝两面扇开。一群粗汉跌跌撞撞涌出来，队尾是个吉普赛模样的青年，头上缠一条绿布巾，拖拽着一个小个子男人——那人一副昔日也曾发号施令的气派。阿拉斯泰尔认出这是两名军士中的另一个：方才一个还在禁止旁人赌咒，这一个此刻却满天空撒着五颜六色的咒骂。这另一个双手叫手帕捆了，还有一条绳索把合拢的手掌拴在膝上，那模样活像一个跪着祷告的人，说不出的滑稽。

「怎么没把循道萨姆也捆上？」那吉普赛人朝灵缇的主人嚷道。

军士向阿拉斯泰尔投来哀求的一瞥。人群里似乎不见兵刃，不过一两根棍棒，还有那吉普赛人的短刀。这样的哀告，年轻人承袭的门风不容他拒绝。
===Original===
“Have patience, gentlemen,” he cried. “I cannot have you prejudging the case. Forward with your prisoner, but first untie these bonds. Quick.”

The gypsy opened his mouth in an insolent refusal, when he saw something in the horseman’s eye which changed his mind. Also he noted his pistols, and his light travelling sword.

“That’s maybe fair,” he grunted, and with his knife slit his prisoner’s bonds.

“Now, out with your grievances.”

The gypsy could talk, and a very damning indictment he made of it. “We was ’listed for overseas, with good chance of prize money, and a nobleman’s freedom. And now we’re bidden stop at home as if we was lousy lobsters that took the King’s money to trick the gallows. Is that fair and English, my sweet pretty gentleman? We’re to march tomorrow against the naked Highlanders that cut out a man’s bowels with scythes, and feed their dogs with his meat. Is that the kind of fighting you was dreaming of, my precious boys? No, says you, and we’ll be damned, says you, if we’ll be diddled. Back we goes to our pretty homes, but with a luckpenny in our pocket for our wasted time and our sad disappointment. Them sergeants has the money, and we’ll hold them upside down by the heels till we shake it out of them.”

===Chinese===
「诸位稍安勿躁，」他喝道。「案子未审，不许你们先判。把犯人带上来，不过先把这些绳索解了。快。」

那吉普赛人张口就要蛮横地回绝，忽而瞥见骑马人眼里的一点什么，便改了主意。他又留意到他的双枪，还有那柄轻巧的旅行佩剑。

「这样倒也公道，」他咕哝一声，拔刀割断了俘虏身上的绳索。

「好了，把你们的冤屈都倒出来吧。」

那吉普赛人好一张嘴，一篇控诉叫他说得振振有词、字字诛心。「俺们应募是去海外，好挣一笔赏金，享一份老爷似的自在。如今却叫俺们留在国内，好像俺们是那号满身虱子的红虾兵，领了国王的银钱，只为躲过绞架似的。这算公道吗？算英格兰的规矩吗，俺俊俏体面的绅士老爷？明儿个俺们就得开拔，去斗那些光着身子的高地人——他们用长镰刀开人的膛，拿人肉喂他们的狗。俺亲爱的哥儿们，你们梦里想的就是这种仗吗？不，你们说；俺们要是肯这么受坑，你们说，俺们就该天打雷劈。俺们这就回俺们的好老家去，不过兜里总得揣一笔吉利钱，赔俺们白耗的光阴和伤透的心。钱都在那两个军士手里，俺们要拎着他们的脚脖子倒提起来，抖上三抖，非把银子抖出来不可。」

===Original===
Methody Sam replied, looking at Alastair. “It’s crool ’ard, but orders is orders. Them folks enlisted to do the King’s commands and if ’Is Majesty ’appens to change ’is mind, it’s no business o’ theirs or mine. The money me and Bill ’as is Government money, and if they force it from us they’ll be apprehended and ’anged as common robbers. I want to save their poor innocent souls from ’anging felony.”

The crowd showed no desire for salvation. There was a surge towards the two men and the gypsy’s hand would have been on the throat of Methody Sam had not Alastair struck it up. The smaller of the two noncommissioned officers was chafing his wrists, which his recent bonds had abraded, and lamenting that he had left his pistols at home.

“What made you come here with money and nothing to guard it?” Alastair asked.

“The General’s orders, sir. But it was different when we was temptin’ them with Ameriky and the Spaniards’ gold. Now we’ll need a file o’ loaded muskets to get ’em a step on the road. Ay, sir, we’ll be fort’nate if by supper time they’ve not all scattered like a wisp o’ snipes, takin’ with ’em ’Is Majesty’s guineas.”

“Keep beside me!” Alastair whispered. A sudden rush would have swept the little man off, had not Methody Sam plucked him back.

===Chinese===
循道萨姆答话了，眼睛望着阿拉斯泰尔。「这事儿狠是狠，可军令就是军令。那帮人应募是来听国王差遣的，陛下圣心若忽然改了主意，干他们什么事，也干俺什么事。俺同比尔手里的钱是官家的钱，他们要是硬抢了去，便要当寻常强盗拿办，吊上绞架。俺是想救救他们那点可怜的无辜灵魂，别为一桩绞刑重罪送了命。」

人群却毫无领这份情的意思。一阵人浪朝那两人涌去，那吉普赛人的手眼看就要掐上循道萨姆的喉咙，被阿拉斯泰尔一把格开。两个军士中个头小的那个正揉着腕子——方才的绳索磨破了皮——一面哀叹自己竟把手枪落在了家里。

「你们带着饷银出门，怎么不带点护饷的家伙？」阿拉斯泰尔问。

「将军的将令，先生。可当初俺们拿美洲、拿西班牙人的金子诱他们那阵子，是另一码事。如今得要一排上了膛的火枪，才能押着他们挪一步路。唉，先生，到吃晚饭的时辰，他们若没有像一群鹬鸟似的四散飞光、卷走国王陛下的几尼，就算俺们烧了高香。」

「贴着我站！」阿拉斯泰尔低喝。若不是循道萨姆一把将他拽回，一阵突然的冲撞早把小个子卷走了。
===Original===
“Better yield quiet,” said the gypsy. “We don’t want no blood-lettin’, but we’re boys as is not to be played with. Out with the guineas, tear up the rolls, and the two of ye may go to Hell for all we care.”

“What are you going to do?” Alastair asked his neighbours.

The little man looked bleakly at the crowd. “There don’t seem much of a chance, but we’re bound to put up a fight, seein’ we’re in charge of ’Is Majesty’s property. That your notion, Sam?”

The Methody signified his assent by a cheerful groan.

“Then I’m with you,” said Alastair. “To the inn wall? We must get our backs protected.”

===Chinese===
「还是乖乖交出来的好，」吉普赛人说。「俺们不想放血，可俺们这帮哥儿们也不是好耍弄的。把几尼交出来，把花名册撕了，你们两个爱上哪儿上哪儿，就是下了地狱，俺们也不管。」

「你们打算怎么办？」阿拉斯泰尔问身旁两位。

小个子惨然望了望人群。「看着是没什么指望了，可俺们受命保管国王陛下的财物，好歹得拼上一拼。萨姆，你是这个意思吧？」

循道萨姆用一声乐乐呵呵的哼哼，表示了赞同。

「那好，我与你们同在，」阿拉斯泰尔说。「退到客栈墙根去？得让咱们背后有所凭依。」

===Original===
The suddenness of the movement and the glint of Alastair’s sword opened a way for the three to a re-entrant angle of the inn, where their flanks and rear were safe from attack. Alastair raised his voice.

“Gentlemen,” he said, “as a soldier I cannot permit mutiny. You will not touch a penny of His Majesty’s money, and you will wait here on General Oglethorpe’s orders. If he sees fit to disband you, good and well; if not, you march as he commands.”

Even as he spoke inward laughter consumed him. He, a follower of the Prince, was taking pains that certain troops should reach Wade, the Prince’s enemy. Yet he could not act otherwise, for the camaraderie of his profession constrained him.

The power of the armed over the unarmed was in that moment notably exemplified. There was grumbling, a curse or two, and sullen faces, but no attempt was made to rush that corner where stood an active young man with an ugly sword. The mob swayed and muttered, the gypsy went off on an errand behind the inn, one of the drunkards lurched forward as if to attack and fell prone. A stone or two was thrown, but Alastair showed his pistols, and that form of assault was dropped. The crowd became stagnant, but it did not disperse.

“I must get on to Flambury,” Alastair told his neighbours. “I cannot wait all day here. There is nothing for it but that you go with me. My pistols will get us a passage to my horse yonder, and we can ride and tie.”

===Chinese===
这一下行动的迅疾，加上阿拉斯泰尔剑上一道寒光，为三人在客栈墙角的凹进处辟出一方立身之地，两翼与背后都攻不进去。阿拉斯泰尔扬起声音。

「诸位，」他说，「身为军人，我不能坐视哗变。陛下的钱，你们一个子儿也不许碰，就地听候奥格尔索普将军的将令。他若看得开恩、遣散你们，很好；不然，他叫你们开拔，你们就得开拔。」

话虽如此，说着说着，他心里已笑开了。他，王子殿下的追随者，竟在处心积虑，要让一支兵马顺利开到韦德——王子殿下的敌人——麾下。可他又别无选择，行伍同侪之义，容不得他袖手。

有兵刃者对无兵刃者的威慑，此刻得了淋漓的印证。人群咕哝着，骂了一两声，个个脸色阴沉，却没有人敢扑向那个墙角——那里立着一个精悍的年轻人，握着一柄不祥的剑。人群涌动着、嗡嗡着，那吉普赛人绕到客栈后头办事去了，一个醉汉踉跄抢前似要进攻，却一头栽倒在地。飞来了一两块石头，可阿拉斯泰尔亮出了双枪，这路进攻便告作罢。人群滞住了，却不散去。

「我必须赶到弗兰伯里去，」阿拉斯泰尔对同伴说。「不能在这儿耗上一整天。没法子，只有你们随我同走。我这双枪能替咱们开一条路，到那边我的马跟前，一人骑马、一人步行，轮换着赶路就是了。」
===Original===
The plan was never put into action. For at the moment from a window over their heads descended a shower of red-hot embers. All three leaped forward to avoid a scorching and so moved outside the protecting side wall. Then, neatly and suddenly, the little man called Bill was plucked up and hustled into the crowd. Alastair could not fire or draw upon a circle of gaping faces. He looked furiously to his right, when a cry on his left warned him that the Methody also had gone.

But him he could follow, for he saw the boots of him being dragged inside the inn door. Clearing his way with his sword, he rushed thither, stumbling over the greyhound and with a kick sending it flying. There were three steps to the door, and as he mounted them he obtained a view over the heads of the mob and down the village street. He saw his horse still peacefully tethered to the signpost, and beyond it there came into view a mounted troop clattering up the cobbles.

The door yielded to his foot and he received in his arms the Methody, who seemed to have made his escape from his captors. “They’ve got Bill in the cellar,” he gasped. “It’s that Gypsy Ben.” And then he was stricken dumb at something which he saw below Alastair’s armpits.

The crowd had scattered and its soberer members now clustered in small knots with a desperate effort at nonchalance. Opposite the inn door horsemen had halted, and the leader, a tall man with the black military cockade in his hat, was looking sternly at the group, till his eye caught the Methody. “Ha! Sewell,” he cried, and the Methody, stricken into a ramrod, stood erect before him.

“These are recruits of ours?” he asked. “You have explained to them the new orders?”

===Chinese===
这计划终未得施行。就在此时，他们头顶的一扇窗子里泼下一阵烧得通红的炭火。三人急忙跃前躲烫，便离开了那道护身的侧墙。说时迟那时快，那个名叫比尔的小个子被人一把掳起，裹进人群里去了。面对一圈张口呆看的面孔，阿拉斯泰尔既不能开枪，也不能拔剑。他狂怒地望向右边，左边一声喊叫才让他知道，循道萨姆也没了。

这一个他却追得上，因为他看见那双靴子正被拖进客栈门里。他挥剑开路，直冲过去，绊上那条灵缇，一脚把它踢飞出去。门前有三层台阶，他一跨上去，视线便越过人群头顶，直望到村街尽头。他看见自己的马仍安然拴在招牌柱上，而在马之外，一队骑兵正踏上圆石街面，铿铿地驰上村来。

门被他一脚踹开，循道萨姆正好扑进他怀里，像是刚从掳他的人手里脱身。「他们把比尔弄到地窖里去了，」他喘着说。「是那个吉普赛本（Gypsy Ben）干的。」随即他便哑了——在比阿拉斯泰尔腋窝还低的地方，他看见了什么，惊得说不出话来。

人群已作鸟兽散，其中较清醒的此刻三三两两攒成小堆，拼命装出一副若无其事的模样。客栈门对面停住了骑队，领队是个高个子，帽上缀着黑色的军用帽徽，正冷峻地打量这一伙人，直到目光落在循道萨姆身上。「哈！休厄尔（Sewell），」他喝道，循道萨姆登时僵成一根枪通条，直挺挺立在他面前。

「这些是我们募的兵？」他问。「新的将令，你向他们宣示过了？」

===Original===
“Sir,” said the ramrod, raising his voice so that all could hear, “I have explained, as in dooty bound, and I ’ave to report that, though naturally disappointed, they bows to orders, all but a gypsy rapscallion, of whom we be well quit. I ’ave likewise to report that Bill and me ’as been much assisted by this gentleman you sees before you, without whom things might ’ave gone ugly.”

The tall soldier’s eyes turned towards Alastair and he bowed.

“I am in your debt, sir. General Oglethorpe is much beholden to you.”

“Nay, sir, as a soldier who chanced upon a difficult situation I had no choice but to lend my poor aid.”

The General proffered his snuffbox. “Of which regiment?”

===Chinese===
「回大人，」那根通条提高嗓门，好让人人听见，「俺已恪尽职守，宣示过了。俺还得禀报：他们虽则自然失望，却都俯首听命，只除了一个吉普赛小流氓——走得好，省了俺们的心。俺还要禀报，比尔同俺多蒙您眼前这位爷的大力相助，若没有他，事情怕要闹得难看。」

高个子军人的眼睛转向阿拉斯泰尔，欠身一礼。

「先生，我欠您一份情。奥格尔索普将军对您感激不尽。」

「哪里，先生。一个军人撞上一桩难局，除了献上自己微薄的一臂之力，别无选择。」

将军递上鼻烟盒。「隶属哪个团？」
===Original===
“Of none English. My service has been outside my country, on the continent of Europe. I am born a poor Scottish gentleman, sir, whose sword is his livelihood. They call me Maclean.”

General Oglethorpe looked up quickly. “A most honourable livelihood. I too have carried my sword abroad﻿—to the Americas, as you may have heard. I was returning thither, but I have been intercepted for service in the North. Will you dine with me, sir? I should esteem your company.”

“Nay, I must be on the road,” said Alastair. “Already I have delayed too long. I admire your raw material, sir, but I do not covet your task of shaping it to the purposes of war.”

The General smiled sourly. “In Georgia they would have been good soldiers in a fortnight. Here in England they will be still raw after a year’s campaigning.”

They parted with elaborate courtesies, and looking back, Alastair saw what had five minutes before been an angry mob falling into rank under General Oglethorpe’s eye. He wondered what had become of Ben the Gypsy.

===Chinese===
「不属于英格兰的任何一团。我的军旅都在国外，在欧洲大陆。先生，我生来是个穷苏格兰绅士，一柄剑便是我的营生。人们叫我麦克莱恩（Maclean）。」

奥格尔索普将军猛地抬起眼来。「顶体面的营生。我也曾提剑远赴海外——去过美洲，想必您有耳闻。我本要回那边去的，却被半路截下，调来北方效力。先生，可愿与我共进晚餐？有您作陪，不胜荣幸。」

「不成了，我必须赶路，」阿拉斯泰尔说。「已经耽搁得太久。您这批生坯子我是佩服的，先生，可要把它们锻成打仗的料，这份苦差我可不眼红。」

将军苦笑了一下。「在佐治亚，两礼拜就能把他们练成好兵。在英格兰这儿，打满一年的仗，他们怕还是生坯子。」

两人极尽礼数地作别。阿拉斯泰尔回头望去，只见五分钟前还气势汹汹的暴众，已在奥格尔索普将军的注视下排成了队列。他不知道那个吉普赛人本的下落如何。

===Original===
Flambury proved but a short two-hours’ journey. It was a large village with a broad street studded with ancient elm trees, and, as Alastair entered it, that street was thronged like a hiring fair. The noise of human voices, of fiddles and tabrets and of excited dogs, had greeted him half a mile off, like the rumour of a battlefield. Wondering at the cause of the din, he wondered more when he approached the houses and saw the transformation of the place. There were booths below the elm trees, protected from possible rain by awnings of sacking, where ribbons and crockery and cheap knives were being vended. Men and women, clothed like mummers, danced under the November sky as if it had been May Day. Games of chance were in progress, fortunes were being spae’d, fairings of gingerbread bought, and, not least, horses sold to the accompaniment of shrill cries from stable boys and the whinnyings of startled colts and fillies. The sight gave Alastair a sense of security, for in such an assemblage a stranger would not be questioned. He asked a woman what the stir signified. “Lawk a mussy, where be you borned,” she said, “not to know ’tis Flambury Feast-Day?”

The Dog and Gun was easy to find. Already the darkness was falling, and while the street was lit with tarry staves, the interior of the hostelry glowed with a hundred candles. The sign was undecipherable in the half light, but the name in irregular letters was inscribed above the ancient door. Alastair rode into a courtyard filled with chaises and farmers’ carts, and having with some difficulty found an ostler, stood over him while his horse was groomed, fed and watered. Then he turned to the house, remembering Mr. Kyd’s recommendation to the landlord. If that recommendation could procure him some privacy in this visit, fortunate would have been his meeting with the laird of Greyhouses.

The landlord, discovered not without difficulty, was a lusty florid fellow, with a loud voice and a beery eye. He summoned the traveller into his own parlour, behind the taproom, from which all day his bustling wife directed the affairs of the house. The place was a shrine of comfort, with a bright fire reflected in polished brass and in bottles of cordials and essences which shone like jewels. The wife at a long table was mixing bowl after bowl of spiced liquors, her face glowing like a moon, and her nose perpetually wrinkled in the task of sniffing odours to detect the moment when the brew was right. The husband placed a red-cushioned chair for Alastair, and played nervously with the strings of his apron. It occurred to the traveller that the man had greeted him as if he had been expected, and at this he wondered.

===Chinese===
到弗兰伯里不过短短两小时的路程。那是座大村，一条宽阔的街道两旁古榆成列。阿拉斯泰尔进村时，那条街正拥挤得像雇工集市。人声、提琴声、小鼓声，还有狗群兴奋的吠叫，半英里外便迎面而来，像战场风声的先导。他正自纳闷这喧闹的缘由，待走近房舍，看见村子改头换面的光景，愈加惊奇了。榆树底下搭起一个个摊棚，遮着防雨的麻袋布篷，售卖缎带、陶器和廉价小刀。男男女女穿得像戏台上的丑角，在十一月的天空下跳舞，只当是五朔节到了。赌运气的把戏正玩得热闹，有人给人算命，有人买姜饼小礼，最要紧的还有马匹买卖——马夫们的尖声叫喊，与小马驹小母马受惊的嘶鸣，交响成一片。这景象让阿拉斯泰尔心生安稳：在这样一堆人里，一个外乡人是不会受人盘问的。他问一个妇人这热闹是何名堂。「哎呀我的天，你是哪儿生哪儿长的，」她说，「连今天是弗兰伯里的节庆日都不知道？」

狗与枪客栈（the Dog and Gun）好找。夜幕已经落下，街上点着浸沥青的火把照明，客栈里面则百来支蜡烛烛火通明。招牌在昏光里辨不清楚，好在古旧的大门上方题着字迹歪斜的店名。阿拉斯泰尔骑马进了一个院子，院里挤满轻便马车和庄户人的板车。他费了些周折寻到一个马夫，便站在一旁督着，看自己的马刷洗、上料、饮水，一应齐全。然后他转身进屋，想起基德先生给店主的引荐。此番投宿若能凭这引荐讨得几分清静，那么与格雷豪斯领主的那番相遇，便真是他的造化了。

店主也颇费了些周折才寻到，是个红光满面、精壮结实的汉子，嗓门洪亮，一双眼泡着酒意。他把旅人让进自家客堂，在酒厅后头，他那闲不住的老婆整日在这里调度全店的营生。这地方是一座安乐的圣殿：炉火明亮，映在擦得锃亮的黄铜器上，映在一瓶瓶甜酒香露上，宝光闪闪赛过珠宝。老板娘在长桌旁一碗接一碗地调制加香料的酒，脸庞灿烂如一轮明月，鼻子不住地皱着，嗅着气味，好捉住火候正好的那一瞬。店主为阿拉斯泰尔端来一把红垫椅子，自己却神经质地捻着围裙的系带。旅人心念一动：这人招呼他时，那样子竟像早就候着他来；他不由得纳罕。
===Original===
The name of Mr. Kyd was a talisman that wrought mightily upon the host’s goodwill, but that goodwill was greater than his powers.

“Another time and the whole house would have been at your honour’s service,” he protested. “But today﻿—” and he shrugged his shoulders. “Oh, you shall have a bed, though I have to lie myself on bare boards, but a private room is out of my power. We’ve but the three of them, and they’re all as throng as a beehive. There’s Tom Briggs in the Blue Room, celebrating the sale of his string of young horses﻿—an ancient engagement, sir; and there’s the Codgers’ Supper in the Gents’ Attic, and in Shrewsbury there’s five pig dealers sleeping on chairs. That’s so, mother?”

“Six in Shrewsbury,” said the lady, “and there’s five waiting on the Attic, as soon as the Codgers have supped.”

“You see, sir, how I’m situated. You’ll have a good bed to yourself, but I fear I must ask you to sup in the bar parlour with the other gentry that’s here today. Unless your honour would prefer the kitchen?” he added hopefully.

Alastair, who had a vision of a company of drunken squirelings of an inquisitive turn, announced that he would greatly prefer the kitchen. The decision seemed to please the landlord.

===Chinese===
基德先生的名号是一道符咒，把店主的善意大大催动起来，无奈这份善意大过了他的能耐。

「换个日子，小店上上下下都听凭老爷使唤，」他分辩道。「可是今儿个——」他耸了耸肩。「床嘛，总归给老爷匀一张出来，哪怕小人自家去睡光板，可单间是实在无能为力了。统共就三间，间间挤得跟蜂巢似的。蓝屋里住着汤姆·布里格斯（Tom Briggs），正庆贺他那批小马驹出了手——这是老早就定下的席面，先生；绅士阁楼里摆着老哥们的晚宴；什鲁斯伯里（Shrewsbury）那边，五个猪贩子还睡在椅子上呢。是不是这么回事，孩子他妈？」

「什鲁斯伯里是六个，」太太说，「阁楼那边还有五个候着，等老哥们一散席就上。」

「老爷您瞧，小人的境况就是这样。一张好床单睡是有的，只是晚饭恐怕得屈尊到酒吧雅座间，同今天住店的别的体面客人一处用。除非老爷您老更愿意上厨房？」他满怀希望地补了一句。

阿拉斯泰尔脑中闪过一伙好奇心盛、醉醺醺的小乡绅，忙不迭宣布自己大大地宁愿去厨房。这决定似乎正中店主下怀。

===Original===
“There’s a good fire and not above half a dozen for company at present. Warm yourself there, sir, and your supper will be ready before your feet are comforted. A dish of pullets and eggs, mutton chops, a prime ham, a good cut of beef, and the best of double Gloucester. What say you to that now? And for liquor a bowl of mother’s spiced October, with a bottle of old port to go with the cheese.”

Alastair was hungry enough to approve of the lot, and tired and cold enough to welcome the chance of a roaring kitchen hearth. In the great shadowy place, the rafters loaded with hams and the walls bright with warming-pans, there was only a handful of topers, since the business out-of-doors was still too engrossing. The landlord was as good as his word, and within half an hour the traveller was sitting down to a most substantial meal at the massive board. The hostess’s spiced October was delicate yet potent, the port thereafter﻿—of which the host had a couple of glasses﻿—a generous vintage. The young man at length drew his chair from the table to the fireside and stretched his legs to the blaze, replete and comfortable in body, and placid, if a little hazy, in mind.﻿ ﻿… Presently the leaping flames of the logs took odd shapes; the drone of voices from the corner became surf on a shore: he saw a fire on a beach and dark hills behind it, and heard the soft Gaelic of his kin.﻿ ﻿… His head nodded on his breast and he was sound asleep.

He woke to find an unpleasant warmth below his nose and to hear a cackle as of a thousand geese in his ears. Something bright and burning was close to his face. He shrank from it and at once sprawled on his back, his head bumping hard on the stone floor.

The shock thoroughly awakened him. As he sprang to his feet he saw a knot of flushed giggling faces. One of the group had been holding a red-hot poker to his face, while another had drawn away the chair from beneath him.

===Chinese===
「那儿炉火旺，眼下作伴的不过五六个客人。老爷先去暖暖身，等您脚还暖过来，晚饭就得齐了。一盘雏鸡烩蛋、羊排、一只上等火腿、一块好牛肉，还有顶好的双重格洛斯特（double Gloucester）干酪。您说这成不成？喝的呢，来一碗老板娘的加香十月酿，再配一瓶陈年波特酒佐干酪。」

阿拉斯泰尔饿得可以，照单全收；又乏又冷，正巴不得有一炉熊熊的厨房旺火。这间阔大幽暗的屋子里，椽子上挂满火腿，墙上的铜暖床盘熠熠生光，眼下只有寥寥几个酒客，因为外头的热闹还正浓着。店主言而有信，不到半个钟头，旅人已在那张厚重的长案前坐下来，用一顿丰盛至极的晚餐。老板娘的加香十月酿清醇而后劲绵长；随后的波特酒——店主自家也蹭了两杯——更是慷慨的陈年佳酿。年轻人末了把椅子从桌边拖到炉边，向火光伸直了两腿，肚里充实，浑身舒坦，心里安恬，只是有点迷糊。……不多时，柴火上跳跃的火苗幻出种种奇形；角落里人语的嗡嗡变成拍岸的浪涛：他看见沙滩上的一堆篝火，火后是黑黢黢的群山，还听见族人们柔软的盖尔语。……他的头垂到胸前，沉沉睡熟了。

他一觉醒来，觉出鼻子底下有一股说不出的热，耳朵里灌进一阵仿佛一千只鹅齐声的聒噪。一样明亮烧灼的东西正凑在他脸前。他慌忙一缩，登时仰面朝天摔在地上，后脑勺在石板上磕得不轻。

这一震把他彻底震醒了。他一跃而起，看见一小团涨红窃笑的面孔。这群人里一个，方才正把一根烧红的火钎凑在他脸前，另一个则把他屁股底下的椅子抽走了。
===Original===
His first impulse was to buffet their heads, for no man is angrier than a sleeper rudely awakened. The kitchen was now crowded, and the company seemed to appreciate the efforts of the practical jokers, for there was a roar of applause and shouts of merriment. The jokers, who from their dress were hobbledehoy yeomen or small squires, were thus encouraged to continue, and, being apparently well on the way to drunkenness, were not disposed to consider risks. Two of them wore swords, but it was clear that the sword was not their weapon.

Alastair in a flaming passion had his hand on his blade, when his arm was touched from behind and a voice spoke. “Control your temper, sir, I beseech you. This business is premeditated. They seek to fasten a quarrel on you. Don’t look round. Smile and laugh with them.”

The voice was familiar though he could not put a name to it. A second glance at the company convinced him that the advice was sound and he forced himself to urbanity. He took his hand from his sword, rubbed his eyes like one newly awakened, and forced a parody of a smile.

“I have been asleep,” he stammered. “Forgive my inattention, gentlemen. You were saying﻿ ﻿… Ha ha! I see! A devilish good joke, sir. I dreamed I was a blacksmith and woke to believe I had fallen in the fire.”

The hobbledehoys were sober enough to be a little nonplussed at this reception of their pleasantry. They stood staring sheepishly, all but one who wore a mask and a nightcap, as if he had just come from a mumming show. To judge by his voice he seemed older than the rest.

“Tell us your dreams,” he said rudely. “From your talk in your sleep they should have been full of treason. Who may you be, sir?”

===Chinese===
他的第一个冲动是给这几颗脑袋一人一记老拳——被人从睡梦里粗暴搅醒的人，火气是天下最大的。厨房里此刻挤满了人，看客们似乎很欣赏这几个恶作剧者的手段，四下里爆出喝彩与哄笑。那几个恶作剧的，看衣装是些半大不小的自耕农或是小乡绅，得了彩头愈发来劲，又分明已有八九分醉意，全不去想什么风险。其中两个佩着剑，可一看便知，剑不是他们的家伙。

阿拉斯泰尔怒火中烧，手已按上剑柄，忽觉手臂被人从背后一碰，一个声音开了口。「务必按住性子，先生，我求您了。这事儿是有预谋的。他们要寻衅把一场争吵栽到您头上。别回头。跟他们一起笑，一起乐。」

这声音耳熟，他却一时想不起名姓。再扫一眼满屋的人，他确信这忠告有理，便强自做出一副温文模样。他的手从剑上放下来，像初醒的人那样揉着眼睛，挤出一丝勉强的笑。

「方才睡着了，」他讷讷地说。「诸位先生恕我不恭。你们方才说到……哈哈！我明白了！妙极了的玩笑，先生。我梦见自己是个铁匠，醒来还当真掉进火炉里了。」

那几个小子还算清醒，被这份对待他们玩笑的回应弄得有些下不来台，讪讪地站着瞪眼，只除了一人——那人脸上蒙着面具，头上扣一顶睡帽，像刚从化装戏台上下来。听嗓音，他比其余的都年长些。

「说说你的梦，」他无礼地说。「照你梦话里讲的，你的梦里该满是大逆不道。敢问足下是什么人，先生？」

===Original===
Alastair, at sight of a drawer’s face round the corner of the taproom door, called for a bowl of punch.

“Who am I?” he said quietly. “A traveller who has acquired a noble thirst, which he would fain share with other good fellows.”

“Your name, my thirsty friend?”

“Why, they call me Watson﻿—Andrew Watson, and my business is to serve his Grace of Queensberry, that most patriotic nobleman.” He spoke from a sudden fancy, rather than from any purpose; it was not likely that he could be controverted, for Mr. Kyd was now posting into Wiltshire.

His questioner looked puzzled, but it was obvious that the name of a duke, and Queensberry at that, had made an impression upon the company. The man spoke aside with a friend, and then left the kitchen. This was so clear a proof that there had been purpose in his baiting that Alastair could have found it in him to laugh at such clumsy conspirators. Somehow word had been sent of his coming, and there had been orders to entangle him; but the word had not been clear and his ill-wishers were still in doubt about his identity. It was his business in no way to enlighten them, but he would have given much to discover the informant.

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔瞥见酒厅门角露出一张跑堂的脸，便叫了一碗潘趣酒。

「我是什么人？」他不动声色地说。「一个赶路的，赶出了一肚子顶体面的渴，正想同别的好汉子们分享分享。」

「尊姓大名，我渴着的朋友？」

「哦，人们叫我沃森——安德鲁·沃森，我的营生是伺候昆斯伯里公爵大人，那位最忠君爱国的贵人。」这话是灵机一动说出的，倒非存心安排；反正也不大可能有人对证——基德先生这会儿正乘车赶往威尔特郡（Wiltshire）去了。

发问的人露出困惑之色，可公爵的名号——偏还是昆斯伯里——显然给满座留下了印象。那人跟一个同伴耳语了几句，随即离了厨房。这就再清楚不过地证明，方才的挑衅是有的放矢，阿拉斯泰尔简直要笑出声来——好一群笨手笨脚的同谋。不知怎么，他前来的消息已被人递了出去，还有人下了令要缠住他；只是口信含糊，他的仇家们对他的身份仍拿不准。他要做的，就是绝不给他们点破；可若能查出那个报信的，他情愿付出许多。
===Original===
He had forgotten about the mentor at his elbow. Turning suddenly, he was confronted with the queer figure of the tutor of Chastlecote, who was finishing a modest supper of bread and cheese at the main table. The man’s clothes were shabbier than ever, but his face and figure were more wholesome than at Cornbury. His cheeks had a faint weathering, his neck was less flaccid, and he held himself more squarely. As Alastair turned, he also swung round, his left hand playing a tattoo upon his knee. His eye was charged with confidences.

“We meet again,” he whispered. “Ever since we parted I have had a premonition of this encounter. I have much for your private ear.”

But it was not told, for the leader of the hobbledehoys, the fellow with the mask and nightcap, was again in the kitchen. It looked as if he had been given instructions by someone, for he shouted, as a man does when he is uncertain of himself and would keep up his courage.

“Gentlefolk all, there are vipers among us tonight. This man who calls himself a duke’s agent, and the hedge schoolmaster at his elbow. They are naught but lousy Jacobites and ’tis our business as good Englishmen to strip and search them.”

===Chinese===
他把肘边那位高人忘在了脑后。猛一回头，正撞上查斯特科特（Chastlecote）那位家庭教师的古怪身影——他正在大桌旁吃完一顿面包就奶酪的简朴晚饭。此人的衣衫比先前更加寒酸，面容身形却比在科恩伯里那时健旺了。他脸颊上添了一层风吹日晒的薄色，脖颈不那么松垂了，身架子也端方了许多。阿拉斯泰尔一转身，他也跟着旋过身来，左手在膝盖上敲着一串急促的点子。他一眼睛都是按捺不住的心腹话。

「我们又见面了，」他悄声道。「自分别那天起，我就预感到必有此遇。我有许多话，只说与您一人听。」

可这心腹话没能出口，因为那伙小子的头儿——蒙面具扣睡帽的那位——又回到了厨房。看那样子像是受了什么人的指使，因为他大声嚷嚷，正像一个人心下发虚、要给自己壮胆时那样。

「列位上等人，今晚咱们中间混进了毒蛇。就是这个自称公爵代理人的家伙，还有他手边那个乡野塾师。他们不过是满身虱子的雅各布党徒，咱们身为正经英格兰人，就该剥了他们的衣裳搜身。」

===Original===
The others of his party cried out in assent, and there was a measure of support from the company at large. But before a man could stir the tutor spoke.

“Bad law!” he said. “I and, for all I know, the other gentleman are inoffensive travellers moving on our lawful business. You cannot lay hand on us without a warrant from a justice. But, sirs, I am not one to quibble about legality. This fellow has insulted me grossly and shall here and now be brought to repentance. Put up your hands, you rogue.”

The tutor had suddenly become a fearsome figure. He had risen from his chair, struggled out of his coat, and, blowing like a bull, was advancing across the floor on his adversary, his great doubled fists held up close to his eyes. The other gave ground.

“I do not fight with scum,” he growled. But as the tutor pressed on him, his hand went to his sword.

He was not permitted to draw it. “You will fight with the natural weapon of Englishmen,” his assailant cried, and caught the sword strap and broke it, so that the weapon clattered into a corner and its wearer spun round like a top. The big man seemed to have the strength of a bull. “Put up your hands,” he cried again, “or take a coward’s drubbing.”

===Chinese===
他那伙人齐声喊好，满座看客中也有几分随声附和。可还没等有人挪动一步，那家庭教师开腔了。

「于法不合！」他说。「据我所知，我同这位先生都是安分赶路的旅客，各有各的正务。没有治安法官签发的拘票，你们不得动我们一根毫毛。不过，列位，我不是死抠法条的人。这家伙方才恶语侮辱了我，此时此地就得叫他悔罪。举起手来，你这无赖。」

那家庭教师霎时间变成了一尊可怖的人物。他从椅子上站起，挣扎着褪下外套，像公牛一般喘着粗气，一双攥紧的大拳头高举齐眼，一步一步朝对头逼过去。对头退了。

「我不跟下三滥过招，」他低吼道。可家庭教师步步进逼，他的手便摸向了剑柄。

剑是不容他出鞘的。「英格兰人的天然兵器才是你的对手，」进逼者喝道，一把攥住剑带挣断了，那剑哗啦一声摔进角落，佩剑的人却像陀螺似的原地打了个转。这大个子的力气俨然是一头公牛。「举起手来，」他又喝道，「不然就领一顿专打懦夫的臭揍。」
===Original===
The company was now in high excitement, and its sympathies were mainly against the challenged. Seeing this, he made a virtue of necessity, doubled his fists, ducked and got in a blow on the tutor’s brisket. The latter had no skill, but immense reach and strength and the uttermost resolution. He simply beat down the other’s guard, reckless of the blows he received, and presently dealt him such a clout that he measured his length on the floor, whence he rose sick and limping and departed on the arm of a friend. The victor, his cheeks mottled red and grey and his breath whistling like the wind in a chimney, returned amid acclamation to the fireside, where he accepted a glass of Alastair’s punch.

For a moment the haggardness was wiped from the man’s face, and it shone with complacence. His eyes shot jovial but martial glances at the company.

“We have proved our innocence,” he whispered to Alastair. “Had you used sword or pistol you would have been deemed spy and foreigner, but a bout of fisticuffs is the warrant of the true-born Englishman.”

===Chinese===
满座此刻群情鼎沸，人心大半不帮那位被挑战的。他见势如此，索性把不得已装成美德，攥起双拳，一低头，朝教师的前胸捣进一拳。教师毫无拳技，可臂展、膂力俱属上乘，心志更是决绝到了头。他全不理会自己挨的打，只管硬砸开对方的门户，不多时便一记重拳，把那人结结实实放倒，直挺挺在地上量了个全身长。那人爬起来时头晕欲呕、一瘸一拐，由一个朋友搀着去了。得胜者两颊红一块灰一块，喘气像风灌烟囱似的呼哨作响，在满堂彩声中回到炉边，接过了阿拉斯泰尔的一杯潘趣酒。

片刻之间，那人脸上的憔悴一扫而光，焕发出志得意满的光彩。他向满座投去既豪爽又威武的眼色。

「咱们的清白已然证得，」他悄声对阿拉斯泰尔说。「方才您若用了剑或枪，就要被当作奸细和外邦人了；而一场拳头架，正是土生英格兰人的凭证。」

## Dedication / 题献

===Original===
To 

 **Vernon Watney**
We two confess twin loyalties﻿—
 

 Wychwood beneath the April skies
 

 Is yours, and many a scented road
 

 That winds in June by Evenlode.
 

 Not less when autumn fires the brake,
 

 Yours the deep heath by Fannich’s lake,
 

 The corries where the dun deer roar
 

 And eagles wheel above Sgurr Mór.
 

 So I, who love with equal mind
 

 The southern sun, the northern wind,
 

 The lilied lowland water-mead
 

 And the grey hills that cradle Tweed,
 

 Bring you this tale which haply tries
 

 To intertwine our loyalties.

===Chinese===
献给

**弗农·沃特尼（Vernon Watney）**

你我二人，同怀一对孪生的忠诚——
四月晴空下的威奇伍德森林，
连同条条飘香的小路，
六月里傍着埃文洛德河蜿蜒，都归于你。

而当秋色燃遍丛莽，
法尼奇湖畔深深的石楠荒原也归于你，
褐鹿吼鸣的山间幽谷，
还有盘旋于斯古尔·莫尔峰上空的苍鹰。

于是我，怀着同样的心肠
爱南国的太阳、北地的长风，
爱百合盛开的低地水草甸，
和摇篮般环抱特威德河的灰色群山，
谨以此篇相赠——它或许正试着
将你我两人的忠诚交织为一。

## XIV Duchess Kitty on the Road / 十四、基蒂公爵夫人在途中

===Original===
Five hours’ sleep were not enough to rest his body, but they were all that his unquiet mind would permit. He woke to a sense of great weariness combined with a feverish impulse to drive himself to the last limits of his strength. His limbs were desperately stiff, and at his first attempt to rise he rolled over. A bed had been made for him in the attic of the farm, and the view from the window showed only the benty shoulder of a hill. Slowly the doings of the night came back to him; from the bowels of the earth he seemed to hear the mutterings of Journeyman John, and he crawled down the trap-ladder in a fret to escape from the place of horror.

In the kitchen the Spainneach was cooking eggs in a pan, smiling and crooning to himself as if the morning and the world were good. He put Alastair in a chair and fed him tenderly, beating up an egg in a cup with French brandy.

===Chinese===
五个钟头的睡眠，不足以歇息他的身体，可他那颗不得安宁的心，也只容得下这五个钟头。醒来时，他只觉困乏已极，却又有一股热病似的冲动，驱使他把气力驱到最后一限。他的四肢僵得厉害，头一回试着起身，竟又翻倒回去。人家在农舍的阁楼里给他支了一张床，从窗口望出去，只见一座山峦那长满野草的肩坡。昨夜的种种，慢慢回到了他心头；他仿佛还听得见地腹深处帮工约翰（Journeyman John）的咕哝，便烦躁地爬下活板门的梯子，只想快些逃离这处恐怖之地。

厨房里，西班牙人正用平底锅煎蛋，一面微笑着低声哼唱，仿佛这个早晨和整个世界都甚是美好。他把阿拉斯泰尔安顿在椅中，慈爱地喂他吃食，又把一枚蛋打在杯里，兑上法国白兰地搅匀。

===Original===
“Have that for your morning’s draught, Sir Sandy,” he said. “You are with your friends now, so let your anxieties sleep.”

“They cannot,” said the young man. “I have lost weeks of precious time. My grief! but I have been the broken reed to lean on! And the Prince is in this very shire.”

“Tonight he will lie in Derby. Lord George Murray has led a column in advance to Congleton and the Duke of Kingston has fled back to Lichfield. His Grace of Newcastle has sent offers to the Prince. All goes well, heart’s darling. Your friends have given Cumberland the slip and are on the straight road to London.”

The news stirred his languid blood.

“But the West,” he cried. “What news of the West﻿—of Barrymore and Sir Watkin and Beaufort? There is the rub.” And with the speaking of the words the whole story of the past weeks unrolled itself clear and he dropped his head into his hands and groaned. Then he staggered to his feet.

“There is a man reaches Brightwell this day. He must be seized﻿—him and his papers.” Swiftly he told the story of Kyd. “Let me lay hands on him and I will extort the truth though I have to roast him naked, and that truth the Prince must have before a man of us sleep. It is the magic key that will unlock St. James’s. Have you men to lend me?”

===Chinese===
「把这个当您清晨的提神饮子吧，桑迪爵士，」他说。「您如今是同自己的朋友们在一处了，且让那些焦心事都睡去吧。」

「它们睡不得，」年轻人说。「我已经虚掷了几个星期的宝贵光阴。我的天！我竟成了那根折断的芦苇，教人倚不得！而王子殿下此刻就在这个郡里。」

「今夜他将宿在德比。乔治·默里勋爵已率一支纵队先行到了康格尔顿（Congleton），金斯顿公爵则败退回了利奇菲尔德（Lichfield）。纽卡斯尔公爵大人也已向王子殿下递了和议的条件。一切都顺遂，心爱的。您的朋友们已经甩脱了坎伯兰，正直趋伦敦的大道而去。」

这消息搅动了他委顿的血液。

「可是西部呢，」他叫道。「西部有何消息——巴里摩尔（Barrymore）、沃特金爵士、博福特公爵那边如何？症结正在这里。」话一出口，过去几个星期的全部情事便清清楚楚在眼前铺展开来，他把头埋进双手里呻吟起来。随即他摇摇晃晃地站起身。

「今天有一个人要到布莱特韦尔。必须把他拿下——连人带文件一齐拿下。」他三言两语把基德的事讲了一遍。「只要教我的一双手够得着他，便是把他剥光了烤，我也要榨出真相来；而这真相，王子殿下必须在我们中任何人入睡之前到手。它是一把魔钥，能开圣詹姆斯宫的锁。你可有人手借我？」

===Original===
The Spainneach smiled. “Last night they tracked you, as few men in England could, and they were here to overpower the rascaldom that held the door. Now they are scattered, but I have a call to pipe them back like curlews. The Spoonbills are at your back, Sir Sandy.”

“Then for God’s sake let us be going,” Alastair cried. “Have you a horse for me, for my legs are like broomshanks?”

“Two are saddled and waiting outbye. But first I have a little errand to fulfil, which the Master charged on me.”

From a shed he brought armfuls of hay and straw and piled them in a corner where the joists of the roof came low and the thatch could be reached by a man’s hand. Into the dry mass he flung a smouldering sod from the fire. As Alastair, stiffly feeling his stirrups, passed between the dry-stone gateposts, he heard a roaring behind him, and, turning, saw flames licking the roof.

“Presently Journeyman John will lie bare to the heavens,” said the Spainneach, “and the wayfaring man, though a fool, will understand. Brightwell is your goal, Sir Sandy? ’Tis fifteen moorland miles.”

===Chinese===
西班牙人微微一笑。「昨夜他们一路追踪你，那份本事英格兰没有几人及得上；他们又赶到这里，制服了把住门口的那伙匪类。如今他们散在各处，可我自有哨音，能把他们像杓鹬一般一只只唤回来。篦鹭会便是你的后盾，桑迪爵士。」

「那看在上帝份上，我们这就动身，」阿拉斯泰尔喊道。「你有马给我么？我这两条腿已经瘦得像金雀花秆了。」

「两匹马已经备好鞍，在外头候着。不过先得办一件小小的差事，是会主（the Master）吩咐我的。」

他从棚屋里抱出一抱抱干草麦秸，堆在屋角——那里屋梁低垂，人一伸手便够得着茅草顶。他往那堆干燥的柴草里，扔进一块从灶膛里掏出的阴燃草皮。待阿拉斯泰尔僵硬地摸索着够上马镫、穿过干砌的石门柱时，只听身后一声轰响，回头看时，火舌已舔上屋顶。

「不多时，帮工约翰便要赤条条地朝天躺卧了，」西班牙人说，「这样一来，便是路上的愚人，也能明白底细。布莱特韦尔是你的去处吧，桑迪爵士？去那儿是十五英里荒原路。」

===Original===
“First let us go to the Sleeping Deer,” was the answer. “I have a beard weeks old, and my costume is not my own. Please God, this day I am going into good society and have a high duty to perform, so I would be decently attired.”

The Spainneach laughed. “Still your old self. You were always for the thing done in order. But for this Kyd of yours﻿—he comes to Brightwell today, and may depart again, before you take order with him. It is desirable that he be detained?”

“By God, he shall never go,” cried Alastair.

“The Spoonbills do not fight, but they can make a hedge about a man, and they can bring us news of him.”

So at a grey cottage in the winding of a glen the Spainneach turned aside, telling Alastair that he would overtake him, and when he caught him up his face was content. “Mr. Kyd will not enter Brightwell unknown to us,” he said, “and he will assuredly not leave it.”

===Chinese===
「先让我们去一趟睡鹿客栈，」他答道。「我脸上的胡子已经蓄了几个星期，身上这行头也不是我自己的。如蒙上帝垂允，今天我要去见上等人物，还有一桩重大的使命要完成，所以我要穿得体面些。」

西班牙人大笑。「你还是从前的你，凡事总要按部就班。只是你那位基德——他今天到布莱特韦尔，也许还没等你部署停当，人又走了。最好把他绊住，是也不是？」

「凭上帝起誓，他休想走脱，」阿拉斯泰尔喊道。

「篦鹭会的人不动刀兵，可他们能围出一道人墙，还能给我们传他的行踪。」

于是，在一道幽谷的转折处、一座灰色的村舍前，西班牙人拨转马头，只说随后便赶来。待他重新追上，满面都是得色。「基德先生进布莱特韦尔，休想瞒过我们的耳目，」他说，「而要想离开，那是万万不能。」

===Original===
The day had been bright in the morning, but ere they descended from the high moors to the wider valleys the wind had veered to the north, and a cold mist had blown up, which seemed a precursor of storm. Rain fell heavily and then cleared, leaving a windy sky patched with blue and ruffled with sleet blasts. The tonic weather did much to refresh Alastair’s body, and to add fuel, if that were possible, to the fire in his brain. He knew that he was living and moving solely on the passion in his spirit, for his limbs were fit only for blankets and sleep. When his horse stumbled or leaned on the bit he realised that the strength had gone out of his arms. But his mind amazed him by its ardour of resolution, as if all the anxieties of the past week had been fused into one white-hot fury.﻿ ﻿… So far the Prince had not failed, and these forced marches which would place him between Cumberland and the capital were surely proof of undivided counsels. Perhaps he had news of the West after all. There was his own letter to Lochiel﻿—but in that he had promised proofs at Derby, and this day the Prince would be in Derby and would not find him.

“You have seen His Highness?” he asked the Spainneach.

“At Manchester, for a brief minute, surrounded by white cockades.”

“How did he look?”

“Sad and reflective﻿—like a man who has staked much against odds and does not greatly hope.”

===Chinese===
清晨天色本是晴明的，可不等他们走下高荒原、进入开阔的谷地，风已转北，一股冷雾迎面卷起，像是风暴的先声。大雨倾盆而下，随即又放晴了，只留下一片劲风鼓荡的天空，缀着几块蓝色，不时被一阵阵雨雪吹皱。这提神的天气大大振作了阿拉斯泰尔的身体，也给他脑中的火——若那火还能更旺的话——再添了柴。他心里明白，自己如今全凭胸中一股热忱支撑着行走坐卧，因为他的四肢只配裹着毯子安睡。马一个趔趄，或是衔口上缰绳一沉，他便觉出臂上的力气已经使尽。可他心志的炽烈却叫他自己吃惊，仿佛过去一星期的种种焦灼，全都熔成了同一种白热的怒火。……到此为止，王子殿下还没有失手，而这些将把他送到坎伯兰与京城之间的强行军，足见营中谋议齐心、号令划一。也许他终究还是得到了西部的消息。还有他自己写给洛希尔的那封信——可信里他许的是在德比献上证据，而今天王子殿下便要到德比，却寻不着他这个人。

「您见过殿下？」他问西班牙人。

「在曼彻斯特，匆匆一面，前后不过片刻功夫，他周围簇拥着一片白帽章（white cockades）。」

「他气色如何？」

「忧郁而若有所思——像个把大注押在劣势上、并不抱多大指望的人。」

===Original===
It was the picture he had made in his own mind. But by Heaven he would change it, and bring a sparkle again to those eyes and the flush of hope to that noble brow.﻿ ﻿… For weeks no news could have reached the camp from the West, for Kyd would have passed it to Norreys and Norreys to one of the Whig Dukes in Nottinghamshire, and if the levies had marched from Wales the Government had had ample warning to intercept them.﻿ ﻿… Probably they had not started, for Kyd could no doubt counterfeit orders from the Prince. But the point was that they were there﻿—men, armed men, and money﻿—ready and eager for the field. His thoughts were drawing to a point now, and he realised what had been the vague fear that so long had tormented him. It was that the Prince would lose heart﻿—nay, not he, but his Council, and instead of striking for St. James’s, fall back to a defensive war inside the Scottish Border. That way lay destruction, slow or speedy﻿—with England unconverted and France uncommitted. But the bold road, the true road, would bring France and England to their side, and strike terror to the heart of their already perplexed enemy. Tower Hill or St. James’s! Would to God he was now by the Prince’s side, instead of Lord George with his slow Atholl drawl, or the Secretary Murray, fussy and spluttering and chickenhearted, or the Teagues, whose boldness was that of kerns and only made the others more cautious. At the thought of his Prince’s haggard face he groaned aloud.

But, please God, it was still in his power to find the remedy, and by evening the peril might be past. He spurred his horse at the thought, and, since the beasts were fresh and they were now on the good turf of the vales, the miles flew fast, and they rode out of sleet showers into sun. To his surprise he found that his attitude to Kyd had changed. He loathed the man and longed to crush him, but it was as a vile creeping thing and not as a personal enemy. But against Sir John Norreys he felt a furious hatred. The thing was illogical﻿—to hate a tool rather than the principal, the more as Norreys had done him no personal ill, while Kyd had connived at his death. But had the two been on the sward before him with drawn swords he could have left the laird of Grey houses to the Spainneach and taken the baronet for himself. Why? His heart inexorably gave the answer. The man was the husband of the russet lady; to her ears he had lied, and with his lies drawn a moan of pity from her gentle lips. For Sir John Norreys, Alastair reserved a peculiar vengeance. Kyd might fall to a file of the Prince’s muskets, but Norreys must die before the cold point of his own steel. And then﻿ ﻿… ? Claudia would be a free woman﻿—sorrowful, disillusioned, shamefaced, but still a child with the world before her, a white page on which love could yet write a happy tale.

===Chinese===
这正是他心中自己描画过的那幅图景。可是上天作证，他要把这图景改过来，要让那双眼睛重新迸出光彩，让希望的红晕重新染上那高贵的额头。……几个星期以来，西部的消息根本无从递到营中，因为基德必已把它递给诺里斯，诺里斯再递给诺丁汉郡的哪一位辉格党公爵；威尔士的募兵若果真开拔，政府也早有充分的预警去拦截他们。……多半他们压根没有开拔，因为基德无疑伪造得出王子殿下的手令。可要紧的是：他们就在那里——人，带枪的人，还有钱——整装待发，只求一战。此刻他的思绪渐渐收拢成一点，他也明白了那么久以来折磨着他的那团模糊的恐惧究竟是什么。那就是：王子殿下会失去胆气——不，不是他本人，而是他的议事会；不去直捣圣詹姆斯宫，反而退到苏格兰边境之内打一场守势的仗。那样便通向毁灭，或迟或速——英格兰之心未附，法兰西之师不出。而那条大胆的路，那条真正的路，却能把法兰西和英格兰拉到自己一边，给那早已心慌意乱的敌人胸中再添一阵恐怖。要么上塔丘（Tower Hill），要么进圣詹姆斯宫！但愿上帝让他此刻就在王子殿下身旁——而不是让乔治勋爵带着他那阿索尔（Atholl）腔慢条斯理的嗓音，不是让那位大惊小怪、唾沫横飞、胆小如鼠的国务秘书默里，也不是让那些爱尔兰蒂格兵（Teagues）——那种人的勇悍，不过是凯恩轻卒（kerns）之勇，只会教旁人愈发谨慎。一想到王子殿下那憔悴的面容，他不禁出声呻吟。

可是，如蒙上帝恩准，补救之权仍在他手中，到得今晚，这场危局也许已然过去。想到这里他一夹马刺；马匹精力正盛，脚下又已是谷地里的好草皮，里程飞快地向后掠去，他们冲出一阵阵雨雪，驰进了阳光。他惊讶地发现，自己对基德的态度变了。他仍旧憎恶此人，渴望把他碾碎，但那是对一条阴毒爬虫的憎恶，而不是对私敌的仇恨。可对约翰·诺里斯爵士，他却怀着狂怒的憎恨。这于理不通——恨一件工具反胜过恨正主，何况诺里斯并不曾亲身加害于他，基德倒是默许过害他性命。可这两人若此刻拔剑立在他面前的草地上，他尽可以把格雷豪斯的庄主留给西班牙人，自己单取那从男爵。为什么？他的心毫不容情地给出了答案。此人是栗衣女士的丈夫；他对她的耳朵撒了谎，又用那些谎话，从她温柔的唇间骗出一声怜惜的叹息。对约翰·诺里斯爵士，阿拉斯泰尔留着一手特别的报复。基德尽可以死在王子殿下一排火枪之下，诺里斯却必须死在他自己的剑锋寒芒之前。到那时……？克劳迪娅便是自由之身了——哀恸的、幻灭的、含羞的，却依然是个前程铺展在面前的孩子，一页白纸，爱情仍能在上面写出一个幸福的故事。

===Original===
They skirted the little hill on which Alastair had stood with Midwinter, and came to the high road and the door of the Sleeping Deer. There was now no need of back stairs, and Alastair, giving up his horse to an ostler, boldly entered the hall and made for the landlord’s sanctum. But an elegant travelling trunk caught his eye, its leather bearing the blazon of a crowned heart, and by the fire a lackey in a red-and-blue livery was warming himself. A glance through the open door of the stable-yard revealed more red and blue, and a fine coach which three stable-boys were washing. The landlord was not in his room, but in the kitchen, superintending the slicing of hams, the plucking of pullets and the spicing of great tankards of ale. At the sight of Alastair he started, called another to take his place at the table and beckoned him out-of-doors.

“I’m joyful to see ye again, for I feared ye had come by foul play. That Scotch serving-man was here seeking ye more than once, and”﻿—lowering his voice﻿—“word came from the Spoonbills, and you not here to answer, and me not knowing where in hell or Derbyshire ye had got to. Ye’ve happened on a rare to-do at the Sleeping Deer. Her right honourable Grace, the Duchess of Queensberry, has come here to lie the night, before journeying down into the West country. She has been at Chatsworth, but the gentles is all a-fleeing south now, for fear of the wild Highlandmen. Duke William himself escorted her here, and that pretty lad, his eldest son, the Lord Hartington, and dinner is ordered for three, and my wife’s like to fire the roof with perplexity. Ye’ll be for your old room, doubtless. It’s been kept tidy against your return, and I’ll see that a bite of dinner is sent up to ye, when Her Grace is served.”

===Chinese===
他们绕过阿拉斯泰尔曾与仲冬同立的那座小山，上了大路，来到睡鹿客栈门前。如今再不必走什么后楼梯了；阿拉斯泰尔把马交给马夫，昂然走进大厅，径直奔店主的小内间去。可一只雅致的旅行皮箱攫住了他的目光——箱皮上饰着一枚戴冠之心的纹章；炉火边，一个穿红蓝号衣的男仆正在烘手取暖。从马厩院子敞开的门口一望，红蓝号衣更不止这一处，还有一辆漂亮马车，三个马童正在刷洗。店主不在自己房里，而是在厨房里坐镇，督着人切火腿、褪小母鸡的毛、往一大罐一大罐的麦酒里下香料。一见阿拉斯泰尔，他猛地一惊，唤另一个人来替他盯着案桌，便招手引他到门外。

「再见着您，我真打心眼里欢喜，我还怕您遭了毒手呢。您那位苏格兰仆人上这儿寻过您不止一回。还有——」他压低了嗓音，「篦鹭会那边也递过话来，偏您不在，没人回话，我也闹不清您究竟上了天还是入了地，还是就在德比郡的哪个犄角旮旯。您可赶上睡鹿客栈一场少有的热闹了。尊贵无比的昆斯伯里公爵夫人殿下驾到，要在此歇宿一夜，再启程往西边去。她先前在查茨沃斯做客，如今绅士贵人们都正往南逃呢，怕那些蛮野的高地人。是威廉公爵亲自送她来的，还有那位俊俏的少爷、他的长子哈廷顿勋爵（Lord Hartington）；晚饭定下三人份，我家婆娘急得险些把房顶都点着了。您多半还是要住老房间吧。那屋子一直收拾得干干净净，专候您回来；等侍候完公爵夫人，我管保给您送一口晚饭上去。」

===Original===
The Spainneach had disappeared, so Alastair mounted to his attic and set about the long process of his toilet. His cramped fingers made a slow business of shaving, but at last his chin and cheeks were smooth, and the mirror showed a face he recognised, albeit a face hollow in the cheeks and dark about the eyes. As his dressing proceeded his self-respect stole back; the fresh-starched shirt, the well-ironed cravat, were an assurance that he had returned from savagery. By the time he had finished he felt his bodily health improved, and knew the rudiments of an appetite. The meal and the glass of brandy which the landlord brought him assisted his transformation, and he seemed to breathe again without a burden on his chest. He had bidden the landlord look out for the Spainneach, and meantime he had an errand to do on his own account; for it occurred to him that the arrival of the Duchess Kitty was the solution of one perplexity.

He walked through the store-closet to the landing above the staircase. At the half-opened door of the Brown Room stood a footman in the Queensberry colours, one who had been with his mistress at Cornbury and recognised Alastair. He bowed and let him pass; indeed he would have pushed the door wide for him had not the young man halted on the threshold. There were voices inside the room, and one of them had a familiar sound.

The sight which greeted his eyes made him shut the door firmly behind him. Duchess Kitty, still wearing the cloak of grey fur and the velvet mittens which had kept her warm in the coach, sat in the chair which Claudia had once sat in, one little foot on the hearthstone, the other tapping impatiently on the hearthrug. On a table lay the remains of a meal, and beside it, balancing himself with one large hand among the platters, stood Mr. Samuel Johnson. It was not the Mr. Johnson to whom he had bade farewell three weeks ago, but rather the distraught usher who had made the midnight raid on Cornbury. His dress was the extreme of shabbiness, his hair was in disorder, his rusty small clothes and coarse stockings were splashed with mud; and he seemed to be famished, too, for his cheeks were hollow, and for all his distress, he could not keep his eyes from straying towards the table.

===Chinese===
西班牙人已经不知去向，阿拉斯泰尔便上到自己的阁楼，着手梳洗这桩漫长的大工程。僵硬的手指把刮脸的事办得拖沓，但他的下巴和两颊终于光洁了，镜中显出一张他认得的脸——虽说两颊凹陷，眼圈发黑。随着衣冠齐整，他的自尊悄悄溜了回来；浆得挺括的衬衫、熨得平整的领巾，都向他担保：他已经从蛮荒之地归来了。收拾停当时，他觉得身子松快了些，也觉出了几分胃口的苗头。店主送来的饭食和那杯白兰地促成了他的蜕变，他仿佛又能畅快呼吸，胸口不再压着一块重物。他已嘱托店主留心西班牙人的动静，而此刻他自己还有一桩私事要办；因为他想到，基蒂公爵夫人的到来，恰好解开了他的一桩难题。

他穿过储物壁橱，走到楼梯上方的平台。棕室的门半开着，门口立着一个穿昆斯伯里号衣的男仆——此人当年随女主人去过科恩伯里，认得阿拉斯泰尔。他躬身放行；若不是年轻人在门槛上顿住了脚，他真要替他把门整个推开。屋里有说话的声音，其中一个听来耳熟。

眼前所见，让他随手把门紧紧掩上。基蒂公爵夫人还裹着那件灰皮斗篷、戴着车上御寒的天鹅绒连指手套，坐在克劳迪娅曾坐过的那把椅子里，一只小小的脚搁在炉石上，另一只在炉前地毯上不耐烦地点着。一张桌上摆着吃剩的饭菜，桌边，一只大手撑在盘盏之间稳住身形的，正是塞缪尔·约翰逊先生。这已不是三个星期前道别时的那位约翰逊先生，倒像是那个夜闯科恩伯里、神魂失措的西席先生。他衣衫褴褛到了极点，头发蓬乱，锈渍斑斑的及膝短裤和粗袜子上溅满泥浆；他似乎还饿极了——两颊凹陷，而纵有满腹愁苦，那双眼睛仍不由自主地往桌上溜。

===Original===
“I beseech your Grace to remember your common womanhood,” he was saying when Alastair’s entrance diverted the Duchess’s attention.

She recognised him, and a look which was almost alarm crossed her face.

“Here enters the first of the conquerors,” she cried, and swept him a curtsey. “What is the latest news from the seat of war? My woman tells me that the Prince is already in Bedfordshire and that London is ablaze and King George fled to Holland. Your news, Captain Maclean?”

“I have none, madam. I have been no nearer the Prince’s camp than I am at this moment.”

Her eyes opened wide. “Faith, you have dallied long in the South. Have you been sick, or is Beaufort’s conscience a tender plant? Or did you return to Cornbury?” Her face had grown stern.

===Chinese===
「我恳求夫人顾念天下女人共有的那份天性，」他正说到这里，阿拉斯泰尔一进来，公爵夫人的注意力便被引开了。

她认出了他，脸上掠过一丝近乎惊惶的神色。

「征服者的头一位进城来啦，」她扬声道，朝他深深一福。「战场那边最新的军报如何？我的女仆告诉我，王子已经到了贝德福德郡（Bedfordshire），伦敦一片烈焰，乔治王逃去了荷兰。您的消息呢，麦克莱恩上尉？」

「我没有什么消息，夫人。此刻我离王子殿下营地的远近，便是我到过的地方里离它最近的。」

她双眼睁大了。「老天，您在南方可耽搁得够久的。您是害了病，还是博福特公爵的良知太娇嫩、经不得风霜？还是说，您又回科恩伯里去了？」她的脸色严峻起来。

===Original===
“I left Cornbury on the day you remember, and I have not since seen my lord, your brother.”

“That is well,” she said, with an air of relief. “I ask no further questions lest they embarrass you. But you are come opportunely, for you can give me counsel. This gentleman,” and she turned to Johnson, “has forced his company upon me, and, when you arrived, had embarked upon a monstrous tale. He bespeaks my pity, so I have composed myself to listen.”

“The gentleman and I are acquainted, and I can vouch for his honesty. Nay, madam, I have a fancy that his errand is also mine.”

She looked curiously from one to the other, as Johnson, rolling his head like a marionette, seized Alastair’s hand. “It is the mercy of God, sir, that you have returned,” the tutor cried. “I have missed you sorely, for that house of Brightwell is no better than a prison. Its master is aged and bedridden and demented, and it is governed by two malevolent spinsters. Brightwell! Bridewell is its true name. I myself have eaten little and slept bare, but that matters nothing. It is my poor lady I grieve for. ’Tis true, she has her husband, but he is little at home, and is much engrossed with affairs. Soon, too, he will ride south with his Prince, and Miss Claudia cannot travel with him nor can she be left behind in that ill-omened den. She must have a woman to befriend her in these rough days, and conduct her to Chastlecote or Weston, but she has few female friends of her rank and I knew not where to turn. But today, walking on the high road, I saw an equipage and learned that it was Her Grace travelling south, and that she would lie at this inn. So I ran hither like a Covent-garden porter, and have been admitted to her presence, though my appearance is not so polite as I could have desired.” He bowed to the Duchess, and in his clumsiness swept her travelling-mask from the table to the floor.

She looked at him for a little without speaking, and then fixed her eyes on Alastair, those large childlike eyes which were rarely without a spark of impish humour.

===Chinese===
「我在您记得的那一天离开了科恩伯里，此后再没有见过大人，您的胞弟。」

「那就好，」她说，神色宽慰。「旁的我一概不问，免得叫您为难。可您来得正巧，正可以替我出个主意。这位先生，」她转向约翰逊，「硬是把他的陪伴塞给了我；您进来的时候，他刚开头讲一个荒诞透顶的故事。他求的是我的怜悯，我便定下心来听他讲。」

「这位先生与我相识，他的诚实我可以作保。不，夫人，我简直疑心，他的使命也正是我的使命。」

她好奇地望望这个，又望望那个；约翰逊像木偶似的晃着脑袋，一把攥住阿拉斯泰尔的手。「先生，您回来了，这是上帝的慈悲，」这位西席先生嚷道。「我想您想得好苦，因为布莱特韦尔那座宅子，简直不比监狱强。宅子的老主人年迈卧床、神志昏聩，家业全握在两个歹毒的老处女手里。布莱特韦尔！它的真名该叫布莱德韦尔（Bridewell，伦敦的著名监狱）才是。我自己吃没吃好、睡没睡稳，这都不打紧。我心疼的是我那可怜的夫人。诚然，她有丈夫，可他难得归家，又一门心思扑在大事上。不多几时，他还要随王子殿下策马南下，克劳迪娅小姐既不能随行，又断断不能撇在那个不祥的巢窟里。这艰难时世，她身边须得有一位女眷扶持，护送她去查斯特科特或韦斯顿；可与她身份相当的闺中知己寥寥无几，我简直不知该往何处求告。谁知今日我在大路上行走，望见一列车驾，打听之下，才知是公爵夫人南行，要在此客栈歇宿。我便一路奔来，活像个考文特花园（Covent Garden）的脚夫，蒙夫人召见——只是我这副仪表，实在不如自己所愿的那般体面。」他向公爵夫人鞠了一躬，慌乱间手一挥，把她的出行面罩从桌上扫落在地。

她默然打量了他片刻，随后把目光定在阿拉斯泰尔身上——那双大大的、孩子气十足的眼睛，难得不带一点顽皮笑意的火花。

===Original===
“Your friend,” she said, “has already opened his tale to me, but his manner of telling it is not of the clearest. Since you say that his errand may be yours, I pray you expound it. But be seated, gentlemen both. I have already a crick in my neck from looking up to such enormities.”

Mr. Johnson, as if glad of the permission, dropped into a chair, but Alastair remained standing. His legs no longer felt crazy, but they were amazingly stiff, and once in a chair he distrusted his ability to rise. He stood at the opposite side of the hearth to the Duchess, looking down on the elfin figure, as pretty as porcelain in the glow of firelight.

“I do not ask your politics,” he said, “which I take to be your husband’s. But you are an honourable lady, by the consent of all, and, I can add of my own knowledge, a kind one. To you a traitor must be doubly repulsive.”

Her answer was what Claudia Norreys’s had been in that very room.

“You judge rightly, sir. If I thought I could betray a friend or a cause I should hang myself forthwith to avert the calamity.”

===Chinese===
「您这位朋友，」她说，「已经向我开了话头，只是他讲的方式实在算不得清楚。您既说他的使命多半也是您的，就请您来讲个分明。二位先生都请坐吧。仰着脖颈看你们这样的庞然大物，我的脖子已经酸了。」

约翰逊先生得了这话，仿佛如释重负，一屁股坐进椅中；阿拉斯泰尔却仍站着。他的双腿已不再发虚，却僵得厉害，他生怕一旦坐进椅里，便再没有气力站起。他站在炉台的另一侧、与公爵夫人相对，低头望着那精灵般的小小身影——在炉光的红晕里，她秀美得像一件瓷器。

「我不问您的政见，」他说，「那想必是尊夫的政见。但您是一位光明磊落的夫人，这是众口一词的；我还可凭亲身见闻补上一句：您是一位仁厚的夫人。在您眼里，叛徒必是加倍可憎的东西。」

她的回答，正是克劳迪娅·诺里斯当初在这同一间屋里的回答。

「您看得准，先生。我若想到自己竟能出卖一位朋友、一桩大业，我会立刻自缢，好躲开那场灾殃。」

===Original===
Alastair bowed. “Mr. Johnson has told you of this girl, my lady Norreys. She is own sister to you, tender and brave and infinitely faithful. Her husband is otherwise. Her husband is a black traitor, but she does not know it.”

Mr. Johnson cried out. “I had thought better of him, sir. Have you got new evidence?”

“I have full evidence. News of desperate import is sent to him here by another in the South, that other being one of the foremost agents of our Cause. That news should go forthwith to the Prince’s camp. It goes forthwith to the enemy’s.”

“For what reward?” the Duchess asked.

“For that reward which is usual to traitors in times of civil strife. They induce honest but weak-kneed souls to take a bold step, and then betray them to the Government, receiving a share of the fines and penalties that ensue. Great fortunes have been built that way.”

“But if the rebellion wins?”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔欠身一礼。「约翰逊先生已向您讲过这位女子，诺里斯夫人。她与您情同一母同胞的姐妹，温柔、勇敢、无限忠贞。她的丈夫却是另一路人。她的丈夫是个黑心叛徒，而她自己毫不知情。」

约翰逊先生失声叫起来。「先生，我原把他想得好些。您得了新的证据么？」

「我已有确凿证据。南方另有一人，把干系重大的消息递送到这里给他，而那人正是我们大业数一数二的密使。那消息本该即刻送往王子殿下的营地，却即刻送到了敌人手里。」

「图什么酬报？」公爵夫人问。

「图的是内乱时节叛徒们惯图的那份酬报。他们诱使心地诚实却膝盖发软的人迈出大胆一步，随即向政府告发，从随之而来的罚金与抄没里分一杯羹。多少泼天的家私，便是这样积起来的。」

「可若是举事的一方赢了呢？」

===Original===
“Then they are lost, unless indeed they are skilful enough to make provision with both sides and to bury whichever of the two villainies is unprofitable.”

“He is a young man,” she said. “He shows a shocking precocity in guile. And the poor child his wife dreams nothing of this?”
