“Ah, madam,” cried Johnson. “She is the very soul and flower of loyalty. If she suspected but a tithe of it, her heart would break.”

“His precocity is remarkable,” said Alastair, “but he is not the principal in the business. The principal is that other I have mentioned who is in the very centre of the Prince’s counsels.”

She put her hands to her ears. “Do not tell me,” she cried. “I will be burdened with no secrets that do not concern me. I take it that this other has not a wife whom you would have me befriend.”

“Nevertheless I fear that I must outrage your ears, madam. This other is known to you﻿—closely allied with you.”

===Chinese===
「那他们便完了——除非他们当真手眼通天，两头都下了注，再把无利可图的那一桩罪孽埋个干净。」

「他还是个年轻人，」她说。「他在奸诈上头竟早熟得骇人。他那可怜的孩子一般的妻子，对这一切竟一无所觉，还做着好梦？」

「唉，夫人，」约翰逊嚷道。「她正是忠贞的灵魂与菁华。哪怕只疑到其中的十分之一，她的心也要碎的。」

「他的早熟确乎惊人，」阿拉斯泰尔说，「可他还不是这桩买卖的正主。正主是我方才提到的那一位，他就坐在王子殿下枢机的正当中。」

她把双手掩上耳朵。「别告诉我，」她叫道。「与我不相干的秘密，我一个也不背。我且料定，这另一位总没有一位要我照拂的妻子吧。」

「只怕我仍须冒犯夫人的耳朵。这另一位，您是熟识的——与您过从甚密。」

===Original===
Her eyes were suddenly bright with anxiety.

“His name is Mr. Nicholas Kyd.”

Her face showed relief; also incredulity.

“You are certain? You have proof?”

“I have long been certain. Before night I will have full proof.”

She fell into a muse. “Kyd﻿—the bluff honest *bon enfant*! The man of the sad old songs and ready pathos, who almost makes a Jacobite of me﻿—Kyd to play the rogue! Faith, His Grace had better look into his accounts. What do you want of me, Captain Maclean?”

===Chinese===
她的眼睛蓦地亮了，透着焦灼。

「他的名字，是尼古拉斯·基德先生。」

她脸上现出释然，也现出难以置信。

「您拿得准么？您有证据？」

「我早已拿准。入夜之前，我便会握有全部证据。」

她沉思起来。「基德——那个粗豪忠厚的老好人（bon enfant）！那个唱着凄凉老歌、动辄便要催人泪下、差一点把我的心都唱成雅各布党人的汉子——基德竟做奸贼！老天，公爵大人真该查一查他的账目了。您要我做什么，麦克莱恩上尉？」

===Original===
“Two things, madam. My purpose is to do justice on rogues, but justice is a cruel thing, and I would spare the lady. I want you to carry her southward with you, and leave her at Chastlecote or Weston, which you please, or carry her to Amesbury. She shall never know her husband’s infamy﻿—only that he has gone to the Prince, and when he does not return will think him honourably dead.”

The Duchess nodded. “And the other?”

“I beg your presence when Mr. Kyd is confounded. He is on his way to Brightwell and this night will sleep there. His errand in the West is now done, and tomorrow, as I read it, he descends into Nottinghamshire to the Government headquarters to receive his reward. Therefore he will have papers with him, and in those papers I look for my proof. If they fail, I have other sources.”

“And if he is found guilty, what punishment?”

Alastair shrugged his shoulders. “That is not for me. Both he and Norreys go bound to the Prince.”

She brooded with her chin on her hand. Then she stood up, laughing.

===Chinese===
「两件事，夫人。我的本分是惩办奸恶，可公正是件酷烈的事，我想让那位夫人免受其苦。我要您带她一同南下，把她留在查斯特科特，或韦斯顿——随您心意——或者索性带去埃姆斯伯里。她永远不必知道丈夫的丑行——只须知道他投王子殿下去了；待他不归，她只当他已光荣捐躯。」

公爵夫人点了点头。「另一件呢？」

「我恳请夫人到场，亲眼看着基德先生被戳穿。他此刻正在赴布莱特韦尔的路上，今夜便宿在那里。他在西部的事已经办完，依我推断，明日他便要下诺丁汉郡，去政府军大营领他的酬赏。所以他身上必带着文件，而我的证据，正要向那些文件里去找。若那里找不到，我另有门路。」

「若他罪名坐实，又如何处置？」

阿拉斯泰尔耸了耸肩。「那不归我裁夺。他和诺里斯两个，都要捆了押到王子殿下面前。」

她以手支颐，默默想了一阵。随后她站起身，笑了起来。

===Original===
“I consent. ’Twill be better than a play. But how will you set the stage?”

“I go to Brightwell presently, and shall force admission. My lady Norreys will keep her chamber, while in another part of the house we deal with grimmer business. I nominate you of our court of justice. See, we will fix an hour. Order your coach for six, and you will be at Brightwell by seven. By that time the house will be ours, and we shall be waiting to receive you. You will bring Mr. Johnson with you, and after that you can comfort the lady.”

She nodded. “I will come masked,” said she, “and I do swear that I will not fail you or betray you﻿—by the graves of Durrisdeer I swear it, the ancient Douglas oath. Have you men enough? I can lend you two stout fellows.”

“Your Grace has forgotten that you are a Whig,” said Alastair, laughing.

“I have forgotten all save that I am trysted to a merry evening,” she cried.

===Chinese===
「我依了。这可比看一出戏还热闹。只是这台戏，你打算怎么摆布？」

「我即刻动身去布莱特韦尔，强行进门。诺里斯夫人自会待在她房里，宅子另一头，我们另有一桩更森然的正事要办。我提名您为我们公审法庭的一员。来，我们定个时辰。吩咐下去六点套车，七点您便可抵达布莱特韦尔。到那时宅子已在我们的掌握之中，我们专候夫人大驾。您把约翰逊先生一并带来；事毕之后，便请夫人去宽慰那位小夫人。」

她点了点头。「我会蒙面而来，」她说，「我郑重起誓，决不误您的事，也决不出卖您——我以杜里斯迪尔（Durrisdeer）的坟茔起誓，这是道格拉斯（Douglas）家古老的誓言。您的人手够么？我可借您两个壮实的汉子。」

「夫人倒忘了，自己原是辉格党，」阿拉斯泰尔笑道。

「我什么都忘了，只记得自己约下了一个快活的夜晚，」她嚷道。

---
===Original===
When Alastair returned to his attic he found the Spainneach.

“Your Kyd is nearing port,” he said. “I have word that he slept at Blakeley and dined early at Little Laning. In two hours or less he will be at Brightwell.”

“And the Spoonbills?”

“Await us there. Haste you, Sir Sandy, if you would arrive before your guest.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔回到阁楼，见西班牙人已在屋里。

「您那位基德快进港了，」他说。「我得了信儿，他在布莱克利（Blakeley）过的夜，一早又在小莱宁（Little Laning）用了饭。不出两个钟头，他就到布莱特韦尔了。」

「篦鹭会呢？」

「都在那儿候着我们。快走吧，桑迪爵士，您总不想让您的客人抢了先。」

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===

1. See Boswell’s *Life of Johnson*, anno 1754. ↩︎

===Chinese===

1. 参见鲍斯韦尔（Boswell）《约翰逊传》，1754 年。↩︎

## VII How a Man May Hunt with the Hounds and Yet Run with the Hare / 七、何以既随猎犬出猎，又与野兔同奔

===Original===
The butler Giles conducted him through long corridors to the door which separated the manor proper from its ancient Edwardian tower, and then up stone stairways to a room under the roof which had once been the sleeping apartment of the lord of the castle. The walls were two yards thick, the windows mere slits for arrows, the oaken floor as wavy as a ploughland. He had refused supper and asked only peace to collect his wits. Giles set a candle down on an oak table, and nodded to a cavernous canopied bed. “There’s blankets enow to keep you warm, since the night be mild for the time o’ year. Good sleep to ye and easy dreams.” The key turned in the lock, and the shuffle of heelless shoes died on the stair.

Alastair flung himself on the bed, and lay staring at the roof of the canopy, fitfully illumined by the dancing candle. A light wind must have crept into the room from some cranny of the windows, for the flame flickered and queer shadows chased each other over the dark walls. He was in a torment of disquietude since hearing the warrant﻿—not for his own safety, for he did not despair of giving these chaw-bacons the slip, but for the prospects of the Cause. There was black treason somewhere in its innermost councils. The man who had betrayed every danger-point in his own career could do the same thing for others. The rogue﻿—Kyd’s servant or whoever he might be﻿—was in the way of knowing the heart of every secret. Kyd, charged with a most vital service on which the future of England hung, had this Judas always at his elbow to frustrate or falsify any message to the North, to play the devil with the Prince’s recruiting, and at the end to sell his master’s head for gold. The thought made the young man dig his nails into his palms. God’s pity that in an affair so gossamer-fine there should be this rude treachery to rend the web.﻿ ﻿… But if the miscreant was Kyd’s servant, how came he in this neighbourhood? Had he been dismissed Kyd’s service? Or was Kyd himself at hand and the journey into Wiltshire relinquished? His mind was in utter confusion.

Nevertheless the discovery had quickened his spirit, which of late he thought had been growing languid. He was a campaigner, and made his plans quick. His immediate duty was to escape, his next to reach the Prince and concert measures to meet the case of West England. Fortunate for him that the letter of Brother Gilly had fallen into his hand, for now he knew the magnitude of the business. But first he must sleep, for all evening he had been nodding. He had the soldier’s trick of snatching odd hours of slumber, so, drawing a blanket round him and resolutely shutting off all thoughts, he was soon unconscious.

===Chinese===
管家贾尔斯领着他穿过一道道长廊，来到一扇门前——这扇门将宅邸的正身与古旧的爱德华朝塔楼分隔开来——随后又拾级登上石梯，直抵屋顶下的一个房间；那里曾是城堡领主的寝居。墙壁厚达两码，窗子不过是些射箭用的窄缝，栎木地板起伏不平，宛如犁过的田垄。他谢绝了晚饭，只求一方清静，好收拢自己的神思。贾尔斯把一支蜡烛搁在栎木桌上，朝那张幔帐深垂、宛如洞窟的大床点了点头。「毯子尽够您取暖的，这样的时节，夜里还算温和。祝您睡个好觉，做个安稳梦。」钥匙在锁孔里一转，那双无跟便鞋趿趿的声息便在楼梯上渐渐消逝。

阿拉斯泰尔一头倒在床上，仰面凝望着床顶的幔盖，烛光跳荡，把它映得忽明忽暗。必有微风从窗隙潜入室内，因为烛焰摇曳，怪影在幽暗的墙上追逐不休。自打听见那道拘票，他便深陷于焦灼的煎熬——不是为了自身安危，因为要甩开这班乡巴佬，他并不灰心；他忧的是大业的前途。大业最核心的谋议之中，某处正潜藏着漆黑的背叛。这个把自己一路行程中每一处险地都出卖了的人，也能照样出卖旁人。那恶棍——基德的仆人也罢，不管他究竟是什么人——正处在一个洞悉每一桩机密内情的地位上。基德身负重任，英格兰的前途悬于其上，臂弯边却时刻带着这个犹大，足以阻挠或篡改一切发往北方的信讯，搅坏王子的募兵，到头来再把主人的首级卖作金钱。这念头教这年轻人把指甲掐进了掌心。天可怜见，如此蛛丝般纤微精妙的一桩大事，竟有这等粗暴的背叛来撕破罗网。……可那恶徒若真是基德的仆人，他怎会到这一带来？莫非已叫基德辞了差？还是基德本人就在左近，威尔特郡之行已然作罢？他心里乱作了一团。

然而这一发现却令他精神一振——近来他只觉自己的心气日渐怠惰。他是惯于征战的人，计议立定。眼下的本分是脱身，其次是赶上王子，共筹方略，以应西英格兰之局。幸好吉利兄弟那封信落到了他手里，如今他总算明白了此事的分量。但首先得睡一觉，一晚上他都在打盹。他有军人的本事，能抓紧零星时辰补眠，于是裹上一条毯子，断然屏去一切念头，不多时便沉沉睡去。

===Original===
He slept lightly, and woke to see the candle, which he had left burning, guttering over the edge of the iron candlestick. A swift shadow ran across the wall before him, and a sudden waft of air caused the candle-end to flare like a torch. He glanced at the door, and it seemed to move. Then the place was quiet again, but it was brighter, for a new light had come into it. He scrambled from the bed to see the glow of a shaded lantern, and a slim cloaked figure slipping the key from the door.

The lantern was set beside the candle on the table. The figure wore a furred bed-gown and a nightcap of lace and pink satin, and its brown eyes in the shadow were bright as a squirrel’s and very merry.

“La, la, such a commotion ere I could come to you, sir,” she said. “Giles must carry Nunkie to bed and hoist Squire Bretherton and Sir Ambrose on their horses, and get a message from me to Black Ben, and pass a word to Stable Bill about Moonbeam. You have slept, wise man that you are? But it is time to be about your business of escaping, for in three hours it will be daylight.”

She was like a pixie in the half darkness, a tall pixie, that had a delicious small stammer in its speech. Alastair was on his feet now, bowing awkwardly.

===Chinese===
他睡得轻浅，醒来时看见那支留着没吹熄的蜡烛，正在铁烛台的边口上熬泪将尽。一道迅影掠过他面前的墙壁，一股急风教烛头倏地窜起，亮如火炬。他朝门上瞥了一眼，门似乎动了一动。随后屋内复归寂静，却比先前更亮，因为有一线新的光透了进来。他从床上一跃而起，看见一盏罩纱提灯的光晕，还有一条披斗篷的纤细身影，正把钥匙从门上悄悄退下。

提灯放到了桌上，在蜡烛旁边。那身影穿一件毛皮晨袍，戴一顶花边粉缎的睡帽，阴影里一双褐色的眼睛，亮得像松鼠的眼睛，还笑意盈盈。

「哎呀呀，我脱身来见您之前，竟有这样一番折腾呢，先生，」她说。「贾尔斯得把好叔叔抱上床，把布雷瑟顿乡绅（Squire Bretherton）和安布罗斯爵士（Sir Ambrose）架上马背，还得替我捎话给黑本杰明，再跟马夫比尔（Stable Bill）交代月华的事。您睡过了，真不愧是个明白人？可该动手办您逃走的正事了，再过三个钟头，天就要亮了。」

半明半暗里，她活像个小精灵——一个高挑的小精灵，说起话来带着一点小小的口吃，教人心醉。阿拉斯泰尔此刻已站起身来，行礼行得笨手笨脚。

===Original===
“Tell me,” she whispered. “The warrant is true? You are Alastair Maclean, a captain in Lee’s Regiment of France, and a messenger from the Prince in Scotland. Oh, have no fear of me, for I am soul and body for the Cause.”

“The warrant spoke truly,” he said.

“And you will join the Prince at the first possible moment? How go things in the North? Have you any news, sir?”

“The Prince crossed the Border yesterday. He marches to Lancashire.”

===Chinese===
「告诉我，」她低声说。「那拘票是真的？您是阿拉斯泰尔·麦克莱恩，法兰西李氏团（Lee’s Regiment）的上尉，苏格兰的王子派来的信使。啊，别怕我，我可是连魂带身都献给大业的人。」

「拘票所言不虚。」他说。

「您要立刻去与王子会合，一刻也不耽搁？北边形势如何？先生，您可带来了什么消息？」

「王子昨天已经跨过边境，正向兰开夏进军。」

===Original===
She twined her fingers in excitement. “You dare not delay an hour. And you shall not. I have made everything ready. Sir, you will find I have made everything ready. See, you shall follow me downstairs and Giles will be waiting. The lock of your door fits badly, for the wood around is worm-eaten. Tomorrow it will be lying on the floor, to show my uncle how you escaped. Giles will take you by a private way to the Yew Avenue, and there Bill from the stables will await you with Moonbeam saddled and ready﻿—my uncle’s favourite, no less. You will ride down the avenue very carefully, keeping on the grass and making no sound, till you reach the white gate which leads to Wakehurst Common. There Ben will meet you and guide you out of this county so that by the evening you may be in Cheshire.”

“Ben the Gypsy?” he asked.

“The same. Do you know him? He is on our side and does many an errand for me.”

“But, madam, what of yourself? What will your uncle say when he finds his horse gone?”

===Chinese===
她激动地绞着手指。「您一个钟头也耽搁不起。也决不容您耽搁。我已把一切都备妥了。先生，您会看到我把一切都备妥了。喏，您随我下楼，贾尔斯自会候着。您房门那把锁本来就锁不严，四周的木头都叫虫蛀空了。明天它会躺在地板上，好教我舅舅明白您是怎么脱身的。贾尔斯会领您走一条僻静小路，到紫杉大道（Yew Avenue），马房的比尔会在那儿候着您，月华已上了鞍、备齐了——那可是我舅舅的心头爱马。您顺着大道下行时须万分小心，只走草地，不出一点声响，直到抵达那扇通往韦克赫斯特荒原（Wakehurst Common）的白门。本会在那里接您，领您出这个郡，到傍晚您就能进柴郡了。」

「吉普赛本？」他问。

「正是他。您认得？他是我们一边的人，替我办过许多差事。」

「可是，夫人，您自己怎么办？您舅舅发觉爱马没了，会说什么呢？」

===Original===
“Stolen by the gypsies﻿—I have the story pat. There will be a pretty hue and cry, but Ben will know of its coming and take precautions. I am grieved to tell fibs, but needs must in the day of war.”

“But I leave you alone to face the consequences.”

“Oh, do not concern yourself for me. My dear uncle is indulgent and, though a Whig, is no bigot. He will not grieve for your absence at breakfast tomorrow. But I fear the loss of Moonbeam will put him terribly out, and I should be obliged if you could find some way of restoring his horse when his purpose is served. As for myself, I propose leaving this hospitable house no later than tomorrow and journeying north into Derbyshire. I will take Mr. Johnson with me as company and protector, and I have also my servants from Weston.”

She spoke with the air of a commander-in-chief, an air so mature and mistressly that it betrayed her utter youth.

===Chinese===
「叫吉普赛人偷的——这套说辞我早已编得滚瓜烂熟。到时候少不了一场热闹的追查喧腾，可本会先得到风声，早作提防。我本不忍心撒谎，无奈战时年月，不得不如此。」

「可我把后果留给您一人承担了。」

「啊，您不必替我操心。我亲爱的舅舅是个宽厚人，虽是辉格党，倒不偏执。明天早饭桌上见不着您，他不会伤心的。只是月华一失，我怕他要大大着恼；等它的差事办完，您若能想个法子把这匹马还给他，我就感激不尽了。至于我自己，我打算最迟明日便离开这座好客的宅子，北上德比郡去。我带约翰逊先生同行，做个伴儿，也做个保护人，另外还有我从韦斯顿带来的仆人。」

她说这话时自有一副统帅的气派，那气派如此老成、如此当家主妇般地干练，反倒泄露了她全然的年少。

===Original===
“I am most deeply beholden to you, my lady,” said Alastair. “You know something of me, and I will beg in return some news of my benefactress. You are my lady Norreys?”

The matronly airs fled and she was a shy child again.

“I am m-my lady,” she stammered, “this week back. How did you know, sir? The faithful Puffin? My dear Sir John has gone north to join his Prince, by whose side you will doubtless meet him. Tell him I too have done my humble mite of service to the Cause, and that I am well, and happy in all things but his absence.﻿ ﻿… See, I have written him a little letter which will serve equally to present you to him and to assure him of my love. He is one of you﻿—one of the trusted inner circle, I mean.” She lowered her voice. “He bears the name of ‘Achilles.’ ”

The hazel eyes had ceased to sparkle and become modest and dim.

===Chinese===
「我受夫人的恩惠，实在没齿难忘，」阿拉斯泰尔说。「您既知道我的一些来历，我也冒昧请教我的恩人。您就是诺里斯夫人（Lady Norreys）吧？」

那副主妇般的架子霎时飞散，她又变回一个羞怯的孩子。

「我、我是夫人，」她结结巴巴地说，「才做了一个礼拜呢。先生，您怎么知道的？莫非是忠心的老风箱？我亲爱的约翰爵士已经北上投王子去了，您日后无疑会在王子身边遇上他。请告诉他，我也为大业尽了自己微末的一份力；还请告诉他，我一切都好，事事都快活，只除了一样——他不在。……喏，我给他写了一封短信，既可把您引荐给他，也可向他担保我的爱。他是你们一伙的——我是说，是深受信任的内圈中人。」她放低了声音。「他的代号是『阿喀琉斯』。」

那双榛色的眼睛已不再闪亮，变得温顺而朦胧了。

===Original===
“Tell me one thing, my lady, before I go. My mission to the South was profoundly secret, and not four men in the Prince’s army knew of it. Yet I find myself and my doings set forth in a justice’s warrant as if I had cried them in the streets. There is a traitor abroad and if he goes undetected he spells ruin to our Cause. Can you help me to unearth him?”

She wrinkled her brows and narrowed her startled eyes.

“I cannot guess. Save you and Sir John I have seen no professor of our faith. Stay, who was the mummer last night in the Justice Room?”

“Some common jackal of Hanover. No, the danger is not there. But, madam, you have a quick brain and a bold heart. If you can lay your finger on this fount of treason, you will do a noble work for our Prince. Have you the means to send a message to the North?”

===Chinese===
「夫人，临行前请告诉我一件事。我南下的使命是绝顶的机密，王子军中知情的不满四人。可我竟发现自己和自己的行止被写进了一纸治安官的拘票，仿佛我曾在街市上把它们高声叫卖过似的。眼下有一个奸细在外活动，若任他漏网，我们大业必毁在他手里。您能帮我把他揪出来么？」

她蹙起眉头，吃惊的眼睛眯细了。

「我猜不出。除了您和约翰爵士，我没见过第二个信奉我们这条道的人。慢着——昨夜在审理室里扮戏的那位是谁？」

「不过是汉诺威的一头寻常走狗。不，危险不在那里。可是，夫人，您有敏慧的头脑，有大胆的心。您若能按住这股叛卖的源头，便是为王子建下了一件大功。您可有法子往北方递信？」

===Original===
She nodded. “Assuredly﻿—by way of Sir John.﻿ ﻿… But you must start forthwith, sir. I will take your mails into Derbyshire in my charge, for you must ride fast and light. Now, follow me, and tread softly when I lift my hand.”

Down the long stone stairs the lantern fluttered, and at a corner the man who followed caught a glimpse of bare rosy ankles above the furred slippers. In the manor galleries, where oaken flooring creaked, a hand was now and then raised to advise caution. Once there came the slamming of a door, and the lantern-bearer froze into stillness behind an armoire, while Alastair, crouched beside her, felt the beating of her heart. But without mishap they reached the Great Hall, where the last red embers crackled fitfully and a cricket ticked on the hearthstone. Through a massive door they entered another corridor and the girl whistled long and soft. The answer was a crack of light from a side door, and Giles appeared, cloaked like a conspirator and carrying a pewter candlestick. Gone was the decorum of the butler who had set the stage in the Justice Room, and it was a nervous furtive old serving-man who received the girl’s instructions.

“Oh, my lady, I’m doing this for your mother’s sake, her as I used to make posies for when I was no more’n buttery lad. But my knees do knock together cruel, for what Squire would say if he knew makes my blood freeze to think on.”

“Now, don’t be a fool, Giles. I can manage your master, and you have nothing to do but lead this gentleman to the Yew Avenue, and then back to your bed with a clear conscience.”

===Chinese===
她点点头。「自然有——可以走约翰爵士的路子。……可您必须即刻动身，先生。您的行囊交我带走，替您保管到德比郡，因为您必须轻骑快走。来，跟着我，我一抬手，您就放轻脚步。」

提灯飘飘忽忽下了长长的石梯，在转角处，后面跟着的人瞥见毛皮拖鞋上方一抹裸露的粉红脚踝。在宅邸的画廊里，栎木地板咯吱作响，一只手不时举起，示意谨慎。有一回传来砰的关门声，掌灯人立时僵住，躲到一口大橱后面，阿拉斯泰尔蹲在她身旁，觉出了她心口的跳动。总算一路无恙，他们到了大厅，炉膛里最后一点红炭时断时续地噼啪作响，一只蟋蟀在炉石上嗒嗒地叫。穿过一扇厚重的门，他们进了另一条走廊，姑娘打了一声悠长而轻柔的呼哨。应答是一道边门里漏出的一线光，贾尔斯出现了，身披斗篷，活像个阴谋家，手里端着一只锡蜡台。在审理室里布设那场戏的管家的体统荡然无存，此刻听姑娘吩咐的，是个战战兢兢、鬼鬼祟祟的老仆人。

「唉，我的小姐，我这是看在您母亲的份上——当年我还不过是个酒窖小厮，常给她扎小花束呢。可我这双膝盖实在抖得厉害，一想到乡绅若知道了会说什么，我的血就凝住了。」

「好了，别犯傻，贾尔斯。你主人那边我自有办法。你只消把这位先生领到紫杉大道，然后回床上去，睡个心安理得。」

===Original===
She laid a hand on the young man’s arm﻿—the gesture with which a boy encourages a friend.

“Adieu, sir, and I pray God that He lead you swift and straight to your journey’s end. I will be in Derbyshire﻿—at Brightwell under the Peak, waiting to bid you welcome when you come south to the liberation of England.” He took her hand, kissed it, and, with a memory of wistful eyes and little curls that strayed from her cap’s lace and satin, he followed the butler through the kitchen postern into the gloom of the night.

A short and stealthy journey among shrubberies brought them to a deeper blackness which proved to be a grove of yews. Something scraped and rustled close ahead, and the hoarse whisper of Giles received a hoarse answer. The night was not so dark as to hide objects outside the shade of the trees, and on a patch of grass Alastair made out a horse with a man beside it. Bill the stableman put the bridle into his hand, after making certain by a word with Giles that he was the person awaited. Alastair found a guinea for each, and before their muttered thanks were done was in the saddle, moving, as he had been instructed, into the blackness of the great avenue.

The light mouth, the easy paces, the smooth ripple of muscle under his knees told him that he was mounted on no common horse, but his head was still too full of his late experience to be very observant about the present. The nut-brown girl, the melodious voice with a stammer like a break in a nightingale’s song, seemed too delicious and strange for reality. And yet she was flesh and blood; he had felt her body warm against his when they sheltered behind the armoire: it was her doing that he was now at liberty and posting northward. Now he understood Mr. Johnson’s devotion. To serve such a lady he would himself scale the blue air and plough the high hills, as the bards sang.

===Chinese===
她把一只手搭上年轻人的臂膀——那是男孩子给同伴壮胆的姿态。

「再会了，先生，我祈求上帝引领您，迅捷而笔直地抵达旅程的终点。我会在德比郡——在皮克山下的布莱特韦尔——等着您，等您南下解放英格兰的时候，迎您进门。」他握住她的手，吻了一下，怀着对那双脉脉含愁的眼睛、对从她睡帽的花边与缎面里逸出的几绺小鬈发的记忆，跟着管家穿过厨房后门，走进了沉沉的夜色。

在灌木丛间潜行短短一程，他们来到一片更深的黑暗跟前，原来是一座紫杉林。前方近处有什么东西刮擦作响、窸窸窣窣，贾尔斯嘶哑的低语得到一声同样嘶哑的回答。夜色还没有暗到遮蔽树荫之外的物件，在一块草地上，阿拉斯泰尔辨出一匹马和马旁的一个人。马夫比尔与贾尔斯对了一句口令，确证他就是所候之人，便把缰绳塞进他手里。阿拉斯泰尔摸出几尼，一人一枚，还没等他们低声道完谢，他已翻身入鞍，遵着嘱咐，驰进那条大道的黑暗之中。

轻灵的口衔、从容的步态、膝下平滑涌动的肌理，都告诉他胯下绝非凡马；只是他头脑里还满装着方才的经历，无暇细察眼前。那栗发褐眸的姑娘，那莺啼般悦耳、带一点口吃、宛如夜莺歌声中一处小小断折的嗓音，美妙而奇异得不像真有其事。然而她终究是血肉之躯；两人在大橱后避匿时，他感到过她温热的身子贴着自己的臂膀：如今他能脱然自由、驿马北去，正是她的功劳。这下他懂得约翰逊先生的赤诚了。为这样一位小姐效劳，他自己也愿攀上青冥、犁开峻岭，一如行吟歌人所唱。

===Original===
The bemusement took him down the avenue till the trees thinned out and on the right came the ghostly glimmer of a white gate. He turned and found it open, and by it another horseman.

“The gentleman from Miss Claudy﻿—beg y’r pardon﻿—from m’lady?” a voice asked.

“The same,” Alastair replied. The speech was that of the gypsy he had met the day before.

The man shut the gate with his whip. “Then follow me close and not a cheep o’ talk. We’ve some cunning and fast journeying to do before the day breaks.”

===Chinese===
这份恍惚伴着他走完大道，直到树木渐稀，右手边现出一扇白门幽幽的微光。他转过去，见门已敞开，门边另有一个骑马的人。

「这位是克劳迪小姐（Miss Claudy）——恕我失言——是夫人的先生吧？」一个声音问。

「正是。」阿拉斯泰尔答道。听那口音，正是他前一天遇上的那个吉普赛人。

那人用马鞭把门带上。「那就紧跟着我，一路别吱一声。天亮之前，咱们还须赶一段又刁又快的路。」

===Original===
They swept at a canter down a long lane, deeply rutted, and patched here and there with clumps of blackberries. Then they were on a heath, where the sky was lighter and the road had to be carefully picked round sandpits and quarry-holes. Alastair had no guess at direction, for the sky showed never a star, and though the dark was not impenetrable it was hopeless to look for landmarks. A strange madcap progress they made over every kind of country, now on road, now in woodland, now breasting slopes of heath with the bracken rubbing on the stirrups. Oftenest they were in forest land, where sometimes there was no path and Alastair found it best to give his horse its head and suffer it to do the steering. He had forgotten that England could be so wild, for these immense old boles and the miles of thicket and mere belonged surely to a primeval world. Again the course would be over fallow and new plough, and again in lanes and parish roads and now and then on the turnpike. The pace was easy﻿—a light canter, but there were no halts, and always ahead over hedge and through gap went the slim figure of the gypsy.

The air was chilly but not cold, and soon the grey cloth of darkness began to thin till it was a fine veil dimming but not hiding objects, and the light wind blew which even on the stillest night heralds the dawn. The earth began to awake, lights kindled in farms and cottages, lanterns flickered around steadings. Movement through this world just struggling out of sleep was a joy and an exhilaration. It reminded Alastair of a winter journey from Paris to Beauvais﻿—part of a Prince’s wager﻿—when with relays of horses he had ridden down the night, through woods and hamlets dumb with snow, intoxicated with his youth, and seeing mystery in every light that glimmered out of the dark. Now he was in the same mood. His spirits rose at the signs of awaking humanity. That lantern by a brook was a shepherd pulling hay for the tups now huddled in the sheepcote. The light at that window was the goodwife grilling bacon for the farmer’s breakfast, or Blowselinda of the Inn sweeping the parlour after the night’s drinking. And through that homely ritual of morn he was riding north to the Wars which should upturn thrones and make nobles of plain captains. Youth! Romance! And somewhere in the background of his brain a voice sounded like a trill of music. “Adieu, sir. I pray God﻿ ﻿… I go to B-Brightwell under the P-Peak﻿ ﻿…”

The light had grown and he had his first view of Black Ben, and Ben of him. They jostled at a gate and stared at each other.

“We meet again,” he said.

===Chinese===
两人策马小跑着掠下一条车辙深陷的长巷，路面坑洼里东一丛西一簇长着黑莓。随后他们上了荒原，那里天色亮些，道路须小心挑拣，绕开一个个沙坑和采石坑。阿拉斯泰尔全然猜不出方向，天上不见一颗星，黑暗虽非穿不透，要找地标却是休想。他们横穿各式各样的乡野，走出一程古怪的狂野行旅：忽而在大路上，忽而在林间，忽而迎着石楠丛生的坡地攀行，欧洲蕨擦着马镫沙沙作响。多半时候他们在林地里赶路，有时根本没有路径，阿拉斯泰尔发觉最好的法子是由着马的性子，让它自己掌舵。他已忘了英格兰竟能这般荒僻，这些苍老巨大的树身，这几英里连绵的灌木丛与湖沼，分明属于一个太古的世界。忽而路径又越过休耕地和新犁的田垄，忽而又穿行在小巷与教区土路之间，间或也上一上收费大道。脚程不算紧——一路轻快的跑步，只是从不停歇，前方那吉普赛人瘦削的身影，总是越篱穿隙，一路领先。

天气清寒而不严冷，不多时，黑暗的灰幕开始变薄，薄成一层轻纱，使物象朦胧而不至隐没；那阵微风也起来了——哪怕在最寂静的夜里，它也是破晓的先声。大地开始醒来，农庄与村舍里点起灯火，提灯在院落间闪烁不定。穿行于这个方从睡梦中挣出怀抱的世界，是一种欢悦，一种振奋。这教阿拉斯泰尔想起一回从巴黎到博韦（Beauvais）的冬日旅行——那是一场王子赌约中的一程——他换乘一站站的驿马，把黑夜一路赶落身后，穿过被雪封得哑然的树林与村落，为自己的青春所陶醉，把黑暗中每一星闪烁的灯火都看作一桩神秘。此刻他正是这般心绪。见到人烟初醒的迹象，他的兴致便随之高涨。溪边那盏提灯，是个牧人在抱草料，喂圈里挤作一团的公羊。那扇窗里的灯光，是主妇在为庄稼汉的早饭煎腊肉，或是客栈里的布洛瑟琳达（Blowselinda）在通宵畅饮之后打扫堂屋。穿过这一套家常的晨间仪轨，他正纵马向北，奔赴那将掀翻王座、教寻常上尉一跃而为贵族的战争。青春！罗曼史！在他脑海深处的什么地方，有个声音在回响，像一串颤动的乐音：「再会了，先生。愿上帝……我去皮、皮克山下的布、布莱特韦尔……」

天光已足，他头一回看清了黑本杰明的面目，黑本杰明也头一回看清了他。两人在一道门前凑近，彼此打量。

「我们又见面了。」他说。

===Original===
“Happy meeting, my dear good gentleman. But you were on a different errand yesterday when my duty drove me the way of hot ashes. No offence took along of a poor man’s honesty, kind sir?”

“None,” said Alastair. He saw now the reason for the gypsy’s presence with the recruits. He was in Jacobite pay, hired to scatter Oglethorpe’s levies and so reduce Wade’s command. But none the less he disliked the man﻿—his soft sneering voice, and the shifty eyes which he remembered from yesterday.

It was now almost broad day, about eight in the morning, and Alastair reckoned that they must have travelled twenty miles and be close on the Cheshire border. The country was featureless﻿—much woodland interspersed with broad pastures, and far to the east a lift of ground towards a range of hills. The weather was soft and clear, a fine scenting morning for the hunt, and far borne on the morning air came the sound of a horn.

The gypsy seemed to be at fault. He stopped and considered for a matter of five minutes with his ear cocked. Then he plunged into a copse and emerged in a rushy bottom between high woods. Here the sound of the horn was heard again, apparently from the slopes at the end of the bottom.

===Chinese===
「幸会啊，我亲爱的好先生。不过昨天您我的差使可不同，那时节我的职责逼着我走的，是滚烫灰烬上的路。小人凭着一副穷人的本分行事，您可别见怪，好心的先生？」

「不见怪。」阿拉斯泰尔说。他这才明白那吉普赛人昨天何以混在新兵中间。原来他受着雅各布党的雇金，专事搅散奥格尔索普征募的兵员，借以削弱韦德的麾下。尽管如此，他仍旧不喜欢这个人——那软绵绵带着讥诮的嗓音，还有那双他从昨天起就记得的、溜转不定的眼睛。

这时天已差不多大亮，约莫早晨八点，阿拉斯泰尔估量他们已走了二十英里，柴郡地界就在眼前。这一带乡野平衍无奇——大片林地间杂着开阔的草场，远在东面，地势朝一带山峦微微抬升。天气温软晴朗，正是猎狐凭嗅觉出猎的好晨光，晨风远远送来了号角之声。

那吉普赛人似乎一时没了主意。他停下马，侧着耳朵寻思了足足五分钟，随后一头扎进一片杂木林，从高大的树林之间一条灯芯草丛生的洼地里穿了出来。这里又听见号角声了，显是从洼地尽头的坡上传来。

===Original===
“The turnpike runs yonder at the back of the oak clump,” he said. “Best get to it by the brook there and the turf bridge. I must leave you, pretty gentleman. You take the left turn and hold on, and this night you will sleep in Warrington.”

They were jogging towards the brook when Alastair took a fancy to look back, and saw between the two woods a tiny landscape neatly framed in the trees. There was a church tower in it, and an oddly shaped clump of ashes. Surely it was familiar.

Across the brook the hunting horn sounded again, this time from beyond a spinney at the top of the slope.

“There lies your road, pretty sir,” and the gypsy pointed to the left of the spinney and wheeled his horse to depart.

===Chinese===
「收费大道就在那边栎树丛的后面，」他说。「最好打那边的小溪和草皮桥过去。我得同您分手了，俊俏的先生。您靠左边转，一路别停，今夜就睡在沃灵顿了。」

两人正朝小溪缓辔而行，阿拉斯泰尔忽然心血来潮回头一望，只见两片树林之间嵌着一幅小小的风景，被树木框得整整齐齐。景中有一座教堂钟楼，还有一丛形状古怪的白蜡树。他总觉得在哪儿见过。

溪流对岸又响起猎号，这一回是打坡顶那片小树林后面传来的。

「您的路在那儿，俊俏的先生，」吉普赛人朝小树林左边一指，拨转马头就要离去。

===Original===
But Alastair was looking back again. The higher ground of the slope gave him a wider prospect, and he saw across one of the enclosing woods the tall chimneys of a great house. That did not detain his eye, which was caught by something beyond. There on a low ridge was sprawled a big village with square-towered church and a blur of smoke above the line of houses. England must be a monotonous land, for this village of Cheshire was the very image of Flambury, and the adjacent mansion might have been Squire Thicknesse’s manor.

At the same moment the music of hounds crashed from the spinney ahead, and a horn was violently blown. Round the edge of the spinney came the hunt, and the pack was spilled out of its shade like curds from a broken dish. The sight, novel in his experience, held him motionless. He saw the huntsman struggling with outrunners, and the field, urged on by the slope, crowding on the line. In the rear he saw a figure which was uncommonly like the magistrate who had presided last night in the Justice-room. As he observed these things he realised that his twenty miles of the morning had been a circuit, and that he was back now at the starting-point, mounted on a stolen horse, and within a hundred yards of the horse’s owner. The gypsy had set spurs to his beast and was disappearing round the other end of the spinney, and even in the hubbub of the hunt he thought he detected the man’s mocking laugh.

To hesitate was to be lost, and there was but the one course open. A tawny streak had slid before the hounds towards the brook. That must be the fox, and if he were not to become the quarry in its stead he must join in the chase. The huntsman was soon twenty yards from him, immediately behind the hounds, and fifty yards at his back came the van of the field. In that van he could see Squire Thicknesse mounted on a powerful grey, and he seemed to have eyes only for the hounds. Alastair cut in well behind him, in the hope that he would be taken for a straggler at covert-side, and in three seconds was sweeping forward in the second flight.

The morning’s ride had been for Moonbeam no more than a journey to the meet, and the beautiful animal now laid back his ears and settled down to his share in that game which he understood as well as any two-legged mortal. But in the very perfection of the horse lay the rider’s peril. Moonbeam was accustomed to top the hunt, for Squire Thicknesse was famed over three shires as a good goer. He would not be content to travel a field or two behind hounds; he must keep them company. Alastair found that no checking could restrain his mount. The animal was lightly bitted and he had not the skill or the strength to hold him back. True, he could have swerved and fetched a wide circuit, but in that first rush these tactics did not suggest themselves, and he set himself to a frantic effort at reining in, in which he was worsted. Moonbeam crossed the brook like a swallow; in a boggy place he took off badly, topped an ox-bar in the hedge, and all but fell on his nose in the next meadow. But after that he made no mistake, and in five minutes Alastair found himself looking from ten yards’ distance at the broad back of the huntsman, with no rider near him except Squire Thicknesse on the grey.

===Chinese===
可阿拉斯泰尔又回头望去。坡上的高处给了他更开阔的视野，隔着围拢的一片树林，他望见一座大宅高耸的烟囱。这还不曾留住他的眼睛，倒是很远处的什么把他吸住了。那边低低的山脊上趴着一座大村庄，教堂带着方形的塔楼，一线房舍之上浮着一抹炊烟。英格兰想必是片单调的土地，这柴郡的村庄竟与弗兰伯里一模一样，近旁那座大宅，简直就可能是西克尼斯乡绅的宅邸。

就在此刻，前方小树林里轰然响起群犬的乐声，一支猎号被猛烈地吹响。猎队绕着树林边缘涌出，犬群从树荫里泼洒出来，好似摔破的盘子里泼出的凝乳。这景象他生平未见，惊得他一动也不能动。他看见驭犬人费力约束着跑在前头的先锋犬，随猎的众人被坡势催逼，直压向嗅迹线。殿后的位置上，他看见一个人影，像极了昨夜在审理室里主审的那位治安官。他看着这一切，方才醒悟：自己清晨这二十英里竟是兜了个大圈，如今又回到了起点，胯下骑着一匹偷来的马，离马的主人不过百码。那吉普赛人已用马刺催动坐骑，正从小树林另一头消失，纵然猎队里人喧马嘶，他仿佛还是听出了那人嘲弄的笑声。

稍一迟疑便万事皆休，摆在面前的只有一条路。一道黄褐色的细影已抢在犬群前面滑向小溪。那必是狐狸；他若不想自己顶替它去做猎物，便只得入伙同猎。不多时驭犬人已到了他二十码之内，紧贴犬群后尾，再往后五十码，是随猎众人的前队。前队里他看得见西克尼斯乡绅，骑着一匹雄健的灰马，仿佛眼里只有猎犬。阿拉斯泰尔在他身后不远切了进去，指望被当作林边掉队的散骑，三秒钟后已随着第二梯队一路席卷向前。

清晨这一程，对月华不过是去赴猎集的一趟闲走；这匹俊美的牲口此刻耳朵向后一伏，一门心思做起这场游戏里自己的一份——这场游戏，他懂得不亚于任何两条腿的凡人。然而骑手之险，恰恰埋伏在这马的完美里。月华惯于领先全队压阵，因为西克尼斯乡绅以脚程快名闻三郡。他不甘心落在犬群后一两个田块的地方慢行，他非得与它们作伴不可。阿拉斯泰尔发现，怎样收缰也约束不住坐骑。这马戴的是轻衔，他既没有那份手法，也没有那份膂力去勒住它。诚然，他本可以拨转马头，兜一个大圈，可是开头那一阵冲刺里，这些招数全然不曾入脑，他只是一心一意拼命收缰，结果一败涂地。月华像燕子一般掠过小溪；在一处泥沼里它起跳失当，擦着树篱上一根横木越过，险些在下一片草场栽个鼻尖着地。但此后它再没有失误，五分钟后，阿拉斯泰尔发觉自己离驭犬人宽阔的后背只隔十码，近旁除了灰马上的西克尼斯乡绅，再无别的骑手。

===Original===
The going was good over old pasture, and the young man had leisure to recover his breath and consider his position. He had hunted buck in France﻿—stately promenades in the forests of Fontainebleau and Chantilly, varied by mad gallops along grassy rides where the only risk was the cannoning with other cavaliers. But this chase of the fox was a very different matter, the glory of it went to his head like strong wine, and he would not have cried off if he could. So far he was undiscovered. Were the fumes of last night’s revel still in the Squire’s head, or had he never meant to ride Moonbeam that day and his groom kept the loss from him? Crossing a thickset hedge neck by neck, Alastair stole a glance at him, and decided that the former explanation was the true one. His late host was still in the process of growing sober.﻿ ﻿… It could not last forever. Sooner or later must come a check or a kill, when he would have a chance to look at his neighbour and his neighbour’s horse﻿ ﻿… Then he must ride for it, become himself the fox, and trust to Moonbeam. Pray God that the run took them to the north and ended many miles from Flambury.

For the better part of an hour hounds ran without a check﻿—away from the enclosed fields and the woodlands to a country of furzy downs and bracken-filled hollows, and then once more into a land of tangled thickets. It took about twenty minutes to clear Squire Thicknesse’s brain. Alastair heard a sudden roar behind him and looked over his shoulder to see a furious blue eye fixed on him, and to hear a bellow of﻿—“Damme, it’s my horse. It’s my little Moonbeam!” He saw a whip raised, and felt it swish a foot from his leg. There was nothing for it but to keep his distance from the wrathful gentleman, and so gallantly did Moonbeam respond that he was presently at the huntsman’s elbow.

Had he known it, the grey was the faster of the two, though lacking Moonbeam’s sweet paces and lionlike heart. His enemy was up on him at once, and it looked as if there was nothing before him but to override hounds. But the discipline of the sport was stronger than a just wrath. The Squire took a pull on the grey and drew back. He was biding his time.

Alastair seized the first chance, which came when hounds were engulfed in a wide wood of oaks on the edge of a heath. Taking advantage of a piece of thick cover, he caught Moonbeam by the head and swung him down a side glade. Unfortunately he was observed. An oath from Squire Thicknesse warned him that that sportsman had forgone the pleasure of being in at the death for the satisfaction of doing justice on a horse-thief.

===Chinese===
老草场上脚程甚佳，年轻人得空喘匀了气息，盘算自己的处境。他在法兰西也猎过鹿——在枫丹白露（Fontainebleau）与尚蒂伊（Chantilly）的森林里，那是一场场气派堂皇的巡游，间或在草径上来一通狂奔，唯一的险处不过是与旁的骑士撞个满怀。可这猎狐完全是另一回事，其中的荣光像烈酒一样冲上他的头，哪怕退得出，他也不肯退了。至今他没有被识破。是昨夜宴饮的酒气还熏在乡绅头上，还是他那天本就不打算骑月华、马夫把失马的事瞒了下来？并肩跳过一道密实的树篱时，阿拉斯泰尔偷眼觑了觑他，断定前一种解释才对。他昨夜的主人，眼下还在一寸寸地醒酒。……这情形总不能持久。迟早会来一次停嗅，或是一场扑杀，那时他便有机会看清旁边的人和旁边的马……到那时他必须夺路而逃，自己变作那只狐狸，一切托付给月华。只求上天让这一程向北而去，在离弗兰伯里许多英里外收场。

将近一个钟头，犬群一路循嗅迹奔驰，不曾停歇——先离开圈围的田亩与林莽，进入一片金雀花覆坡、蕨草塞谷的地方，随后又钻进一带纠结缠绕的灌木丛莽。西克尼斯乡绅的脑子，用了约莫二十分钟才算清亮。阿拉斯泰尔听得身后一声怒吼，回头望去，只见一只狂怒的蓝眼睛死死盯住他，接着听得一声咆哮——「他妈的，这是我的马！这是我的小月华！」他看见一条马鞭扬起，感到鞭风在离他的腿一英尺处嗖嗖掠过。他别无办法，只有拉开与这位怒气冲冲的老爷的距离，而月华应命应得那样神勇，转眼间他已到了驭犬人的肘边。

他若知道底细便好了：灰马其实是两匹中更快的一匹，只是没有月华那样美妙的步态和狮子般的心胆。他的对头立刻追了上来，看那光景，他前方便只剩冲垮犬群一条路。好在猎场的规矩比理直气壮的怒火更有力量。乡绅把灰马收了一收，退了下去。他在候着时机。

阿拉斯泰尔抓住了第一个机会——犬群涌入荒原边上一片开阔的栎树林时，机会来了。他借一段浓密的掩体遮蔽，拽住月华的头，把它拨进一条岔出的林间甬道。不巧还是叫人瞧见了。西克尼斯乡绅一声咒骂警告他：那位运动家为了亲手惩办偷马贼的快意，甘愿舍弃亲见猎物毙命的乐子了。

===Original===
Now there was no hunt etiquette to be respected. The grey’s hooves spurned the rotten woodland turf, and pursuer and pursued crashed into a jungle of dry bulrushes and sallows. Alastair was saved by the superior agility of his horse, which could swerve and pivot where the heavier grey stumbled. He gained a yard or two, then a little more by a scramble through a gap, and a crazy scurry down a rabbit track.﻿ ﻿… He saw that his only chance was to slip off, for Moonbeam had the madness of the chase on him, and if left riderless would rejoin the hounds. So when he had gained some forty yards and was for the moment out of the Squire’s sight, he took his toes from the stirrups and flung himself into a bed of bracken. He rolled over and over into a dell, and when he came to a halt and could look up he saw the grey’s stern disappearing round the corner, and heard far off the swish and crash of Moonbeam’s flight.

Not a second was to be lost, for the Squire would soon see that the rider had gone and turn back in the search for him. Alastair forced his stiff legs to a run, and turned in the direction which he thought the opposite of that taken by hounds. Up a small path he ran, among a scrub of hazels and down into a desert of red bracken and sparse oak trees. The noises in the wood grew fainter, and soon his steps were the loudest sound, his steps and the heavy flight of an occasional scared pigeon. He ran till he had put at least a mile of rough land behind him, and had crossed several tracks, which would serve to mislead the pursuit. Lacking a bloodhound, it would not be easy to follow his trail. Then in a broader glade he came upon a thatched hovel, such as foresters and charcoal-burners use when they have business abroad in the night hours.

Alastair crept up to it cautiously, and through a crack surveyed the interior. His face hardened and an odd light came into his eye. He strode to the door and pushed the crazy thing open.

Within, breakfasting on a hunch of bread and cheese, sat the man Edom, Mr. Kyd’s servant.

===Chinese===
如今再不必讲究什么猎场礼数了。灰马的铁蹄踢碎朽坏的林间草皮，追的和被追的一头撞进一片干枯的香蒲与杞柳织成的莽丛。阿拉斯泰尔全亏坐骑更为矫捷才得脱身——沉重的灰马踉跄失蹄之处，月华却能闪转腾挪。他先挣出一两码，又靠着钻一个豁口再多挣了一点，顺着一条兔径疯跑一程。……他明白，唯一的活路是溜下马来，因为月华正犯着逐猎的疯魔，一旦没了骑手，自会归队追犬去。于是趁挣出四十来码、一时出了乡绅视线的当口，他把脚尖脱出马镫，一头扎进一丛蕨草。他一连几个滚，滚进一道凹谷，待到停住、能够抬头看时，只见灰马的臀尾正消失在拐角处，远远还听得见月华一路奔逃的蹄声与折枝之声。

一秒也耽搁不得，乡绅很快就会发现骑手已经不在，拨转马回来搜寻。阿拉斯泰尔强令两条僵直的腿奔跑起来，朝着他估量与犬群相反的方向跑去。他跑上一条小径，穿过一片榛树丛，又跑进一片红蕨与疏落栎树的荒地。林中的声响渐渐微弱，不多时他的脚步便成了最响的声音——除了他的脚步，只有偶尔一只受惊野鸽沉重的扑翅。他一直跑到把至少一英里崎岖地面甩在身后，又横穿过好几条小径，足以教追兵迷向。若没有猎血犬，要循迹追他可不是件容易事。随后，在一片较宽的林间空地上，他望见了一座茅顶的棚屋——护林人和烧炭人夜里在外头有事时落脚的那一种。

阿拉斯泰尔小心翼翼地挨上前去，从一道缝隙里窥看屋内。他的脸色沉了下来，眼里透出一道异样的光。他大步走到门前，把那扇摇摇欲坠的破门推了开。

屋里，一个汉子正就着一块面包和干酪用早饭——正是以东，基德先生的仆人。

## I In Which a Highland Gentleman Misses His Way / 一、高地绅士迷途记

===Original===
The road which had begun as a rutted cart-track sank presently to a grassy footpath among scrub oaks, and as the boughs whipped his face the young man cried out impatiently and pulled up his horse to consider. He was on a journey where secrecy was not less vital than speed, and he was finding the two incompatible. That morning he had avoided Banbury and the high road which followed the crown of Cotswold to the young streams of Thames, for that way lay Beaufort’s country, and at such a time there would be jealous tongues to question passengers. For the same reason he had left the main Oxford road on his right, since the channel between Oxford and the North might well be troublesome, even for a respectable traveller who called himself Mr. Andrew Watson, and was ready with a legend of a sea-coal business in Newcastle. But his circumspection seemed to have taken him too far on an easterly course into a land of tangled forests. He pulled out his chart of the journey and studied it with puzzled eyes. My Lord Cornbury’s house could not be twenty miles distant, but what if the twenty miles were pathless? An October gale was tossing the boughs and whirling the dead bracken, and a cold rain was beginning. Ill weather was nothing to one nourished among Hebridean northwesters, but he cursed a land in which there were no landmarks. A hilltop, a glimpse of sea or loch, even a stone on a ridge, were things a man could steer by, but what was he to do in this unfeatured woodland? These soft south-country folk stuck to their roads, and the roads were forbidden him.

A little further and the track died away in a thicket of hazels. He drove his horse through the scrub and came out on a glade, where the ground sloped steeply to a jungle of willows, beyond which he had a glimpse through the drizzle of a grey-green fen. Clearly that was not his direction, and he turned sharply to the right along the edge of the declivity. Once more he was in the covert, and his ill-temper grew with every briar that whipped his face. Suddenly he halted, for he heard the sound of speech.

It came from just in front of him﻿—a voice speaking loud and angry, and now and then a squeal like a scared animal’s. An affair between some forester and a poaching hind, he concluded, and would fain have turned aside. But the thicket on each hand was impenetrable, and, moreover, he earnestly desired advice about the road. He was hesitating in his mind, when the cries broke out again, so sharp with pain that instinctively he pushed forward. The undergrowth blocked his horse, so he dismounted and, with a hand fending his eyes, made a halter of the bridle and dragged the animal after him. He came out into a little dell down which a path ran, and confronted two human beings.

===Chinese===
这条路起初还是一道车辙纵横的马车道，渐渐地沦为矮橡林间一条青草蔓生的小径。枝条抽打在他脸上，年轻人不耐烦地喝了一声，勒住马停下来思量。他此行的要务，隐秘与迅捷同样性命攸关，而他偏偏发现这两者难以两全。那天早晨，他避开了班伯里（Banbury），也避开了那条沿着科茨沃尔德（Cotswold）高地脊线、直下泰晤士河（Thames）上游诸源的坦荡大道，因为那一带是博福特公爵（Beaufort）的世业，值此时节，少不得有好事的舌头要盘诘过路的客人。也是出于这层顾忌，他把通往牛津（Oxford）的干道撇在了右手，须知牛津与北方之间的这条孔道多半要惹麻烦，哪怕对一个自称安德鲁·沃森先生（Mr. Andrew Watson）、随时编得出一套纽卡斯尔（Newcastle）海煤生意来历的体面旅人，也未必例外。然而他的谨慎似乎矫枉过正，把他引得太偏东，带进了一片盘根错节的莽林之国。他抽出此行的路线图，满腹狐疑地端详。科恩伯里勋爵（Lord Cornbury）的宅邸绝不会远过二十英里，可这二十英里若是无路可通，又当如何？十月的狂风摇撼着枝柯，卷扬起枯死的蕨草，冷雨也初初落下。对于一个在赫布里底（Hebrides）西北风中长大的人，坏天气本不算什么，他诅咒的，是这片没有方位之标的土地。一座山头，一眼海或湖的光影，甚至山脊上的一块立石，都是可供人辨向的东西；可在这一片浑然无别的林地里，他又有什么办法？这些南方柔靡之民死守着他们的大路，而大路偏偏于他是禁途。

再往前去，小径便湮没在一丛榛树之中。他驱马穿过灌木，出到一片林间旷地，地面陡然下倾，直落向一带柳林莽丛；透过蒙蒙细雨，他瞥见沼泽灰绿的一角。那显然不是他的去处，于是他猛然右转，沿着坡缘行去。他重新没入树丛，每有一根荆棘抽上脸颊，他的火气便涨一分。蓦地他勒住了马——他听见了人语之声。

声音就在他前方——一个又高又怒的嗓门，不时夹着几声像受惊野兽似的尖叫。他忖道，多半是哪个林务汉在收拾一个偷猎的雇农，本想绕道避开。可两侧的树丛密不透风，况且他还亟盼有人指点路径。他正自犹疑，那哭喊又骤然响起，痛楚得刺耳，他不由自主地赶马上前。矮树挡住了马，他便翻身下来，一手护着眼，将缰绳挽作牵索，把马拖在身后。他出到一条小小的谷地，一条小径顺谷而下，眼前正是两个人。

===Original===
They did not see him, being intent on their own business. One was a burly fellow in a bottle-green coat, a red waistcoat and corduroy small clothes, from whose gap-toothed mouth issued volleys of abuse. In his clutches was a slim boy in his early teens, a dark sallow slip of a lad, clad in nothing but a shirt and short leather breeches. The man had laid his gun on the ground, and had his knee in the small of the child’s back, while he was viciously twisting one arm so that his victim cried like a rabbit in the grip of a weasel. The barbarity of it undid the traveller’s discretion.

“Hold there,” he cried, and took a pace forward.

The man turned his face, saw a figure which he recognised as a gentleman, and took his knee from the boy’s back, though he still kept a clutch on his arm.

“Sarvant, sir,” he said, touching his hat with his free hand. “What might ’ee be wanting o’ Tom Heather?” His voice was civil, but his face was ugly.

“Let the lad go.”

===Chinese===
那两人一心顾着自己的营生，没有看见他。一个是个横壮汉子，穿一瓶绿色外套、红背心、灯芯绒马裤，豁牙的嘴里连珠炮似的吐着咒骂。他爪下攥着的是个十三四岁的瘦削少年，黑黄瘦条的一副身架，身上只有一件衬衣和一条短皮裤。那人已把枪撂在地上，膝盖顶着孩子的后腰，狠命拧着他一条胳膊，疼得那受难的孩子像落在黄鼠狼爪下的兔子一般哀叫。这等残暴，顿时教旅人把谨慎忘了个干净。

「住手！」他喝道，抢前一步。

那人回过脸来，看清来者是个上等人模样，便把膝盖从孩子背上撤了下来，手里却仍攥着他的胳膊。

「小的给老爷请安，」他腾出一只手碰了碰帽檐，「不知老爷找汤姆·赫瑟（Tom Heather）有何贵干？」话音倒还客气，脸色却凶恶。

「放开那孩子。」
===Original===
“Sir Edward’s orders, sir﻿—that’s Sir Edward Turner, Baronet, of Ambrosden House in this ’ere shire, ’im I ’as the honour to serve. Sir Edward ’e says, ‘Tom,’ ’e says, ‘if ’ee finds a poacher in the New Woods ’ee knows what to do with ’im without troubling me’; and I reckon I does know. Them moor-men is the worst varmints in the country, and the youngest is the black-heartedest, like foxes.”

The grip had relaxed and the boy gave a twist which freed him. Instantly he dived into the scrub. The keeper made a bound after him, thought better of it and stood sullenly regarding the traveller.

“I’ve been a-laying for the misbegotten slip them five weeks, and now I loses him, and all along of ’ee, sir.” His tones suggested that silver might be a reasonable compensation.

But the young man, disliking his looks, was in no mood for almsgiving, and forgot the need of discretion. Also he came from a land where coin of the realm was scarce.

“If it’s your master’s orders to torture babes, then you and he can go to the devil. But show me the way out of this infernal wood and you shall have a shilling for your pains.”

===Chinese===
「这是爱德华爵士的规矩，老爷——便是本郡安布罗斯登府（Ambrosden House）的从男爵爱德华·特纳爵士（Sir Edward Turner），小人有幸伺候的主子。爱德华爵士他说：『汤姆，』他说，『你要是在新林子里拿住了偷猎的，你知道该怎么发落，不必来烦我。』小人想，小人自然是知道怎么发落的。这帮沼地人（moor-men）是全郡顶顶坏的害人精，越是年幼的心越黑，跟狐狸一个样。」

那铁钳般的手一松，孩子一拧挣脱了身，霎时间便钻进灌木丛里。看守人朝他猛追一步，转念一想不合算，便闷闷地站住，打量起这位旅人来。

「小人为这个野种崽子蹲了五个礼拜，如今倒教他跑了，全怪老爷您哪。」他的口气里透着：赏几枚银钱，这事便算两清。

可是那年轻人看他不顺眼，全无施舍的心情，把谨慎的需要也忘了。何况他所来的那个地方，通行的银钱向来稀罕。

「若是你们主子的规矩便是拷打孩童，那你和他都见鬼去吧。不过，给我指出这片鬼林子的出路，便赏你一先令辛苦钱。」

===Original===
At first the keeper seemed disposed to obey, for he turned and made a sign for the traveller to follow. But he swung round again, and, resting the gun which he had picked up in the crook of his arm, he looked the young man over with a dawning insolence in his eyes. He was beginning to see a more profitable turn in the business. The horseman was soberly but reputably dressed, and his beast was good, but what did he in this outlandish place?

“Making so bold,” said the keeper, “how come ’ee a-wandering ’ere, sir? Where might ’ee be a-making for?”

“Charlbury,” was the answer.

The man whistled. “Charlbury,” he repeated. “Again begging pardon, sir, it’s a place known for a nest of Papishes. I’d rather ha’ heerd ’ee was going to Hell. And where might ’ee come from last, sir?”

The traveller checked his rising temper. “Banbury,” he said shortly.

===Chinese===
起初看守人像是打算从命，转身做了个手势，请旅人跟上。可他又猛地兜转回来，把拾起的枪挎在臂弯里，一双眼里渐渐浮起放肆的光，把这年轻人上上下下打量。他开始咂摸出这桩买卖里更有油水的一转。这位骑客衣着素净而体面，坐骑也是好马，可他到这等偏僻地界来做甚？

「恕小人冒昧，」看守人说，「老爷怎会逛荡到这搭儿来了？您这是要往哪儿去呀？」

「查尔伯里（Charlbury）。」

那人打了个唿哨。「查尔伯里，」他重复一遍，「再请老爷恕罪，那地方是出了名的天主佬（Papishes）窝子。老爷就说要下地狱，小人听了心里倒还舒坦些。敢问老爷方才又是打哪儿来的？」

旅人按下心头火气。「班伯里。」他短短地答道。
===Original===
The keeper whistled again. “ ’Ee’ve fetched a mighty roundabout way, sir, and the good turnpike running straight for any Christian to see. But I’ve heard tell of folks that fought shy of turnpikes.”

“Confound you, man,” the traveller cried; “show me the road or I will find it myself and you’ll forfeit your shilling.”

The keeper did not move. “A shilling’s no price for a man’s honesty. I reckon ’ee mun come up with me to Sir Edward, sir. He says to me only this morning﻿—‘ ’Ee watch the Forest, Tom, and if ’ee finds any that can’t give good account of themselves, ’ee fetch them up to me, and it’ll maybe mean a golden guinea in your pocket.’ Sir Edward ’e’s a Parliament man, and a Justice, and ’e’s hot for King and country. There’s soldiers at Islip bridge-end asking questions of all as is journeying west, and there’s questions Sir Edward is going to ask of a gentleman as travels from Banbury to Charlbury by the edges of Otmoor.”

The servility had gone from the man’s voice, and in its place were insolence and greed. A guinea might have placated him, but the traveller was not accustomed to bribe. A hot flush had darkened his face, and his eyes were bright.

===Chinese===
看守人又是一声唿哨。「老爷这一趟，可绕了好大一个圈子，明明一条规规矩矩的收费大道笔直摆在那儿，是个人都瞧得见。不过小人也听说过，有些人就是见了收费大道要躲着走的。」

「你这混账东西，」旅人喝道，「再不指路，我自己也会寻着，你那一先令也就休想了。」

看守人纹丝不动。「一先令，可买不动一个老实人的良心。小人看，老爷还得跟小人上爱德华爵士那儿走一遭。就在今儿早上，他还对小人说——『汤姆，你替我盯着林子，但凡拿住了那些说不出个底细的，就给我带上府来，说不定你兜里就能落进一枚金几尼（guinea）。』爱德华爵士他是国会议员，又是治安法官，一心热着国王与国家。伊斯利普（Islip）桥头就驻着大兵，正挨个儿盘问往西去的人；爱德华爵士呢，也自有话要问一问这位沿着奥特沼泽（Otmoor）边上、从班伯里往查尔伯里走的贵客。」

那人话音里的奴态已褪得干干净净，换上来的是放肆与贪心。一枚几尼原可打发他，无奈这位旅人不惯行贿。一股热血涌上来，黑了他的脸，他的眼睛也亮了。

===Original===
“Get out of my way, you rogue,” he cried.

The keeper stood his ground. “ ’Ee will come to Sir Edward with me if ’ee be an honest man.”

“And if not?”

“It’s my duty to constrain ’ee in the name of our Lord the King.”

The man had raised his gun, but before he could bring the barrel forward he was looking at a pistol held in a very steady hand. He was no coward, but he had little love for needless risks, when he could find a better way. He turned and ran up the steep path at a surprising pace for one of his build, and as he ran he blew shrilly on a whistle.

===Chinese===
「滚开，你这无赖！」他喝道。

看守人钉在原地不动。「老爷若是个正派人，就随小人去见爱德华爵士。」

「要是我偏不去呢？」

「小人便奉我主国王之名，锁了老爷去。」

那人已端起枪来，可枪管还不及转过来，他眼前已对着一只握得纹丝不乱的手枪。他并非懦夫，但既然寻得着更稳妥的路数，便不爱冒无谓的险。他转身顺着陡径飞奔上去，那脚程之快，与他那副身坯全不相称，一面跑，一面吹起尖锐的唿哨。
===Original===
The traveller left alone in the dell bit his lips with vexation. He had made a pretty mess of a journey which above all things should have been inconspicuous, and had raised a hue and cry after him on the domain of some arrogant Whig. He heard the keeper’s steps and the note of his whistle grow fainter; he seemed to be crying to others and answers came back faintly. In a few minutes he would be in a brawl with lackeys.﻿ ﻿… In that jungle there was no way of escape for a mounted man, so he must needs stand and fight.

And then suddenly he was aware of a face in the hazels.

It was the slim boy whom his intervention had saved from a beating. The lad darted from his cover and seized the horse’s bridle. Speaking no word, he made signs to the other to follow, and the traveller, glad of any port in a storm, complied. They slithered at a great pace down the steep bank to the thicket of willows, which proved to be the brink of a deep ditch. A little way along it they crossed by a ford of hurdles, where the water was not over a man’s riding boots. They were now in a morass, which they threaded by a track which showed dimly among the reeds, and, as the whistling and cries were still audible behind them, they did not relax their pace. But after two more deep runnels had been passed, and a mere thick with water-lilies crossed by a chain of hard tussocks like stepping-stones, the guide seemed to consider the danger gone. He slowed down, laughing, and cocked snooks in the direction of the pursuit. Then he signed to the traveller to remount his horse, but when the latter would have questioned him, he shook his head and put a finger on his lips. He was either dumb, or a miracle of prudence.

The young man found himself in a great green fenland, but the falling night and the rain limited his view to a narrow circle. There was a constant crying of snipe and plover around him, and the noise of wild fowl rose like the croaking of frogs in the Campagna. Acres of rank pasture were threaded with lagoons where the brown water winked and bubbled above fathomless mud. The traveller sniffed the air with a sense of something foreign and menacing. The honest bitter smell of peat-bogs he loved, but the odour of this marsh was heavy and sweet and rotten. As his horse’s hooves squelched in the sodden herbage he shivered a little and glanced suspiciously at his guide. Where was this gipsy halfling leading him? It looked as if he had found an ill-boding sanctuary.

===Chinese===
旅人独自留在谷地里，烦恼地咬着嘴唇。一段本该悄没声息的行程，叫他弄得一塌糊涂，还在某位飞扬跋扈的辉格党（Whig）地主的庄场上，招来了一路追捕的喧声。他听着看守人的脚步声和唿哨声渐渐远去；那人似乎还在朝旁人呼喊，隐隐有回应传来。再过几分钟，他就要跟一班恶仆纠斗了。……这片丛林里，骑马的人无路可逃，那便只好站定脚跟，厮杀一场。

也就在这时，他蓦然察觉榛丛里有一张脸。

正是那被他解救、才免了一顿毒打的瘦削少年。孩子从藏身处窜出来，一把抓住马勒。他一言不发，只做手势教他跟来，旅人正如风暴中望见港口，哪有不依的，便随了他去。两人顺着陡坡飞滑而下，直抵那带柳丛，原来那是一道深沟的沟沿。沿沟行不多远，他们从一道枝排铺成的渡口过了水，水深不过骑者的马靴。此刻他们已身在一片沼泽之中，循着一条在芦苇间若隐若现的小径穿行；身后唿哨与喊声犹自可闻，两人便不敢放慢脚步。待又越过两道深汊，又踩着一条硬草墩连成的「垫脚石」链，穿过一片睡莲密布的池沼，那向导似乎认为危险已经远了。他放缓脚步，笑起来，朝追兵的方向做了几下嘲弄的手势。然后他示意旅人重新上马；旅人正要开口发问，他却摇一摇头，竖起一根手指抵在唇上。他要么是个哑巴，要么便是谨慎得近乎神异。

年轻人发觉自己置身于一片浩大的绿色沼乡，但沉沉夜幕和雨水把他的眼界圈在狭小的一环之内。四周鹬鸟和鸻鸟的啼叫不绝于耳，野禽的喧腾像罗马坎帕尼亚（Campagna）平原上的蛙鸣一般升起。一英亩又一英亩的丰茂草场间，潟湖纵横交织，褐色的水在深不见底的泥淖上闪烁、冒泡。旅人嗅着这空气，觉出一种异样而不祥的气息。泥炭沼那股老实的苦味，他是喜爱的，可这沼泽的气味却滞重、甜腻而腐坏。马蹄在浸透的水草间咕唧作响，他微微打了个寒噤，狐疑地瞥了那向导一眼。这个吉卜赛模样的半大孩子，究竟要把自己领到哪里去？看来，他是寻着了一处凶兆重重的庇身之所。

===Original===
With every yard that he advanced into the dank green wilderness his oppression increased. The laden air, the mist, the clamour of wild birds, the knowledge that his horse was no advantage since a step aside would set it wallowing to the girths, all combined to make the place a prison-house, hateful to one on an urgent mission.﻿ ﻿… Suddenly he was above the fen on a hard causeway, where hooves made a solid echo. His spirits recovered, for he recognised Roman work, and a Roman road did not end in sloughs. On one side, below the level of the causeway, was a jungle of blackthorn and elder, and a whiff of wood-smoke reached his nostrils. The guide halted and three times gave a call like that of a nesting redshank. It was answered, and from an alley in the scrub a man appeared.

He was a roughly dressed countryman, wearing huge leathern boots muddied to the knee. Apparently the guide was not wholly dumb, for he spoke to him in an odd voice that croaked from the back of his throat, and the man nodded and bent his brows. Then he lifted his eyes and solemnly regarded the horseman for the space of some seconds.

“You be welcome, sir,” he said. “If you can make shift with poor fare there be supper and lodging waiting for you.”

The boy made signs for him to dismount, and led off the horse, while the man beckoned him to follow into the tunnel in the scrub. In less than fifty yards he found himself in a clearing where a knuckle of gravel made a patch of hard ground. In the centre stood a small ancient obelisk, like an overgrown milestone. A big fire of logs and brushwood was burning, and round it sat half a dozen men, engaged in cooking. They turned slow eyes on the newcomer, and made room for him in their circle.

===Chinese===
他在这片湿漉漉的绿色荒野里每前进一步，胸中的郁结便又重一分。饱含水汽的空气，迷雾，野禽的聒噪，再加上明知坐骑已无半分助益——稍一失蹄，马就要陷到肚带——这一切合在一处，把这地方变成了一座囚牢，教一个身负急务的人憎厌。……蓦地，他已凌驾于沼泽之上，踏上一条坚硬的堤道，马蹄敲出结实的回响。他的精神为之一振，因为他认出这是罗马人的工程，而罗马路是决不会通到泥潭里断头的。堤道一侧，低于道面的地方，是一带黑刺李与接骨木的莽丛，一缕柴烟的气味钻进他的鼻孔。向导停下脚步，学了三声营巢红脚鹬般的啼叫。有人应声，灌丛间一条小径里走出一个人来。

那是个衣着粗陋的乡下人，脚上一双硕大的皮靴，膝盖以下糊满了泥。那向导显然并非全哑，因为他用一种从喉咙深处挤出来的沙哑怪腔对那人说着话，那人点头，皱起了眉。然后他抬起眼来，肃然打量了这位骑马人好几秒钟。

「你老来了，欢迎，」他说，「你老若不嫌粗茶淡饭，晚饭和宿处都是现成的。」

少年做手势请他下马，把马牵走了；那人便招手引他走进灌木间的甬道。不到五十码，他便置身一片林间空地，一角砾石高地铺出巴掌大的一块硬土。空地中央立着一座古旧的小方尖碑，像一根疯长了的里程碑。一大堆枝柴正烧得熊熊，围着火坐着六七条汉子，忙着做饭。他们把迟缓的目光转向来客，在圈子里给他腾出了位子。
===Original===
“Tom Heather’s been giving trouble. He cotched Zerry and was a-basting him when this gentleman rides up. Then he turns on the gentleman, and, being feared o’ him as man to man, goes whistling for Red Tosspot and Brother Mark. So Zerry brings the gentleman into the Moor, and here he be. I tell him he’s kindly welcome, and snug enough with us moor-men, though the King’s soldiers was sitting in all the Seven Towns.”

“He’d be safe,” said one, “though Lord Abingdon and his moor-drivers was prancing up at Beckley.”

There was a laugh at this, and the newcomer, cheered by the blaze and the smell of food, made suitable reply. He had not quite understood their slow burring speech, nor did they altogether follow his words, for he spoke English in the formal clipped fashion of one to whom it was an acquired tongue. But the goodwill on both sides was manifest, and food was pressed on him﻿—wild duck roasted on stakes, hunks of brown bread, and beer out of leather jacks. The men had been fowling, for great heaps of mallard and teal and widgeon were piled beyond the fire.

The traveller ate heartily, for he had had no meal since breakfast, and as he ate, he studied his companions in the firelight. They were rough-looking fellows, dressed pretty much alike in frieze and leather, and they had the sallowish skin and yellow-tinged eyes which he remembered to have seen among dwellers in the Ravenna marshes. But they were no gipsies or outlaws, but had the assured and forthright air of men with some stake in the land. Excellent were their manners, for the presence of a stranger in no way incommoded them; they attended to his wants, and with easy good-breeding talked their own talk. Understanding little of that talk, he occupied himself in observing their faces and gestures with the interest of a traveller in a new country. These folk were at once slower and speedier than his own kind﻿—more deliberate in speech and movement, but quicker to show emotion in their open countenances. He speculated on their merits as soldiers, for against such as these he and his friends must presently fight.

===Chinese===
「汤姆·赫瑟又惹事了。他拿住了泽里（Zerry），正鞭他呢，这位老爷骑马赶到。他随后又冲老爷来了，可一对一又怕老爷，就去吹哨子搬红脸醉鬼（Red Tosspot）和马克兄弟（Brother Mark）。泽里就把老爷带进了沼地，老爷这就到了。我告诉他，他大驾光临，咱们由衷欢迎；跟咱们这些沼地人（moor-men）在一处，保管安安稳稳，哪怕国王的大兵把七镇（Seven Towns）坐满了也使得。」

「保管没事，」一个说，「哪怕阿宾登勋爵（Lord Abingdon）带着他那班牧倌在贝克利（Beckley）耀武扬威。」

这话引来一阵哄笑，新来的客人被火光和饭菜香气一暖，也得体地应酬了几句。他们那种慢吞吞、喉音浓重的土话，他没有全然听懂；他们也没全听明白他的话，因为他讲的英语，带着一种后学语言的正式而短促的腔调。但双方的善意显而易见，饭菜更是一味地敬上来——架在木签上烤的野鸭，大块的黑面包，皮囊里的麦酒。这些人白日里必是猎水禽去了，火堆之外堆着一垛垛的绿头鸭、绿翅鸭和赤颈鸭。

旅人吃得畅快，早饭以来他还粒米未进呢。他一面吃，一面就着火光端详他的同伴们。都是些相貌粗豪的汉子，穿着大同小异的粗呢与皮革，皮肤灰黄，眼珠泛着黄晕，他记得在拉文纳（Ravenna）沼泽的居民中间见过这样的相貌。但他们并非吉卜赛人或亡命之徒，倒有一种在乡土中略有产业之人特有的笃定爽直的气概。他们的举止更是极佳：生人在座，丝毫不以为意；他们照应他的需要，又以从容的好教养谈着自家的话。那些话他不大懂，便以初到一国之旅人的兴味，专心致志地观察他们的面孔与手势。这些人比起他的族人，既更迟缓又更迅捷——言语行动更沉着，可一旦喜怒浮上开朗的脸面，却又更快。他掂量他们作为军人的品性，因为他和他的朋友们，不久便要与这样的人兵戎相见。

===Original===
“ ’Morrow we’d best take Mercot Fleet,” said one. “Mas’r Midwinter reckons as the floods will be down come Sunday.”

“Right, neighbour Basson,” said another. “He knows times and seasons better’n Parson and near as well as Almighty God.”

“What be this tale of bloody wars?” asked a third. “The Spoonbills be out, and that means that the land is troubled. They was saying down at Noke that Long Giles was seen last week at Banbury fair and the Spayniard was travelling the Lunnon road. All dressed up he were like a fine gentleman, and at Wheatley Green Man he was snuffing out o’ Squire Norreys’ box.”

“Who speaks of the Spoonbills?” said the man who had first welcomed the traveller. “We bain’t no alehouse prattlers. What Mas’r Midwinter wants us to know I reckon he’ll tell us open and neighbourly. Think you he’ll make music the night?”

“He’s had his supper the best part of an hour, and then he’ll take tobacco. After that happen he’ll gie us a tune.”

===Chinese===
「明儿个咱们最好去梅科特水道（Mercot Fleet），」一个说，「仲冬先生（Mas'r Midwinter）估摸着，洪水到礼拜天就退了。」

「说得对，巴松老哥（neighbour Basson），」另一个说，「节令的事，他比牧师还懂得多，快赶上全能的天主了。」

「这血腥战事的风声，又是怎么说的？」第三个人问，「篦鹭会（Spoonbills）出动了，那就是说世道乱了。诺克（Noke）那边的人说，上礼拜有人在班伯里集市上瞧见了高个贾尔斯（Long Giles），那个西班牙人（the Spayniard）也正走在伦敦大道上。他一身装束，派头像个阔气的老爷，在惠特利（Wheatley）的绿人客栈里，正拿着诺里斯乡绅（Squire Norreys）的鼻烟壶嗅鼻烟呢。」

「谁在议论篦鹭会？」最先迎接旅人的那人说，「咱们可不是酒馆里的碎嘴子。仲冬先生要咱们知道的事，我料他自会光明正大、街坊邻里面前当面相告。你们说，他今儿夜里会弄音乐么？」

「他晚饭用罢，快有一个钟头了，接下来要抽烟斗。抽完烟，兴许会给咱们来上一曲。」
===Original===
The speaker had looked over his shoulder, and the traveller, following his glance, became aware that close on the edge of the thicket a small tent was pitched. The night had fallen thick and moonless, but the firelight, wavering in the wind, showed it as a grey patch against the gloom of the covert. As the conversation droned on, that patch held his eyes like a magnet. There was a man there, someone with the strange name of Midwinter, someone whom these moor-men held in reverence. The young man had the appetite of his race for mysteries, and his errand had keyed him to a mood of eager inquiry. He looked at the blur which was the tent as a terrier watches a badger’s earth.

The talk round the fire had grown boisterous, for someone had told a tale which woke deep rumbling laughter. Suddenly it was hushed, for the thin high note of a violin cleft the air like an arrow.

The sound was muffled by the tent-cloth, but none the less it dominated and filled that lonely place. The traveller had a receptive ear for music and had heard many varieties in his recent wanderings, from the operas of Rome and Paris to gypsy dances in wild glens of Apennine and Pyrenees. But this fiddling was a new experience, for it obeyed no law, but jigged and wailed and chuckled like a gale in an old house. It seemed to be a symphony of the noises of the moor, where unearthly birds sang duets with winds from the back of beyond. It stirred him strangely. His own bagpipes could bring tears to his eyes with memory of things dear and familiar; but this quickened his blood, like a voice from a far world.

===Chinese===
说话的人回头望了一眼，旅人顺着他的目光，才发觉就在树丛边缘不远处，支着一顶小帐篷。夜已落得又浓又黑，不见月亮，火光在风中摇曳，把它映成幽暗树影跟前的一块灰斑。谈话声嗡嗡地继续着，那块灰斑却像磁石一般攫住了他的眼睛。那里面有一个人物——一个顶着「仲冬」这古怪名号的人物，一个被这些沼地人奉若神明的人物。这年轻人天生一副族人共有的嗜好神秘的胃口，他的使命又把他的心绪调到了渴于探问的音键上。他盯着那团帐篷的暗影，活像一头梗犬盯着獾洞。

火堆边的谈笑喧腾起来，因为有人讲了个什么故事，激起一阵深沉滚动的哄笑。突然间一切静了，一缕细而高的小提琴声，如箭矢般划破了空气。

帐篷布把琴声闷住了，可它依旧主宰着、填满了这荒凉的一隅。旅人有一副善纳的耳朵，近年游历中听过千般品类的音乐，从罗马和巴黎的歌剧，到亚平宁（Apennine）与比利牛斯（Pyrenees）荒山幽谷里的吉卜赛舞曲。可这琴声却是新的经验，因为它不守任何法度，只管跳荡、悲号、低笑，像一场穿堂扫过老宅的疾风。它仿佛是这荒沼种种天籁的一阕交响，冥世的禽鸟与来自鸿蒙之外的罡风，在其中唱着二重唱。这琴声异样地撼动了他。他自己家乡的风笛也能催他落泪，教他忆起亲爱熟稔的事物；可这琴声却是催沸他的血，像另一个世界传来的呼声。

===Original===
The group by the fire listened stolidly with their heads sunk, but the young man kept his eyes on the tent. Presently the music ceased, and from the flap a figure emerged with the fiddle in its hand. The others rose to their feet, and remained standing till the musician had taken a seat at the other side of the fire from the traveller. “Welcome, Mas’r Midwinter,” was the general greeting, and one of them told him the story of Tom Heather and their guest.

The young man by craning his neck could see the new figure clear in the glow of the embers. He made out a short man of an immense breadth of shoulder, whose long arms must have reached well below his knees. He had a large square face, tanned to the colour of bark, and of a most surprising ugliness, for his nose was broken in the middle, and one cheek and the corner of one eye were puckered with an old scar. Chin and lips were shaven, and the wide mouth showed white regular teeth. His garments seemed to be of leather like the others, but he wore a cravat, and his hair, though unpowdered, was neatly tied.

He was looking at the traveller and, catching his eye, he bowed and smiled pleasantly.

“You have found but a rough lodging, Mr.﻿—” he said, with the lift of interrogation in his voice.

===Chinese===
火边的一群人垂着头木然听着，年轻人却目不转睛地盯着那顶帐篷。不多时琴声停了，帐帘内闪出一条人影，手里提着提琴。众人纷纷起身，肃立着，直到乐师在火堆另一侧、旅人的对面落了座。「欢迎，仲冬先生。」众人齐声致意，便有一人把汤姆·赫瑟的事和这位客人讲给他听。

年轻人伸长脖颈，便能就着余烬的红光看清这新来者的形貌。他看出这是个身量短小、肩膀极阔的汉子，一双长臂垂下来，怕是远过膝头。一张宽大的方脸，晒成了树皮的颜色，丑得惊人：鼻梁当中断过，一边脸颊和一只眼角上，一道旧疤扯出褶皱。下颏和嘴唇剃得精光，阔嘴里露出洁白齐整的牙齿。衣裳似乎与旁人一样是皮革的，但他系着领巾，头发虽未扑粉，却扎得整整齐齐。

他正望向旅人，两下目光一接，他便欠身一礼，和颜悦色地笑了。

「您寻着的这处宿头，未免简陋了，贵客尊姓——」他说，尾音上挑，带着询问。
===Original===
“Andrew Watson they call me. A merchant of Newcastle, sir, journeying Bristol-wards on a matter of business.” The formula, which had sounded well enough hitherto, now seemed inept, and he spoke it with less assurance.

The fiddler laughed. “That is for change-houses. Among friends you will doubtless tell another tale. For how comes a merchant of the North country to be so far from a high road? Shall I read the riddle, sir?”

He took up his violin and played very low and sweetly a Border lilt called “The Waukin’ o’ the Fauld.” The young man listened with interest, but his face did not reveal what the musician sought. The latter tried again, this time the tune called “Colin’s Cattle,” which was made by the fairies and was hummed everywhere north of Forth. Bright eyes read the young man’s face. “I touch you,” the fiddler said, “but not closely.”

For a moment he seemed to consider, and then drew from his instrument a slow dirge, with the rain in it and the west wind and the surge of forlorn seas. It was that lament which in all the country from Mull to Moidart is the begetter of long thoughts. He played it like a master, making his fiddle weep and brood and exult in turn, and he ended with a fantastic variation so bitter with pain that the young man, hearing his ancestral melody in this foreign land, cried out in amazement.

===Chinese===
「在下安德鲁·沃森，纽卡斯尔的商人，此番往布里斯托尔（Bristol）办货。」这一套说辞，此前一路讲来倒也像模像样，此刻却显得驴唇不对马嘴，他说得也没了底气。

琴师笑了。「那是讲给客栈听的。在朋友中间，阁下想必会讲另一套故事。北方来的商人，怎会离了大道这许多？要不要我来猜破这个谜，先生？」

他提起琴，低回而甜美地奏起一支边境小调，唤作《羊栏守夜曲》（The Waukin' o' the Fauld）。年轻人饶有兴味地听着，脸上却未露出乐师所要寻觅的神色。他又试了一回，这回是一支名为《科林的牛群》（Colin's Cattle）的曲子——那是仙女编就的调子，福斯河（Forth）以北处处传哼。一双明亮的眼睛读着年轻人的脸。「我触着您了，」琴师说，「可触得不深。」

他似乎沉吟片刻，随即从琴上引出一支缓慢的哀歌，里头藏着雨，藏着西风，藏着孤海的怒涛。那支挽歌，在从马尔（Mull）到莫伊代特（Moidart）的全境之内，素来是引人悠长遐思的曲子。他奏得如一位宗师，令他的琴轮番地哭泣、沉思、欢跃，末了以一段奇诡的变奏收束，苦痛彻骨，教这年轻人在异域听见了祖先的旋律，惊呼出声。

===Original===
The musician lowered his violin, smiling. “This time,” he said, “I touch you at the heart. Now I know you. You have nothing to fear among the moor-men of the Seven Towns. Take your ease, Alastair Maclean, among friends.”

The traveller, thus unexpectedly unveiled, could find no words for his astonishment.

“Are you of the honest party?” he stammered, more in awe than in anxiety.

“I am of no party. Ask the moor-men if the Spoonbills trouble their heads with Governments?”

===Chinese===
乐师放下提琴，微笑道：「这一回，我可触着您的心了。如今我认得您了。在七镇的沼地人中间，您没有什么可怕的。请宽坐，阿拉斯泰尔·麦克莱恩（Alastair Maclean），在这里都是朋友。」

旅人这样猝不及防地被揭了底，惊得一时找不出话来。

「您是忠义一党的人么？」他讷讷地问，敬畏多于忧急。

「我哪一个党也不是。去问问这些沼地人，篦鹭会可曾为政府操过心？」
===Original===
The answer from the circle was a laugh.

“Who are you, then, that watches thus the comings and goings of travellers?”

“I am nothing﻿—a will-o’-the-wisp at your service﻿—a clod of vivified dust whom its progenitors christened Amos Midwinter. I have no possession but my name, and no calling but that of philosopher. Naked I came from the earth, and naked I will return to it.”

He plucked with a finger at the fiddle-strings, and evoked an odd lilt. Then he crooned:

===Chinese===
圈子里的回答，是一片大笑。

「那么您究竟是谁，这样窥伺着行人的来去？」

「我什么也不是——一团听候您差遣的鬼火——一撮先人施洗时唤作埃莫斯·仲冬（Amos Midwinter）的活尘土。我一无所有，唯有这个名字；一无所业，唯有一门哲学。我赤条条从土里来，还要赤条条回土里去。」

他用一根手指拨了拨琴弦，唤出一支古怪的小调，随即低吟起来：

===Original===
> “Three naked men I saw,
 

 One to hang and one to draw,
 

 One to feed the corbie’s maw.”


The men by the fire shivered, and one spoke. “Let be, Mas’r Midwinter. Them words makes my innards cold.”

===Chinese===
> 「我看见三个赤身人，
> 一个受绞，一个车裂，
> 一个去填渡鸦的肚肠。」

火边的汉子们打了个寒噤，一个开口道：「别唱了，仲冬先生，这些个词儿，唱得人心里发凉。」
===Original===
“I will try others,” and he sang:

> “Three naked men we be,
 

 Stark aneath the blackthorn tree.
 

 Christ ha’ mercy on such as we!”

The young man found his apprehensions yielding place to a lively curiosity. From this madman, whoever he might be, he ran no risk of betrayal. The thought flashed over his mind that here was one who might further the cause he served.

===Chinese===
「那我另换一首。」他又唱道：

> 「我等正是赤身人，
> 光脱脱卧在黑刺李树下，
> 基督怜悯我等这般人！」

年轻人发觉自己的疑惧，正让位给一股活跃的好奇。这个疯子，不管他究竟是谁，总不会出卖他。一个念头闪过心头：此人兴许能襄助他所效忠的大业。

===Original===
“I take it you are not alone in your calling?” he said.

“There are others﻿—few but choice. There are no secrets among us who camp by Jacob’s Stone.” He pointed to the rude obelisk which was just within the glow of the fire. “Once that was an altar where the Romans sacrificed to fierce gods and pretty goddesses. It is a thousand years and more since it felt their flame, but it has always been a trysting place. We Christian men have forsworn Apollo, but maybe he still lingers, and the savour of our little cooking fires may please him. I am one that takes no chances with the old gods.﻿ ﻿… Here there is safety for the honest lawbreaker, and confidence for the friend, for we are reverent souls. How does it go?﻿—*Fides et Pax et Honos Pudorque priscus.*”

“Then tell me of your brotherhood?”

The man laughed. “That no man can know unless he be sealed of it. From the Channel to the Tyne they call us the Spoonbills, and on Cumbrian moors they know us as the Bog-blitters. But our titles are as many as the by-names of Jupiter. Up in your country I have heard that men talk of us as the Left-Handed.”

===Chinese===
「依我看，干您这一行的，不止您一位？」他说。

「另有其人——少而精。在雅各之石（Jacob's Stone）旁扎营的我们中间，没有秘密。」他指了指火光尽处那座粗陋的方尖碑。「它曾是一座祭坛，罗马人在上面向凶恶的神明和美丽的女神献祭。距它感受他们的火焰，已有一千多年，可它始终是一处盟会的圣地。我们这些基督徒早已誓弃了阿波罗（Apollo），但说不定他仍在此流连，我们这几堆小小的炊火，兴许正合他的意。我是那种不肯在老神明身上冒险的人。……在这里，诚实的犯禁者得到安全，朋友得到信托，因为我们都是敬畏神明的灵魂。那话怎么说的？——*Fides et Pax et Honos Pudorque priscus*（信义、和平、荣誉，与古时的廉耻）。」

「那么，给我讲讲你们的兄弟会？」

那人笑了。「不曾在它里面受了印的，谁也无缘知晓。从英伦海峡到泰恩河（Tyne），人家叫我们篦鹭会；在坎伯兰（Cumbrian）的荒沼上，人家管我们叫沼怪帮（Bog-blitters）。可我们的名号，多得像朱庇特（Jupiter）的别号。在贵国那边，我听说人们把我们唤作『左手党』（the Left-Handed）。」
===Original===
He spoke the last word in Gaelic﻿—*ciotach*﻿—and the young man at the sound of his own tongue almost leapt to his feet.

“Have you the speech?” he cried in the same language.

The man shook his head. “I have nothing. For our true name is that I have sung to you. We are the Naked Men.” And he crooned again the strange catch.

For an instant Alastair felt his soul clouded by an eeriness which his bustling life had not known since as a little boy he had wandered alone into the corries of Sgurr Dubh. The moonless night was black about him, and it had fallen silent except for the sputter of logs. He seemed cut off from all things familiar by infinite miles of midnight, and in the heart of the darkness was this madman who knew all things and made a mock of knowledge. The situation so far transcended his experience that his orderly world seemed to melt into shadows. The tangible bounds of life dislimned and he looked into outer space. But the fiddler dispelled the atmosphere of awe, for he pulled out a pipe and filled and lit it.

===Chinese===
末一个词，他是用盖尔语（Gaelic）说的——*ciotach*（左手）——年轻人听见自己的母语，几乎跳起身来。

「您会说这语言？」他用同一语言喊道。

那人摇摇头。「我一无所有。我们的真名，就是我方才唱给您的。我们是赤身人（the Naked Men）。」他又低声哼起那支古怪的小调。

一瞬间，阿拉斯泰尔只觉魂灵被一层阴森罩住，自打幼年独自迷闯斯古尔·杜（Sgurr Dubh）山间幽谷以来，他奔忙的生涯还从未识得这种滋味。无月的夜在他四周黑沉沉压着，除了柴火的毕剥，万籁俱寂。他仿佛被午夜的茫茫千里，与一切熟悉之物隔断，而黑暗的中心，正是这一个无所不知、又嘲弄知识的疯子。这局面远远超出了他的阅历，他那井然有序的世界，仿佛熔成了幢幢暗影。生命的有形界限消融了，他直望进了太虚。但琴师驱散了这敬畏的气氛——他掏出烟斗，装上烟，点着了。

===Original===
“I can offer you better hospitality, sir, than a bed by the fire. A share of my tent is at your service. These moor-men are hardened to it, but if you press the ground this October night you will most surely get a touch of the moor-evil, and that is ill to cure save by a week’s drinking of Oddington Well. So by your grace we will leave our honest friends to their talk of latimer and autumn markets.”

Accompanied by deep-voiced “Good nights” Alastair followed the fiddler to the tent, which proved to be larger and more pretentious than it had appeared from the fire. Midwinter lit a small lamp which he fastened to the pole, and closed the flap. The traveller’s mails had been laid on the floor, and two couches had been made up of skins of fox and deer and badger heaped on dry rushes.

“You do not use tobacco?” Midwinter asked. “Then I will administer a cordial against the marsh fever.” From a leathern case he took a silver-mounted bottle, and poured a draught into a horn cup. It was a kind of spiced brandy which Alastair had drunk in Southern France, and it ran through his blood like a mild and kindly fire, driving out the fatigue of the day but disposing to a pleasant drowsiness. He removed his boots and coat and cravat, loosened the points of his breeches, replaced his wig with a kerchief, and flung himself gratefully on the couch.

Meantime the other had stripped almost to the buff, revealing a mighty chest furred like a pelt. Alastair noted that the underclothes which remained were of silk; he noticed, too, that the man had long fine hands at the end of his brawny arms, and that his skin, where the weather had not burned it, was as delicately white as a lady’s. Midwinter finished his pipe, sitting hunched among the furs, with his eyes fixed steadily on the young man. There was a mesmerism in those eyes which postponed sleep, and drove Alastair to speak. Besides, the lilt sung by the fire still hummed in his ears.

===Chinese===
「先生，我能款待您比火边一铺更好的宿处。我帐篷里分一半给您。这些沼地人睡惯了，可这十月的夜里，您若贴地而眠，十有八九要沾上沼地邪病（moor-evil），那可难治，除非上奥丁顿圣井（Oddington Well）连喝上一个礼拜的水。所以承您俯允，咱们这就把那些老实朋友，留给他们的拉蒂默（latimer）和秋集闲话吧。」

在一片低沉嗓音的「晚安」声中，阿拉斯泰尔跟着琴师来到帐篷，帐中比从火边望去的要宽敞讲究些。仲冬点亮一盏小灯，挂在帐柱上，掩上了帐帘。旅人的行囊已在地上安置停当，两张铺位是用狐皮、鹿皮和獾皮堆在干灯芯草上做成的。

「您不用烟斗？」仲冬问，「那我敬您一剂抵御沼热的药酒。」他从一只皮匣里取出一只银箍瓶子，往角杯里斟了一盅。那是一种加香料的白兰地，阿拉斯泰尔在法兰西南部喝过，此刻它像一股温厚的火淌过血行，驱尽了一日的劳顿，又催出一身适意的困倦。他卸下马靴、外衣和领巾，解开马裤的束带，用一方头巾换下假发，感激地倒身躺在铺上。

那一边，主人已脱得近乎赤条，露出毛毡一般毛茸茸的宽阔胸膛。阿拉斯泰尔留意到，那人身上剩下的贴身衣物是绸的；他还留意到，那粗壮手臂的尽头是一双修长秀美的手，而凡未被天时蚀过的地方，那人的肤色白皙得像一位贵妇。仲冬抽完烟斗，弓身坐在毛皮中间，目光定定地锁着年轻人。那双眼睛里有一种催眠的磁力，把睡意迟滞住了，逼得阿拉斯泰尔开口。何况，火边那支小调，还在他耳中嗡嗡作响。
===Original===
“Who told you my name?” he asked.

“That were too long a tale. Suffice it to say that I knew of your coming, and that long before Banbury you entered the orbit of my knowledge. Nay, sir, I can tell you also your errand, and I warn you that you will fail. You are about to beat at a barred and bolted door.”

“I must think you mistaken.”

“For your youth’s sake, I would that I were. Consider, sir. You come from the North to bid a great man risk his all on a wild hazard. What can you, who have all your days been an adventurer, know of the dragging weight of an ordered life and broad lands and a noble house? The rich man of old turned away sorrowful from Christ because he had great possessions! Think you that the rich man nowadays will be inclined to follow your boyish piping?”

===Chinese===
「谁把我的名字告诉了您？」他问。

「那说来话长。只消一句：您的到来，我早已知晓；远远在班伯里之前，您已进入我知识的轨道。不，先生，我连您的使命也说得出，并且我警告您：您必失败。您是要去叩一扇上了闩、落了锁的门。」

「我只得当您看错了。」

「为了您的青春，我倒巴望是我错了。想想吧，先生。您从北方来，是要请一位大人物把身家性命整个押在一场孤注一掷的豪赌上。您一生到头都是个冒险家，哪里懂得那规整生活、广土大宅、高门华第拖沓沉坠的分量？古时的富人，因为家业太大，忧忧愁愁地离了基督！您以为今日的富人，倒肯跟着您那少年人的笛声走么？」

===Original===
Alastair, eager to hear more but mindful of caution, finessed.

“I had heard better reports of his Grace of Beaufort,” he said.

The brown eyes regarded him quizzically. “I did not speak of the Duke, but of Lord Cornbury.”

The young man exclaimed. “But I summon him in the name of loyalty and religion.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔急欲多听，又记着谨慎，便使了个虚招。

「我听到的，可是博福特公爵大人（his Grace of Beaufort）更好的口碑。」

一双棕色的眼睛，谑意盈盈地望着他。「我说的不是公爵，是科恩伯里勋爵。」

年轻人失声道：「可是，我是以忠义和宗教的名义召他前来的啊。」
===Original===
“Gallant words. But I would remind you that loyalty and religion have many meanings, and self-interest is a skilled interpreter.”

“Our Prince has already done enough to convince even self-interest.”

“Not so. You have for a moment conquered Scotland, but you will not hold it, for it is written in nature that Highlands will never for long control Lowlands. England you have not touched and will never move. The great men have too much to lose and the plain folk are careless about the whole quarrel. They know nothing of your young Prince except that he is half foreigner and whole Papist, and has for his army a mob of breechless mountainers. You can win only by enlisting Old England, and Old England has forgotten you.”

“Let her but remain neutral, and we will beat the Hanoverian’s soldiers.”

“Maybe. But to clinch victory you must persuade the grandees of this realm, and in that I think you will fail. You are Johnnie Armstrong and the King. ‘To seek het water beneath cauld ice, surely it is a great follie.’ And, like Johnnie, the time will come for you to say good night.”

===Chinese===
「好豪迈的言辞。可我提醒您，忠义和宗教各有各的讲法，而自利，是解释它们的老手。」

「我们的王子殿下已建下大功，足以折服哪怕自利之心。」

「不然。你们一时征服了苏格兰，却守不住它，因为这是写定在天地间的道理：高地永远管不长低地。英格兰，你们未尝触动，也永远撼不动。大人物身家的牵累太重，小百姓对这整桩争端漠不关心。对你们那位年轻的王子，他们只知道他半个是外国人，十足是天主教徒，麾下的军队是一群没穿裤子的山民。你们要赢，唯有把老英格兰（Old England）拉进来，而老英格兰早把你们忘了。」

「只消她袖手旁观，我们就打得赢汉诺威（Hanoverian）的兵。」

「也许。可要锁定胜局，您还须说动这个国度的巨宦显贵，而这一层，我看您必败。您就是那约翰尼·阿姆斯特朗（Johnnie Armstrong），跟您对局的是国王。『到冷冰底下去寻热水，千真万确是天大的愚行。』而且，同约翰尼一样，您道晚安的时辰，早晚也要来。」

===Original===
“What manner of man are you, who speak like an oracle? You are gentle born?”

“I am gentle born, but I have long since forfeited my heritage. Call me Ulysses, who has seen all the world’s cities and men, and has at length returned to Ithaca. I am a dweller in Old England.”

“That explains little.”

“Nay, it explains all. There is an Old England which has outlived Roman and Saxon and Dane and Norman and will outlast the Hanoverian. It has seen priest turn to presbyter and presbyter to parson and has only smiled. It is the land of the edge of moorlands and the rims of forests and the twilight before dawn, and strange knowledge still dwells in it. Lords and Parliament-men bustle about, but the dust of their coaches stops at the roadside hedges, and they do not see the quiet eyes watching them at the fords. Those eyes are their masters, young sir. I am gentle born, as you guess, and have been in my day scholar and soldier, but now my companions are the moor-men and the purley-men and the hill-shepherds and the raggle-taggle gypsies. And I am wholly content, for my calling is philosophy. I stand aside in life, and strike no blows and make no bargain, but I learn that which is hid from others.”

===Chinese===
「您究竟是个什么人，说起话来像神谕一般？您是世家出身？」

「我是世家出身，只是早已把世业丢尽了。叫我尤利西斯（Ulysses）吧——看遍了世上所有的城邦与人，末了回到伊萨卡（Ithaca）。我是老英格兰的一个居民。」

「这可说明不了什么。」

「不然，这说明了全部。有一个老英格兰，它送走了罗马人、撒克逊人、丹麦人和诺曼人，也终将送走汉诺威人。它眼看祭司变成长老、长老又变成牧师，只微微一笑。它是荒原边缘与森林外缘的土地，是破晓之前的微明，奇诡的知识至今栖居其中。勋贵们和国会议员们熙来攘往，可他们车马扬起的尘土，到路边的树篱便止了步，他们看不见渡口边静静注视着他们的眼睛。那些眼睛才是他们的主人，年轻的先生。正如您猜的，我是世家出身，当年做过学者、当过兵，可如今我的同侪，是沼地人、林缘人、山野牧羊人，和那些衣衫褴褛的吉卜赛人。而我全然自足，因为我的使命是哲学。我在人生之侧袖手而立，不挥一拳，不做一桩买卖，只学旁人无缘得见的隐秘。」
===Original===
Alastair stirred impatiently.

“You are not above forty,” he said. “You have health and wits and spirit. Great God, man, have you no cause or leader to fight for? Have you no honest ambition to fulfil before you vanish into the dark?”

“None. You and I are at opposite poles of mind. You are drunken with youth and ardent to strike a blow for a dozen loves. You value life, but you will surrender it joyfully for a whimsy of honour. You travel with a huge baggage of ambitions and loyalties. For me, I make it my business to travel light, caring nothing for King or party or church. As I told you, I and my like are the Naked Men.”

Alastair’s eyes were drooping.

“Have you no loyalties?” he asked sleepily.

The answer wove itself into his first dream. “I have the loyalties of Old England.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔不耐地动了动身子。

「您还不过四十岁，」他说，「您有康健，有头脑，有血性。伟大的上帝啊，阁下，您就没有一桩可效忠的事业，一位可效命的领袖？您就没有一点正当的抱负，要趁没入黑暗之前一偿夙愿？」

「没有。您和我的心智，居于两极。您沉醉于青春，热衷于为一打心爱之事挥拳效力。您珍重性命，却又会为了一念之荣誉，欢欢喜喜地舍出去。您携着满载雄心与忠义的重行李赶路。至于我，我以轻装上路为本分，于国王、党派、教会，一概无所萦怀。我告诉过您，我等同类，是赤身人。」

阿拉斯泰尔的眼皮已沉沉垂下。

「您就没有忠义么？」他睡意朦胧地问。

那回答，织进了他的头一个梦里。「我有老英格兰的忠义。」

===Original===
When Alastair awoke he found his boots cleaned from the mud of yesterday, and his coat well brushed and folded. The moor-men had gone off to their fowling, and the two were alone in the clearing, on which had closed down a dense October fog. They breakfasted off a flagon of beer and a broiled wild-duck, which Midwinter cooked on a little fire. He had resumed his coarse leather garments, and looked like some giant gnome as he squatted at his task. But daytime had taken from him the odd glamour of the past night. He now seemed only a thickset countryman﻿—a horse-doctor or a small yeoman.

The boy Zerry appeared with the horse, which had been skilfully groomed, and Midwinter led the young man to the Roman causeway.

“It is a clear road to Oddington,” he told him, “where you can cross the river by the hurdle bridge. Keep the bells of Woodeaton that we call the Flageolets on your left hand﻿—they will be ringing for St. Luke’s morn. Presently you will come to the Stratford road, which will bring you to Enstone and the fringe of Wychwood forest. You will be at Cornbury long before the dinner-hour.”

When Alastair was in the saddle, the other held out his hand.

“I have a liking for you, and would fain serve you. You will not be advised by me but will go your own proud road. God prosper you, young sir. But if it so be that you should lose your fine baggage and need a helper, then I have this word for you. Find an alehouse which, whatever its sign, has an open eye painted beneath it, or a crossroads with a tuft of broom tied to the signpost. Whistle there the catch I taught you last night, and maybe the Naked Men will come to your aid.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔醒来时，发现马靴已擦净了昨日的泥污，外衣也刷得干干净净、叠得整整齐齐。沼地人打水禽去了，两人独处在空地上，十月的浓雾已四合而下。他们的早饭是一壶麦酒和一只炙烤的野鸭，仲冬在一小堆火上烹治。他已换回粗陋的皮衣，蹲在活计跟前，像个巨人样的地精。但白昼已从他身上褪去了昨夜那股诡奇的魔力。此刻他看上去，不过是个粗壮的乡下人——一个医马的大夫，或者个小小的自耕农。

少年泽里牵着马来了，那马已给精心刷洗过，仲冬把年轻人领到罗马堤道上。

「去奥丁顿这条路走得，」他告诉他，「到了那儿，有枝排桥可以过河。把伍德伊顿（Woodeaton）的钟——我们管它叫『哨笛』（the Flageolets）——留在您的左手，它们要为圣路加节（St. Luke's morn）的清晨鸣钟呢。走一程您便会撞上斯特拉特福德大道，它会带您到恩斯通（Enstone）和威奇伍德森林（Wychwood forest）的边上。不到正餐的钟点，您就到科恩伯里了。」

阿拉斯泰尔上了鞍，对方伸出手来。

「我喜欢您，也愿效一点微劳。您不会听我的劝，您要走您自己那条骄傲的路。愿上帝眷佑您，年轻的先生。但倘若您当真丢了您那体面的行装，需要个帮手，那么我留给您这一句话：寻一家酒馆，不管它挂的什么字号，招牌底下画着一只睁开的眼睛；或寻一个十字路口，路标上系着一束金雀花。在那里，吹起我昨夜教您的那支小调，赤身人兴许就会来搭救您。」
