## II In Which a Nobleman Is Perplexed / 二、一位贵族为之踌躇

===Original===
By midday Alastair, riding at leisure, had crossed the first downs of Cotswold and dropped upon the little town of Charlbury, drowsing by Evenlode in a warm October noon. He had left the fog of morning behind in the Cherwell valley, the gale of the previous day had died, and the second summer of St. Luke lay soft on the countryside. In the benign weather the events of the night before seemed a fantastic dream. No mystery could lurk in this land of hedgerows and fat pastures; and the figure of Midwinter grew as absurd in his recollection as the trolls that trouble an indifferent sleeper. But a vague irritation remained. The fellow had preached a cowardly apathy towards all that a gentleman held dear. In the rebound the young man’s ardour flamed high; he would carve with his sword and his wits a road to power, and make a surly world acknowledge him. Unselfish aims likewise filled his mind﻿—a throne for his Prince, power for Clan Gillian, pride for his land, and for his friends riches and love.

In Charlbury he selected his inn, the Wheatsheaf, had his horse fed and rubbed down, drank a tankard of ale, rid himself of the dust of the roads, and deposited his baggage. A decorous and inconspicuous figure, in his chocolate coat and green velvet waistcoat with a plain dark hat of three cocks, the servants of the inn were at once civil and incurious. He questioned the landlord about the Forest of Wychwood, as if his errand lay with one of the rangers, and was given a medley of information in a speech which had the slurred “*s*’s” and the burred “*r*’s” of Gloucestershire. There was the Honourable Mr. Baptist Leveson-Gower, at the Rangers’ Lodge, and Robert Lee at the Burford Lawn Lodge, and Jack Blackstone, him they called Chuffle Jack, at the Thatched Lodge, and likewise the Verderers, Peg Lee and Bob Jenkinson. He assumed that his guest’s business lay with Mr. Leveson-Gower, and Alastair did not undeceive him, but asked casually where lay Cornbury. The landlord took him by the arm, and pointed beyond the stream to the tree-clad hills. “Over the river, sir, by the road that turns right-handed at the foot of the street. You passes the gate on your way to Rangers’ Lodge. His Lordship be in residence, and entertains high quality. His lady sister, the Scotch Duchess, arrived two days back, and there’s been post-chaises and coaches going to and fro all week.”

Alastair remounted his horse in some disquiet, for a houseful of great folks seemed to make but a poor setting for urgent and secret conclaves. By a stone bridge he crossed the Evenlode which foamed in spate, the first free-running stream he had seen since he left the North, and passed through massive iron gates between white lodges built in Charles the Second’s day. He found himself in an avenue of chestnuts and young limes, flanked by the boles of great beeches, which stretched magnificently up the slopes of a hill. In the centre was a gravelled road for coaches, but on either side lay broad belts of turf strewn with nuts and fallen leaves.﻿ ﻿… His assurance began to fail, for he remembered Midwinter’s words on the Moor. The place was a vast embattled fortress of ease, and how would a messenger fare here who brought a summons to hazard all? In his own country a gentleman’s house was a bare stone tower, looking out on moor or sea, with a huddle of hovels round the door. To such dwellings men sat loose, as to a tent in a campaign. But the ordered amenities of such a mansion as this﻿—the decent town at the gates richer than a city of Scotland, the acres of policies that warded the house from the vulgar eye, the secular trees, the air of long-descended peace﻿—struck a chill to his hopes. What did a kestrel in the home of peacocks?

===Chinese===
晌午时分，阿拉斯泰尔（Alastair）策马徐行，已翻过科茨沃尔德（Cotswold）头一带的丘原，下到查尔伯里（Charlbury）小镇——十月温煦的正午，小镇正在埃文洛德河（Evenlode）畔打着盹。他已把清晨的雾气留在身后的切尔韦尔（Cherwell）河谷，前一日的大风止息了，圣路加节的小阳春温柔地铺满原野。在这样和暖的天气里，昨夜种种恍如一场荒诞的梦。在这片绿篱纵横、牧草丰腴的土地上，哪里藏得住什么秘密；“仲冬”的身影在他记忆里也变得荒唐起来，如同搅扰浅睡之人的山魈。可心头仍残留着一缕说不清的懊恼。那家伙鼓吹的，是对绅士所珍视的一切抱一种怯懦的冷漠。年轻人的一腔热忱反倒烈焰般腾起：他要凭手中的剑与胸中的才智劈出一条通往权势的路，教这乖戾的世间承认他。他心里同样盈满无私的抱负——为他的王子赢得王座，为吉利安氏族（Clan Gillian）赢得权势，为他的故土挣得荣光，为他的朋友谋来财富与爱情。

在查尔伯里，他拣定了客栈——麦捆客栈（Wheatsheaf），叫人喂马刷马，自己灌下一大杯麦酒，洗去一路风尘，把行李寄放妥当。他一身巧克力色外套、绿丝绒马甲，头上是顶素净的三翘檐黑帽，看去体面而不惹眼，客栈仆役待他客客气气，也懒得多问。他向店家打听威奇伍德森林（Forest of Wychwood），仿佛正事是寻访哪位看林人，于是得了满耳朵杂七杂八的消息，那口音带着格洛斯特郡含混的“s”与滚喉的“r”：护林官屋里有位巴普蒂斯特·莱维森-高尔先生（the Honourable Mr. Baptist Leveson-Gower），伯福德草坪屋有位罗伯特·李（Robert Lee），茅顶屋有个杰克·布莱克斯通（Jack Blackstone）——人都管他叫“呼噜杰克”——另有几位林务官，佩格·李（Peg Lee）和鲍勃·詹金森（Bob Jenkinson）。店家认定这位客人的事体必与莱维森-高尔先生相干，阿拉斯泰尔也不点破，只随口问一句科恩伯里（Cornbury）在何处。店家挽起他的手臂，指着河对岸林木葱茏的群山。“过了河，先生，顺街到底往右手一拐便是。去护林官屋打那扇门前经过。老爷正在府里，宴着高贵客人呢。他那位胞姐、苏格兰来的公爵夫人，前两天也到了，这一礼拜驿车马车来来去去没个消停。”

阿拉斯泰尔重新上马，心里颇有些忐忑——满宅子的达官贵人，岂是紧急密议的合适布景。他从一道石桥跨过正逢汛期的埃文洛德河，白沫翻涌——这是他离开北方以来见到的第一条自在奔流的河——又穿过查理二世年间所筑的两座白色门房之间那扇厚重的铁门。眼前是一条栗树与幼椴夹峙的林荫道，两侧衬着高大山毛榉的躯干，气派堂皇地沿山坡逶迤而上。中央是供马车行走的砾石道，两旁铺开宽阔的草带，散落着坚果与落叶。……他的底气渐渐泄了，因为想起“仲冬”在荒原上说过的话。这地方是一座壁垒森严的安乐堡垒，一个奉命召人孤注一掷的信使，在此地能有什么指望？在他自己的家乡，绅士的宅子不过一座光秃秃的石塔，面朝荒原或大海，门口挤着几间破屋。人对那样的居所本就无所留恋，如同行军途中的一顶帐篷。可这样一座巨宅的井然惬意——门房外那座体面镇子，富庶赛过苏格兰的任何城市；大片园囿将宅子与俗人的目光隔开；参天古木；世泽绵长的安宁气象——却教他的希望凉了半截。红隼飞进孔雀的家门，算怎么一回事呢？

===Original===
At the summit of the hill the road passed beneath an archway into a courtyard; but here masons were at work and Alastair turned to the left, in doubt about the proper entrance. Fifty yards brought him in sight of a corner of the house and into a pleasance bright with late flowers, from which a park fell away into a shallow vale. There in front of him was a group of people walking on the stone of the terrace.

He was observed, and from the party a gentleman came forward, while the others turned their backs and continued their stroll. The gentleman was in the thirties, a slim figure a little bent in the shoulders, wearing his own hair, which was of a rich brown, and dressed very plainly in a country suit of green. He advanced with friendly peering eyes, and Alastair, who had dismounted, recognised the master of the house from a miniature he had seen in M. de Tremouille’s hands.

“Have I the honour to address Lord Cornbury?” he asked.

The other bowed, smiling, and his shortsighted eyes looked past the young man, and appraised his horse.

“My lord, I have a letter from M. de Tremouille.”

===Chinese===
到了山顶，道路穿过一道拱门进入庭院；但那里石匠正在施工，阿拉斯泰尔便折向左边，一时拿不准正门何在。行不到五十码，屋宇的一角已入眼帘，他也走进一座犹自繁花照眼的园囿，园外的鹿苑缓缓沉入一道浅谷。就在他面前，一小群人正沿着石砌平台信步。

人家瞧见了他，人群中走出一位绅士，余者转过身去，照旧散步。那绅士三十开外，身形清瘦，微微佝肩，不戴假发，一头浓棕色的天然头发，一身绿色乡居装束，朴素至极。他迎上前来，一双近视的眼睛友善地眯望着；下了马的阿拉斯泰尔认出这正是宅子的主人——他在德·特雷穆耶先生（M. de Tremouille）手里见过他的袖珍画像。

“我是否有幸在向科恩伯里勋爵说话？”他问。

对方含笑欠身，一双近视眼却越过年轻人，端详起他的坐骑来。

“大人，我带来德·特雷穆耶先生的一封信。”
===Original===
Lord Cornbury took the letter, and, walking a few paces to a clump of trees, read it carefully twice. He turned to Alastair with a face in which embarrassment strove with his natural kindliness.

“Any friend of M. de Tremouille’s is friend of mine, Captain Maclean. Show me how I can serve you. Your baggage is at the inn? It shall be brought here at once, for I would not forgive myself if one recommended to me by so old a friend slept at a public hostelry.”

The young man bowed. “I will not refuse your hospitality, my lord, for I am here to beg an hour of most private conversation. I come not from France, but from the North.”

A curious embarrassment twisted the other’s face.

“You have the word?” he asked in a low voice.

“I am ‘Alcinous,’ of whom I think you have been notified.”

===Chinese===
科恩伯里勋爵接过信，走到几步外一丛树旁，仔仔细细读了两遍。他转向阿拉斯泰尔，脸上为难之色与天生的和善正自相持不下。

“德·特雷穆耶先生的任何朋友，也是我的朋友，麦克莱恩上尉。有什么用得着我的地方，尽管吩咐。行李还在客栈？这就派人取来——故人引荐来的客人竟去宿客店，我断乎不能原谅自己。”

年轻人躬身行礼。“大人的盛情我不敢辞，因为我此来正是求一个时辰的密谈。我不是从法国来，是从北方来。”

一种古怪的窘迫扭动了对方的脸。

“你有口令么？”他低声问。

“我是‘阿尔基努斯’，想必您已奉到知会。”

===Original===
Lord Cornbury strode off a few steps and then came back. “Yes,” he said simply, “I have been notified. I expected you a month back. But let me tell you, sir, you have arrived in a curst inconvenient hour. This house is full of Whiggish company. There is my sister Queensberry, and there is Mr. Murray, His Majesty’s Solicitor.﻿ ﻿… Nay, perhaps the company is the better cloak for you. I will give you your private hour after supper. Meantime you are Captain Maclean﻿—of Lee’s Regiment, I think, in King Louis’ service﻿—and you have come from Paris from Paul de Tremouille on a matter of certain gems in my collection that he would purchase for the Duc de Bouillon. You are satisfied you can play that part, sir? Not a word of politics. You do not happen to be interested in statecraft, and you have been long an exile from your native country, though you have a natural sentiment for the old line of Kings. Is that clear, sir? Have you sufficient of the arts to pose as a virtuoso?”

Alastair hoped that he had.

“Then let us get the first plunge over. Suffer me to introduce you to the company.”

===Chinese===
科恩伯里勋爵抢行几步，又折了回来。“是的，”他简单地说，“我奉到了知会。一个月前就恭候你了。可是先生，恕我直言，你到得真是个别扭透顶的时辰。这宅子里满是从伦敦来的辉格党客人。舍妹昆斯伯里公爵夫人在此，还有默里先生，国王陛下的副检察长。……也罢，兴许这满座宾客倒正好给你作幌子。晚膳之后，我给你那一小时的密谈。眼下你是麦克莱恩上尉——记得是在李氏团效力，在路易国王麾下——从巴黎来，替保罗·德·特雷穆耶先生办事，为的是他欲代布永公爵（Duc de Bouillon）购置我收藏中的几件珠宝珍玩。这出戏你演得下来么，先生？一个字也不许谈政治。你于治国之道素无兴致，虽则对本国旧王统怀着几分天生的情分，却已离乡流亡多年。清楚了么，先生？你可有一星半点的本事，装扮成一个鉴赏家？”

阿拉斯泰尔但愿自己有。

“那就趁早把这头一跳跳了吧。容我把你引见给诸位。”
===Original===
The sound of their steps on the terrace halted the strollers. A lady turned, and at the sight of the young man her eyebrows lifted. She was a slight figure about the middle size, whose walking clothes followed the new bergère fashion. Save for her huge hooped petticoats, she was the dainty milkmaid, in her flowered chintz, her sleeveless coat, her flat straw hat tied with ribbons of cherry velvet, her cambric apron. A long staff, with ribbons at the crook, proclaimed the shepherdess. She came toward them with a tripping walk, and Alastair marked the delicate bloom of her cheeks, unspoiled by rouge, the flash of white teeth as she smiled, the limpid depth of her great childlike eyes. His memory told him that the Duchess had passed her fortieth year, but his eyes saw a girl in her teens, a Flora of spring whose summer had not begun.

“Kitty, I present to you Captain Maclean, a gentleman in the service of His Majesty of France. He has come to me on a mission from Paul de Tremouille﻿—a mission of the arts.”

The lady held out a hand. “Are you by any happy chance a poet, sir?”

“I have made verses, madam, as young men do, but I halt far short of poetry.”

“The inspiration may come. I had hoped that Harry would provide me with a new poet. For you must know, sir, that I have lost all my poets. Mr. Prior, Mr. Gay, Mr. Pope﻿—they have all been gathered to the shades. I have no one now to make me verses.”

===Chinese===
两人踏上平台，脚步声惊得散步者驻足。一位夫人转过身来，一见年轻人便扬起了眉。她身量适中而纤秀，行装是一派时新的牧羊女式样。除了那袭撑得极大的箍环衬裙，她俨然便是俏丽的挤奶姑娘：印花棉布长裙，无袖短外衣，平顶草帽系着樱桃红丝绒缎带，细白布围裙。一柄长杖，弯头垂着缎带，更点明牧羊女的身份。她碎步盈盈地向他们走来，阿拉斯泰尔看清她双颊娇嫩的红晕——并非脂粉之功——含笑时一口皓齿一闪，还有那双大眼睛里孩童般的澄澈幽深。记忆告诉他，公爵夫人已年逾四旬；他眼前看见的却是个二八年华的少女，一位春日里的花神芙罗拉，夏天还尚未开始。

“基蒂，我给你引见麦克莱恩上尉，法兰西国王陛下麾下的一位绅士。他奉保罗·德·特雷穆耶先生之命来见我——一桩风雅差事。”

夫人伸出一只手。“先生该不会凑巧是位诗人吧？”

“夫人，年轻人总免不了涂几句诗，我却远远够不上诗人二字。”

“兴许哪天灵感就来了呢。我本指望哈利会给我物色一位新诗人。先生有所不知，我的诗人们已凋零殆尽。普赖尔先生、盖伊先生、蒲柏先生——都已归了黄泉。如今再没有人给我写诗了。”

===Original===
“If your Grace will pardon me, your charms can never lack a singer.”

“La, la! The singers are as dry as a ditch in midsummer. They sigh and gloom and write doleful letters in prose. I have to fly to Paris to find a well-turned sonnet.﻿ ﻿… Here we are so sage and dutiful and civically minded. Mary thinks only of her lovers, and Mr. Murray of his lawsuits, and Mr. Kyd of his mortgage deeds, and Kit Lacy of fat cattle﻿—nay, I do not think that Kit’s mind soars even to that height.”

“I protest, madam,” began a handsome sheepish young gentleman behind her, but the Duchess cut him short.

“Harry!” she cried, “we are all Scotch here﻿—all but you and Kit, and to be Scotch nowadays is to be suspect. Let us plot treason. The King’s Solicitor cannot pursue us, for he will be *criminis particeps*.”

Mr. Murray, a small man with a noble head and features so exquisitely moulded that at first sight most men distrusted him, pointed to an inscription cut on the entablature of the house.

“*Deus haec nobis otia fecit*,” he read, in a voice whose every tone was clear as the note of a bell. “We dare not offend the genius loci, and outrage that plain commandment.”

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，夫人，您的风姿何愁没有歌者。”

“哎呀呀！如今的歌者干得像盛夏的沟渠。他们不是唉声叹气，便是愁眉苦脸，写起信来净是散文体的哭腔。想觅一首像样的十四行诗，也只得飞一趟巴黎。……瞧咱们这儿，个个老成持重、恪守本分，满脑子的公益。玛丽心里只有她的情人，默里先生只有他的官司，基德先生只有他的抵押契据，基特·莱西只有他的肥牛——不，我只怕基特的心思连那点高度也飞不到。”

“夫人，我可要抗辩了，”她身后一位俊俏而讷讷的年轻绅士开口道，公爵夫人却不容他说完。

“哈利！”她嚷道，“咱们这儿全是苏格兰人——只除了你和基特，而如今做苏格兰人，就等于做个嫌疑犯。来密谋叛逆吧。国王的副检察长追究不得咱们，因为他自己便是*criminis particeps*——同案之犯。”

默里先生生得矮小，头颅却贵气逼人，五官雕琢得太过精巧，以致初见之人多怀戒心。他指着宅邸檐板上镌刻的一行铭文。

“*Deus haec nobis otia fecit*——‘上帝赐了我们这份安闲’，”他念道，嗓音字字清亮如钟。“此地之神明不可冒犯，那条明白的诫命更不可亵渎。”
===Original===
“But treason is not business.”

“It is apt to be the most troublous kind of business, madam.”

“Then Kit shall show me the grottos.” She put an arm in the young man’s, the other in the young girl’s, and forced them to a pace which was ill suited to his high new hunting boots. Alastair was formally introduced to the two men remaining, and had the chance of observing the one whom the Duchess had called Mr. Kyd. He had the look of a country squire, tall, heavily built and deeply tanned by the sun. He had brown eyes, which regarded the world with a curious steadiness, and a mouth the corners of which were lifted in a perpetual readiness for laughter. Rarely had Alastair seen a more jovial and kindly face, which was yet redeemed from the commonplace by the straight thoughtful brows and the square cleft jaw. When the man spoke it was in the broad accents of the Scotch lowlands, though his words and phrases were those of the South. Lord Cornbury walked with Mr. Murray, and the other ranged himself beside Alastair.

===Chinese===
“可叛逆不算正经事呀。”

“夫人，它偏偏最容易成为最伤脑筋的那一种正经事。”

“那就叫基特领我看洞窟去吧。”她一手挽住那年轻人，一手挽住那少女，硬拽着两人迈开一种步子，可苦了他那双崭新的高筒猎靴。阿拉斯泰尔被正式引见给留下的两位男士，得以细看公爵夫人唤作基德先生的那一位。此人一副乡绅相貌，高个子，身板厚实，脸膛让太阳晒得黝黑。一双棕色眼睛看人看世，稳得出奇；嘴角永远微微上翘，随时预备着发笑。这样一张乐呵呵的善脸，阿拉斯泰尔平生少见，而那两道挺直的思虑之眉与方正有沟的下巴，又使它免于流于平庸。此人开口时，是低地苏格兰的宽厚口音，遣词用句却全是南边的做派。科恩伯里勋爵与默里先生同行，这一位便挨到了阿拉斯泰尔身边。

===Original===
“A pleasant habitation, you will doubtless be observing, sir. Since you’re from France you may have seen houses as grand, but there’s not the like of it in our poor kingdom of Scotland. In the Merse, which is my countryside, they stick the kitchen-midden up against the dining-room window, and their notion of a pleasance is a wheen grosart bushes and gillyflowers sore scarted by hens.”

Alastair looked round the flowery quincunx and the trim borders where a peacock was strutting amid late roses.

“I think I would tire of it. Give me a sea loch and the heather and a burn among birchwoods.”

“True, true, a man’s heart is in his calf-country. We Scots are like Ulysses, and not truly at home in Phaeacia.” He spoke the last word with the slightest lift of his eyebrows, as if signalling to the other that he was aware of his position. “For myself,” he continued, “I’m aye remembering sweet Argos, which in my case is the inconsiderable dwelling of Greyhouses in a Lammermoor glen. My business takes me up and down this land of England, and I tell you, sir, I wouldn’t change my crow-step gables for all the mansions ever biggit. It’s a queer quirk in us mercantile folk.”

===Chinese===
“‘好一处安乐窝’，先生，您心里准是这么念叨的。您从法国来，兴许见过一般气派的宅子，可我们苏格兰那可怜的王国里，再没有第二座这样的了。俺们默斯乡下——那便是我的老家——人人把泔水堆抵着饭厅窗户，他们心目的园子哩，不过是几丛醋栗、几畦香紫罗兰，还叫母鸡刨得七零八落。”

阿拉斯泰尔环顾这梅花形的花圃与齐整的花境——一只孔雀正在晚玫瑰间开屏踱步。

“我想我迟早要生腻的。还是给我一处海湾湖、一片石楠、一道白桦林间的溪流吧。”

“正是，正是，人的心总拴在生身之地。我们苏格兰人就像尤利西斯，在费阿西亚终究不是真在家。”说到末一个词，他眉毛微微一挑，仿佛向对方示意：他知道对方的身份底细。“至于我自己，”他接着说，“我心里总惦着甜蜜的阿尔戈斯——在我，便是拉默缪尔山谷里那座不起眼的宅子格雷豪斯。我的营生驱着我在这英格兰上上下下地跑，可我告诉您，先生，把天下盖起的大宅都堆到我面前，我也不换我那阶梯山墙的老屋。我们做买卖的人，就有这么一股子拗脾气。”
===Original===
“You travel much?”

“I needs must, when I’m the principal doer of the Duke of Queensberry. My father was man of business to auld Duke James, and I heired the job with Duke Charles. If you serve a mighty prince, who is a duke and marquis in two kingdoms and has lands and messuages to conform, you’re not much off the road. Horses’ iron and shoe-leather are cheap in that service. But my pleasure is at home, where I can read my Horace and crack with my friends and catch trout in the Whitader.”

Mr. Kyd’s honest countenance and frank geniality might have led to confidences on Alastair’s part, but at the moment Lord Cornbury rejoined them with word that dinner would be served in half an hour. As they entered the house, Alastair found himself beside his host and well behind the others.

===Chinese===
“您常出门在外？”

“没法子呀，谁叫我是昆斯伯里公爵（Duke of Queensberry）的头号办事人呢。家父当年是老公爵詹姆斯的账房总管，这份差事传到我手里，又伺候了公爵查尔斯。伺候这样一位大贵人——在两个王国里身兼公爵侯爵，名下的田产宅院又得相称——就少不得在路上跑。在那份差事里，马蹄铁和鞋底皮都算不得什么稀罕开销。我的乐子却在家里：读读贺拉斯，跟朋友们闲磕牙，到惠塔德河里钓钓鳟鱼。”

基德先生一脸忠厚，坦率和气，本足以引得阿拉斯泰尔吐露几分心腹之言，偏在此时，科恩伯里勋爵回来了，说半个钟点后便开席。进屋时，阿拉斯泰尔发觉自己正走在主人身侧，远远落在众人后头。

===Original===
“Who is this Mr. Kyd?” he whispered. “He mentioned Phaeacia, as if he knew my character.”

Lord Cornbury’s face wore an anxious look. “He is my brother Queensberry’s agent. But he is also one of you. You must know of him. He is ‘Menelaus.’ ”

Alastair shook his head. “I landed from France only three weeks back, and know little of Mr. Secretary Murray’s plans.”

“Well, you will hear more of him. He is now on his way to Badminton, for he is said to have Beaufort’s ear. His connection with my brother is a good shield. Lord! how I hate all this business of go-betweens and midnight conclaves!” He looked at his companion with a face so full of a quaint perplexity that Alastair could not forbear to laugh.

“We must creep before we can fly, my lord, in the most honest cause. But our wings are fledging well.”

===Chinese===
“这位基德先生是什么人？”他低声问。“他提起费阿西亚，倒像识得我的身份。”

科恩伯里勋爵面有忧色。“他是我内兄昆斯伯里公爵的代理人。可他也是你们一边的人。你总该听说过他。他就是‘墨涅拉俄斯’。”

阿拉斯泰尔摇头。“我三星期前才从法国登陆，于默里大臣的计划所知甚少。”

“往后你自会常听到他。他眼下正在去巴德明顿的路上，据说博福特公爵（Duke of Beaufort）对他言听计从。他与我家内兄这层关系，正是一面好盾牌。老天！这等穿针引线、深夜密议的勾当，我打心眼里厌恶！”他望着同伴，一脸古怪的为难，惹得阿拉斯泰尔忍俊不禁。

“大人，哪怕是最光明正大的事业，也得先学爬再学飞。好在我们的翅膀正日渐丰满。”
===Original===
A footman led him to his room, which was in the old part of the house called the Leicester Wing, allotted to him, he guessed, because of its remoteness. His baggage had been brought from the inn, and a porcelain bath filled with hot water stood on the floor. He shaved, but otherwise made no more than a traveller’s toilet, changing his boots for silk stockings and buckled shoes, and his bob for an ample tie-wig. The mirror showed a man not yet thirty, with small sharp features, high cheek bones, and a reddish tinge in skin and eyebrows. The eyes were of a clear, choleric blue, and the face, which was almost feminine in its contours, was made manly by a certain ruggedness and fire in its regard. His hands and feet were curiously small for one with so deep a chest and sinewy limbs. He was neat and precise in person and movement, a little finical at first sight, till the observer caught his quick ardent gaze. A passionate friend, that observer would have pronounced him, and a most mischievous and restless enemy.

His Highland boyhood and foreign journeyings had not prepared him for the suave perfection of an English house. The hall, paved with squares of black and white marble, was hung with full-length pictures of the Hyde and Danvers families, and the great figures of the Civil War. The party assembled beneath them was a motley of gay colours﻿—the Duchess in a gown of sky-blue above rose-pink petticoats; the young girl, whose name was Lady Mary Capell, all in green like a dryad; Mr. Murray wore black velvet with a fuller wig than was the fashion of the moment; while Sir Christopher Lacy had donned the blue velvet and ermine collar of the Duke of Beaufort’s Hunt, a garb in which its members were popularly believed to sleep. Mr. Kyd had contented himself with a flowered waistcoat, a plum-coloured coat and saffron stockings. Only the host was in sad colours, and, as he alone wore his natural hair, he presented a meagre and dejected figure in the flamboyant company.

The Duchess talked like a brook.

“Harry must show you the Vandykes,” she told Mr. Murray. “He knows the age and tale of everyone as I know my boys’ birthdays. I wish he would sell them, for they make me feel small and dingy. Look at them! We are no better than valets-de-chambre in their presence.”

===Chinese===
一名男仆引他去他的房间，在宅子的旧部——唤作莱斯特翼（Leicester Wing）的一隅，他猜想，正因地处偏僻才拨给了他。行李已从客栈送到，地板上放着一只盛满热水的瓷浴盆。他刮了脸，其余不过一番旅人梳洗：脱下马靴，换上丝袜与带扣便鞋，摘了那顶短发式假发，换上一顶蓬大的系带假发。镜中人不满三十，眉眼小巧而锐利，颧骨高高，皮肤眉毛都透着淡淡的红。一双眼睛是清亮的、火爆的蓝色；那脸轮廓几乎有几分女子气，全凭眼神里的一股粗粝与火性，才立起男儿气概。以他那样深厚的胸膛和精健的四肢，手脚却小得出奇。他周身上下、一举一动都干净利落，乍看近乎讲究过分，直到观者撞上他迅疾热切的目光。这样的观者必会断言：此人是个可以倾心相与的热血朋友，也是个最不安分、最难对付的仇敌。

他在高地的童年与海外的游历，都未曾教他领教过英格兰宅邸这般熨帖的完美。大厅以黑白大理石方砖铺地，四壁挂着海德、丹弗斯两族的全幅画像，以及内战诸位大家的巨制。画像之下聚着的这一群人五彩缤纷——公爵夫人一袭天蓝长裙，衬着玫瑰粉的衬裙；那位少女名唤玛丽·卡佩尔小姐（Lady Mary Capell），通身绿色，宛如林间仙子；默里先生一身黑丝绒，假发比时下风行的样式更为丰茂；克里斯托弗·莱西爵士则穿上了博福特公爵猎队的蓝丝绒貂皮领制服——世人向来相信，队员连睡觉也不肯脱下这行头。基德先生只穿一件印花马甲、梅子色外套，配番红花色长袜。唯有主人一身素净颜色，又唯有他不着假发、戴自己的天然头发，在这一团繁华里显得清瘦而颓唐。

公爵夫人说起话来像一条小溪。

“哈利定要领你看那些凡·戴克，”她对默里先生说。“每幅画的年代掌故他都烂熟于心，就像我熟记几个孩子的生日。我倒巴望他把它们卖了，它们衬得我又渺小又灰暗。你瞧瞧！站在它们面前，我们比贴身男仆又强得了多少。”

===Original===
The majordomo conducted them to dinner, which was served in the new Indian Room. On the walls was a Chinese paper of birds and flowers and flower-hung pagodas; no pictures adorned them, but a number of delicately carved mirrors; and at intervals tall lacquer cabinets glowed on their gilt pedestals. The servants wore purple (“like bishops,” Mr. Kyd whispered), and, since the room looked west, the declining October sun brought out the colours of wall and fabric and set the glasses and decanters shimmering on the polished table. Through the open windows the green slopes of the park lay bathed in light, and a pool of water sparkled in the hollow.

To Alastair, absorbed in his errand, the scene was purely phantasmal. He looked on as at a pretty pageant, heard the ladies’ tinkling laughter, discussed the manège in France at long range with Lord Cornbury, who was a noted horsemaster, answered Lady Mary’s inquiries about French modes as best he could, took wine with the men, had the honour to toast the Duchess Kitty﻿—but did it all in a kind of waking dream. This daintiness and ease were not of that grim world from which he had come, or of that grimmer world which was soon to be.﻿ ﻿… He noticed that no word of politics was breathed; even the Duchess’s chatter was discreet on that point. The ice was clearly too thin, and the most heedless felt the need of wary walking. Here sat the King’s Solicitor, and the wife of a Whig Duke cheek by jowl with two secret messengers bearing names out of Homer, and at the head of the table was one for whom both parties angled. The last seemed to feel the irony, for behind his hospitable gaiety was a sharp edge of care. He would sigh now and then, and pass a thin hand over his forehead. But the others﻿—Mr. Solicitor was discussing Mr. Pope’s “Characters of Women” and quoting unpublished variants. No hint of embarrassment was to be detected in that mellow voice. Was he perhaps, thought Alastair, cognisant of the strange mixture at table, and not disapproving? He was an officer of the Government, but he came of Jacobite stock. Was he not Stormont’s brother?﻿ ﻿… And Mr. Kyd was deep in a discussion about horses with the gentleman in the Beaufort uniform. With every glass of claret the even rosiness of his face deepened, till he bloomed like the God of Wine himself﻿—a Bacchus strictly sober, with very wide-awake eyes.

Then to complete the comedy the catch he had heard on Otmoor began to run in Alastair’s head. “Three naked men we be”﻿—a far cry from this bedecked and cosseted assemblage. He had a moment of suffocation, until he regained his humour. They were all naked under their fine clothes, and for one of them it was his business to do the stripping. He caught Lord Cornbury’s eye and marked its gentle sadness. Was such a man content? Had he the assurance in his soul to listen to one who brought to him not peace but swords?

===Chinese===
大总管引众人入席，晚膳设在新落成的印度厅。四壁糊着中国壁纸，绘满花鸟与繁花垂挂的宝塔；墙上不悬画作，只挂着数面雕工精致的镜子；一座座高漆柜在鎏金座上灼灼放光。仆役一色紫衣（“活像主教”，基德先生耳语道）；此厅朝西，十月西斜的日头照得壁上颜色与织物光泽愈发浓艳，光亮的长桌上杯盏酒樽流光闪烁。敞开的窗外，鹿苑绿坡浸在夕照里，洼地上一泓池水波光潋滟。

在心系使命的阿拉斯泰尔眼里，这一幕恍同幻境。他像看一场华丽庆典似的旁观着：听贵妇们珠玉般的笑声，与科恩伯里勋爵——一位赫赫有名的相马大家——不着边际地谈谈法国的驯马之术，勉力应付玛丽小姐关于巴黎时样的种种垂询，与诸先生举杯，荣幸地为基蒂公爵夫人祝酒——而这一切都做在半梦半醒之间。这份精致安逸，不属于他来的那个严酷世界，也不属于行将到来的那个更严酷的世界。……他留意到席间不闻一句政治；连公爵夫人的闲谈在这方面也极有分寸。冰面显然太薄，再没心没肺的人也觉得须步步留神。这里坐着国王的副检察长，坐着一位辉格公爵的夫人，与两位顶着荷马史诗里名字的秘密信使比肩同席；而首席上坐的，正是两党都在下钩垂钓的那一位。这后一位似乎也觉出了这层讽刺，因为殷勤的欢容背后藏着一道锐利的忧痕。他不时叹息，抬起一只瘦手抚过额头。可是其余诸位——副检察长大人正侃侃而谈蒲柏先生的《妇人性情》，还引诵着未刊的异文，那圆润的嗓音里听不出一丝局促。莫非，阿拉斯泰尔想，他对席间这奇怪的混合心里透亮，而且并不以为忤？他是政府官员，却出身雅各布世家。他不是斯托蒙特勋爵的兄弟么？……基德先生则与那位博福特制服的绅士纵谈马匹，每干一杯红葡萄酒，他脸上匀净的红润便深一层，直红得像酒神本尊——一位滴酒不乱、目光雪亮的巴克斯。

偏在这时，为给这出喜剧再添一笔，他在奥特沼泽（Otmoor）听来的那支小调竟在阿拉斯泰尔脑子里转起来。“咱们是三个赤身露体的汉子”——与眼前这班衣香鬓影、受人娇宠供养的人物，相去何啻天壤。他一时憋闷得几乎透不过气，随即又寻回了幽默。华服底下，人人赤身露体，而其中一人的差使，恰恰便是动手剥衣。他接住科恩伯里勋爵的目光，看出其中温柔的悲意。这样的人，心满意足么？他灵魂里可有那样的把握，去听一个带给他的“不是和平，而是刀剑”的人说话？
===Original===
The late autumn afternoon was bright and mild, with a thin mist rising from the distant stream. The company moved out-of-doors, where on a gravelled walk stood a low carriage drawn by a pair of cream-coloured ponies. A maid brought the Duchess a wide straw hat and driving gloves, and, while the others loitered at the garden door, the lady chose her companion. “*Sa singularité*,” Mr. Murray whispered. “It is young Mr. Walpole’s name for her. But how prettily she plays the rustic!”

“Who takes the air with me?” she cried. “I choose Captain Maclean. He is the newest of you, and can tell me the latest scandal of Versailles.”

It was like an equipage fashioned out of Chelsea porcelain, and as Alastair took his place beside her, with his knees under a driving cloth of embroidered silk, he felt more than ever the sense of taking part in a play. She whipped up the ponies and they trotted out of the wrought-iron gates, which bounded the pleasance, into the wide spaces of the park. Her talk, which at first had been the agreeable prattle of dinner, to which he responded with sufficient ease, changed gradually to interrogatories. With some disquiet he realised that she was drifting towards politics.

===Chinese===
深秋的午后晴和，远处河上浮起薄薄一层雾。众人移步户外，砾石步道旁停着一辆低座马车，驾着两匹乳白色小马。侍女给公爵夫人取来宽边草帽和驾车手套；众人还在园门口流连，夫人已拣定了同车的人。“*Sa singularité*（她那独一份的怪脾气）”，默里先生低声道。“这是小沃波尔先生送她的雅号。瞧她扮乡下女人扮得多俊！”

“谁陪我兜风去？”她喊道。“我挑麦克莱恩上尉。他是你们中间最新的面孔，能给我讲讲凡尔赛最新的风流案。”

这一行车驾，宛如切尔西瓷窑里烧出的一套瓷偶。阿拉斯泰尔在她身旁落座，膝上盖着绣花丝绸的驾车毯，愈发觉得自己是在戏中串演一角。她一扬鞭，小马小跑着出了游苑的铁花门，奔进鹿苑的开阔天地。她的谈锋起初还是席上那类悦耳的絮语，他也应答得颇为自如，渐渐却变成了连珠的盘问。他带着几分不安察觉，话头正往政事上漂去。

===Original===
“What do they think in France of the young man’s taste in womankind?” she asked.

He raised his eyebrows.

“The Prince﻿—Charles Stuart﻿—the Chevalier. What of Jenny Cameron?”

“We heard nothing of her in Paris, madam. You should be the better informed, for he has been some months on British soil.”

“Tush, we hear no truth from the North. But they say that she never leaves him, that she shares his travelling carriage. Is she pretty, I wonder? Dark or fair?”

===Chinese===
“法国人怎么看那位年轻人赏鉴女人的口味？”她问。

他扬了扬眉毛。

“那位王子——查理·斯图亚特——‘骑士’。珍妮·卡梅伦是怎么回事？”

“她在巴黎的事，我们一点风声也没听到，夫人。您这边的消息该灵通些，他登不列颠的土地已有几个月了。”

“呸，北方来的就没一句真话。可人家说她寸步不离他，连出门都是同乘一辆旅行马车。我倒纳闷，她标致么？深色头发还是浅色的？”
===Original===
“That I cannot tell, but, whatever they be, her charms must be mature. I have heard on good authority that she is over forty years old.”

It did not need the Duchess’s merry laughter to tell him that he had been guilty of a bêtise. He blushed furiously.

“La, sir,” she cried, “you are ungallant. That is very much my own age, and the world does not call me matronly. I had thought you a courtier, but I fear﻿—I gravely fear﻿—you are an honest man.”

===Chinese===
“这我说不上。不过无论深浅，她的魅力想必是熟透的那一种。我听得可靠消息，她已年过四十。”

其实用不着公爵夫人那串爽朗的笑来点破，他也已明白自己犯了一桩 *bêtise*——一句失言的蠢话。他臊得满脸通红。

“哎呀，先生，”她叫道，“您可真不怜香惜玉。那与我的年纪也相去无几呢，可世人还不至于叫我半老徐娘。我本当您是个廷臣，如今怕是——大大地怕是——您是个老实人。”

===Original===
They were now on the west side of the park, where a road led downhill past what had once been a quarry, but was now carved into a modish wilderness. The scarps of stone had been fashioned into grottos and towers and fantastic pinnacles; shrubs had been planted to make shapely thickets; springs had been turned to cascades or caught in miniature lakes. The path wound through midget Alps, which were of the same scale and quality as the chaise and the cream ponies and the shepherdess Duchess.

“We call this spot Eden,” she said. “There are many things I would fain ask you, sir, but I remember the consequence of Eve’s inquisitiveness and forbear. The old Eden had a door and beyond that door lay the desert. It is so here.”

They turned a corner by the edge of a small lake and came on a stout palisade which separated the park from Wychwood Forest. Through the high deer-gate Alastair looked on a country the extreme opposite of the enclosed paradise. The stream, which in the park was regulated like a canal, now flowed in rough shallows or spread into morasses. Scrub clothed the slopes, scrub of thorn and hazel and holly, with now and then an ancient oak flinging gnarled arms against the sky. In the bottom were bracken and the withered blooms of heather, where bees still hummed. The eye looked up little glens towards distant ridges to which the blue October haze gave the air of high hills.

===Chinese===
这时车已行到鹿苑西侧，一条道路顺坡而下，经过一处旧日的采石场——如今已凿成一座时新的野趣园。石壁断崖装点成洞窟、塔楼和奇形怪状的尖峰；灌木栽出亭亭如盖的树丛；泉水或引作小瀑，或汇成小小的湖泊。小径在迷你阿尔卑斯之间蜿蜒——与这辆马车、这对乳色小马、这位牧羊女公爵夫人，正是一个尺码、一种品级。

“我们管这地方叫伊甸园，”她说。“我有许多话想请教您，先生，可我记得夏娃多事的报应，只好忍住。老伊甸园有一道门，门外便是荒漠。此地也是一样。”

他们在小湖边转过一隅，迎面一道结实的栅栏，将鹿苑与威奇伍德森林隔开。透过高大的鹿门，阿拉斯泰尔望见的，正是这围合乐园的反面世界。园中被调理得运河似的溪流，在这里流成粗粝的浅滩，或漫成片片沼泽。坡上覆着灌木——山楂、榛子、冬青的灌木，间或一株老橡树，虬枝怒张地戳向天空。谷底是蕨草与枯萎的石楠花，蜂群犹在嗡嗡。放眼循着一条条小峡谷望向远处的山脊，十月的蓝霭给它们添上高山的气象。
===Original===
As Alastair gazed at the scene he saw again his own countryside. These were like the wild woods that cloaked Loch Sunart side, the wind brought him the same fragrance of heath and fern, he heard the croak of a raven, a knot of hinds pushed from the coppice and plashed through a marshy shallow. For a second his eyes filled with tears.

He found the Duchess’s hand on his. It was a new Duchess, with grave kind face and no hint of petulance at her lips or artifice in her voice.

“I brought you here for a purpose, sir,” she said. “You have before you two worlds﻿—the enclosed garden and the wild beyond. The wild is yours, by birthright and training and choice. Beyond the pale is Robin Hood’s land, where men adventure. Inside is a quiet domain where they make verses and read books and cherish possessions﻿—my brother’s land. Does my parable touch you?”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔凝望这片景色，恍惚又见自己的家乡。这与披覆苏纳特湖（Loch Sunart）畔的野林何其相像，风里送来同样石楠与蕨草的芬芳，他听得见渡鸦的聒噪，一小群母鹿从树丛里惊出，溅着水花蹚过一片沼泽浅滩。有一瞬，他眼眶里噙满了泪。

他发觉公爵夫人的手已覆在自己手上。这已是一位截然不同的公爵夫人，面容庄重仁慈，唇边没有一丝乖戾，声音里没有半点做作。

“我把您带到这里，是有用意的，先生，”她说。“您面前是两个世界——围起来的园子，和外面的荒野。荒野是您的，凭出身、凭教养、凭您自己的选择。栅栏之外是罗宾汉的天地，是男人们历险的地方。里面则是一片清静疆土，人们在其中写诗、读书、珍爱家业——那是我弟弟的天地。我这番譬喻，可说中了您的心么？”

===Original===
“The two worlds are one, madam﻿—one in God’s sight.”

“In God’s sight, maybe, but not in man’s. I will be plainer still with you. I do not know your business, nor do I ask it, for you are my brother’s friend. But he is my darling and I fear a threat to his peace as a mother-partridge fears the coming of a hawk. Somehow﻿—I ask no questions﻿—you would persuade him to break bounds and leave his sanctuary for the wilds. It may be the manlier choice, but oh, sir, it is not for him. He is meant for the garden. His health is weak, his spirit is most noble but too fine for the clash of the rough world. In a year he would be in his grave.”

Alastair, deeply perplexed, made no answer. He could not lie to this woman, nor could he make a confidante of the wife of Queensberry.

“Pardon me if I embarrass you,” she went on. “I do not ask a reply. Your secrets would be safe with me, but if you told me them I should stop my ears. For politics I care nothing, I know nothing. I speak on a brother’s behalf, and my love for him makes me importunate. I tell you that he is made for the pleasance, not for the wilderness. Will you weigh my words?”

===Chinese===
“这两个世界本是一个，夫人——在上帝眼中原为一体。”

“在上帝眼中也许是，在世人眼中却不然。我索性同您挑明了罢。我不知道您的来意，也不打听，因为您是我弟弟的朋友。可他是我心尖上的人，我一感到有祸患逼近他的安宁，就像母鹧鸪惧怕鹰隼将至那样坐立难安。不知怎的——我什么也不问——您总要劝他越出藩篱，离开他的圣所，走向荒野。这或许是更见男子气概的选择，可是，先生啊，这条路不属于他。他天生是园子里的人。他身子骨单薄，心气虽极高贵，却太纤细，经不起粗粝世道的冲撞。不出一年，他就要进坟墓了。”

阿拉斯泰尔大感踌躇，无言以对。他不能对这妇人撒谎，却也万不能拿昆斯伯里公爵夫人当心腹。

“若是我叫您为难了，请想罪，”她接着说。“我并不求答复。您的秘密交给我自然稳妥，可您若真说了，我反倒要掩耳不听。政治，我一无所好，也一无所知。我以姐妹的身份进言，对他的爱使我不能缄默。我告诉您：他是为园囿而生的人，不是为荒野而生的人。您肯把我的话放在心上掂一掂么？”
===Original===
“I will weigh them most scrupulously. Lord Cornbury is blessed in his sister.”

“I am all he has, for he never could find a wife to his taste.” She whipped up the ponies and her voice changed to its old lightness. “La, sir, we must hasten. The gentlemen will be clamouring for tea.”

In the great gallery, among more Vandykes and Knellers and Lelys and panels of Mortlake tapestry, the company sipped tea and chocolate. The Duchess made tea with her own hands, and the bright clothes and jewels gleaming in the dusk against dim pictures had once more the airy unreality of a dream. But Alastair’s mood had changed. He no longer felt imprisoned among potent shadows, for the glimpse he had had of his own familiar country had steadied his balance. He saw the life he had chosen in fairer colours, the life of toil and hazard and enterprise, in contrast with this airless ease. The blood ran quicker in his veins for the sight of a drugged and sleeping world. Ancient possessions, the beauty of women, the joy of the senses were things to be forsworn before they could be truly admired. Now he looked graciously upon what an hour ago had irked him.

When the candles were lit and the curtains drawn the scene grew livelier. The pretty Lady Mary, sitting under the Kneller portrait of her mother, was a proof of the changelessness of beauty. A pool was made at commerce, in which all joined, and the Duchess’s childlike laughter rippled through the talk like a trout-stream. She was in her wildest mood, the incomparable Kitty whom for thirty years every poet had sung. The thing became a nursery party, where discretion was meaningless, and her irreverent tongue did not refrain from politics. She talked of the Stuarts.

===Chinese===
“我必字字掂量，不敢有负。科恩伯里勋爵有这样一位姐姐，真是天赐的福分。”

“我是他在世上仅有的一切，他始终没能娶上一房合他心意的太太。”她一扬鞭催动小马，声音又恢复了先前的轻盈。“哎呀先生，得赶紧些了。先生们该嚷着要茶了。”

长廊里挂着更多的凡·戴克、克内勒、莱利，还有莫特莱克挂毯的镶板，众人就在这里品茶吃巧克力。公爵夫人亲手沏茶，鲜衣华服与珠光宝气在暮色里、在黯淡的画像前莹莹闪烁，又添了一层梦也似的虚渺。可阿拉斯泰尔的心境已变。他不再觉得自己被困在重重威严的暗影之间，因为方才那一眼故园风物，已扶稳了他的心神。他把自己选定的生涯看得更美了——那劳碌、冒险、建功的生涯，恰与这份不透气的安逸对照。看到这麻醉沉睡的世界，他血流愈加快意。古老的产业、女子的美色、感官的欢愉，唯有先行舍弃，方能真正鉴赏。此刻再看一小时前令他生烦的一切，他眼中已有了宽和。

烛火点起，帘幕垂下，场面愈发生动。俏丽的玛丽小姐坐在她母亲的克内勒画像之下，恰是美色不变的明证。众人凑起一桌“康默斯”牌局，个个下场，公爵夫人孩童般的笑声像一条鳟鱼溪，在谈笑间淙淙流过。她到了兴头最浓的时候——三十年间每一位诗人都歌咏过的、无双的基蒂。这一席渐渐变成一场幼儿游戏会，谨慎二字毫无意义，她那张不知忌惮的嘴也不再回避政治。她谈起斯图亚特家。

===Original===
“They intermarried with us,” she cried, “so I can speak as a kinswoman. A grave dutiful race﻿—they were, tragically misunderstood. If their passions were fierce, they never permitted them to bias their statecraft.”

A portrait of Mary of Scots hung above her as she spoke. Mr. Murray cast a quizzical eye upon it.

“Does your summary embrace that ill-fated lady?” he asked.

“She above all. Her frailties were not Stuart but Tudor. Consider Harry the Eighth. He had passions like other monarchs, but instead of keeping mistresses he must marry each successive love, and as a consequence cut off the head of the last one. His craze was not for amours but for matrimony. So, too, with his sister Margaret. So, too, with his great-niece Mary. She might have had a hundred lovers and none would have gainsaid her, but the mischief came when she insisted on wedding them. No! No! What ruined the fortunes of my kinsfolk was not the Stuart blood but the Tudor﻿—the itch for lawful wedlock which came in with the Welsh bourgeoisie.”

===Chinese===
“他们与我们家世代联姻，”她嚷道，“所以我有资格以亲戚的身份说话。一个庄重、尽职的种族——却悲惨地被误解了。纵然他们的激情炽烈，却从不许激情左右他们的治国之道。”

她说话时，头顶正悬着苏格兰玛丽女王的画像。默里先生朝画像投去调侃的一瞥。

“您这段总论，也包括那位命途多舛的贵妇么？”他问。

“她更是头一个。她的弱点不是斯图亚特家的，倒是都铎家的。想想亨利八世。他与别的君主一样有激情，可他不肯蓄情妇，非得把一个个情人明媒正娶，结果把最后一个的脑袋砍了下来。他的痴病不在偷香窃玉，而在婚娶。他妹妹玛格丽特如此，他的甥孙女玛丽也是如此。她本可以有一百个情人，谁也不会说半个不字，祸事偏偏出在她执意要嫁给他们。不！不！毁了我这些亲戚前程的，不是斯图亚特的血，是都铎的血——是随那威尔士中产人家进宫来的、对明媒正娶的癖痒！”
===Original===
“Your Grace must rewrite the histories,” said Mr. Murray, laughing.

“I have a mind to. But my Harry will bear me witness. The Stuart stock is sad and dutiful. Is not that the character of him who now calls himself the rightful King of England?”

“So I have heard it said,” Lord Cornbury answered, but the eyes which looked at his sister were disapproving.

===Chinese===
“公爵夫人怕是非得重写史书不可了，”默里先生笑道。

“我真有这个心思呢。不过我的哈利可以为我作证：斯图亚特这一族，忧郁而尽职。如今自称英格兰合法国王的那一位，不正是这个品性么？”

“人家也这么说他，”科恩伯里勋爵答道，可他望着姐姐的那双眼睛里，满是不以为然。

===Original===
The ladies went early to bed, after nibbling a sweet biscuit and sipping a glass of negus. Supper was laid for the gentlemen in the dining-room, and presently Mr. Murray, Mr. Kyd and Sir Christopher Lacy were seated at a board which they seemed to have no intention of leaving. Alastair excused himself on the plea of fatigue, and lit a bedroom candle. “I will come to your room,” his host whispered as they crossed the hall. “Do not undress. We will talk in my little cabinet.”

The young man flung himself into a chair, and collected his thoughts. He had been chosen for this mission, partly because of his address and education, but mainly because of the fierce ardour which he had hitherto shown in the Prince’s cause. He knew that much hung on his success, for Cornbury, though nothing of a soldier and in politics no more than Member of Parliament for the University of Oxford, was so beloved that his adherence would be worth a regiment. He knew his repute. Such a man could not quibble in matters of principle; the task was rather to transform apathy into action. He remembered the Duchess’s words﻿—honest words, doubtless, but not weighty. Surely in so great a test of honour a man could not hesitate because his health was weak or his home dear to him.

There was a knock at the door and Lord Cornbury entered with a silk dressing-gown worn over his clothes. He looked round the room with his sad restless eyes.

===Chinese===
女士们早早嚼了一块甜饼、呷了一杯尼格斯热酒，便上楼安歇了。绅士们的消夜设在饭厅，不多时，默里先生、基德先生和克里斯托弗·莱西爵士已围坐桌前，看那样子全无离席之意。阿拉斯泰尔推说旅途劳顿，告退出来，点起一支卧房蜡烛。“我到你房里来，”穿过大厅时主人低声道。“先别宽衣。到我那间小书斋里谈。”

年轻人把自己掷进一把椅子，定神思索。选中他来办这趟差，一半因为他的机变与教养，更因为他在王子的大业里向来燃烧着的那股炽热。他知道自己此行干系重大：科恩伯里虽非将才，在政治上也不过是代表牛津大学出席国会议员，却如此得人望，他若归附，抵得上一支军团。他深知此人的令誉。这样的人在原则问题上无从搪塞；难的是把冷漠化为行动。他想起公爵夫人的话——想必是肺腑之言，却分量不足。在这样一场荣誉攸关的大考验面前，一个人总不至因身子单弱、因家园可爱而踌躇不前罢。

门上响起叩声，科恩伯里勋爵走了进来，衣服外面罩着一袭丝质晨衣。他用那双忧郁不安的眼睛环视房间。
===Original===
“Here Lord Leicester died﻿—Elizabeth’s favourite. They say that when the day of his death comes round his spirit may be heard tapping at the walls. It is a commentary on mortal ambition, Captain Maclean. Come with me to my cabinet. Mr. Solicitor is gone to bed, for he is ready enough for an all-night sitting at St. James’s among the wits, but has no notion of spoiling his sleep by potations among bumpkins. Kit Lacy and Mr. Kyd will keep it up till morning, but happily they are at the other end of the house.”

He led the way down a narrow staircase to a little room on the ground floor, which had for its other entrance a door giving on a tiny paved garden. It was lined with books and a small fire had been lit on the hearth.

“Here we shall be secure, for I alone have the keys,” Lord Cornbury said, taking a seat by a bureau where the single lamp was behind his head. “You have something private for my ear? I must tell you, sir, I have been plagued for many months by portentous secret emissaries. There was my lord Clancarty, a Cyclops with one eye and a shocking perruque, who seemed to me not wholly in possession of his wits. There was a Scotch gentleman﻿—Bahaldy﻿—Bohaldy﻿—whom I suspected of being a liar. There was Traquair, whose speech rang false in every stutter. They and their kind were full of swelling words, but they were most indisputably fools. You are not of their breed, sir. From you I look for candour and good sense. What have you to say to me?”

===Chinese===
“莱斯特伯爵就死在这间屋里——伊丽莎白女王的那位宠臣。据说每逢他的忌日，他的魂灵便敲响墙壁，给人听见。这倒给世人的野心下了一条注脚，麦克莱恩上尉。随我到我的书斋来。副检察长大人已经睡了——在圣詹姆斯宫同才子们坐通宵，他求之不得，可要他陪着乡下人灌黄汤熬坏瞌睡，他是万万不肯的。基特·莱西和基德先生会闹到天亮，好在他们宿在宅子的另一头。”

他引路走下一道窄梯，到底层一间小屋；屋子另一道门开向一方砖铺的小园。四壁皆书，炉膛里已生起一炉小火。

“在这里咱们稳当，钥匙只有我一人有，”科恩伯里勋爵说着，在书案旁落座，案上那盏独灯正照在他脑后。“你有机密话要对我讲？先生，不瞒你说，这几个月我被神神叨叨的秘密密使们缠苦了。有位克兰卡蒂勋爵，活脱一只独眼的独眼巨人，顶着一头吓人的假发，我看他神智就不甚齐全。有位苏格兰绅士——巴霍尔迪——还是博霍尔迪——我疑心他是个骗子。还有特雷夸尔，他每结巴一下，话音里都是假的。这班人满口大言，却是不折不扣的蠢材。你与他们不同族，先生。我指望你的是坦白与见识。你有什么话要对我说？”

===Original===
“One thing only, my lord. From me you will get no boasts or promises. I bring you a summons.”

Alastair took from his breast a letter. Lord Cornbury broke the seal and revealed a page of sprawling irregular handwriting, signed at the foot with the words “Charles P.” He read it with attention, read it again, and then looked at the messenger.

“His Royal Highness informs me that I will be ‘inexcusable before God and men’ if I fail him. For him that is a natural opinion. Now, sir, before answering this appeal, I have certain questions to ask you. You come from the Prince’s army, and you are in the secrets of his Cabinet. You are also a soldier. I would hear from you the Prince’s strength.”

“He can cross the Border with not less than five thousand horse and foot.”

===Chinese===
“只有一句话，大人。您从我这里听不到夸口，也听不到许诺。我带给您的是一道召唤。”

阿拉斯泰尔从怀里取出一封信。科恩伯里勋爵拆了火漆，展开一页潦草散乱、不守行款的手迹，落款是“查理·P.”。他凝神读了一遍，又读一遍，然后望着信使。

“王子殿下知会我，我若负他，便‘在上帝和世人面前无可辩解’。在他看来，这是天经地义的意见。那么，先生，在答复这项吁请之前，我有几件事要问你。你从王子的军中来，与闻他枢机的秘密，又是军人。我要听听王子的实力。”

“他跨过边境时，步骑不会少于五千。”
===Original===
“Highlanders?”

“In the main, which means the best natural fighting stock in this land. They have already shown their prowess against Cope’s regulars. There are bodies of Lowland horse with Elcho and Pitsligo.”

“And your hopes of increment?”

“More than half the clans are still to raise. Of them we are certain. There are accessions to be looked for from the Lowlands. In England we have promises from every quarter﻿—from Barrymore, Molyneux, Grosvenor, Fenwick, Petre, Cholmondeley, Leigh, Curzon in the North; from the Duke of Beaufort and Sir Watkin Wynn in the West. Likewise large sums of money are warranted from the city of London.”

===Chinese===
“都是高地人？”

“十之八九是，也就是说，是这块土地上最天生的兵源。他们对科普的正规军已亮过身手。另有埃尔乔与皮茨利戈统领的几队低地骑兵。”

“增兵的前景呢？”

“一大半氏族尚未征发，那都是十拿九稳的。低地方面也可望有人来投。在英格兰，四面八方都有人许诺——北边有巴里莫尔、莫利纽克斯、格罗夫纳、芬威克、皮特雷、乔姆利、利、柯曾；西边有博福特公爵和沃特金·温爵士。伦敦城里还有大宗款项得到担保。”

===Original===
“You speak not of sympathy only, but of troops? Many are no doubt willing to drink His Royal Highness’s health.”

“I speak of troops. There is also the certain aid from France. In this paper, my lord, you will find set down the numbers and dates of troops to be dispatched before Christmas. Some are already on the way﻿—Lord John Drummond with his regiment of Royal Ecossais and certain Irish companies from the French service.”

“And you have against you?”

“In Scotland﻿—nothing. In England at present not ten thousand men. Doubtless they will make haste to bring back troops from abroad, but before that we hope to conquer. His Royal Highness’s plan is clear. He seeks as soon as possible to win a victory in England. In his view the land is for the first comer. The nation is indifferent and will yield to boldness. I will be honest with you, my lord. He hopes also to confirm the loyalty of France, for it is certain that if his arms triumph but once on English soil, the troops of King Louis will take the sea.”

===Chinese===
“你说的不止是人心向背，而是实实在在的兵马？无疑不少人乐意举杯祝王子殿下健康。”

“我说的是兵马。还有法国的可靠后援。这张文书上，大人，您可以看到圣诞节前将派出的部队数目与日期。有几路已经上路——约翰·德拉蒙德勋爵率他的皇家苏格兰团，还有几支法国军中的爱尔兰连队。”

“与你们为敌的又有哪些？”

“在苏格兰——没有。在英格兰，眼下凑不足一万人。他们无疑会急忙从海外调兵，但我们指望在那之前已定胜局。王子殿下的方略很清楚：尽早在英格兰打一场胜仗。照他看，这国土属于先到者。国人无动于衷，自会屈服于胆气。我不瞒您，大人：他还指望借此坐实法兰西的忠诚——因为只要他的兵器在英格兰土地上得胜一次，路易国王的兵马就必然渡海。”
===Original===
The other mused. “It is a bold policy, but it may be a wise one. I would raise one difficulty. You have omitted from your calculation the British Fleet.”

Alastair shrugged his shoulders. “It is our prime danger, but we hope with speed and secrecy to outwit it.”

“I have another objection. You are proposing to conquer England with a foreign army. I say not a word against the valour of your Highland countrymen, but to English eyes they are barbarous strangers. And France is the ancient enemy.”

“Then, my lord, it is a strife of foreigner against foreigner. Are King George’s Dutch and Danes and Hessians better Englishmen than the Prince’s men? Let England abide the issue, and join the victor.”

===Chinese===
对方沉吟。“这是着险棋，却未必不是步好棋。我要提一个难处。你的盘算里漏了一样——英国的舰队。”

阿拉斯泰尔耸耸肩。“那是我们的头号险情，但我们指望凭神速与隐秘骗过它。”

“我还有一桩异议。你是想用一支外国军队征服英格兰。你高地同乡的骁勇，我一个字也不贬低，可在英格兰人眼里，他们是蛮邦生客。而法兰西是宿仇。”

“既然如此，大人，这不过是外国人与外国人的相争。乔治国王的荷兰人、丹麦人、黑森人，难道比王子的人更是英格兰人么？让英格兰静待其变，归附胜者便是。”

===Original===
“You speak reasonably, I do not deny it. Let me ask further. Has any man of note joined your standard?”

“Many Scots nobles, though not the greatest. But Hamilton favours us, and there are grounds for thinking that even the Whig dukes, Argyll and Montrose and Queensberry, are soured with the Government. It is so in England, my lord. Bedford﻿ ﻿…”

“I know, I know. All are waiting on the tide. But meantime His Royal Highness’s Cabinet is a rabble of Irishmen. Is it not so? I do not like to have Teague in the business, sir, and England does not like it.”

“Then come yourself, my lord.”

===Chinese===
“你说得在理，我不否认。容我再问一句。可有哪位知名之士投到你们的旗下了？”

“苏格兰贵族来了不少，虽非顶尖的几位。但汉密尔顿公爵心向我们，而且有理由相信，连辉格党的几位公爵——阿盖尔、蒙特罗斯、昆斯伯里——也对政府心存怨怼。英格兰也一样，大人。贝德福德……”

“我知道，我知道。人人都在观望潮水。可眼下王子殿下的枢机里，却是一窝爱尔兰人。难道不是么？先生，我不喜欢这事里掺爱尔兰佬，英格兰也不喜欢。”

“那么大人就亲自来罢。”
===Original===
Lord Cornbury smiled. “I have not finished my questions. What of his Royal Highness’s religion? I take it that it is the same as your own.”

“He has already given solemn pledges for liberty and toleration. Many Presbyterians of the straitest sect are in his camp. Be sure, my lord, that he will not be guilty of his grandfather’s blunder.”

Lord Cornbury rose and stood with his back to the fire.

“You are still in the military stage, where your first duty is a victory in the field. What does His Royal Highness wish me to do? I am no soldier, I could not raise a dozen grooms and foresters. I do not live in Sir Watkin’s county, where you can blow a horn and summon a hundred rascals. Here in Oxfordshire we are peaceable folk.”

===Chinese===
科恩伯里勋爵微微一笑。“我的问题还没问完。王子殿下的宗教信仰呢？我料想与你的相同。”

“他已为自由与宽容立下庄重的誓约。最严苛的长老会教派中也有许多人投在他营中。请您放心，大人，他绝不会重蹈他祖父的覆辙。”

科恩伯里勋爵起身，背炉而立。

“你们还处在武力阶段，首要之务是战场上的胜利。王子殿下要我做什么呢？我不是军人，召集一打马夫林务也办不到。我又不住在温爵士的郡里，吹一声号角便能召来百十个亡命徒。我们牛津郡人，是安分的百姓。”

===Original===
“He wants you in his Council. I am no lover of the Irish, and there is sore need of statesmanship among us.”

“Say you want me for an example.”

“That is the truth, my lord.”

“And, you would add, for statecraft. Then let us look at the matter with a statesman’s eye. You say truly that England does not love her Government. She is weary of foreign wars, and an alien Royal house, and gross taxes, and corruption in high places. She is weary, I say, but she will not stir to shift the burden. You are right; she is for the first comer. You bring a foreign army and it will fight what in the main is a foreign army, so patriotic feeling is engaged on neither side. If you win, the malcontents, who are the great majority, will join you, and His Royal Highness will sit on the throne of his fathers. If you fail, there is no loss except to yourselves, for the others are not pledged. Statesmanship, sir, is an inglorious thing, for it must consider first the fortunes of the common people. No statesman has a right to risk these fortunes unless he be reasonably assured of success. Therefore I say to you that England must wait, and statesmen must wait with England, till the issue is decided. That issue still lies with the soldiers. I cannot join His Royal Highness at this juncture, for I could bring no aid to his cause and I might bring needless ruin to those who depend on me. My answer might have been otherwise had I been a soldier.”

A certain quiet obstinacy had entered the face which was revealed in profile by the lamp on the bureau. The voice had lost its gentle indeterminateness and rang crisp and clear. Alastair had knowledge enough of men to recognise finality. He made his last effort.

===Chinese===
“他要您入他的枢机。我本不爱与爱尔兰人为伍，而我们中间正痛感缺少治国之才。”

“不如说，你们要我做一面招牌。”

“正是实情，大人。”

“再加一句——还要我的治术。那就让我们以政治家的眼光来看看这桩事。你说得对，英格兰不爱她的政府。她厌倦了对外战争，厌倦了异邦的王室，厌倦了苛捐杂税，厌倦了高官的贪墨。她厌倦了，我说，但她不肯动一动去搬掉这副重担。你说得对，她是先来者的。你们带来一支外国军队，同它交战的基本也是一支外国军队，两边都沾不上爱国心。你们若胜，心怀不满者——他们占着绝大多数——会来归附，王子殿下便坐上他列祖列宗的王座。你们若败，除你们自身之外谁也无损，因为旁人并未许下什么。政治家这一行啊，先生，是捞不到名声的行当，因为它首先得考虑黎民百姓的祸福。没有相当把握的成功，任何政治家都无权拿百姓的祸福去冒险。所以我告诉你：英格兰必须等，政治家也必须同英格兰一起等，等到大局底定。这大局眼下仍操在军人手里。值此时机，我不能归附王子殿下，因为我带不去一臂之助，反倒可能给我所照管的人们招来无谓的毁灭。我若是个军人，答案或许另当别论。”

书案上那盏灯照出他的侧脸，脸上已透出一种静静的固执。那嗓音失去了温软的含糊，字字斩截清朗。阿拉斯泰尔阅人已多，听得出这是定局。他使出最后一分气力。
===Original===
“Are considerations of policy the only ones? You and I share the same creeds, my lord. Our loyalty is owed to the House which has the rightful succession, and we cannot in our obedience to God serve what He has not ordained. Is it not your duty to fling prudence to the winds and make your election before the world, for right is right whether we win or lose.”

“For some men maybe,” said the other sadly, “but not for me. I am in that position that many eyes are turned on me and in my decision I must consider them. If your venture fails, I desire that as few Englishmen as possible suffer for it, it being premised that for the moment only armed men can help it to success. Therefore I wait, and will counsel waiting to all in like position. Beaufort can bring troops, and in God’s name I would urge him on, and from the bottom of my heart I pray for the Prince’s welfare.”

“What will decide you, then?”

“A victory on English soil. Nay, I will go farther. So soon as His Royal Highness is in the way of that victory, I will fly to his side.”

===Chinese===
“利害的盘算就是一切么？您与我奉着同一信仰，大人。我们的忠诚属于名分正当的那一姓王室；我们既顺服上帝，便不能事奉他所未曾设立的东西。把谨慎抛到九霄云外，当着世人作出您的抉择——这不正是您的本分么？因为对的就是对的，不问成败。”

“对某些人也许是，”对方黯然道，“于我却不然。我的地位有千百双眼睛望着，我作决断时不能不顾念他们。倘若你们的事败了，我盼遭殃的英格兰人越少越好——何况眼下唯有执干戈者能助它成功。所以我等待，也要劝与我处境相同的人一齐等待。博福特能带兵马，凭上帝之名，我恨不得推他一把；我也发自肺腑，祈求王子平安。”

“那么，什么才能使您下定决心？”

“在英格兰土地上的一场胜仗。不，我再说透一层：只要王子殿下走上了那条通往胜利的路，我便飞奔到他的身边。”

===Original===
“What proof will you require?”

“Ten thousand men south of Derby on the road to London, and the first French contingent landed.”

“That is your answer, my lord?”

“That is the answer which I would have you convey with my most humble and affectionate duty to His Royal Highness.﻿ ﻿… And now, sir, will you join me in a turn on the terrace, as the night is fine. It is my habit before retiring.”

===Chinese===
“您要什么凭据？”

“一万人马南下德比、直指伦敦，外加第一支法国远征军登岸。”

“这就是您的答复么，大人？”

“这就是我要你转呈王子殿下的答复，请代我致以最谦卑、最敬爱的忱意。……好了，先生，夜色正好，可肯陪我到平台上走一遭？这是我睡前雷打不动的习惯。”
===Original===
The night was mild and very dark, and from the lake rose the honk of wild fowl and from the woods the fitful hooting of owls. To Alastair his failure was scarcely a disappointment, for he realised that all day he had lived in expectation of it. Nay, inasmuch as it placed so solemn a duty upon the soldiers of the Cause, it strung his nerves like a challenge. Lord Cornbury put an arm in his, and the sign of friendship moved the young man’s affection. It was for youth and ardour such as his to make clear the path for gentler souls.

They left the stones of the terrace and passed the lit window of the dining-room, where it appeared that merriment had advanced, for Sir Christopher Lacy was attempting a hunting-song.

“Such are the squires of England,” whispered Cornbury. “They will drink and dice and wench for the Prince, but not fight for him.”

“Not yet,” Alastair corrected. “But when your lordship joins us he will not be unattended.”

===Chinese===
夜色温凉而浓黑，湖上雁鸣，林间枭啼断续。在阿拉斯泰尔看来，这番失败算不得失望，因为他明白，自己一整天就活在失败的预感里。不，这一失败既然把如此庄严的责任压到大业军人的肩上，反倒像一道战书，绷紧了他的神经。科恩伯里勋爵挽住他的手臂，这亲昵的表示撩动了年轻人的情肠。为温软的灵魂开路，正该由他这样的青春与烈性来担。

他们离开平台的石径，走过饭厅灯烛通明的窗前——里面的欢兴看来又深了一层，克里斯托弗·莱西爵士正扯着嗓子唱一支猎歌。

“英格兰的乡绅就是这副德行，”科恩伯里低声道。“为王子喝酒、掷骰、追女人都行，就是不肯为他打仗。”

“是还不肯，”阿拉斯泰尔纠正道，“但等大人您入了伙，他就不愁没人作伴了。”

===Original===
They reached the corner of the house from which in daylight the great avenue could be seen, the spot where that morning Alastair had delivered his credentials.

“I hear hooves,” said Cornbury, with a hand to his ear. “Nay, it is only the night wind.”

“It is a horse,” said the other. “I have heard it for the last minute. Now it is entering the courtyard. See, there is a stable lantern.”

A light swayed, and there was the sound of human speech.

===Chinese===
他们走到宅子的一角，白天从这里望得见那条气派的大林荫道——正是那天早晨阿拉斯泰尔呈递书信的地方。

“我听见马蹄声了，”科恩伯里侧耳道。“不，不过是夜风罢了。”

“是一匹马，”另一位说。“这一分钟我一直在听。这不，进院子了。瞧，有一盏马厩的灯。”

一点灯火摇曳着，人语之声随之而来。
===Original===
“That is Kyd’s Scotch servant,” Cornbury said. “Let us inquire into the errand of this nightrider.”

As they moved towards the lantern a commotion began, and the light wavered like a ship’s lamp in a heavy sea.

“Haud up, sir,” cried a voice. “Losh, the beast’s foundered, and the man’s in a dwam.”

===Chinese===
“那是基德的苏格兰仆人，”科恩伯里说。“咱们去问问这位夜行人有什么贵干。”

两人朝那盏灯走去，那头忽然骚动起来，灯火剧烈摇晃，像怒海上一盏船灯。

“扶一把，先生！”一个声音喊道。“天哪，牲口垮了，马上的人也昏死过去了！”

## III In Which Private Matters Cut Across Affairs of State / 三、私事横亘于国事

===Original===

In the circle of the lantern’s light the horseman, a big shambling fellow, stood swaying as if in extreme fatigue, now steadying himself by a hand on the animal’s neck, now using the support of the groom’s shoulder. His weak eyes peered and blinked, and at the sight of the gentlemen he made an attempt at a bow.

“My lord!” he gasped with a dry mouth. “Do I address my lord Cornbury?”

He did not wait for an answer. “I am from Chastlecote, my lord. I beg﻿—I supplicate﻿—a word with your lordship.”

“Now?”

“Now, if it please you. My business is most urgent. It is life or death, my lord, the happiness or despair of an immortal soul.”

“You are the tutor from Chastlecote, I think. You appear to have been trying your beast high.”

===Chinese===

马灯的光晕里，那骑手——一条高大松垮的汉子——摇摇晃晃地站着，仿佛疲惫已极，时而伸手扶住马颈稳住身子，时而倚上马夫的肩头借力。他一双病弱的眼眯缝着直眨，望见两位绅士，便勉强躬身，行了个礼。

“大人！”他口干舌燥，喘着气道。“我是在同科恩伯里勋爵（Lord Cornbury）说话么？”

他不等回答。“我是从查斯特科特（Chastlecote）来的，大人。我恳求——我哀告——求大人赏一句话。”

“现在就说？”

“若蒙大人俯允，现在就说。我的事万分紧急。这是生死攸关的事，大人，关系着一个不朽灵魂的幸福还是绝望。”

“我想，你就是查斯特科特那位家庭教师吧。你这牲口，看来被你催逼得不轻。”

===Original===

“I have ridden to Weston and to Heythrop since midday.”

“Have you eaten?”

“Not since breakfast, my lord.” The man’s eyes were wolfish with hunger and weariness.

“Then you shall eat, for there can be no business between a full man and a fasting. The groom will see to your horse. Follow me.”

Lord Cornbury led the way past the angle of the house to where the lit windows of the dining-room made a glow in the dark.

“ ’Tis a night of queer doings,” he whispered to Alastair, as they heard the heavy feet of the stranger stumbling behind them. “We will surprise Kit Lacy in his cups, but there will be some remnants of supper for this fellow. ’Pon my soul, I am curious to know what has shifted such a gravity out of bed.”

===Chinese===

“从晌午到现在，我跑了韦斯顿（Weston），又跑了海斯罗普（Heythrop）。”

“用过饭没有？”

“早饭以后就没吃过，大人。”那人一双眼睛，饿得累得像狼一样。

“那你先吃饭。饱汉与饿汉之间，是谈不成事情的。马夫会照看你的马。跟我来。”

科恩伯里勋爵引路，绕过宅子的墙角，朝饭厅走去——那几扇亮着的窗，在黑暗里映出一片暖光。

“今夜真是怪事迭出，”他低声对阿拉斯泰尔（Alastair）说，一面听着生客沉重的脚步在身后跌跌撞撞。“咱们要撞见基特·莱西醉态可掬的样子了，不过晚饭总还剩些残肴给这位仁兄。说实在的，我倒真好奇，什么事把这样一位端方人物从床上惊动起来。”

===Original===

He unlocked the garden-door and led the way through the great hall to the dining-room. Sir Christopher, mellow but still sober, was interrupted in a song, and, with admirable presence of mind, cut it short in a view holloa. Mr. Kyd, rosy as the dawn, hastened to place chairs.

“Your pardon, gentlemen, but I bring you a famished traveller. Sit down, sir, and have at that pie. There is claret at your elbow.”

The newcomer muttered thanks and dropped heavily into a chair. Under the bright candelabrum, among crystal and silver and shining fruit and the gay clothes of the others, he cut an outrageous figure. He might have been in years about the age of Lord Cornbury, but disease and rough usage had wiped every sign of youth from his face. That face was large, heavily-featured and pitted deep with the scars of scrofula. The skin was puffy and grey, the eyes beneath the prominent forehead were pale and weak, the mouth was cast in hard lines as if from suffering. His immense frame was incredibly lean and bony, and yet from his slouch seemed unwholesomely weighted with flesh. He wore his own hair, straight and lank and tied with a dusty ribbon. His clothes were of some coarse grey stuff and much worn, and, though on a journey, he had no boots, but instead clumsy unbuckled shoes and black worsted stockings. His cuffs and neckband were soiled, and overcrowded pockets made his coat hang on him like a sack. Such an apparition could not but affect the best-bred gentleman. Kit Lacy’s mouth was drawn into a whistle, Mr. Kyd sat in smiling contemplation. Alastair thought of Simon Lovat as he had last seen that vast wallowing chieftain, and then reflected that Simon carried off his oddity by his air of arrogant command. This fellow looked as harassed as a mongrel that boys have chivvied into a corner. He cut himself a wedge of pie and ate gobblingly. He poured out a tankard of claret and swallowed most of it at a gulp. Then he grew nervous, choked on a crumb, gulped more claret and coughed till his pale face grew crimson.

The worst pangs of hunger allayed, he seemed to recollect his errand. His lips began to mutter as if he were preparing a speech. His tired eyes rested in turn on each member of the company, on Lacy and Kyd lounging at the other side of the table, on Cornbury’s decorous figure at the head, on Alastair wrapped in his own thoughts at the foot. This was not the private conference he had asked for, but it would appear that the urgency of his need must override discretion. A spasm of pain distorted the huge face, and he brought his left hand down violently on the table, so that the glasses shivered.

===Chinese===

他开了花园门上的锁，领着人穿过大厅来到饭厅。克里斯托弗爵士（Sir Christopher）正唱在兴头上——微醺而神志尚清——被人这一打断，凭着令人叹服的急智，就势把歌声收作一嗓子见猎的欢呼。基德先生（Mr. Kyd）满面红光赛似朝霞，忙不迭地张罗椅子。

“列位先生恕罪，我给你们带来一位饿坏了的旅人。请坐，先生，尝尝那馅饼。你手边就有红葡萄酒。”

新来者含糊道了谢，一屁股沉甸甸坐进椅子里。明亮的枝形烛台底下，在水晶、银器、鲜亮的水果和众人的华服之间，他那副模样实在碍眼已极。论年纪，他本与科恩伯里勋爵相仿，可是疾病与磋磨早把他脸上的青春痕迹抹得干干净净。那张脸宽大，五官粗重，密密麻麻布着瘰疬留下的深疤。皮肤浮肿灰败，凸出的额头底下一双眼睛淡弱无神，嘴边的线条僵硬，仿佛是苦楚铸成的。他身躯庞大得出奇，却瘦骨嶙峋，可他那副萎靡的坐相里，又像坠着一身不健康的赘肉。他不戴假发，一头又直又腻的头发，拿一条落满尘土的缎带束着。衣裳是粗灰呢料，磨损得厉害；虽在旅途，脚上却没有靴子，只有一双不曾扣上扣袢的笨重鞋子，配一双黑绒线长袜。袖口和领巾都污渍斑斑，塞得鼓鼓囊囊的口袋，让外衣挂在他身上活像一条麻袋。这样一位人物一出场，教养再好的绅士也不能无动于衷。基特·莱西（Kit Lacy）的嘴撇得像要吹口哨，基德先生坐着，笑吟吟地端详。阿拉斯泰尔想起西蒙·洛弗特（Simon Lovat），想起他上次见到的那个庞大得打滚也似的族长；转念又想，西蒙那份古怪，自有他倨傲发号施令的气派撑得住。眼前这位，却像被顽童们追逼进角落的小土狗，一脸走投无路的狼狈。他给自己切下一大角馅饼，狼吞虎咽地吃。又斟满一大杯红葡萄酒，一口气灌下大半。接着他紧张起来，被一点饼屑呛住，又灌下几口酒，咳得苍白的脸涨成深红。

最难熬的饥痛平息下去，他似乎想起了自己的使命。嘴唇开始嚅动，像在打一篇演说的腹稿。他一双倦眼，依次在席间众人身上停留：在桌子另一侧懒洋洋歪坐着的莱西和基德身上，在上首科恩伯里端方的身影上，在下首沉在自己心事里的阿拉斯泰尔身上。这并不是他所求的密谈，但看来事急，也顾不得斟酌了。一阵痛苦的痉挛扭歪了那张大脸，他左手狠狠拍在桌上，震得杯盏乱颤。

===Original===

“My lord,” he said, “she is gone.”

The company stared, and Sir Christopher tittered.

“Who is your ‘she,’ sir?” he asked as he helped himself to wine.

“Miss Grevel﻿ ﻿… Miss Claudia.”

The young baronet’s face changed.

“The devil! Gone! Explain yourself, sir.”

===Chinese===

“大人，”他说，“她走了。”

满座愕然相顾，克里斯托弗爵士嗤地一笑。

“你那位‘她’是谁呀，先生？”他一面自斟，一面问。

“格雷维尔小姐（Miss Grevel）……克劳迪娅小姐（Miss Claudia）。”

年轻的准男爵脸色变了。

“见鬼！走了！你把话说清楚，先生。”


===Original===

The man had swung round so that he faced Lord Cornbury, with his head screwed oddly over his right shoulder. As he spoke it bobbed in a kind of palsied eagerness.

“You know her, my lord. Miss Claudia Grevel; the cousin and housemate of the young heir of Chastlecote, who has been committed to my charge. Three days ago she was of age and the controller of her fortune. This morning the maids found her bed unslept in, and the lady flown.”

Lord Cornbury exclaimed. “Did she leave no word?” he asked.

“Only a letter to her cousin, bidding him farewell.”

“Nothing to you?”

“To me nothing. She was a high lady and to her I was only the boy’s instructor. But I had marked for some weeks a restlessness in her deportment and, fearing some rash step, I had kept an eye on her doings.”

===Chinese===

那人已转过身子面对科恩伯里勋爵，脑袋却古怪地拧向右肩上方。说话的时候，那脑袋一颠一颠，透着一种近乎痉挛的急切。

“大人是认识她的。克劳迪娅·格雷维尔小姐，查斯特科特年轻继承人的表姐，同住一宅——那孩子是托付给我照管教导的。三天以前她方始成年，自己执掌自己的财产了。今天早晨，女仆们发现她的床不曾睡过，小姐已经飞走了。”

科恩伯里勋爵失声惊呼。“她没有留下只言片语么？”他问。

“只给她表弟留了一封信，道了别。”

“没有给你的话？”

“给我的什么也没有。她是位心气高的小姐，在她眼里，我不过是那孩子的塾师。可是好几个星期以来，我察觉她举止浮动，惟恐她行差踏错，便留意着她的行止。”

===Original===

“You spied on her?” said Kyd sweetly. “Is that part of an usher’s duties?”

The man was too earnest to feel the rudeness of the question.

“She was but a child, sir,” he said. “She had neither father nor mother, and she was about to be sole mistress of a rich estate. I pitied her, and, though she in no way condescended to me, I loved her youth and beauty.”

“You did right,” Lord Cornbury said. “Have your observations given you no clue to the secret of her flight?”

“In some measure, my lord. You must know that Miss Grevel is ardent in politics, and, like many gentlewomen, has a strong sentiment for the young Prince now in Scotland. She has often declared that if she had been a man she would long ago have hastened to his standard, and she was wont to rage against the apathy of the Oxfordshire squires. A scrap of news from the North would put her into a fury or an exaltation. There was one gentleman of the neighbourhood who was not apathetic and who was accordingly most welcome at Chastlecote. From him she had her news of the Prince, and it was clear by his manner towards her that he valued her person as well as shared her opinions. I have been this day to that gentleman’s house and found that at an early hour he started on a journey. I was ill received there and told little, but I ascertained that he had departed with a coach and led horses. My lord, I am convinced that the unhappy girl is his companion.”

===Chinese===

“你刺探她？”基德甜滋滋地说。“这也算塾师职分里的事么？”

那人心念太切，竟没有觉出这话的唐突。

“她不过是个孩子，先生，”他说。“无父无母，又眼看要独当一面，做一份厚产的女主人。我怜惜她；她虽从不屑于折节下交，我却爱惜她的青春与美貌。”

“你做得对，”科恩伯里勋爵说。“那么，你的观察可曾给你什么线索，窥见她出走的隐情？”

“略有所得，大人。大人须知，格雷维尔小姐热衷政事，又与许多闺秀一样，对如今身在苏格兰的年轻王子殿下（the Prince）怀着一片热肠。她常说，自己若是个男儿，早便投到王子麾下去了；她也惯于痛骂牛津郡（Oxfordshire）乡绅们的冷漠。北方来的一星半点消息，都能叫她或勃然动怒，或欢腾若狂。邻近有一位绅士并不冷漠，因此在查斯特科特极受欢迎。她关于王子的消息都从他那里得来，而瞧他对她那副神气，分明既与她同志，又看重她这个人。我今天到过那位绅士府上，得知他一早便动身上路了。我在那边受了冷遇，问不出多少话，但探得他是带着马车和预备替换的驿马上路的。大人，我确信，那不幸的姑娘正与他同行。”

===Original===

“The man’s name?” Lord Cornbury asked sharply.

“Sir John Norreys of Weston.”

The name told nothing to two of the company, but it had a surprising effect on Sir Christopher Lacy. He sprang to his feet, and began to stride up and down the room, his chin on his breast.

“I knew his father,” said Lord Cornbury, “but the young man I have rarely seen. ’Tis a runaway match doubtless; but such marriages are not always tragical. Miss Grevel is too highly placed and well dowered for misadventure. Let us hope for the best, sir. She will return presently a sober bride.”

“I am of your lordship’s opinion,” Mr. Kyd observed with a jolly laugh. “Let a romantic maid indulge her fancy and choose her own way of wedlock, for if she get not romance at the start she will not find it in the dreich business of matrimony. But you and me, my lord, are bachelors and speak only from hearsay.”

===Chinese===

“那人姓甚名谁？”科恩伯里厉声问。

“韦斯顿的约翰·诺里斯爵士（Sir John Norreys）。”

这名字于席间两人毫无意义，对克里斯托弗·莱西爵士却生出惊人的效力。他霍然起身，下巴抵着胸口，在屋里来回踱起步来。

“他父亲我是认识的，”科恩伯里说，“那年轻人却难得见到。这无疑是一桩私奔；可这样的婚姻未必都以悲剧收场。格雷维尔小姐门第既高，妆奁又厚，不至于有何闪失。往好里想吧，先生。不多时她自会作为一位端庄的新妇回来。”

“我附议大人高见，”基德先生呵呵一笑，发表议论道。“任凭多情的小姐纵她的痴想，自择成亲的路吧；起头若寻不着浪漫，往后在那桩熬人的婚姻俗务里，更是休想寻着。不过大人和我都是单身汉，这话不过是道听途说罢了。”

===Original===

The tutor from Chastlecote seemed to be astounded at the reception of his news.

“You do not know the man,” he cried. “It is no case of a youthful escapade. I have made inquiries, and learned that he is no better than a knave. If he is a Jacobite it is for gain, if he weds Miss Grevel it is for her estate.”

“Now what the devil should a dominie like you know about the character of a gentleman of family?”

The words were harsh, but, as delivered by Mr. Kyd with a merry voice and a twinkle of the eye, they might have passed as a robust pleasantry. But the tutor was not in the mood for them. Anger flushed his face, and he blew out his breath like a bull about to charge. Before he could reply, however, he found an ally in Sir Christopher. The baronet flung himself again into his chair and stuck both elbows on the table.

“The fellow is right all the same,” he said. “Jack Norreys is a low hound, and I’ll take my oath on it. No scamp is Jack, for his head is always cool and he has a heart like a codfish. He has a mighty good gift for liquor﻿—I say that for him﻿—but the damnable fellow profits by the generous frailties of his betters. He is mad for play, but he loves the cards like an attorney, not like a gentleman, and he makes a fat thing out of them. No, damme! Jack’s no true man. If he wants the girl ’tis for her fortune, and if he sings Jacobite, ’tis because he sees some scoundrelly profit for himself. I hate the long nose and the mean eyes of him.”

===Chinese===

查斯特科特来的家庭教师，似乎对自己消息所受的接待惊愕不已。

“你们不认得那个人，”他叫道。“这绝不是少年人一时荒唐。我打听过，得知此人无异于一个恶棍。他若做雅各布党人，是为图利；他若娶格雷维尔小姐，是为图她的产业。”

“再说，像你这样的教书匠，又懂得什么世家绅士的人品？”

这话说得刺耳，可是出诸基德先生之口，声气欢快，眼里又是闪闪的促狭，本可以当作一句爽直的戏言揭过去。无奈家庭教师没有那份闲心。怒火涌上他的脸，他鼓起鼻息，像一头即将冲阵的公牛。不等他回嘴，克里斯托弗爵士倒做了他的同盟。准男爵一屁股扔回椅子里，双肘往桌上一拄。

“不过这人说得没错，”他说。“杰克·诺里斯是条下作的猎狗，这话我可以起誓。杰克倒不是那种毛躁的无赖——他脑袋从来冷静，心肠却冷得像条鳕鱼。他的酒量好得出奇——这我得替他说句公道话——可这该死的家伙，专从上等人的豪爽弱点里渔利。他赌起来不要命，可是爱牌爱得像讼师，不像绅士，还借此大发一笔。不，见他的鬼！杰克不是个正派人。他要那姑娘，是为她的财产；他唱雅各布党的调子，是因为他瞧出里头有卑鄙的进项。我恨透了他那只长鼻子和那双刻薄眼。”


===Original===

“You hear?” cried the tutor who had half risen from his seat in his excitement. “You hear the verdict of an honest man!”

“You seem to know him well, Kit,” said Lord Cornbury, smiling.

“Know him! Gad, I have had some chances. We were birched together at Eton, and dwelt in the same stairway at Christ Church. I once rode a match with him on the Port Meadow and bled him for a hundred guineas, but he has avenged himself a thousandfold since then at the Bibury meetings. He may be Lord High Chancellor when I am in the Fleet, but the Devil will get him safe enough at the end.”

Lord Cornbury looked grave, Mr. Kyd wagged a moralising head.

“The thing has gone too far to stop,” said the former. Then to the tutor: “What would you have me do?”

===Chinese===

“听见了么？”家庭教师激动得半站起来，喊道。“听见这位老实人下的断语了么！”

“你对他倒知之甚深啊，基特，”科恩伯里含笑说。

“知之甚深！老天，机会有的是。当年在伊顿（Eton），我们一处挨藤条；在基督堂学院（Christ Church），住同一条楼梯。我曾在波特草甸（Port Meadow）同他赛过一场马，宰了他一百几尼；可打那以后，他在比伯里（Bibury）赛会上赢回去的，何止千倍。哪天我进了弗利特监狱（the Fleet），他没准儿已做到大法官；可到头来，魔鬼准会稳稳当当把他收了去。”

科恩伯里神色凝重，基德先生摇着一颗说教的头。

“事已至此，拦不住了，”前者说。又转向家庭教师：“你要我做什么呢？”

===Original===

The visitor’s uncouth hands were twisting themselves in a frenzy of appeal.

“My mistress at Chastlecote is old and bedridden, my charge is but a boy, and Miss Grevel has no relatives nearer than Dorset. I come to you as the leading gentleman in this shire and an upright and public-spirited nobleman, and I implore you to save that poor pretty child from her folly. They have gone north, so let us follow. It may not be too late to prevent the marriage.”

“Ah, but it will be,” said Mr. Kyd. “They can find a hedge-parson any hour of the day to do the job for a guinea and a pot of ale.”

“There is a chance, a hope, and, oh sir, I beseech you to pursue it.”

“Would you have me mount and ride on the track of the fugitives?” Lord Cornbury asked.

“Yes, my lord, and without delay. Grant me a chair to sleep an hour in, and I am ready for any labour. We can take the road before daybreak. It would facilitate our task if your lordship would lend me a horse better fitted for my weight.”

===Chinese===

来客那双粗笨的手绞来绞去，恳求得近乎狂乱。

“我在查斯特科特的女主人年迈卧病，我照管的孩子不过是个少年，格雷维尔小姐最亲的亲属也远在多塞特（Dorset）。我投奔大人，因为您是本郡头一份的绅士，一位正直而有公心的贵族；我恳求您救救那可怜的美人儿，莫叫她毁在自己的痴愚里。他们往北去了，我们便追上去。拦下这桩婚事，或许还来得及。”

“唉，只怕是来不及了，”基德先生说。“花一个几尼外加一壶麦酒，随便哪个时辰都能寻着个篱笆牧师（hedge-parson），替他们把事儿办了。”

“总有一线机会，一线希望。唉，先生，我求您抓住它。”

“你要我上马，去追赶那对私奔的人么？”科恩伯里勋爵问。

“正是，大人，而且刻不容缓。赏我一把椅子睡上一个钟头，我便什么辛苦都担得。天亮之前咱们就能上路。大人若肯借我一匹更配得上我这身量的马，这事便更顺手了。”

===Original===

The naiveness of the request made a momentary silence. Then in spite of himself Alastair laughed. This importunate usher was on the same mission as himself, that mission which an hour earlier had conclusively failed. To force their host into activity was the aim of both, but one whom a summons from a Prince had not moved was not likely to yield to an invitation to pursue a brace of green lovers. Yet he respected the man’s ardour, though he had set him down from his looks as a boor and an oddity; and regretted his laugh, when a distraught face was turned towards him, solemn and reproachful like a persecuted dog’s.

Lord Cornbury’s eyes were troubled and his hands fidgeted with a dish of filberts. He seemed divided between irritation at a preposterous demand and his natural kindliness.

“You are a faithful if importunate friend, sir. By the way, I have not your name.”

“Johnson, my lord﻿—Samuel Johnson. But my name matters nothing.”

“I have heard it before.﻿ ﻿… Nay, I remember.﻿ ﻿… Was it Mr. Murray who spoke of it? Tell me, sir, have you not published certain writings?”

===Chinese===

这请求的天真，使满座一时无言。随后阿拉斯泰尔不由自主地笑出声来。这位纠缠不清的教书先生，竟与他负有同一使命——而那使命，一个钟头之前刚被断然回绝。逼这宅子的主人行动起来，是两人共同的目的；可是，王子殿下的召唤都催不动的人，还指望他会应一场追捕青涩恋人的邀约么？然而他敬重此人的热忱——虽说凭那副尊容，他本已把他归作粗汉兼怪物；待到一张失魂落魄的脸转向他，庄严而含着责备，像一条受迫害的狗，他便懊悔自己方才那一笑了。

科恩伯里的眼睛乱了，两只手只顾摆弄一碟榛子。

“你是个忠心耿耿的朋友，只是急切得叫人受不住，先生。对了，我还不曾请教尊姓大名。”

“约翰逊，大人——塞缪尔·约翰逊（Samuel Johnson）。不过在下的名字无足轻重。”

“这名字我听说过。……不，我想起来了。……是默里先生（Mr. Murray）提起过的吧？告诉我，先生，你可曾刊行过一些著述？”

===Original===

“Sir, I have made a living by scribbling.”

“Poetry, I think. Was there not a piece on the morals of Town﻿—in the manner of Juvenal?”

“Bawdy, I’ll be bound,” put in Mr. Kyd. He seemed suddenly to have grown rather drunk and spoke with a hiccup.

The tutor looked so uncouth a figure for a poet that Alastair laughed again. But the poor man’s mind was far from humour, for his earnestness increased with his hearers’ cynicism.

“Oh, my lord,” he cried, “what does it matter what I am or what wretched books I have fathered? I urge you to a most instant duty﻿—to save a noble young lady from a degrading marriage. I press for your decision, for the need is desperate.”

“But what can I do, Mr. Johnson? She is of age, and they have broken no law. I cannot issue a warrant and hale them back to Oxfordshire. If they are not yet wed I have no authority to dissuade, for I am not a kinsman, not even a friend. I cannot forbid the banns, for I have no certain knowledge of any misdeeds of this Sir John. I have no locus, as the lawyers say, for my meddling. But in any case the errand must be futile, for if you are right and she has fled with him, they will be married long ere we can overtake them. What you ask from me is folly.”

===Chinese===

“先生，在下就靠涂鸦糊口。”

“诗歌，我记得。是不是有一篇咏京城风化的——尤维纳利斯（Juvenal）一路的笔法？”

“我敢保是伤风败俗的玩意儿，”基德先生插嘴道。他仿佛突然醉得厉害了，说话直打嗝。

家庭教师那副模样，实在不像个诗人，阿拉斯泰尔又笑了。可是这可怜人的心离玩笑有十万八千里——听的人越冷嘲，他的恳切越深。

“唉，大人，”他叫道，“我是什么人、又造下过什么破书，有什么要紧？我恳请大人担下一件刻不容缓的义务——把一位高贵的少女从一桩辱没她的婚姻里救出来。我催请大人决断，因为事情已到绝境。”

“可是我能做什么呢，约翰逊先生？她已成年，他们也没有犯法。我不能签发拘票，把他们押回牛津郡。他们若还未成婚，我也无权劝阻，因为我非亲非故，连朋友也算不上。我不能对结婚预告提出异议，因为我并没有掌握这位约翰爵士任何劣迹的实据。正如律师们所说，我毫无置喙的名分（locus）。况且无论如何，这趟追逐必是徒劳：你若说得不错，她确是与他私奔，那不等我们追上，他们早已成婚。你向我求的，是一件傻事。”


===Original===

The tutor’s face changed from lumpish eagerness to a lumpish gloom.

“There is a chance,” he muttered. “And in the matter of saving souls a chance is enough for a Christian.”

“Then my Christianity falls short of yours, sir,” replied Lord Cornbury sharply.

The tutor let his dismal eyes dwell on the others. They soon left Mr. Kyd’s face, stayed longer on Alastair’s and came to rest on Sir Christopher’s, which was little less gloomy than his own.

“You, sir,” he said, “you know the would-be bridegroom. Will you assist me to rescue the bride?”

===Chinese===

家庭教师那张脸，从一副笨拙的急切，转为笨拙的阴郁。

“总有一线机会，”他喃喃道。“救人灵魂的事，一线机会，在基督徒就已足够。”

“那我的基督徒成色，可比不得先生，”科恩伯里毫不客气地答道。

家庭教师让那双凄惶的眼睛在众人脸上逡巡。目光不久便离开基德先生的脸，在阿拉斯泰尔脸上停留得久些，最后落到克里斯托弗爵士脸上——那张脸的阴沉，比他自己的好不了多少。

“您，先生，”他说，“您认得那位准新郎。可愿助我一臂，救出那位新娘？”

===Original===

The baronet for a moment did not reply and hope flickered in the other’s eyes. Then it died, for the young man brought down his fist on the table with an oath.

“No, by God. If my lord thinks the business not for him, ’tis a million times too delicate for me. You’re an honest man, Mr. usher, and shall hear my reason. I loved Miss Grevel, and for two years I dared to hope. Last April she dismissed me and I had the wit to see that ’twas final. What kind of figure would I cut galloping the shires after a scornful mistress who has chosen another? I’d ride a hundred miles to see Jack Norreys’ neck wrung, but you will not catch me fluttering near the honeypot of his lady.”

“You think only of your pride, sir, and not of the poor girl.”

The tutor, realising the futility of his mission, rose to his feet, upsetting a decanter with an awkward elbow. The misadventure, which at an earlier stage would have acutely embarrassed him, now passed unnoticed. He seemed absorbed in his own reflections, and had suddenly won a kind of rude dignity. As he stood among them Alastair was amazed alike at his shabbiness and his self-possession.

“You will stay the night here, sir? The hour is late and a bed is at your disposal.”

“I thank you, my lord, but my duties do not permit of sleep. I return to Chastlecote, and if I can get no helpers I must e’en seek for the lady alone. I am debtor to your lordship for a hospitality upon which I will not further encroach. May I beg the favour of a light to the stable?”

===Chinese===

准男爵一时没有作声，另一人眼中闪起一线希望的光。随即光灭了，因为年轻人一拳砸在桌上，咒骂了一声。

“不，上帝作证。大人既觉得此事不便插手，那于我便要紧一千倍。你是个老实人，教书先生，我就让你听听我的缘由。我爱过格雷维尔小姐，两年来一直存着痴心妄想。今年四月她回绝了我，我还有几分头脑，看得出此事已成定局。要我纵马驰过一郡又一郡，去追一位鄙薄我、选中了别人的心上人，我算什么人？要我驰上一百英里去看杰克·诺里斯被绞断脖子，我干；可你们休想逮着我在他那位心上人的蜜罐旁边扑腾。”

“先生心里想的只有您的体面，全没有那可怜的姑娘。”

家庭教师明白此行已是徒劳，站起身来，一只笨拙的胳膊肘带翻了一只细颈酒瓶。这桩小小的意外，若在早些时候准叫他窘迫万分，此刻却浑不在意。他仿佛全然沉入了自己的思索，忽然赢得了一种粗粝的尊严。他立在众人之间，阿拉斯泰尔对他的寒酸与他的镇定，同样感到惊异。

“您今夜就在这里歇下可好，先生？夜已深了，有现成的床供您安歇。”

“多谢大人，但在下的职责容不得睡眠。我这就回查斯特科特去；若寻不着帮手，便只好独力去寻那位小姐。大人的盛情，在下受之有愧，不敢再多叨扰。可否乞大人赏个灯火，照我去马厩？”

===Original===

Alastair picked up a branched candlestick and preceded the tutor into the windless night. The latter stumbled often, for he seemed purblind, but the other had no impulse to laugh, for toward this grotesque he had conceived a curious respect. The man, like himself, was struggling against fatted ease, striving to break a fence of prudence on behalf of an honourable hazard.

Kyd’s servant brought the horse, refreshed by a supper of oats, and it was Alastair’s arm which helped the unwieldy horseman to the saddle.

“God prosper you!” Alastair said, as he fitted a clumsy foot into a stirrup.

The man woke to the consciousness of the other’s presence.

“You wish me well, sir? Will you come with me? I desire a colleague, for I am a sedentary man with no skill in travel.”

“I only rest here for a night. I am a soldier on a mission which does not permit of delay.”

===Chinese===

阿拉斯泰尔端起一盏枝形烛台，在前头引着家庭教师走进无风的夜色。后者一路频频踉跄——他看去近乎半盲——前者却再没有一丝要笑的意思，因为对这个怪人，他已生出一种奇异的敬意。此人与他一样，正在同养肥了的安逸搏斗；为了一个体面的冒险，正奋力冲破一道谨慎的藩篱。

基德的仆人牵来了马——一顿燕麦晚餐喂得它重新来了精神——是阿拉斯泰尔的手臂，把那笨重的骑手扶上了马鞍。

“愿上帝助你成功！”阿拉斯泰尔说着，把一只笨拙的脚替他套进马镫。

那人才恍然觉出身旁这个人的存在。

“先生祝我顺利？您肯同我一道去么？我正想寻一位同伴，我是个久坐案头的人，不谙行旅。”

“我只是在此歇宿一夜。我是个军人，身负使命，不容耽搁。”

===Original===

“Then God speed us both!” The strange fellow pulled off his hat like a parson pronouncing benediction, before he lumbered into the dark of the avenue.

Alastair turned to find Kyd behind him. He was exchanging jocularities with his servant.

“Saw ye ever such a physiog, Edom?” he cried. “Dominies are getting crouse, for the body was wanting my lord to up and ride with him like a postboy after some quean that’s ta’en the jee. He’s about as blate as a Cameronian preacher. My lord was uncommon patient with him. D’you not think so, Captain Maclean?”

“The man may be uncouth, but he has a stout heart and a very noble spirit. I take off my hat to his fidelity.”

The reply changed Mr. Kyd’s mood from scorn to a melting sentiment.

“Ay, but you’re right. I hadn’t thought of that. It’s a noble-hearted creature, and we would all be better if we were liker him. Courage, did you say? The man with that habit of body, that jogs all day on a horse for the sake of a woman that has done nothing but clout his lugs, is a hero. I wish I had drunk his health.”

===Chinese===

“那么愿上帝助你我两人成功！”这古怪的家伙像牧师颁赐祝福一般摘下帽子，随即笨重地挪进林荫道的黑暗里去了。

阿拉斯泰尔回过身，见基德就在身后，正同他的仆人说笑。

“你几时见过这样的长相，以东（Edom）？”他嚷道。“如今教书匠们都放肆起来了——那家伙竟要咱们大人翻身上马，像个驿童似的去追一个使性子出逃的妞儿。他那股腼腆劲儿，赛过一位卡梅伦派牧师。大人对他可真是少见的有耐心。你说是不是，麦克莱恩上尉（Captain Maclean）？”

“此人或许粗野，却有一颗坚强的心、一种十分高贵的魂灵。我要摘下帽子，敬他的忠义。”

这一句回答，把基德先生的心绪从讥诮化成了一片感动的柔情。

“是啊，你说得对。我倒没想到这一层。这是个心地高贵的人，我们个个都学得他一分，便都会更好些。勇敢——你说的是这个么？那样一副身板的人，为了一个只会掴他耳光的女人，成天在马背上颠簸，这就是英雄了。可惜方才没敬他一杯健康。”

## XVIII In Which Three Gentlemen Confess Their Nakedness / 十八、三位绅士自陈其窘

===Original===
Fresh horses were found, and at four in the morning, four hours before daylight in that murky weather, Alastair and Johnson left the inn. At the first crossroads Midwinter joined them.
“Set your mind at ease about Sir John,” he said. “He will travel securely to the Cherwell side, and none but the Spoonbills will know of his journey. I think you have read him right, sir, and that he is a prosy fellow who by accident has slipped into roguery and will return gladly to his natural rut. But in case you are mistaken, he will be overlooked by my people, for we are strong in that countryside. Be advised, sir, and ride gently, for you have no bodily strength to spare, and your master will not welcome a sick man.”
“Do you ride to Derby with us?” Alastair asked.
“I have business on that road and will convey you thus far,” was the answer.

===Chinese===
换上了新马。凌晨四点——在那阴晦的天气里，离天亮还有四个钟头——阿拉斯泰尔和约翰逊便离开了客栈。到了第一个岔路口，仲冬加入了进来。

「约翰爵士的事，你只管放心，」他说。「他会一路安稳地走到切尔韦尔河一带，除了篦鹭会，无人会知晓他这一程。我看你把他的底细估量对了，先生：他本是个絮叨乏味的正经人，偶然失足滑进了歹人的营生，如今必是巴不得回到他那本分的老辙里去。不过万一你估量错了，我的人也会一路照看他，因为那一带乡野，我们势力雄厚。听我一句劝，先生，把马骑得从容些——你身上已没有多余的气力可使，你的主上可不欢迎一个病号。」

「你同我们一道去德比么？」阿拉斯泰尔问。

「我恰有事要走那条道，可以送你们这一程。」他答道。

===Original===
It was a morning when the whole earth and sky seemed suffused in moisture. Fog strung its beads on their clothes, every hedgerow tree dripped clammily, the roads were knee-deep in mud, floodwater lay in leaden streaks in the hollows of flat fields, each sluggish brook was a torrent, and at intervals the air would distil into a drenching shower. Alastair’s body was still weary, but his heart was lightened. He had finished now with dalliance and was back at his old trade; and for the moment the memory of Claudia made only a warm background to the hopes of a soldier. Little daggers of doubt stabbed his thoughts﻿—he had sacrificed another day and night in his chase of Sir John, and the Prince had now been at Derby the better part of forty hours without that report which he had promised. But surely, he consoled himself, so slight a delay could matter nothing; an army which had marched triumphant to the heart of England, and had already caused the souls of its enemies to faint, could not falter when the goal was within sight. But the anxiety hung like a malaise about the fringes of his temper and caused him now and then to spur his horse fifty yards beyond his companion.
The road they travelled ran to Derby from the southwest, and its deep ruts showed the heavy traffic it had lately borne. By it coaches, wagons and every variety of pack and riding horse had carried the timid folks of Derbyshire into sanctuaries beyond the track of the Highland army.
