年轻人忽然来了一个不祥的灵机。他放声唱起《赤身人》之歌，嗓音放得洪亮，好教这曲子在居所之外也听得见。吉普赛人一时似乎没有听见；随即皱起眉头，仿佛一段不愉快的记忆被惊醒；随即，他猛然全然醒了过来。

「地狱和天罚！」他狂喊。「哪个妖巫教你这个的？住口，这该死的玩意儿，」说着他一拳打在唱歌人的脸上。

接着，他的眼睛看见了那块表，耳朵也捉住了帮工约翰磨碾声的止息。他的疯病轰然复燃，把戒指忘得一干二净，野猫似的朝囚徒扑去。他把全无抵抗之力的囚徒从长凳上拖起，掼过桌面，晚饭的残局哗啦啦砸了一地。随即他撂下他，抢到木隔板前，把它整个扯开。就这样露出来的黑洞里，仿佛升起一缕稀薄的灰雾，那声响像犬舍里群狗的狺狺撕咬。

===Original===
“John is hungry,” he cried. “I have kept you waiting, my darling, but your meat is ready,” and he was back clutching his prisoner’s middle.

The despair and apathy of the earlier hours had gone, and Alastair steeled himself to fight for his life. The gypsy’s strength was always respectable and now his mania made it prepotent. The young man managed to get his manacled ankles crooked in a leg of the table, but they were plucked away with a dislocating wrench. His head grated on the floor as he was dragged towards the pit. And then he saw a chance, for the rope that bound his wrists caught in a staple fixed in the floor, apparently to make an anchorage for a chain that had worked an ancient windlass. The gypsy pulled savagely, but the good hemp held, and he was forced to drop the body and examine the obstacle. Alastair noted that beyond the pit was a naked dripping wall of cliff, and that the space between the edge and the walls of the shed inclined downward, so that anything that once reached that slope would be easily rolled into the abyss. Death was very near him and yet he could not despair. He lifted up his voice in a great shout for help. A thousand echoes rang in the pit, and following on them came the gypsy’s crazy cackle.

“Do not fear, pretty darling. John’s arms are soft bedding,” and he dragged him over the lip of stone beyond which the slope ran to the darkness.

===Chinese===
「约翰饿了，」他喊。「叫您久等了，我的心肝，您的肉备好了。」说着他又扑回来，一把箍住囚徒的腰身。

先前那几个钟头的绝望与麻木都已散尽，阿拉斯泰尔横下心来，要为性命一搏。吉普赛人的膂力本就不容小觑，此刻疯癫更给它添了倍增的威势。年轻人好容易把戴镣的双踝勾住一条桌腿，却被一记几乎卸胯的猛拽生生扯开。他被朝深坑拖去，头颅擦着地板嘎嘎作响。就在这时他看见一线生机——捆他双腕的绳索勾进了地板上一枚扒钉，那钉子看那样子，当年是拴一条铁链、绞动一架老绞盘用的。吉普赛人凶蛮地拽，上好的麻绳却吃住了劲，他只得撂下人，回头来对付那障碍。阿拉斯泰尔注意到，坑的那一边是一面湿淋淋的赤裸石壁，坑沿与棚屋墙壁之间的地面朝下倾，凡是一旦上了那道斜坡的东西，轻易便会滚进深渊。死亡离他这样近，他却偏偏绝望不起来。他放开嗓门，高声呼救。千百重回声在坑里激荡，回声之后紧跟着的，是吉普赛人癫狂的咯咯怪笑。

「别怕，漂亮的心肝。约翰的臂弯是软和的铺盖。」他把他拖过那道石坎——坎外的斜坡，一路斜进黑暗里。

===Original===
Once again by a miracle his foot caught. This time it was only a snag of rock, but it had a rough edge to it, and by the mercy of God, the bonds at his ankles had been already frayed. The gypsy, who had him by the shoulders and arms, tugged frantically, and the friction of the stone’s edge severed the last strands. Suddenly Alastair found his ankles free, and with a desperate scramble tried to rise. But his feet were cramped and numb and he could not find a stand. A tug from the gypsy brought him to the very edge of the abyss. But the incident had wakened hope, and once again he made the vault ring with a cry for help.

It was answered. The dim place suddenly blazed with light, and there was a sound of men’s voices. For an instant the gypsy loosed his hold to stare, and then with a scream resumed his efforts. But in that instant Alastair’s feet had found on the very brink a crack of stone, which enabled him to brace his legs and resist. The thing was trivial and he could not hold out long, but the purchase was sufficient to prevent that last heave from hurling him into the void.

The gypsy seemed suddenly to change his mind. He let the young man’s shoulders drop, so that he fell huddled by the edge, plucked the long shagreen-handled knife from his belt and struck at his neck. But the blow never fell. For in the same fraction of time something bright quivered through the air, and struck deep in his throat. The man gurgled, then grew limp like a sack, and dropped back on the ground. Then with a feeble clawing at the air he rolled over the brink, struck the side twice, and dropped till the noise of his fall was lost in the moaning of the measureless deep.

===Chinese===
又是一个奇迹，他的脚勾住了什么。这回不过是一截岩石的断茬，却带着一道粗粝的棱；也托上帝的慈悲，他脚踝上的绑绳本已磨得半损。抓住他肩膀和手臂的吉普赛人发疯般猛拽，石棱的摩擦割断了最后几缕绳。冷不丁地，阿拉斯泰尔发觉双脚得了自由，他拼死一挣，想要站起。可双脚抽麻僵硬，寻不见一个立脚处。吉普赛人一拽，把他拽到了深渊的口沿上。但这一下勾起了希望，他再次放声呼救，喊得整个洞窟嗡嗡回响。

呼救有了回应。昏昧之处霎时火光大亮，又起了人语的嘈杂。吉普赛人一怔，松手张望了一瞬，随即一声尖叫，重新发力。可就在那一瞬里，阿拉斯泰尔的双脚已在深渊口沿上找到一道石缝，得以绷住双腿，抗住拖拉。这凭借微末，也支撑不了多久，但这一点借力已够挡住那最后一掀，不教他被掀进虚空里去。

吉普赛人似乎突然改了主意。他撒开年轻人的肩膀，由他瘫作一团倒在坑沿边，自己从腰间抽出那柄长长的鲨革柄刀，朝他的脖颈便刺。可这一刀终究没有落下。就在那同一瞬，一点亮光颤巍巍掠过空中，深深钉进了他自己的咽喉。那人咕噜了一声，随即像条空口袋般瘫软，仰倒在地。而后他朝空中有气无力地抓了两把，翻过坑沿，撞了两下洞壁，直坠下去，直到坠落之声没入那无底深渊的呜咽。

===Original===
Alastair lay sick and trembling, not daring to move, for his heels were overhanging the void. A hand seized him, a strong hand; and though he cried out in terror it dragged him up the slope and into the room.﻿ ﻿… The intense glare stabbed his eyes and he had the same choking nausea as when he had been felled in the hut. Then he came suddenly out of the fit of horror and saw himself on the settle, ready to weep from weariness, but sane again and master of himself.

A dark friendly face was looking down at him.

“You may travel the world’s roads for a hundred years,” said the Spainneach, “and never be nearer death. I warned you, Sir Sandy. You have been overlong in the South.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔躺着，恶心发抖，不敢动弹——他的脚跟正悬空在深渊之上。一只手抓住了他，一只强有力的手；他惊怖得失声大叫，那手却把他拖上斜坡，拖进屋里。……强光刺得他睁不开眼，一阵噎人的恶心涌上来，同在小屋里被击倒时一般无二。随后他猛然从那阵惊怖里脱身出来，看见自己已坐在长凳上，困乏得几乎要落泪，但神智清明，又做了自己的主人。

一张黝黑而友善的脸正俯视着他。

「您就是走遍天下的路，走上它一百年，」西班牙人说，「也再没有比这回更贴近死亡的。我警告过您的，桑迪爵士。您在南方待得太久了。」

## IV Mr. Kyd of Greyhouses / 四、格雷豪斯的基德先生

===Original===
Next morning Alastair rode west, and for the better part of a fortnight was beyond Severn. He met Sir Watkin at Wynnstay and Mr. Savage in Lanthony vale, and then penetrated to the Pembroke coast where he conferred with fisherfolk and shy cloaked men who gave appointments by the tide at nightfall. His task was no longer diplomacy, but the ordinary intelligence service of war, and he was the happier inasmuch as he the better understood it. If fortune favoured elsewhere, he had made plans for a French landing in a friendly countryside to kindle the West and take in flank the defences of London. Now, that errand done, his duty was with all speed to get him back to the North.

On a sharp noon in the first week of November he recrossed Severn and came into Worcestershire, having slept at Ludlow the night before. His plan was to return as he had come, by the midlands and Northumberland, for he knew the road and which inns were safe to lie at. Of the doings of his Prince he had heard nothing, and he fretted every hour at the lack of news. As a trained soldier with some experience of war, he distrusted profoundly the military wisdom of Charles’s advisers, and feared daily to hear of some blunder which would cancel all that had already been won.

He rode hard, hoping to sleep in Staffordshire and next day join the road which he had travelled south three weeks before. An unobtrusive passenger known to none, knowing none, he took little pains to scan the visages of those he met. It was therefore with some surprise that, as he sat in the taproom of an alehouse at Chifney, he saw a face which woke some recollection.

===Chinese===
次日清晨，阿拉斯泰尔策马西行，此后近半月的光景都在塞文河（Severn）以西。他在温斯泰（Wynnstay）会见了沃特金爵士（Sir Watkin），又在兰索尼谷（Lanthony vale）会见了萨维奇先生（Mr. Savage），随后一直深入到彭布罗克（Pembroke）海岸，与渔人以及那些怯生生的披斗篷的汉子们密议——他们相约以夜里的潮水为号。他的差使已不再是外交周旋，而是战场上寻常的谍报功夫，他反倒更为快意，因为这门营生他更拿手。倘若他处时运相济，他已筹划停当：让法军在一片友好的乡野登陆，燃起西部烽火，侧击伦敦的守备。如今使命既了，他的本分便是兼程赶回北方。

十一月第一周一个凛冽的正午，他再度渡过塞文河，进入伍斯特郡（Worcestershire），前一夜宿在勒德洛（Ludlow）。他打算循来路北返——取道中部诸郡与诺森伯兰（Northumberland），因为这条路他认得，也晓得哪些客栈可以安心投宿。至于王子的动静，他一无所闻，为着消息断绝，时时刻刻焦灼难安。身为受过操练、见过些战阵的军人，他对查理身边谋臣的兵略之才深为怀疑，日日唯恐传来什么昏招的噩耗，把已到手的胜果一笔勾销。

他纵马急行，指望当夜宿在斯塔福德郡（Staffordshire），次日便能接上三个星期前南下走的那条大道。他是个不惹眼的过客，无人识得他，他也不识得人，因此对于途中所遇的面孔，他向来懒得多看一眼。正因如此，当他在奇夫尼（Chifney）一间麦酒肆的酒堂里落座时，忽然瞥见一张唤起旧忆的面孔，便不免有些诧异。

===Original===
It was that of a tall, thin and very swarthy man who was engaged in grating a nutmeg into a pot of mulled ale. His clothes had the shabby finery of a broken-down gentleman, but the air of a minor stage-player which they suggested was sharply contradicted by his face. That was grave, strong almost to hardness, and with eyes that would have dictated if they had not brooded. He gave Alastair good day as he entered, and then continued his occupation in such a way that the light from the window fell very clearly upon his features. The purpose, which involved a change of position, was so evident that Alastair’s attention was engaged, and he regarded him over the edge of his tankard.

The memory was baffling. France, London, Rome﻿—he fitted nowhere. It seemed a far-back recollection, and not a coincidence of his present journey. Then the man raised his head, and his sad eyes looked for a moment at the window. The gesture Alastair had seen before﻿—very long before﻿—in Morvern. Into the picture swam other details: a ketch anchored, a sea-loch, a seafarer who sang so that the heart broke, a cluster of boys huddled on hot sand listening to a stranger’s tales.

“The Spainneach!” he exclaimed.

===Chinese===
那是个瘦高个、面色黧黑至深的男子，正往一罐热麦酒里磨着肉豆蔻。他的衣裳带着破落绅士的寒酸体面，那副行头本会教人以为是个末流戏子，他的脸却把这印象断然推翻。那张脸凝重，坚毅得近乎冷硬，一双眼睛若非深于沉思，原是发号施令的眼睛。阿拉斯泰尔进门时他道了声日安，随后继续手里的活计，只是挪了挪身子，好教窗口的光清清楚楚落在自己脸上。这层用意既然如此明显，阿拉斯泰尔的注意便被勾住了，隔着酒罐的边沿打量起他来。

这记忆却教人捉摸不透。法兰西、伦敦、罗马——都对不上号。这似乎是年代久远的旧忆，并非此番旅程中的萍水相逢。接着那人抬起头来，一双忧郁的眼睛朝窗外望了片刻。这个姿态阿拉斯泰尔从前见过——很久很久以前——在莫尔文（Morvern）。画面里又浮出别的细节：一艘下锚的双桅小船，一道峡湾，一个唱歌唱得人心碎的航海人，还有一群孩子挤在滚烫的沙滩上，听一个外乡人讲故事。

「西班牙人！」他失声叫道。

===Original===
The man looked up with a smile on his dark face and spoke in Gaelic. “Welcome, heart’s darling,” he said﻿—the endearment used long ago to the child who swam out to the foreign ship for a prize of raisins. “I have followed you for three days, and this morning was told of your inquiries, divined your route, and took a shortcut to meet you here.”

The picture had filled out. Alastair remembered the swarthy foreigner who came yearly at the tail of the harvest to enlist young men for the armies of Spain or France or the Emperor﻿—who did not brag or bribe or unduly gild the prospect, but who, less by his tongue than by his eyes, drew the Morvern youth to wars from which few returned. An honest man, his father had named this Spainneach, but as secret in his ways as the woodcock blown shoreward by the October gales.

“You have a message for me?” he asked, thinking of Cornbury.

===Chinese===
那人抬起头，黧黑的脸上含着笑，说起盖尔语来。「欢迎，心肝宝贝，」他说——这句亲昵话，是当年对一个孩子说的；那孩子为赢一包葡萄干的彩头，曾游水登上外邦人的船。「我跟着你走了三天，今早听人说起你的打探，猜出了你的路线，便抄近路赶到这里候你。」

画面这下齐全了。阿拉斯泰尔记起那个黧黑的外邦人：每年秋收的尾声，他到这地方来，为西班牙、法兰西或是皇帝的军队招募年轻人——他不高谈阔论，不行贿买路，也不把前程涂抹得金光灿灿，却凭着那双眼睛而非那条舌头，把莫尔文的青年一茬茬引进烽火，能归来的寥寥无几。他父亲曾说这位「西班牙人」是个老实人，只是行事隐秘，像十月狂风里被吹上岸来的山鹬。

「你有话带给我？」他问，心里想着科恩伯里。

===Original===
“A message﻿—but from a quarter no weightier than my own head. You have been over long in the South, Sir Sandy.” The name had been the title given by his boyish comrades to their leader, and its use by this grave man brought to the chance meeting something of the intimacy of home.

“That’s my own notion,” he replied. “But I am now by way of curing the fault.”

“Then ride fast, and ride by the shortest road. There’s sore need of you up beyond.”

“You have news,” Alastair cried eagerly. “Has his Highness marched yet?”

===Chinese===
「有话——不过来头未必比我自个儿这颗脑袋更重。你在南方耽搁得太久了，桑迪爵士（Sir Sandy）。」这称呼是当年一帮少年伙伴赠与他们头领的尊号，此刻出自这位严肃汉子之口，给这场偶遇平添了几分家乡的亲切。

「这也正是我自己的想头，」他答道，「不过如今我正要来补这个过失。」

「那就快马加鞭，拣最近的路走。北边那边正急需你呢。」

「你有消息，」阿拉斯泰尔急切地喊道。「殿下开拔了么？」

===Original===
“This very day he has passed the Border.”

“How﻿—by what route﻿—in what strength?”

“No great increase. He looks for that on the road.”

“Then he goes by Carlisle?”

===Chinese===
「就在今天，他已越过边境。」

「怎么走的——取哪条路——兵力几何？」

「没有多少增益。他指望在路上补足。」

「那么他是取道卡莱尔（Carlisle）了？」

===Original===
The Spaniard nodded. “And Wade lies at Newcastle,” he said.

Alastair brought down his fist on the board so hard that the ale lipped from the other’s tankard.

“The Devil take such blundering! Now he has the enemy on his unprotected flank, when he might have destroyed him and won that victory on English soil which is the key to all things. Wade is old and doited, but he will soon have Cumberland behind him. Who counselled this foolishness? Not his Highness, I’ll warrant.”

The Spaniard shrugged his shoulders. “No. His Highness would have made a beeline for Newcastle. But his captains put their faith in Lancashire, and would have the honest men of North England in their ranks before they risked a battle. They picture them as waiting, each with a thousand armed followers, till the first tartans are south of Shap, and then rushing to the standard.”

===Chinese===
西班牙人点了点头。「而韦德（Wade）正屯兵纽卡斯尔。」他说。

阿拉斯泰尔一拳砸在桌板上，震得对方罐中的麦酒都泼溅出来。

「叫魔鬼把这群糊涂虫收了去！如今敌人正悬在他不设防的侧翼，而本来他尽可以将那老朽一举荡灭，在英格兰的土地上赢下那场关乎全局的胜仗。韦德老迈昏聩，可坎伯兰（Cumberland）很快就要到他的背后。是谁出的这种馊主意？我敢担保，绝不是殿下。」

西班牙人耸了耸肩。「不是。殿下本会直扑纽卡斯尔。可他手下的将官们一心指望兰开夏（Lancashire），非要先把北英格兰的忠义之士收进队伍里，才肯冒险一战。在他们的想象里，那些人个个领着一千武装的部曲，只等第一批格子呢裙越过沙普（Shap）南下，便蜂拥来投王旗。」

===Original===
Alastair, his brows dark with irritation, strode up and down the floor.

“The fools have it the wrong way round. England will not rise to fight a battle, but only when a battle has been won. Wade at Newcastle was a sovran chance﻿—and we have missed it. Blind! Blind! You are right, my friend. Not a second must I lose in pushing north to join my Prince. There are no trained soldiers with him save Lord George, and he had no more than a boyish year in the Royals.﻿ ﻿… You say he travels by Shap?”

The Spaniard nodded. “And your course, Sir Sandy, must be through West England. Ride for Preston, which all Scots invasions must pass. Whitchurch﻿—Tarporley﻿—Warrington are your stages. See, I will make you a plan.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔眉头紧锁，怒气郁结，在地板上来回踱步。

「这些蠢货把事情整个想反了。英格兰不会为了一场还没打的仗起事，只有打赢了一仗，她才会起来。韦德屯兵纽卡斯尔，本是天赐良机——我们却错过了。瞎了眼！瞎了眼！你说得对，我的朋友。我必须分秒不失地赶北上，去我王子的身边。他手下没有一个受过操练的军人，除了乔治勋爵（Lord George），而他在王家团里也只当过短短一年的娃娃兵。……你是说，他取道沙普？」

西班牙人点头。「而你的路线，桑迪爵士，必须穿过英格兰西部。直奔普雷斯顿（Preston），苏格兰人进军，哪一回也绕不开那里。惠特彻奇（Whitchurch）——塔波利（Tarporley）——沃灵顿（Warrington），这是你的三站。来，我给你画个路数。」

===Original===
On the dust of a barrel he traced the route, while Alastair did up the straps of his coat and drew on his riding gloves. His horse was brought, the lawing paid, and as the young man mounted the other stood by his stirrup.

“Where do you go?” Alastair asked.

“Northward, like swallows in spring. But not yet awhile. I have still errands in these parts.”

An ostler inspected the horse’s shoes, and Alastair sat whistling impatiently through his teeth. The tune which came to him was Midwinter’s catch of “The Naked Men.” The Spaniard started at the sound, and long after Alastair had moved off stood staring after him down the road. Then he turned to the house, his own lips shaping the same air, and cast a glance at the signboard. It showed a red dragon marvellously rampant on a field of green, and beneath was painted a rude device of an open eye.

===Chinese===
他在一只酒桶盖的积尘上画出路线，阿拉斯泰尔一面束紧外衣的搭带，一面戴上骑马手套。马牵来了，酒账也清了，年轻人翻身上马时，那人立在马镫旁。

「你往哪里去？」阿拉斯泰尔问。

「往北，像春日的燕子。不过还得待上一阵。我在这一带还有几桩差事。」

马夫查看了马掌，阿拉斯泰尔坐在鞍上，从齿缝间不耐烦地吹着口哨。到他嘴边的调子，正是仲冬爱哼的那支《赤身人》。西班牙人闻声一怔，待阿拉斯泰尔已经去远，他还在路边久久望着他的背影。随后他转身向屋内走去，自己的嘴唇也默然翕动着同一支曲调，又朝招牌瞥了一眼。招牌上，一头红龙在绿底上昂然奋爪，画得神气活现；下面另有一幅粗陋的图记——一只睁开的眼睛。

===Original===
The chill misty noontide changed presently to a chillier drizzle, and then to a persistent downfall. Alastair’s eagerness was perforce checked by the weather, for he had much ado to grope his way in the maze of grassy lanes and woodland paths. Scarcely a soul was about﻿—only a dripping labourer at a gate, and a cadger with packhorses struggling towards the next change-house. He felt the solitude and languor of the rainy world, and at the same time his bones were on fire to make better speed, for suddenly the space between him and the North seemed to have lengthened intolerably. The flat meadows were hideously foreign; he longed for a sight of hill or heath to tell him that he was nearing the North and the army of his Prince. He cursed the errand that had brought him to this friendless land, far from his proper trade of war.

The November dusk fell soon, and wet greyness gave place to wet mirk. There was no moon, and to continue was to risk a lost road and a foundered horse. So, curbing his impatience, he resolved to lie the night at the first hostelry, and be on the move next day before the dawn.

The mist thickened, and it seemed an interminable time before he found a halting-place. The patch of road appeared to be uninhabited, without the shabbiest beerhouse to cheer it. Alastair’s patience was wearing very thin, and his appetite had waxed to hunger, before the sound of hooves and the speech of men told him that he was not left solitary on the globe. A tiny twinkle of light shone ahead, rayed by the falling rain, and, shrouded and deadened by the fog, came human voices.

===Chinese===
阴冷多雾的正午，不多时便化作更阴冷的蒙蒙细雨，继而是下个不停的倾盆大雨。天气所迫，阿拉斯泰尔的急切只得强行按捺；在青草巷路与林间小径织成的迷宫里，他好不容易才摸着道走。四下里几乎不见人影——只有一个湿淋淋的农夫立在栅门边，还有一个赶驮马的货郎，一路挣扎着往下一处换马店去。他体味着这雨天世界的孤寂与慵倦，可与此同时，他浑身的骨头都烧着火，只求更快赶路——忽然之间，他与北方之间的路程，仿佛长得叫人无法忍受。平坦的草场丑陋得全然异己；他渴望望见一座山丘、一片石楠荒原，好告诉他正在临近北方，临近王子的军中。他咒骂那趟把他差到这举目无亲之地的使命，它使他远离了自己本分的战阵生涯。

十一月的暮色来得早，湿漉漉的灰白让位给湿漉漉的漆黑。没有月亮，再赶路便要冒迷路和马匹力竭的风险。于是他强压焦躁，决意在遇到的第一家客栈过夜，来日破晓之前再动身上路。

雾越来越浓，他仿佛走了无穷无尽的时辰，才寻见一处可以落脚的地方。这一段路上似乎渺无人烟，连一间最寒碜的小酒馆也没有，无从给旅人一点慰藉。直到阿拉斯泰尔的耐心已磨得极薄、胃口早已熬成饥火烧心，马蹄声和人语声才告诉他：普天之下并非只剩他孤身一人。前方闪起一星微弱的灯火，被落雨拉出一道道光丝；人声透过浓雾传来，沉闷而模糊。

===Original===
He appeared to be at a crossroads, where the lane he had been following intersected a more considerable highway, for he blundered against a tall signpost. Then, steering for the light, he all but collided with a traveller on horseback, who was engaged in talk with someone on foot. The horseman was on the point of starting, and the light, which was a lantern in the hand of a man on foot, gave Alastair a faint hurried impression of a tall young man muffled in a fawn-coloured riding-coat, with a sharp nose and a harsh drawling voice. The colloquy was interrupted by his advent, the horseman moved into the rain, and the man with the lantern swung it up in some confusion. Alastair saw what he took for an ostler﻿—a short fellow with a comically ugly face and teeth that projected like the eaves of a house.

“Is this an inn, friend?” he asked.

The voice which replied was familiar.

===Chinese===
他似乎是到了一处十字路口，因为他一头撞上一根高高的路牌——看来他循行的小巷在此与一条更宽的大道相交。于是他朝灯火转去，险些撞上一个骑马的旅人，那人正与一个步行的人说话。骑手正要动身，那盏灯——原是步行者手里提的一盏灯笼——匆匆一晃，让阿拉斯泰尔对那人得了一个模糊而仓促的印象：高个子青年，裹一件浅黄褐色的骑装大衣，尖鼻子，说话拖着刺耳的长腔。他的到来打断了那场交谈，骑手策马走进雨里，提灯的人有些慌乱地把灯举高了些。阿拉斯泰尔看清一个他只当是马夫的人——矮个子，一张滑稽的丑脸，牙齿前凸，像屋檐似的支出来。

「朋友，这里是客栈么？」他问。

答话的嗓音却耳熟。

===Original===
“It’s a kind of a public, but the yill’s sma’ and wersh, and there’s mair mice than aits in the mangers. Still and on, it’s better than outbye this nicht. Is your honour to lie here?”

The man took two steps back and pushed open the inn door, so that a flood of light emerged, and made a half-moon on the cobbles. Now Alastair recognised the lantern-bearer.

“You are Mr. Kyd’s servant?” he said.

“E’en so. And my maister’s in bye, waitin’ on his supper. He’ll be blithe to see ye, sir. See and I’ll tak your horse and bed him weel. Awa in wi’ ye and get warm, and I’ll bring your mails.”

===Chinese===
「算是个酒铺吧，只是麦酒又淡又寡，马槽里的老鼠比燕麦还多。话虽这么说，总强过在这黑夜里露宿。老爷是要在这儿过夜么？」

那人退后两步，推开客栈的门，一片灯光涌了出来，在圆石路面上铺出半轮月亮。这时阿拉斯泰尔认出了提灯的人。

「你是基德先生的仆人？」他说。

「正是呢。俺家老爷就在里头，等着吃晚饭呢。他见了您准高兴，先生。您放心，俺来牵马，把它安顿得好好的。您快进屋暖和去，行李俺这就给您送来。」

===Original===
Alastair pushed open the first door he saw and found a room smoky with a new-lit fire, and by a table, which had been spread with the rudiments of a meal, the massive figure of Mr. Nicholas Kyd.

Mr. Kyd’s first look was one of suspicion and his second of resentment; then, as the sun clears away storm clouds, benevolence and good fellowship beamed from his face.

“God, but I’m in luck the day. Here’s an old friend arrived in time to share my supper. Come in by the fire, sir, and no a word till you’re warmed and fed. You behold me labouring to make up for the defeeciencies of this hostler wife with some contrivances of my own. An old campaigner like Nicol Kyd doesna travel the roads without sundry small delicacies in his saddlebags, for in some of these English hedge-inns a merciful man wouldna kennel his dog.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔推开眼前第一扇门，只见一间屋子，新生的炉火烟气弥漫；一张刚摆出饭菜雏形的桌旁，坐着尼古拉斯·基德先生（Mr. Nicholas Kyd）那魁伟的身躯。

基德先生第一眼是狐疑，第二眼是愠怒；随后，如同太阳扫开雨云，满面焕发出慈和与亲善的光彩。

「老天爷，我今日可真是好运气。来了位老朋友，正赶上与我共进晚餐。到火边来，先生，暖和了、吃饱了再开口不迟。您瞧见我正在忙活，要用自家的一点巧思，来弥补这位老板娘这里的亏空。像尼科尔·基德（Nicol Kyd）这等老行伍，出门上路，鞍囊里哪能不备着几样精致的小食？要知道在这些英格兰的篱畔野店里，连慈悲人的狗都不忍心送去寄宿。」

===Original===
He was enjoying himself hugely. A gallon measure full of ale was before him, and this he was assiduously doctoring with various packets taken from a travelling-case that stood on a chair. “Small and sour,” he muttered as he tasted it with a ladle. “But here’s a pinch of soda to correct its acidity, and a nieve-full of powdered ginger-root to prevent colic. Drunk hot with a toast and that yill will no ken itself.”

He poured the stuff into a mulling pot, and turned his attention to the edibles. “Here’s a wersh cheese,” he cried, “but a spice of anchovy will give it kitchen. I never travel without these tasty wee fishes, Captain Maclean. I’ve set the wife to make kail, for she had no meat in the house but a shank-end of beef. But I’ve the better part of a ham here, and a string of pig’s sausages, which I take it is the English equivalent of a haggis. Faith, you and me will no fare that ill. Sit you down, sir, if your legs are dry, for I hear the kail coming. There’s no wine in the place, but I’ll contrive a brew of punch to make up for it.”

The hostess, her round face afire from her labours in the kitchen, flung open the door, and a slatternly wench brought in a steaming tureen of broth. More candles were lit, logs were laid on the fire, and the mean room took on an air of rough comfort. After the sombre afternoon Alastair surrendered himself gladly to his good fortune, and filled a tankard of the doctored ale, which he found very palatable. The soup warmed his blood and, having eaten nothing since morning, he showed himself a good trencherman. Mr. Kyd in the intervals of satisfying his own appetite beamed upon his companion, hospitably happy at being able to provide such entertainment.

===Chinese===
他自得其乐，兴头十足。面前摆着满满一加仑的麦酒，他正从椅上一只旅行药箱里取出各样的纸包，埋头给这酒下「药」。「又淡又酸，」他舀起一勺尝了尝，喃喃道，「可是这一撮苏打，正治它的酸气；再添满满一拳头的姜根粉，防人腹痛。兑上一片烤面包热热地喝下去，这麦酒就认不得自己喽。」

他把这剂药倒进温酒壶，又把心思转到吃食上。「这块奶酪寡淡无味，」他嚷道，「可添一味凤尾鱼，便给它添了滋味。我出门从不少带这些可口的小鱼儿，麦克莱恩上尉。我已叫老板娘去做菜汤，她家里除一截牛胫骨再没有别的荤腥。不过我这儿有上好大半只火腿，还有一串猪肉香肠——我琢磨着，这大约就是英格兰人自家版本的哈吉斯（haggis）了。说真的，你我今晚的伙食差不到哪儿去。请坐，先生，只要您的腿脚还干着——我听见菜汤来了。这店里没有酒，我来调配一钵潘趣酒补上。」

老板娘在灶间操劳得圆脸通红，一把推开门，一个邋遢的使女端进一盆热气腾腾的肉汤。更多的蜡烛点起来，木柴添上炉火，这寒素的屋子顿时有了几分粗粝的温暖。挨过整个阴郁的午后，阿拉斯泰尔欣然委身于自己的好运，斟了满满一罐下了「药」的麦酒，一尝竟十分可口。热汤暖了他的血；他自晨起粒米未进，此时便显出一副好食量来。基德先生在满足自家胃口的空隙里，满面春风地望着同伴，为能摆出这样一场款待而尽兴——主人家的殷勤快活，全写在脸上。

===Original===
“It’s a thing I love,” he said, “to pass a night in an inn with a friend and a bottle. Coming out of the darkness to a warm fire and a good meal fair ravishes my heart, and the more if it’s unexpected. That’s your case at this moment, Captain Maclean, and you may thank the Almighty that you’re not supping off fat bacon and stinking beer. A lucky meeting for you. Now I wonder at what hostel ‘Menelaus’ and ‘Alcinous’ could have foregathered. Maybe, the pair of them went to visit Ulysses in Ithaca and shoot his paitricks. But it’s no likely.”

“How did ‘Menelaus’ prosper at Badminton?” Alastair asked.

“Wheesht, man! We’ll get in the condiments for the punch and steek the door before we talk.”

===Chinese===
「有一样事我最爱，」他说，「便是携一位友人和一瓶酒，在客栈里消磨一夜。从黑地里走进温暖的炉火与一顿好饭，真叫我心花怒放；若这享受来得出其不意，那就更妙。眼下的您正是这般光景，麦克莱恩上尉，您该感谢全能的上帝，没教您晚餐只对着肥咸肉和发臭的啤酒。这对您是一场好遇合。我倒纳闷，『墨涅拉俄斯』和『阿尔基努斯』能在哪家客栈碰头呢？兴许，这一对结伴去伊萨卡拜访尤利西斯，射他的鹧鸪去了。不过多半不是。」

「『墨涅拉俄斯』在巴德明顿顺遂么？」阿拉斯泰尔问。

「嘘，老弟！且把潘趣酒的作料搬进来、把门关严实了，咱们再谈。」

===Original===
The landlady brought coarse sugar in a canister and half a dozen lemons, and placed a bubbling kettle on the hob. Mr. Kyd carefully closed the door behind her and turned the key. With immense care and a gusto which now and then revealed itself in a verse of song, he poured the sugar into a great blue bowl, squeezed the lemons over it with his strong fingers, and added boiling water, with the quantities of each most nicely calculated. Then from a silver-mounted case-bottle he poured the approved modicum of whisky (“the real thing, Captain Maclean, that you’ll no find south of the Highland line”) and sniffed affectionately at the fragrant steam. He tasted the brew, gave it his benediction, and filled Alastair’s rummer. Then he lit one of the churchwardens which the landlady had supplied, stretched his legs to the blaze, and heaved a prodigious sigh.

“If I shut my eyes I could believe I was at Greyhouses. That’s my but-and-ben in the Lammermuirs, sir. It’s a queer thing, but I can never stir from home without the sorest kind of homesickness. I was never meant for this gangrel job.﻿ ﻿… But if I open that window it will no be a burn in the howe and the peesweeps that I’ll hear, but just the weariful soughing of English trees.﻿ ﻿… There’s a lot of the bairn in me, Captain Maclean.”

The pleasant apathy induced by food and warmth was passing from Alastair’s mind, and he felt anew the restlessness which the Spaniard’s news had kindled. He was not in a mood for Mr. Kyd’s sentiment.

===Chinese===
老板娘送来一罐粗糖和六只柠檬，又把一只滚沸的水壶坐上炉台。基德先生小心翼翼地在她身后关上门，上了锁。他以极度的精心调着酒——那股兴头不时化作一两句歌——把糖倒进一只蓝色大钵，用有力的手指把柠檬汁挤在糖上，再冲入滚水，各样分量都算计得毫厘不爽。随后他从一只银箍扁酒瓶里，斟出不多不少、恰到好处的一份威士忌（「货真价实的好东西，麦克莱恩上尉，高地界线以南您哪儿也买不着」），又深情地嗅了嗅那芬芳的热气。他尝了尝这杰作，为它祝了福，斟满阿拉斯泰尔的大酒杯。然后他点燃老板娘备下的一支长杆烟斗，两腿伸向炉火，发出一声悠长的喟叹。

「我只要闭上眼，就能当自己回到了格雷豪斯。那是我坐落在拉默缪尔山间的两间小舍，先生。说来奇怪，我只要一出门，最苦的乡愁便缠上来，怎样也解不开。我天生就不是干这东跑西荡营生的料。……可我若推开那扇窗，听见的不会是谷中溪流与田凫的啼鸣，而只是英格兰树木那种乏味的萧瑟。……我这人身上，孩子气还多着呢，麦克莱恩上尉。」

饮食与温暖酿成的那点惬意的慵懒，正从阿拉斯泰尔心头散去，西班牙人的消息点燃的焦灼又重新袭来。他没有心思领受基德先生的伤感。

===Original===
“You will soon enough be in the North, I take it,” he said.

“Not till the New Year, for my sins. I’m the Duke’s doer, and I must be back at Amesbury to see to the plantings.”

“And the mission of ‘Menelaus?’ ”

“Over for a time. My report went north a week syne by a sure hand.”

“Successful?”

===Chinese===
「我想，您很快就要到北方去了。」他说。

「不到新年去不成，都是我造的孽。我是公爵的代理人，得回埃姆斯伯里（Amesbury）去照管栽种。」

「那么『墨涅拉俄斯』的使命呢？」

「暂告一段落。我的呈文一周前已由一条稳妥的路子送往北边了。」

「顺利么？」

===Original===
Mr. Kyd pursed his lips. “So-so.” He looked sharply towards door and window. “Beaufort is with us﻿—on conditions. And you?”

“I am inclined to be cheerful. We shall not lack the English grandees, provided we in the North play the game right.”

“Ay. That’s gospel. You mean a victory in England.”

Alastair nodded. “Therefore ‘Alcinous’ has done with Phaeacia and returns to the Prince as fast as horse will carry him. But what does ‘Menelaus’ in these parts? You are far away from Badminton and farther from Amesbury.”

===Chinese===
基德先生抿了抿嘴。「马马虎虎。」他机警地朝门窗各扫了一眼。「博福特已入我们一伙——有条件地。你呢？」

「我倒是倾向于乐观。只要我们在北方把这盘棋走对，英格兰的显贵是不会缺的。」

「是啊，这话是福音。你是说，要在英格兰打一场胜仗。」

阿拉斯泰尔点头。「所以『阿尔基努斯』已与法伊阿基亚（Phaeacia）缘尽，正策马加鞭，尽快赶回王子身边。可『墨涅拉俄斯』在这一带做什么？您离巴德明顿远了，离埃姆斯伯里更远。」

===Original===
“I had a kind of bye-errand up this way. Now I’m on my road south again.”

“Has the Cause friends hereabouts? I saw a horseman at the door in talk with your servant.”

Mr. Kyd looked up quickly. “I heard tell of none. What was he like?”

“I saw only a face in the mist﻿—a high collar and a very sharp nose.”

The other shook his head. “It beats me, unless it was some forwandered traveller that speired the road from Edom. I’ve seen no kenned face for a week, except”﻿—and he broke into a loud guffaw﻿—“except yon daft dominie we met at Cornbury﻿—the man that wanted us all to mount and chase a runaway lassie. I passed him on the road yestereen mounted like a cadger and groaning like an auld wife.”

===Chinese===
「我顺道到这边来办一桩小差事。如今又要南返了。」

「大业在这一带有朋友么？方才我在门口看见一个骑马的，正与您的仆人说话。」

基德先生猛地抬头。「我没听说有什么朋友。那人什么模样？」

「雾里只望见一张脸——高高的领子，一个很尖的鼻子。」

对方摇头。「这就教我猜不透了，除非是哪个迷了路的旅人，向以东（Edom）问道。我有一个礼拜没见过一张熟脸了——除了」——他忽然放声大笑——「除了咱们在科恩伯里遇见的那个疯教书匠——就是那位要咱们全体上马去追逃跑姑娘的先生。昨晚我在路上还打他身边过，他骑在马上活像个货郎，呻吟得像个老婆子。」

===Original===
Mr. Kyd’s scornful reference to the tutor of Chastlecote slightly weakened in Alastair the friendliness which his geniality had inspired.

“It will be well for us if we are as eager in our duties as that poor creature,” he said dryly. “I must be off early tomorrow and not spare horseflesh till I see the Standard.”

“Ay, you maun lose no time. See, and I’ll make you a list of post-houses, where you can command decent cattle. It is the fruit of an uncommon ripe experience. Keep well to the east, for there’s poor roads and worse beasts this side of the Peak.”

===Chinese===
基德先生这番对查斯特科特（Chastlecote）那位塾师的讥诮，稍稍冲淡了他先前的热诚在阿拉斯泰尔心中唤起的好感。

「倘若我们办起差来都像那可怜人这般急切，我们的事就有指望了。」他淡淡地说。「我明天一早必须动身，在见到王旗之前，决不吝惜马力。」

「是，你一分光阴也耗不得。来，我给你开一张驿站单子，那些地方都雇得到像样的马。这是我格外老到的阅历结出的果子。尽量靠东走，皮克（the Peak）这一边，路又差，牲口更差。」

===Original===
“That was the road I came, but now I must take a different airt. I had news today﻿—disquieting news. The Prince is over the Border.”

Mr. Kyd was on his feet, his chair scraping hard on the stone floor, and the glasses rattling on the shaken table.

“I’ve heard nothing of it. Man, what kind of news reaches you and not me?”

“It is true all the same. I had it from one who came long ago to Morvern and knows my clan. This day His Highness crossed Liddel.”

===Chinese===
「我来时走的正是那条路，可如今我必须换个方向。我今天得了消息——令人不安的消息。王子已越过边境。」

基德先生霍然起立，椅子在石板地上刮出刺耳的一声，杯盏在震动的桌面上格格作响。

「这我怎么一点没听说？老弟，什么样的消息，进了你的耳朵却进不了我的？」

「千真万确。告诉我的人，多年前到过莫尔文，识得我的氏族。就在今天，殿下渡过了利德尔（Liddel）。」

===Original===
“Liddel!” Mr. Kyd almost screamed. “Then he goes by Carlisle. But Wade’s at Newcastle.”

“That is precisely the damnable folly of it. He is forgoing his chance of an immediate victory over a dotard﻿—and a victory in England. God, sir, His Highness has been ill advised. You see now why I ride north hell-for-leather. I am a soldier of some experience and few of the Prince’s advisers have seen a campaign. My presence may prevent a more fatal error.”

Mr. Kyd’s face was a strange study. Officially it was drawn into lines of tragic melancholy, but there seemed to be satisfaction, even jubilation, behind the despair, and the voice could not escape a tremor of pleased excitement. Alastair, whose life at the French court had made him quick to judge the nuances of feeling, noted this apparent contradiction, and set it down to the eagerness of loyalty which hears at last that the Rubicon is crossed.

===Chinese===
「利德尔！」基德先生几乎尖叫起来。「那他就是取道卡莱尔了。可韦德屯在纽卡斯尔啊。」

「这正是最该死的一步昏棋。他放弃了立即歼灭一个老糊涂的机会——放弃了一场英格兰土地上的胜利。老天爷，先生，殿下听信了坏主意。这下您明白了，我为什么没命似的飞骑北上。我是个有些历练的军人，而殿下的谋士里打过仗的没有几人。有我在场，或许能拦下更致命的失着。」

基德先生的脸是一幅耐人寻味的奇景。表面上，它拉出悲惨哀戚的纹路，可那绝望背后似乎藏着得意，甚至狂喜，嗓音里也逃不出一阵暗自兴奋的震颤。阿拉斯泰尔在法兰西宫廷中养成了对情绪幽微之处的敏锐，他把这明显的矛盾归之于忠忱的炽热——终于听到卢比孔河（Rubicon）已渡时的那种炽热。

===Original===
“They will march through Lancashire,” said Mr. Kyd, “and look to recruit the gentry. If so, they’re a sturdier breed up yonder than on the Welsh Marches﻿—” He hesitated. “I wonder if you’re right in posting off to the North? Does this news not make a differ? What about Cornbury and Sir Watkin? Will the casting of the die not make up their minds for them? Faith, I think I’ll take another look in at Badminton.”

Alastair saw in the other’s face only an earnest friendliness.

“No, no,” he cried. “Nothing avails but the English victory. We must make certain of that. But do you, Mr. Kyd, press the grandees of the Marches, while I prevent fools and schoolboys from overriding the natural good sense of our Prince.”

Mr. Kyd had recovered his composure, and insisted on filling the rummer again for a toast to fortune. The lines about his eyes were grave, but jollity lurked in the corners of his mouth.

===Chinese===
「他们要穿过兰开夏，」基德先生说，「指望在那里招募乡绅。若是这样，那边的乡绅可得比威尔士边区的一族结实得多——」他迟疑了一下。「我在想，你急着奔北方，究竟对不对？这消息难道不改变局面么？科恩伯里和沃特金爵士怎么办？骰子既已掷出，还不替他们拿定主意么？说真的，我想我该再到巴德明顿去一趟。」

阿拉斯泰尔在那张脸上看到的，只有一片诚挚的善意。

「不，不，」他叫道，「唯有英格兰的胜利才顶事，我们必须确保这一仗。至于您，基德先生，请去敦促边区的显贵；我呢，去拦住那帮蠢材和毛孩子，别让他们压倒了我们王子天生的明断。」

基德先生已恢复了从容，执意再斟满大酒杯，为命运干一杯。他眼角的纹路是庄重的，嘴角边却潜藏着笑意。

===Original===
“Then you’ll take the west side of England and make for Warrington? Ay, that’s your quickest road. I’ll draw you an *itinerarium*, for I whiles travel that gait.” He scribbled a list on a leaf from a pocketbook and flung it to Alastair. “The morn’s night you lie at Flambury, and the third night you’ll be in Chester.”

“Flambury,” Alastair exclaimed. “That takes me too far eastward.”

“No, no. In this country the straight road’s apt to be the long road. There’s good going to Flambury, and the turnpike on to Whitchurch. You’ll lie there at the Dog and Gun, and if you speak my name to the landlord you’ll get the best in his house.﻿ ﻿… Man, I envy you, for you’ll be among our own folk in a week. My heart goes with you, and here’s to a quick journey.”

Alastair was staring into the fire, and turned more suddenly than the other anticipated. Mr. Kyd’s face was in an instant all rosy goodwill, but for just that one second he was taken by surprise, and something furtive and haggard looked from his eyes. This something Alastair caught, and, as he snuggled between the inn blankets, the memory of it faintly clouded his thoughts, like a breath on a mirror.

===Chinese===
「那么你就取道英格兰西部，直奔沃灵顿？对，那是你最快的路。我给你开一份行程表（itinerarium），这条路我时不时也走。」他在记事本撕下的一页上潦草开出一串单子，抛给阿拉斯泰尔。「明晚你宿在弗兰伯里（Flambury），第三夜便到切斯特（Chester）。」

「弗兰伯里？」阿拉斯泰尔叫道。「这把我带得偏东了。」

「不，不。在这个国度，笔直的路往往偏偏是最长的路。去弗兰伯里一路好走，再上收费大道直通惠特彻奇。你就宿在『狗与枪』客栈（the Dog and Gun），只要对店主人报上我的名号，他店里顶好的东西都会给你。……老弟，我真羡慕你，不出一个礼拜，你就到咱们自家人中间了。我的心随你同去，祝你一路迅捷。」

阿拉斯泰尔正凝望着炉火，转过头来，比对方预料的突然得多。刹那之间，基德先生脸上已换作满面红润的善意，可就在那一秒里，他猝不及防，眼睛里有一种鬼祟而憔悴的东西一闪而过。这一闪被阿拉斯泰尔捉住了；当他裹进客栈的毯子里时，这记忆像镜上的一口气痕，淡淡地蒙住了他的思绪。

## XI Night at the Same: Two Visitors / 十一、同处夜宿：两位来客

===Original===
Four nights later Alastair was in his little bedroom at the Sleeping Deer, dressing by the light of two homemade candles. He had been taken to this inn by Midwinter because of the honesty of the landlord, who lived only for trout-fishing, and the facilities of the rambling old house for a discreet retirement. He was given an attic at the back where the dwelling part of the building merged in a disused watermill and granary. There was an entrance to it from the first floor, by way of a store cupboard; another from the kitchen regions, and still a third from the millhouse. Accordingly he was able to enter unobtrusively at any hour of the day or night, and had the further advantage that the millhouse road led directly to a covert of elders and so to the hillside. His meals, when he was at home to partake of them, were brought him by the landlord himself, who also would ascend to smoke his pipe of an evening, and discuss the habits of Derbyshire trout as compared with their northern kin.

Clad in his leather and frieze he had spent the days among the valleys and along the great road. The snow had not melted, but it was bound in the stricture of a mild frost, and all day a winter sun shone on the soft white curves of the hills. It was weather to kindle the blood and lift the heart, and Alastair found his journeys pleasant enough, though so far fruitless. He had haunted Brightwell like a cattle-lifting Macgregor looking down on a Lennox byre, and since few could teach him woodcraft in hilly places, he had easily evaded the race of keepers and foresters. Twice he had met the man whom he took to be Sir John Norreys. The first time he had watched him from cover, setting out on horseback by a track which ran from Brightwell to Dovedale﻿—a man in a furious hurry, with a twitching bridle-hand and a nervous eye. The second time he met him full face on the high road, and seemed to be recognised. Sir John half pulled up, thought better of it, and rode on with one glance behind him. He had made certain inquiries in the neighbourhood and learned that the tall gentleman in the fawn coat was a newcomer and beyond doubt sojourned in Brightwell: but he had a notion that in that vast decaying pile a man might lodge unbeknown to the other dwellers. He was curious to discover if Sir John had yet greeted his lady.

Four days ago she had departed in her coach, fresh horsed from Brightwell, attended by Mr. Johnson and Edom Lowrie. Since then he had seen no sign of the party. The old house had swallowed them up, and neither taking the air in the park nor riding on the highway had any one of them emerged to the outer world. The mystery of the place grew upon him, till he came to look on the bleak house lying in the sparkling amphitheatre of hill as the enchanted castle of a fairy tale. It held a princess and it held a secret﻿—*the* secret, he was convinced, most vital to his Prince’s cause. He need not scour the country; in that one dwelling he could read the riddle.

===Chinese===
四夜之后，阿拉斯泰尔已身在睡鹿客栈（the Sleeping Deer）的小小卧房里，就着两支自制蜡烛的光亮穿衣。仲冬把他安顿到这家客栈，一来是相中店主人的老实本分——此人活着只为一桩事，就是钓鳟鱼——二来是这所七弯八拐的老宅，最宜不显山露水地隐居。他分得宅后一间阁楼，宅子的居住部分到那里便与一座废弃的水磨坊和谷仓连成一片。这阁楼有个入口开在二楼，须穿过一间储物壁橱；另有一个从厨房那一带进来；还有第三个，则由磨坊进身。因此无论昼夜什么时辰，他都能悄没声息地进出，另有一层好处：磨坊那条道直通一片接骨木树丛，由那儿便上了山坡。他在「家」用饭的时候，饭菜由店主人亲自送来；晚间店主还会上楼抽一斗烟，谈论德比郡鳟鱼的习性，与其北方同类比较一番。

他披着皮衣和粗呢外套，连日里游走于山谷之间、大道两旁。雪还没有化，却被一场轻霜束得结结实实，冬日的太阳整日照在山峦柔白的曲线上。这样的天气叫人血热心动，阿拉斯泰尔只觉这一路巡游颇为惬意，尽管至今一无所获。他像窥伺伦诺克斯（the Lennox）牛栏的偷牛麦格雷戈（Macgregor）一般，日夜盘桓在布莱特韦尔左近；再说在山地行走，林中本领能教他的人本没有几个，那班看守人、林务员，他轻易便甩脱了。他两次撞见那个他认作约翰·诺里斯爵士的人。头一回，他从隐蔽处望见他骑马启程，走的是从布莱特韦尔通往达夫谷（Dovedale）的一条小径——那是个火急火燎的人，执缰的手抽动着，一只眼惶惶不安。第二回，他在大道上与他迎面相逢，似乎被认了出来。诺里斯爵士勒了半个马，转念又作罢，只回头瞥了一眼，便纵马前行。他在左近打听过一番，得知那位穿浅黄褐色外衣的高个绅士是新来客，无疑下榻在布莱特韦尔：但他有一种念头，在那座塌败倾颓的庞大宅第里，一个人尽可以瞒过其他住户悄然住下。他好奇想探明，诺里斯爵士到底见没见过他的夫人。

四天前，她乘着马车离去了，车马在布莱特韦尔新换过，约翰逊先生与以东·洛里随行。自那以后，他便没再见这一行人的半点踪影。老宅把他们吞了进去，无论到园子里透气，还是上大道骑行，他们中谁都不曾露面于外间。此地在他眼里愈发神秘，到后来，他望着那座卧在雪光莹莹、环如剧场的群山中央的荒寒巨宅，竟觉得它是童话里中了魔法的城堡。堡中锁着一位公主，也锁着一桩秘密——正是那一桩，他深信不疑，与他王子殿下的大业最攸关的秘密。他用不着踏遍四乡；单凭那一座宅子，便能读出谜底。

===Original===
On this, the fourth night of his reconnaissance, he returned to the inn assured that the first part of his task was over. He must find some way of entering Brightwell and growing familiar with the household, and his head was busy with plans as he slipped into the millhouse in the early dark, and climbed the dusty wooden ladder to the loft which gave on his attic. In his bedroom stood the landlord.

“I heard ye come in by the mill,” he said, “and I’m here because I’ve news ye may like to hear. There’s a famous gentleman coming here tonight. Ye’ll have heard o’ General Oglethorpe, him that’s been fighting in Ameriky? He’s coming to his supper, no less. His regiment is lying down the vale, and an officer rides here this afternoon and says the General will be to sup sharp at seven o’clock. After that he’s to meet a friend here and wants to be left quiet. He needs no bed, for he’s riding back to his camp when he’s done his business. Now, what d’ ye make of that, sir?”

===Chinese===
到了这侦察的第四夜，他回到客栈，心里已笃定：任务的第一步完成了。他还须设法进入布莱特韦尔，与宅中上下厮熟起来。天刚擦黑，他溜进磨坊，攀上积尘的木梯，登上与他阁楼相通的磨坊顶层，脑子里一桩桩计划正忙个不停。他的卧室里立着店主人。

「俺听见你打磨坊进来，」他说，「俺上这儿来，是因为有个消息，你许爱听。今夜有位大人物要来。你总听说过奥格尔索普将军吧，就是在亚美利加（Ameriky）打过仗的那位？他要来这儿用晚饭，可不是小事。他的团就驻在谷下，今儿下午一位军官骑马来，说将军七点整要用饭。饭后他要在这儿会一位朋友，要人让他清静。他不用床铺，事情一了就骑马回营。你说说，先生，这是怎么回事？」

===Original===
“Where does he sup?” Alastair asked.

“In the Brown Room, the one my lady had.”

“When he arrives pray give him a message from me. Say that one who had the happiness to oblige him a week back is in the house, and will do himself the honour of waiting on him if he will name the hour. Is that clear? Now fetch me some hot water, for I must make a toilet.”

He got rid of his soaked clothes and assumed his old habit﻿—chocolate coat and green velvet waistcoat, stockings and buckled shoes, and a tie-wig new dressed by the landlord. The exposure of the past days had darkened his skin, and it was a hard-bitten face that looked back at him from the cracked mirror. Before completing his toilet he lay down on the truckle bed and stared at the ceiling. Oglethorpe was friendly to him, and might give him news of moment﻿—he had the name himself of a Jacobite or at any rate of a lukewarm Hanoverian. But the man the General was to meet? He had no doubt it was Sir John and he chuckled at the chance which Fortune had offered him.

===Chinese===
「他在哪儿用饭？」阿拉斯泰尔问。

「在棕室（the Brown Room），就是内人先前住的那一间。」

「他到的时候，劳驾替我捎句话。就说：一周之前有幸为他效过一回劳的人正在店中，若蒙他示下时辰，此人必当亲来拜候，引为荣宠。话说明白了么？这就给我打些热水来，我须得梳洗。」

他脱去湿透的衣裳，换上旧日行头——巧克力色外衣，绿丝绒马甲，长袜配带扣鞋，还有一顶店主人替他重新梳理过的系辫假发。连日餐风宿露，他皮肤黑了，从裂了纹的镜子里望着他的，是一张风霜磨砺过的脸。梳洗未竟，他先在脚轮矮床上躺下，凝望着天花板。奥格尔索普待他友好，或许能给他些要紧的消息——此人自己便背着雅各布党之名，至少也是个不冷不热的汉诺威派。可是将军要会的那个人呢？他毫不怀疑那就是诺里斯爵士，想到命运递到他手里的这个机会，不禁暗自得意。

===Original===
As he lay his thoughts roamed wide but always returned to one centre, the Brown Room at the inn. But it was not Oglethorpe or Sir John that he saw there, but a slim girl with eyes now ardent, now laughing, now misty, and a voice that stammered adorably and sang “Diana” like a linnet. Sometimes he saw Brightwell and its chilly hall, but he saw no human personage other than the girl, a little forlorn and lost now, but still happy and dreaming.﻿ ﻿… He pulled himself up sharply. For the first time in his life a woman’s face was filling the eye of his mind﻿—he, the scorner of trivialities whose whole being was dedicate to a manly ambition! He felt irritated and a little ashamed, and began laboriously to examine himself to prove his resolution. Now in the very crisis of his fate he could least afford a whimsy.

The landlord disturbed him when he had become drowsy.

“The gentleman is here﻿—General Oglethorpe. I give him your message, and he says, pleasant-like, ‘I can guess who the gentleman is. Tell him that my gratitude is not exhausted and that I will be happy if he will add to his obligations by giving me his company at supper.’ Ye’d better hasten, sir, for supper is being dished up.”

Alastair followed the landlord through the cobwebby back regions of the storeroom and out to the gallery at the head of the stairs whence the Brown Room opened. He noticed that the dusky corridor was brightly lit just opposite the room door because of the lamps in the hall below which shone up a side passage. This glow also revealed in full detail the map which he had studied on his first night there. As he glanced at it, the two great roads from the north seemed to stand out like blood, and Brightwell, a bloodred name, to be the tollhouse to shut or open them.

===Chinese===
他躺着，思绪漫无边际地游荡，却总回到一个中心——客栈里的那间棕室。但他在那里看见的并非奥格尔索普，也不是诺里斯爵士，而是一个苗条的姑娘，一双眼睛时而炽热，时而含笑，时而蒙眬，一口嗓音带着教人心醉的微微结巴，唱起《狄安娜》来像只红雀。有时他也看见布莱特韦尔和它那冷峭的大厅，但除了那姑娘，他看不见别的什么人物——她此刻有几分孤单无依，却依然快乐，依然耽于梦幻。……他猛地把自己拽住。生平头一回，一个女人的面容占满了他心灵的眼睛——他，一个鄙夷琐屑、全副身心都许给了丈夫气概的抱负的人！他感到恼火，也有几分羞惭，便苦苦地自我检视一番，来验证自己的决心。此刻正当他命运的紧要关头，最容不下痴念。

他正蒙眬欲睡，店主人来搅扰了他。

「那位老爷到了——奥格尔索普将军。我把您的话带到了，他挺和悦地说：『那位先生是谁，我猜得出。告诉他，我的感激之情还没有用尽，他若肯赏光，陪我共进晚餐，再添一桩人情，我将欣然之至。』您还是快些，先生，晚饭这就端上桌了。」

阿拉斯泰尔跟着店主人穿过蛛网密布的储物间后部，出到楼梯顶头的廊道上，棕室的门便开在那里。他注意到，幽暗的走廊恰在房门对面被照得通亮，那是楼下厅堂里的灯，顺着一条侧道映了上来。这片光也把那幅地图照得纤毫毕现——他头一晚在此地时曾细细研读过。他朝地图瞥了一眼，只见自北方而来的两条大道仿佛血痕般凸现出来，而布莱特韦尔这血红色的名字，正是那启闭两路的税卡。

===Original===
The Brown Room was bright with candles and firelight, and warming his back at the hearth stood a tall man in military undress. He was of a strong harsh aquiline cast of countenance; his skin was somewhat sallow from the hot countries he had dwelt in, but he carried his forty-odd years lightly, and, to Alastair’s soldier eye, would be a serious antagonist with whatever weapon of hand or brain. His face relaxed at the sight of the young man and he held out his hand.

“I am overjoyed to see you again, Mr. Maclean.﻿ ﻿… Nay, I never forget a name or a face﻿ ﻿… I do not ask your business here, nor will I permit you to ask mine, save in so far as all the world knows it. I have my regiment billeted at Marlock, and am on my way across England to Hull, there to join General Wade. In that there is no secret, for every old woman on Trent side proclaims it.﻿ ﻿… Let us fall to, sir, for I am plaguily hungry with the frosty air, and this house has a name for cookery.”

General Oglethorpe proved himself a trencherman of the calibre of Mr. Samuel Johnson; that is to say, he ate heartily of everything﻿—beefsteak pie, roast sirloin, sheep’s tongues, cranberry tarts and a London bag-pudding﻿—and drank a bottle of claret, a quart of ale, and the better part of a bottle of Madeira. But unlike Mr. Johnson he did not become garrulous, nor did the iron restraint of his demeanour relax. The board was cleared and he proceeded to brew a dish of punch, mixing the several ingredients of limes, rum, white sugar and hot water with the meticulosity of an alchemist. Then he produced from a flat silver box which he carried in his waistcoat pocket a number of thin brown sticks, which he offered to his companion.

“Will you try my cigarros, sir? It is a habit which I contracted in Georgia, and I find them mighty comforting to a campaigner.﻿ ﻿… You journey northward, Mr. Maclean, but you make slow progress.” He smiled with a quizzical kindliness which stripped the martinet’s cloak from him and left only benevolence.

===Chinese===
棕室里烛光火光交映生辉，壁炉前站着一位穿军便服的高个子男人，正烘着他的背。他相貌刚硬，鹰钩鼻轮廓分明；因久居炎热之邦，肤色微微发黄，但他把四十开外的年纪扛得很轻，而在阿拉斯泰尔的军人眼里，无论动手还是斗智，此人都是个不可轻慢的对手。见了这年轻人，他脸色和缓下来，伸出了手。

「再见到您，我不胜欣喜，麦克莱恩先生。……不，人名和面孔我从不忘记……我不问您在此的营生，也不许您来问我的，除非那是天下人人皆知的事。我的团驻扎在马洛克（Marlock），我正横穿英格兰前往赫尔（Hull），去那边与韦德将军会合。这里头没有什么秘密，特伦特河（Trent）两岸的老婆子个个都在嚷嚷。……我们动筷吧，先生，这霜天的空气把我饿坏了，何况这一家的烹调是有名的。」

奥格尔索普将军证明了自己是塞缪尔·约翰逊先生那个量级的大食客；这就是说，样样菜他都吃得酣畅——牛排馅饼、烤牛里脊、羊舌、蔓越莓果挞，还有一只伦敦袋布丁（London bag-pudding）——又喝了一瓶红葡萄酒、一夸特麦酒，外加大半瓶马德拉酒（Madeira）。但与约翰逊先生不同，他没有变得滔滔不绝，那副铁一般的自持也不曾松弛分毫。桌子收拾停当，他动手调配一钵潘趣酒：青柠、朗姆酒、白糖与热水诸般材料，他以炼金术士般的精细一一搀和。随后他从马甲口袋里一只扁平的银盒中取出若干支细细的褐色小棒，敬给他的同伴。

「尝尝我的雪茄（cigarros）么，先生？这是我在佐治亚养成的习惯，我发觉对一个征战之人，它们是莫大的慰藉。……您是北上的吧，麦克莱恩先生，只是行程未免迟缓。」他微笑着，那笑容带着揶揄的和善，一下子剥去了他身上军纪官的外衣，只剩下一片慈和。

===Original===
Alastair smiled back. “I journey slowly for I have had mischances. But I must mend my pace, for I am still far from my home, and my time of leave passes quick.”

“From the French King’s service?”

“From the French King’s service.”

“You are aware that there are certain rumours of war in this land?”

“I heard gossip to that effect in Paris.”

General Oglethorpe laughed. “I can guess where your sympathies lie, Mr. Maclean. Your name, your birthplace and your profession are signposts to them.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔报以微笑。「我行得慢，是因为遭了些意外。但我必须加快脚程，我离家还远，假期也过得飞快。」

「是从法王军中告的假？」

「是从法王军中告的假。」

「您可知道这片土地上正谣传着战事？」

「我在巴黎听人说过这等闲话。」

奥格尔索普将军笑了。「您的心向着哪一边，我猜得出，麦克莱恩先生。您的姓氏、您的出生地和您的营生，都是指向那边的路标。」

===Original===
“I too have heard tales from which I could hazard a guess at General Oglethorpe’s sentiments,” said Alastair.

“Tut, tut, sir. I bear His Majesty’s commission and am embarked in His Majesty’s service.”

“I could name some in the same case﻿—and with the same sympathies.”

The other’s brows had descended and he was staring in the fire like a perplexed bird of prey.

“I do not altogether deny it. I have been a Member of Parliament for years and I have never concealed my views on politics, sir. I regret that England ever lost her natural and rightful line of kings. I have no love for Ministers with their courting of this neighbour, and baiting of that, and bleeding the commonalty of England for their crazy foreign wars. I detest and abhor the cabal of greedy bloodsuckers that call themselves Whigs. I am a Tory, sir, I serve the ancient constitution of this realm, I love and reverence its Church, and I hold this mongering of novelties an invention of the Devil. But﻿—and it is a potent *but*﻿—I cannot wish that this attempt of the Chevalier should succeed. I must with all my soul hope that it fail and do my best to ensure that failure.”

===Chinese===
「我也听到过一些传闻，从里头我不妨斗胆猜一猜奥格尔索普将军的心曲，」阿拉斯泰尔说。

「得了，得了，先生。我身受陛下的委任，已投身于陛下的麾下效力。」

「身受同样委任、怀同样心曲的人，我也能点出几位。」

对方的双眉垂了下来，他盯着炉火，像一只困惑的猛禽。

「我不全然否认。我做了多年国会议员，我对政事的看法从不曾隐瞒，先生。英格兰失去了她天然合法的王统，我引为憾事。我不喜欢那班当国的大臣——对这个邻邦曲意逢迎，对那个百般挑逗，为了他们昏聩的对外战争，把英格兰的平民百姓榨出血来。那伙自称辉格党的贪婪吸血鬼结成的阴谋集团，我深恶痛绝。我是托利党人，先生，我效忠本国的古老宪政，我热爱并尊崇它的教会，我认为这等贩卖新奇的勾当乃是魔鬼的发明。但是——这是个分量很重的『但是』——我不能愿那位骑士（the Chevalier）此番举事得成。我必须全心全意盼它失败，并竭尽全力促成这失败。」

===Original===
“Your conclusion scarcely accords with your premises, sir.”

“More than may at first sight appear. What has a young man bred abroad in a vapid Court, and suckled into Papistry, to say to the people of England?”

“His church is the same as mine, sir. But he is no bigot, and has sworn to grant to all beliefs that full tolerance which England has denied to his.”

“It is not enough. He is the young gallant, a figure from an old chivalrous world. Oh, I do not deny his attraction; I do not doubt that he can charm men’s hearts. But, sir, there is a new temper in the land. You have heard of the people they call Methodists﻿—humble folk, humble servants of Almighty God, who carry the Gospel to dark places at the expense of revilings and buffetings and persecutions. I have had them with me in Georgia, and they fight like Cromwell’s Ironsides, they are tender and merciful and brave, and they preach a hope for the vilest. With them is the key of the new England, for they bring healing to the souls of the people.﻿ ﻿… What can your fairy Prince say to the poor and the hungry?”

General Oglethorpe’s eye was lit with a fervour which softened the rigour of his face into something infinitely gentle. Alastair had no words to answer so strange a plea.

===Chinese===
「您的结论与您的前提未免不相契合，先生。」

「其契合之处，比初看要多。一个在国外空虚浮华的宫廷里长大、自幼哺育于天主教之中的人，对英格兰的百姓有什么话可说呢？」

「他的教门与我的相同，先生。但他绝非偏执之徒，且已立誓要给一切信仰以完全的宽容——那宽容，英格兰可从未给过他的教门。」

「这还不够。他是位年轻的豪侠，一个从旧日骑士世界走出来的人物。哦，他的魅力，我不否认；他能摄住人心，我也不怀疑。可是，先生，这片土地上正有一种新的性情在生长。您听说过人家唤作循道会信徒（Methodists）的那班人么——谦卑的人们，全能上帝的谦卑仆人，他们把福音传到黑暗的角落，代价却是辱骂、殴打与迫害。我在佐治亚与他们共过事，他们打起仗来像克伦威尔的铁甲军，他们温良、仁慈、勇敢，他们连最卑污的人也宣讲希望。那新生的英格兰，钥匙正在他们手里，因为他们带来的是人民灵魂的医治。……你们那位仙子一般的王子殿下，对穷人和饥民有什么话可说呢？」

奥格尔索普将军的眼中燃起一股热忱，把他面容的严峻软化成一种无限温柔的东西。对这样奇异的恳切之言，阿拉斯泰尔无词以对。

===Original===
“But﻿—but King George is no more of that way of thinking than my Prince,” he stammered.

The other nodded. “I am not arguing on behalf of his present Majesty. I plead for the English people and I want no change, least of all the violent change of revolution, unless it be to their benefit. A mere transfer of monarchs will do small good to them, and it will bring needless suffering to the innocent. Therefore, I, James Oglethorpe, who am reputed a Jacobite, will do my utmost to nip this rising in the bud and confine it to the barbarous parts of the North. In the service of my country I will pretermit no effort to keep England neutral in the quarrel, for it is in England’s participation that the danger lies.”

Alastair deemed it wise not to answer, but, as he regarded this man who was now his declared opponent, he felt the satisfaction of a fighter who faces an honourable foe. Here was one whose hand he could clasp before he crossed swords.

“I am no Englishman,” he said, “and therefore I am remote from this particular controversy.”

The other’s eye burned with a fanatic’s heat. “I will fight like a tiger for England against all who would do her hurt. God forgive them, but there are many on my side whose hearts are like rotten eggs. They are carrion crows who flock wherever there is blood and pain. In times of civil strife, sir, the base can make money. Had you travelled north by Chester you would have passed through a land of fat pastures and spreading parks and snug manors, and had you asked the name of the fortunate owner you would have been told Sir Robert Grosvenor. You know the name? A worthy gentleman and somewhat of your way of thinking. Now Sir Robert’s mother was an heiress and all the faubourgs of London between St. James’ and Kensington village were her fortune. Whence came that fortune, think you, to enrich the honest knights of Cheshire? ’Twas the fortune of an ancient scrivener who bought up forfeited lands from Cromwell’s Government, bought cheap, and sold most profitably at his leisure. There are other fortunes today waiting for the skilled broker of fines and attainders. But to make the profit there must be a forfeiture, and for the forfeiture there must be first the treason. Therefore it is in the interest of base men to manufacture rebels, to encourage simple folk to take blindly some irrevocable and fatal step. Do you follow me?”

===Chinese===
「可是——可是乔治王（King George）作此想，也并不比我的王子殿下多些，」他结结巴巴地说。

对方点头。「我不是在替当今陛下张目。我恳求的是英格兰人民，我不愿有任何变动，最不愿的莫过于革命那样的暴力变动，除非那变动于他们有益。单单换一个君主，于他们没有多少好处，却会给无辜者带来无谓的苦难。所以，我，詹姆斯·奥格尔索普，人称雅各布党人的这一个，要竭尽全力把这起事扼杀在萌芽之中，把它局限在北方蛮荒的地界。为我的国家效力，我将不遗余力地使英格兰在这场争端中保持中立，因为危险正藏在英格兰的卷入里面。」

阿拉斯泰尔认为以不答为明智，但当他端详这个如今已是公开对手的人时，心头涌起一种斗士面对可敬敌手的快意。这是一位拔剑相向之前还能与之握手的人。

「我不是英格兰人，」他说，「所以这一场特别的争执，离我还远。」

对方的眼中燃着狂热者的火。「为英格兰，我要像猛虎一样战斗，对付一切想伤害她的人。上帝饶恕他们——我这一边也有许多人，心像臭蛋一样烂透了。他们是食腐的乌鸦，哪里有血有痛，就往哪里聚。内乱一起，先生，卑贱之徒就能发财。您若是打切斯特一路北来，就会穿过一片沃草丰茂、园囿绵延、庄园安适的乡野；您若问起那位有福的田主姓甚名谁，人家会告诉您：罗伯特·格罗夫纳爵士（Sir Robert Grosvenor）。您知道这个名字吧？一位可敬的绅士，心思与您有几分相近。这位罗伯特爵士的母亲是位女继承人，伦敦从圣詹姆斯到肯辛顿村（Kensington）之间的全部城郊，都是她的家产。您想想，这份家财是打哪儿来的，竟能叫柴郡的诚实骑士们发起来？那是当年一位老代书人的产业，此人从克伦威尔政府手里大肆收买抄没的田地，贱价买进，从容高价卖出。今日还有旁的家财，正等着精于经手罚金与褫夺文书的掮客呢。可要赚这份钱，先得有人产业被抄没；要有人产业被抄没，先得有叛国之罪。所以，制造叛党、诱使愚民盲目迈出那不可挽回的致命一步，正合卑贱之徒的利益。您听懂了么？」

===Original===
Alastair nodded automatically. He saw as in a long vista a chain of infamies and the name to them was Sir John Norreys.

“The scoundrels must be in the confidence of both sides,” Oglethorpe went on. “With their victims they are honest Jacobites, but next day they are closeted with Mr. Pelham in Whitehall. They will draw a poor innocent so far that he will lose his estate, but they will prevent his loss being of service to the Prince.”

The man had risen and strode about the room, a formidable figure of wrath, with his jaw set sternly and his eyes hard.

“Do you know my purpose, Mr. Maclean? So far as the Almighty permits me, I will save the pigeon from the crow. The pigeon will be hindered from meddling in matters of Government, his estate will be saved to him, and the crow, please God, will be plucked. Do you commend my policy?”

“It is the conduct of an honest gentleman, sir, and though I may not share your politics I would hope to share your friendship.”

===Chinese===
阿拉斯泰尔下意识地点了点头。他仿佛在一条深长的视廊尽头，看见一串接一串的罪恶，而署在这罪恶名下的，是约翰·诺里斯爵士。

「这些恶棍必然两头都得着信任，」奥格尔索普接着说。「在受骗的人面前，他们是忠实的雅各布党；转过天来，却在白厅（Whitehall）与佩勒姆先生（Mr. Pelham）密室共议。他们会把一个可怜的无辜者拖到倾家荡产的地步，却又绝不让他的损失于王子殿下有什么用处。」

这人已经起身，在屋里大步踱着，一副震怒骇人之象，牙关咬得死紧，目光冷硬。

「您知道我的目的么，麦克莱恩先生？只要全能者容许，我要从乌鸦爪下救出那只鸽子。要拦住鸽子再去插手政事，他的产业要替他保住，而那只乌鸦，托上帝的福，要拔光它的毛。您赞许我的方略么？」

「这是一位正直绅士的行止，先生。您的政见我未必能赞同，您的友谊我却希望能共享。」

===Original===
Oglethorpe’s face relaxed into the convivial kindliness it had shown at supper.

“Then two friends and honourable opponents will shake hands and bid farewell. You will be for bed, sir, and I must return presently to my regiment.”

But as the young man left the room the General seemed in no hurry to call for his horse. He flung another log on the fire, and stood by the hearth with his brows knit in meditation.

Alastair retired to his bedroom but did not undress. His brain was dazzled with new light, and he saw all the events of the past weeks in a new and awful perspective. This man Norreys was the traitor, the agent provocateur who lured honest clodpoles to their doom and pocketed his commission on their ruin. That was what Sir Christopher Lacy had said at Cornbury﻿—the man cared only for gain. But he must be a rogue of vast accomplishments, for he had deceived a proud lady, and he had won the confidence of a shrewd Scots lawyer. It was Kyd’s beguilement that staggered him. He, a sagacious man of affairs, had used a traitor as an agent for the most precious news﻿—news which instead of going straight to the Prince would be transferred to the enemy and used for honest men’s undoing. General Oglethorpe would prevent the fellow from making his foul profit; it was the business of Alastair Maclean to stamp the breath from him, to rid the Prince’s cause of a menace and the world of a villain.

===Chinese===
奥格尔索普的脸松弛下来，现出晚饭时那副酒酣耳热的和善。

「那么，两个朋友、一对可敬的对手，就此握手道别。您该就寝了，先生，我也得即刻回团里去。」

但那年轻人离开房间之后，将军却似乎并不急着唤马。他又往火里添了一块柴，站在炉边，双眉紧锁，沉入思索。

阿拉斯泰尔回到卧室，却没有宽衣。他的头脑被一道新的光晃得眩晕，过去数周的一切事件，在他眼里呈现出一种全新而可怖的布局。这个诺里斯正是那个叛徒，那个诱陷密探，把老实的憨汉引向毁灭，再在他们的倾覆之上抽走自己的佣金。克里斯托弗·莱西爵士在科恩伯里说的正是此意——此人心里只认得利。但他必是个本领通天的恶棍，因为他骗过了一位心气高傲的夫人，又赢得了一位精明的苏格兰讼师的信任。最叫他震惊的，是基德竟也被蒙在鼓里。基德那样一个精明干练的人，竟用一个叛徒作传递最珍贵消息的探员——这些消息不去直送王子殿下，反会被转递给敌人，用来断送正人君子。奥格尔索普将军会不让这厮赚到他那肮脏钱；而将这厮的气息踏灭、为王子殿下的大业除一祸患、为世间除一恶徒，则是阿拉斯泰尔·麦克莱恩的本分。

===Original===
He mused on this strange thing, England, which was like a spell on sober minds. Midwinter had told of Old England like a lover of his mistress, and here was this battered traveller, this Oglethorpe, thrilling to the same fervour. That was something he had not met before. He had been trained to love his family and clan and the hills of his home, and a Prince who summed up centuries of wandering loyalty. But his devotion had been for the little, intimate things, and not for matters large and impersonal like a country or a people. He felt himself suddenly and in very truth a stranger and alone. The Prince, the chiefs, the army﻿—they were all of them strangers here. How could they ask for loyalty from what they so little understood?

The reflection pained him and he put it from him and turned to his immediate business. Kicking off his shoes, he tiptoed back through the store-cupboard and into the long corridor, at the end of which he saw the bright reflection from the hall lamp falling on the map and the Brown Room door. He listened, but there was no sound except a faint clatter from far away in the direction of the kitchen, where presumably the General’s servant waited on his master’s orders. He stole to the door of the Brown Room for a second, and played the eavesdropper. Yes, there were voices within, a low voice speaking fast, and another replying in monosyllables. He had no wish to overhear them, so he crept back to the storeroom door, where he was securely hid. Thence he could see all that he wanted, in the patch of light by the map.

He did not wait long. The door opened, and a figure was illumined for one instant in profile before it turned to descend the stairs. It was a tall man in a long riding-coat which he had unbuttoned in the warmth of the room. He bowed his head a little as one does when one walks stealthily, and his lips were tightly pursed. But where was the sharp nose like a pen, and the pale complexion of Sir John? This man had a skin like red sandstone, a short blunt nose and a jovial mouth. He cast one glance at the map, and then went softly down the staircase.

With a queer flutter of the heart Alastair recognised Mr. Nicholas Kyd.

===Chinese===
他细细思量「英格兰」这个奇怪的东西——它像一道咒，缚在清醒的头脑上。仲冬讲起老英格兰，像情人讲起他的爱人；而眼前这个饱经风霜的行客，这个奥格尔索普，也为同一股热忱而震颤。这是他前所未遇的东西。他自幼受的教养，是爱自己的家族、自己的氏族、故乡的山峦，还有一位凝聚着几个世纪流浪忠心的王子。但他的忠爱，向来系于细小亲切的事物，而不是像邦国或人民这样宏大而非私己的东西。他忽然感到自己实实在在是个异乡人，孑然一身。王子、族长们、军队——在这里他们个个都是异乡人。对这他们几乎不了解的东西，他们怎能向它索要忠心呢？

这念头使他痛苦，他便把它逐开，转向眼前的正事。他踢掉鞋，踮着脚从储物壁橱折返出去，进了长廊，望见廊尽头厅灯的亮光正落在那幅地图和棕室的门上。他侧耳细听，四下无声，只有远处厨房方向传来一点轻微的磕碰，想必是将军的仆人在候主人的吩咐。他蹑足到棕室门口片刻，做了一回偷听的人。是的，屋里有说话声，一个低低的声音说得急促，另一个声音只以单字应答。他无意偷听他们的话，便爬回储物间的门口，那里藏得严实。从那里，在地图旁那片灯光里，他想要看的一切都看得见。

他没有久等。门开了，一条身影被照亮了一瞬，侧影一闪，便转身下楼去了。那是个高个子男人，穿一件长长的骑装外衣，在屋里的暖意中已解开了扣子。他微微俯着头，像人蹑足潜行时的模样，双唇抿得紧紧的。可是，那笔尖似的尖鼻子、诺里斯爵士的苍白面色，都到哪里去了？这个人皮肤红如砂岩，鼻子短钝，一张快活的嘴。他朝地图瞥了一眼，便轻轻走下楼梯去了。

心头一阵奇异的悸动，阿拉斯泰尔认出来了：那正是尼古拉斯·基德先生。

## IX Old England / 九、古老英格兰

===Original===

“Yesterday morning your Prince was encamped outside Carlisle. By now the place may have fallen.”

“Who told you?” Alastair asked.

“I have my own messengers who journey in Old England,” said Midwinter. “Consider, Captain Maclean. As a bird flies, the place is not a hundred and fifty miles distant, and no mile is without its people. A word cried to a traveller is taken up by another and another till the man who rubs down a horse at night in a Chester innyard will have news of what befell at dawn on the Scotch Border. My way is quicker than post-horses.﻿ ﻿… But the name of inn reminds me. You have the look of a fasting man.”

Food was brought, and the November brume having fallen thick in the hollow, the windows were curtained, a lamp lit, and fresh fuel laid on the fire. Alastair kicked the boots from his weary legs, and as soon as his hunger was stayed fell to questioning his host; for he felt that till he could point a finger to the spy who had dogged him he had failed in his duty to the Cause. He poured out his tale without reserve.

===Chinese===

“昨天早晨，你们的王子还屯兵卡莱尔城外。到这时候，那座城池多半已经陷落了。”

“谁告诉你的？”阿拉斯泰尔问。

“我有自家的信使，行走于老英格兰之间，”仲冬说。“您想，麦克莱恩上尉。以飞鸟的直线而论，那地方不出一百五十英里，而没有一英里是没有人烟的。向一个旅人喊出的一句话，自会被一个又一个旅人接力传下去，直到夜里在切斯特一家客栈院子中替马擦身的人，也已听得了清晨苏格兰边境上发生的事。我的法子，比驿马还快。……不过说起客栈，倒提醒了我。您一脸斋戒中人的模样。”

饭菜端了上来；十一月的雾霭已在洼地里落得浓重，窗子挂上了帘，灯点上了，炉膛里添了新柴。阿拉斯泰尔把靴子从疲惫的双腿上踢脱，饥饿一平，便开始盘问他的主人；因为他觉得，若不能指认出那个一路盯梢的奸细，自己便有负于大业。他把经历原原本本倾吐了个干净。

===Original===

Midwinter bent his brows and stared into the fire.

“You are satisfied that this servant Edom is honest?” he asked.

“I have observed him for half a day and the man is as much in the dark as myself. If he is a rogue he is a master in dissimulation. But I do not think so.”

“Imprimis, you are insulted in the Flambury inn by those who would fasten a quarrel on you. Item, you are arrested and carried before this man Thicknesse, and one dressed like a mummer presses the accusation. Item, in a warrant you and your purposes are described with ominous accuracy. You are likewise this very day tricked by your gypsy guide, but that concerns rather my lady Norreys. These misfortunes came upon you after you had supped with Kyd, and therefore you suspected his servant, for these two alone in this countryside knew who you were. A fairly argued case, I concede, and to buttress it Kyd appears to have been near Flambury last night, when he professed to be on the road for Wiltshire. But you have ceased to suspect the servant. What of the master?”

===Chinese===

仲冬蹙起眉头，凝视着炉火。

“您确信那仆人以东是诚实的？”他问。

“我观察了他半天，此人和我一样蒙在鼓里。他若是奸徒，便是遮掩功夫的大师。但我不这样想。”

“第一件，您在弗兰伯里的客栈里受人寻衅，那些人存心要把一场口角强扭到您头上。第二件，您遭了拘捕，被解到那个西克尼斯面前，还有一个扮戏的戏子装束的人从旁压着罪名。第三件，一纸拘票，把您和您的来意描述得分毫不爽，令人心惊。就在今天，您还被您的吉普赛向导诓骗了一场，不过那桩事牵扯的毋宁是诺里斯夫人。这些祸事，都是您与基德共进晚餐之后落到头上的，故而您疑心他的仆人，因为在这一带乡间，只有这两人知道您是谁。这案子推论得颇有道理，我承认；而且还有一条佐证：基德昨夜似乎到过弗兰伯里左近，而他自称已在赶往威尔特郡的路上。可您如今已不再疑心那仆人了。那么他的主人呢？”

===Original===

Alastair started. “No, no. That is madness. The man is in the very heart of the Prince’s counsels. He is honest, I swear﻿—he is too deep committed.”

Midwinter nodded. “If he were false, it would indeed go ill with you; for on him, I take it, depends the rising of Wales and the Marches. He holds your Prince in the hollow of his hand. And if all tales be true the omens there are happy.”

Alastair told of the message from Brother Gilly, and, suddenly remembering Edom’s papers, drew them from his pocket, and read them again by the firelight. Here at last was news from Badminton and from Monmouth and Hereford: and at the foot, in the cipher which was that most commonly used among the Jacobites, was a further note dealing with Sir Watkin Wynn. The writer had concerted with him a plan, by which the Welsh levies should march straight through Gloucester and Oxfordshire to cut in between Cumberland and the capital. To Alastair, the thing was proved authentic beyond doubt, for it bore the password which had been agreed between himself and Sir Watkin a week before at Wynnstay.

He fell into a muse from which he was roused by Midwinter’s voice.

===Chinese===

阿拉斯泰尔悚然一惊。“不，不。那是疯了。此人正处于王子殿下机务的心腹之地。他是诚实的，我起誓——他陷得这么深，早已无从反顾。”

仲冬点了点头。“他若是假的，您可真要遭殃了；因为据我看来，威尔士与边区的大举全悬在他身上。他把你们的王子攥在掌心里。倘若各方传言不虚，那边的兆头倒是吉利的。”

阿拉斯泰尔说了吉利兄弟捎来的口信，又猛然记起以东的文书，便从口袋里掏出来，就着炉火再读一遍。这终于有了来自巴德明顿以及蒙茅斯（Monmouth）和赫里福德（Hereford）的消息；文末还有一段用雅各布党人最通行的密码写成的附记，说的是沃特金·温爵士的事。写信人与他议定了一项方略：威尔士的义勇兵将径直穿过格洛斯特和牛津郡，插到坎伯兰与京城之间。在阿拉斯泰尔看来，这文书的真实已不容置疑，因为上面带着一星期前他和沃特金爵士在温斯泰约定的口令。

他坠入沉思，直到仲冬的声音把他唤醒。

===Original===

“Kyd receives messages and forwards them northward, while he himself remains in the South. By what channel?”

“It would appear by Sir John Norreys, who is now, or soon will be, at Brightwell under the Peak.”

As he spoke the words his suspicions took a new course. Johnson had thought the man a timeserver, though he had yesterday recanted that view. Sir Christopher Lacy at Cornbury had been positive that he was a rogue. The only evidence to the contrary was that his wife believed in him, and that he had declared his colours by forsaking his bride for the Prince’s camp. But he had not gone to the army, and it would seem that he had no immediate intention of going there, for according to Edom he would be at Brightwell during the month; and as for his wife’s testimony, she was only a romantic child. Yet this man was the repository of Kyd’s secret information, the use of which meant for the Prince a kingdom or a beggar’s exile. If Kyd were mistaken in him, then the Cause was sold in very truth. But how came Kyd to be linked with him? How came a young Oxfordshire baronet, of no great family, and no record of service, to be “Achilles” of the innermost circle?

He told his companion of his doubts, unravelling each coil carefully, while the other marked his points with jerks of his pipe-bowl. When he had finished Midwinter kept silent for a little. Then “You swear by Kyd’s fidelity?” he asked.

===Chinese===

“基德接了消息，便转递北方，自己却留在南方。走的是哪条渠道？”

“看来是经由约翰·诺里斯爵士，他此刻——或者很快——就在皮克山下的布莱特韦尔（Brightwell）。”

话一出口，他的疑心便转了个方向。约翰逊曾把此人看作趋炎附势之徒，虽然昨天他已收回了这个看法。科恩伯里的克里斯托弗·莱西爵士则咬定他是个恶棍。与此相反的证据只有两条：他的妻子信任他，而且他舍下新嫁娘投奔王子的营地，已然表明了立场。但他并没有去军中，而且似乎也不打算即刻就去，因为据以东说，这个月里他都会待在布莱特韦尔；至于他妻子的见证，她不过是个满脑子罗曼蒂克的孩子。然而正是这个人，掌存着基德的机密情报，这情报用与不用，于王子殿下便是江山与乞食流亡之别。倘若基德在他身上看走了眼，那大业便是真的被出卖了。可是基德怎么会同他联结在一起？一个牛津郡的年轻从男爵，门第不显，又无履历，怎么会成为最内层圈子里的“阿喀琉斯”（Achilles）？

他把这些疑团一一说给同伴听，一圈圈仔细解开，对方则用烟斗锅一磕一磕地应和着他的条目。他说完了，仲冬沉默了片刻，然后问：“您敢以基德的忠贞起誓么？”

===Original===

“God in Heaven, but I must,” cried Alastair. “If he is false, I may return overseas tomorrow.”

“It is well to test all links in a chain,” was the dry answer. “But for the sake of argument we will assume him honest. Sir John Norreys is the next link to be tried. If he is rotten, then the Prince had better bide north of Ribble, for the Western auxiliaries will never move. But even if the whole hive be false, there is still hope if you act at once. This is my counsel to you, Captain Maclean. Write straightway to the Army﻿—choose the man about the Prince who loves you most﻿—and tell him of the great things to be hoped for from the West. Name no names, but promise before a certain date to arrive with full proof, and bid them hasten south without delay. An invasion needs heartening, and if the worst should be true no word from Kyd is likely to reach the Prince. Hearten him, therefore, so that he marches to meet you. That is the first thing. The second is that you go yourself into Derbyshire to see this Sir John Norreys. If he be true man you will find a friend; if not you may be in time to undo his treason.”

The advice was what had dimly been shaping itself in Alastair’s own mind. His ardour to be back with the Army, which for days had been a fever in his bones, had now changed to an equal ardour to solve the riddle which oppressed him. Midwinter was right; the Cause was on a razor edge and with him might lie the deciding.﻿ ﻿… There was black treachery somewhere, and far more vital for the Prince than any victory in Scotland was the keeping the road open for West England to join him. Shadows of many reasons flitted across his mind and gave strength to his resolve. He would see this man Norreys who had won so adorable a lady. He would see the lady again, and at the thought something rose in his heart which surprised him, for it was almost joy.

===Chinese===

“皇天在上，我不起誓也不行啊，”阿拉斯泰尔叫道。“他若是假的，我明天就该滚回海外去了。”

“链子上的每一环都该敲一敲，这是审慎之道，”回答是干巴巴的。“但为着辩论起见，我们不妨假定他是诚实的。下一个该试的环节，便是约翰·诺里斯爵士。他若是朽坏的，那王子殿下最好留在里布尔河以北，因为西路的偏师是决计不会动了的。但即便这一整窝全是假的，只要您即刻行动，也还有希望。这是我给您的忠告，麦克莱恩上尉。立刻写信给大军——挑王子身边最眷爱您的那个人——告诉他西部可望成就的大事。不要提名道姓，只许诺在某日之前携确证赶到，并请他们毫不迟延地兼程南下。南征之师需要鼓舞，而倘若最坏的情形成真，基德那边的话怕是到不了王子耳中的。所以要给他鼓劲，好让他挥师前来与您相会。这是第一件。第二件，您亲自去德比郡走一遭，见一见这位约翰·诺里斯爵士。他若是个真人，您便得到一位朋友；若不是，您或许还来得及拆掉他的叛逆。”

这番忠告，正与阿拉斯泰尔自己心中隐隐成形的打算相合。回到大军身边的热望——连日来如骨中之热病——如今已化作一股同样炽热的渴望，要去解开那压在心上的谜团。仲冬说得对；大业正悬于剃刀之刃，而乾坤一掷，也许正落在他手里。……黑黢黢的叛逆藏在某一处，对王子殿下而言，比苏格兰的任何一场胜仗都更要紧的，是保住西英格兰前来会师的通道。种种理由的暗影掠过他的心头，给他的决意添了力量。他要去见一见这个诺里斯，这个赢得了如此可爱的一位夫人的男子。他还要再见那位夫人，一念及此，有什么东西从他心底涌了起来，连他自己也吃了一惊，因为那几乎算是欢喜。

===Original===

“Have you paper and ink?” he asked, and from a cupboard Midwinter produced them and set them before him.

He wrote to Lochiel, who was his kinsman, for though he knew Lord George Murray there was a certain jealousy between them. Very roughly he gave the figures which he had gleaned from Brother Gilly’s letter and that taken from Edom. He begged him to move the Prince to march without hesitation for the capital, and promised to reach his camp with full information before the month ended. “And the camp will, I trust, be by that time no further from St. James’s than﻿—” He asked Midwinter for a suitable place, and was told “Derby.” He subscribed himself with the affection of a kinsman and old playmate of Morvern and Lochaber.

“I will see that it reaches its destination,” said Midwinter. “And now for the second task. The man Edom is not suspect and can travel by the high road. I will send him with one who will direct him to my lady Norreys’ party, which this day, as you tell me, sets out for Derbyshire. For yourself I counsel a discreeter part. Mark you, sir, you are sought by sundry gentlemen in Flambury as a Jacobite, and by Squire Thicknesse and his Hunt as a horse-thief. In this land suspicion is slow to waken, but in the end it runs fast and dies hard. Rumour of your figure, face, clothes, manner and bloodthirsty spirit will have already flown fifty miles. If you would be safe you must sink into Old England.”

===Chinese===

“您有纸笔么？”他问。仲冬从一只橱里取出，摆在他面前。

他写信给洛希尔（Lochiel）——那是他的亲族，因为乔治·默里勋爵他虽也相识，两人之间却存着几分芥蒂。他把从吉利兄弟的信上和以东文书中拾掇来的数目，粗粗开列了一遍。他恳请他说动王子殿下毫不迟疑地直趋京城，并许诺月底之前携全部情报赶到大营。“而且我信赖，到那时大营离圣詹姆斯宫（St. James’s）不会再远于——”他问仲冬一个合适的地名，得到的是：“德比。”他以莫尔文与洛哈伯（Lochaber）的亲族兼旧日玩伴的情分署了名。

“我会让它送到它该去的地方，”仲冬说。“现在说第二件差事。那人以东不在嫌疑之列，可以走大道。我会差一个人同行引路，送他到诺里斯夫人的队伍里去——照您的说法，她们今天就动身去德比郡。至于您自己，我劝您行止更隐晦些。您可知道，先生，弗兰伯里有几位先生正在缉拿您这个雅各布党人，西克尼斯乡绅和他的猎队则把您当作偷马贼。在这片土地上，疑心醒来得慢，可一旦醒来便跑得飞快、死得极难。关于您的身形、面容、衣着、做派和那股杀气的传闻，此刻只怕已经飞出五十英里外了。您若要平安，就必须沉进老英格兰里去。”

===Original===

“I will sink into Acheron if it will better my purpose.”

Midwinter regarded him critically. “Your modish clothes are in Kit’s locker, and will duly be sent after you. Now you are the born charcoal-burner, save that your eyes are too clear and your finger nails unscorched. The disguise has served your purpose today, but it is too kenspeckle except in great woodlands. Mother Jonnet will find you a better. For the rest I will guide you, for I have the key.”

“Where is this magic country?”

“All around you﻿—behind the brake, across the hedgerow, under the branches. Some can stretch a hand and touch it﻿—to others it is a million miles away.”

===Chinese===

“只要于我的大事有利，便是沉进阿刻戎（Acheron）冥河我也愿意。”

仲冬挑剔地打量着他。“您那身时髦衣裳在基特的柜子里，日后自会送还给您。眼下您是天生的烧炭人，只是眼睛太清亮，指甲也没让火燎过。这身装束今天已为您效了力，但除了在深山大林里，它太打眼了。琼内特妈妈（Mother Jonnet）会给您找一身更好的。其余的路段由我引路，因为钥匙在我手里。”

“这片有魔力的国土在哪里？”

“就在您四周——荆丛背后，树篱那头，枝桠底下。有的人一伸手就能触到它——对另一些人，它远在百万英里之外。”

===Original===

“As a child I knew it,” said Alastair, laughing. “I called it Fairyland.”

Midwinter nodded. “Children are free of it, but their elders must earn admission. It is a safe land﻿—at any rate it is secure from common perils.”

“But it has its own dangers?”

“It makes a man look into his heart, and he may find that in it which destroys him. Also it is ambition’s mortal foe. But if you walk in it you will come to Brightwell without obstruction, for the King’s writ does not run in the greenwood.”

===Chinese===

“我小时候是认得它的，”阿拉斯泰尔笑着说。“我管它叫仙乡。”

仲冬点头。“孩童在里头出入自由，成年人却须挣得入门之资。那是一片安全的土地——至少寻常的危险侵不进它。”

“可它自有它的凶险？”

“它会逼得一个人照见自己的内心，而他也许会在里面看见那毁掉他的东西。它也是雄心的死敌。但您若行在其中，便可一路无阻地走到布莱特韦尔，因为王命行不到绿林之中。”

===Original===

“Whose is the law, then?” Alastair asked.

For answer Midwinter went to the window and flung it open. “My fiddle cannot speak except with free air about it,” he said. “If any drunken rustic is on the heath he will think the pixies are abroad.”

He picked up the violin which had been lying on the table behind him, and drew forth a slow broken music, which presently changed into a rhythmical air. At first it was like the twanging of fine wires in a wind, mingled with an echo of organ music heard over a valley full of treetops. It was tame and homely, yet with a childish inconsequence in it. Then it grew wilder, and though the organ notes remained it was an organ that had never sounded within church walls. The tune went with a steady rhythm, the rhythm of growing things in spring, of seasonal changes; but always ran the undercurrent of a leaping bacchanal madness, of long wild dances in bare places. The fiddle ceased on a soft note, and the fiddler fell to singing in a voice so low that the words and air only just rose above the pitch of silence. “Diana and her darling crew,” he sang.

> “Diana and her darling crew
 

 Will pluck your fingers fine,
 

 And lead you forth right pleasantly
 

 To drink the honey wine﻿—
 

 To drink the honey wine, my dear,
 

 And sup celestial air,
 

 And dance as the young angels dance,
 

 Ah, God, that I were there!”

===Chinese===

“那么那里行的是谁家的法？”阿拉斯泰尔问。

算作回答，仲冬走到窗前，一把推开窗子。“我的提琴，除非四面有自由的空气，否则开不得口，”他说。“荒原上若有哪个喝醉的乡人听见，会当是小精灵出来了。”

他拿起一直搁在身后桌上的提琴，先拉出一段迟缓、断续的乐音，不久便化成一支有节律的曲调。起初它像风里细弦的铮鸣，又混着隔着满谷树梢传来的管风琴的回声。它温驯而家常，其中却又带着一种孩子气的飘忽。随后它野了起来，管风琴的音仍在，却成了一架从不在教堂墙内发声的管风琴。曲子踏着稳健的节拍行进，那是春日万物生长的节拍、四时更迭的节拍；可底下始终奔涌着一股跳跃的酒神狂热的潜流，一场场在空旷之地的漫长野舞。提琴在一个柔和的音符上停住，琴手低声唱了起来，那声音低得词与调只是刚刚浮出寂静的水面。“黛安娜和她心爱的队伍，”他唱道。

> “黛安娜和她心爱的族人
 

 会把你的十指捻得纤纤，
 

 欢欢喜喜地引你上路，
 

 去饮那蜂蜜的酒——
 

 去饮那蜂蜜的酒，亲爱的，
 

 再餐一口天上的清气，
 

 像小天使一般起舞——
 

 啊，上帝，愿我在那里！”

===Original===

“Hers is the law,” he said. “Diana, or as some say, Proserpina. Old folk call her the Queen of Elfhame. But over you and me, as baptized souls, she has no spell but persuasion. You can hear her weeping at midnight because her power is gone.”

Then his mood changed. He laid down the fiddle and shouted on Mother Jonnet to bring supper. Edom, too, was sent for, and during the meal was closely catechised. He bore it well, professing no undue honesty beyond a good servant’s, but stiff on his few modest scruples. When he heard Midwinter’s plans for him, he welcomed them, and begged that in the choice of a horse his precarious balance and round thighs might be charitably considered. Alastair returned him the letter and watched him fold it up with the others and shove it inside his waistcoat. A prolonged study of that mild, concerned, faintly humorous face convinced him that Edom Lowrie was neither fox nor goose. He retired to bed to dream of Mr. Kyd’s jolly countenance, which had mysteriously acquired a very sharp nose.

Edom went off in the early morning in company with the man called Kit and mounted on an ambling forest cob whose paces he wholeheartedly approved. Alastair washed himself like a Brahmin in a tub of hot water in the back-kitchen, and dressed himself in the garments provided by Mother Jonnet﻿—frieze and leather and coarse woollen stockings and square-toed country shoes. The haze of yesterday had gone, and the sky was a frosty blue, with a sharp wind out of the northeast. He breakfasted with Midwinter off cold beef and beer and a dish of grilled ham, and then stood before the door breathing deep of the fresh chilly morning. The change of garb or the prospect before him had rid him of all the languor of the past week. He felt extraordinarily lithe and supple of limb, as in the old days when he had driven deer on the hills before the autumn dawn. Had he but had the free swing of a kilt at his thighs and the screes of Ben Aripol before him he would have recaptured his boyhood.

===Chinese===

“行的是她的法，”他说。“狄安娜（Diana），也有人说是普洛塞庇娜（Proserpina）。老人们称她为精灵乡的王后（Queen of Elfhame）。但对你我这样受过洗礼的灵魂，她除劝诱之外无咒可施。午夜时分你能听见她哭泣，因为她的权能已然消逝。”

随后他的心境一变。他放下提琴，高声唤琼内特妈妈端晚饭来。以东也叫了来，一顿饭间受了严严的盘考。他应对得体，除了一个老实仆人本分之外不滥表清白，只是在自家的几桩小小顾忌上寸步不让。他听了仲冬为他作的安排，满心欢迎，只求在挑马的时候，行行好体谅他坐不稳的身量和浑圆的大腿。阿拉斯泰尔把那封信交还给他，看他把它同另外几件一并折好，塞进马甲里。对那张温和、挂虑、微带诙谐的脸的长久端详，使阿拉斯泰尔确信：以东·洛里（Edom Lowrie）既非狐狸，也非呆鹅。他上床睡了，梦见基德先生那张乐呵呵的脸——那脸上不知怎的长出了一只极尖的鼻子。

一清早，以东便同那个唤作基特的人一道出发了，骑一匹走侧对步的林间矮脚马——那马的步子他是打心眼里赞成。阿拉斯泰尔在后厨的一大盆热水里，像婆罗门似的把自己洗了个净，换上琼内特妈妈备下的衣装——粗呢、皮革、粗毛线袜，和方头的乡下鞋。昨日的雾霭散了，天空是一片霜意的蓝，东北风刮得凛冽。他和仲冬一同用了早餐——冷牛肉、啤酒，还有一碟炙火腿——然后立在门前，深深呼吸清冷的新晨。换了装束也罢，前程在望也罢，过去一周的困顿委靡已从他身上褪尽。他觉得四肢出奇地轻捷柔韧，一如旧日在秋天破晓前于山间赶鹿的时光。若有大腿间自由摆荡的短裙、眼前是本·阿里波尔山（Ben Aripol）的碎石坡，他简直要追回自己的少年了。

===Original===

Midwinter looked at him with approval.

“You are clad as a man should be for Old England, and you have the legs for the road we travel. We do not ride, for we go where no horse can go. Put not your trust in horses, saith the Scriptures, which I take to mean that a man in the last resort should depend on his own shanks. Boot and spur must stick to the paths, and the paths are but a tiny bit of England. How sits the wind? North by east? There is snow coming, but not in the next thirty hours, and if it comes, it will not stay us. *En avant, mon capitaine.*”

At a pace which was marvellous for one of his figure, Midwinter led the way over the heath, and then plunged into a tangled wood of oaks. He walked like a mountaineer, swinging from the hips, the body a little bent forward, and his long even strides devoured the ground. Even so, Alastair reminded himself, had the hunters at Glentarnit breasted the hill, while his boyish steps had toiled in their rear. Sometimes on level ground he would break into a run, as if his body’s vigour needed an occasional burst of speed to chasten it. The young man exulted in the crisp air and the swift motion. The stiffness of body and mind which had beset him ever since he left Scotland vanished under this cordial, he lost his doubts and misgivings, and felt again that lifting ardour of the heart which is the glory of youth. His feet were tireless, his limbs were as elastic as a sword-blade, his breath as deep as a greyhound’s. Two days before, jogging in miry lanes, he had seemed caught and stifled in a net; now he was on a hilltop, and free as the wind that plucked at his hair.

===Chinese===

仲冬赞许地望着他。

“您穿得正是一个行走老英格兰的男子该有的装束，也有一双配得上我们要走的路的腿。我们不骑马，因为我们要去的地方马到不了。经上说，不要倚仗马匹，我把它解作：人到末路，终究要靠自己的两条腿。马靴马刺只能黏在大路上，而大路不过是英格兰极小的一角。风朝哪边吹？北偏东？雪是要来的，但未来三十个钟头还下不来；即便下了，也拦不住我们。*En avant, mon capitaine.*”（开拔吧，我的上尉。）

以他那样一副身量而言堪称惊人的步子，仲冬领路穿过荒原，随即扎进一片枝柯纠缠的橡树林。他走路像个山民，摆动着胯，上身微微前倾，均匀的大步把地面一寸寸吞掉。阿拉斯泰尔想起，当年格伦塔尼特（Glentarnit）的猎手们仰身爬坡正是这般姿态，而自己男孩的碎步只在后面苦追。有时在平地上他会撒开腿跑上一阵，仿佛浑身精力需要不时来一场疾驰加以整肃。这年轻人为清冽的空气和飞快的行进欢欣鼓舞。自离开苏格兰便缠住他的身心的僵滞，在这剂补药之下消散了；他丢开了疑虑与不安，重新感到那种使心头扬起的炽热——那是青春的荣光。他的双脚不知疲倦，四肢像剑刃一样弹韧，呼吸像灵犬一样深沉。两天前颠簸在泥泞的小路上，他还像被网罗住、闷得透不过气；此刻他已在山顶上，像扯他头发的风一样自由。

===Original===

It is probable that Midwinter had for one of his purposes the creation of this happy mood, for he kept up the pace till after midday, when they came to a high deer-fence, beyond which stretched a ferny park. Here they slackened speed, their faces glowing like coals, and, skirting the park, reached a thatched hut which smoked in a dell. A woman stood at the door, who at the sight of the two would have retired inside, had not Midwinter whistled sharply on his fingers. She blinked and shaded her eyes with her hand against the frosty sunshine; then to Alastair’s amazement she curtseyed deep.

Midwinter did not halt, but asked if Jeremy were at the stone pit.

“He be, Master,” was her answer. “Will ye stop to break bread?”

“Nay, Jeremy shall feed us,” he cried, and led the way up the dingle where a brook flowed in reedy pools. Presently there was a sound of axe-blows, and, rounding a corner, they came on a man cutting poles from a thicket of saplings. Again Midwinter whistled, and the woodcutter dropped his tool and turned with a grinning face, pulling at his forelock.

===Chinese===

很可能，酿出这份快意的心境正是仲冬的目的之一，因为这步子他一直维持到过了晌午，两人方到一道高高的鹿栅前，栅外伸展着一片蕨草丛生的苑囿。在这里他们放慢了，两张脸烧得像炭火一般，沿着苑边绕行，走到一间在洼地里冒着烟的茅舍。门前立着一个女人，一见这两人便要退回屋里，幸而仲冬用指头打了一声尖锐的唿哨。她眨眨眼，举手遮着挡住霜日眩目的太阳；随后，令阿拉斯泰尔诧异的是，她深深地行了一个屈膝礼。

仲冬没有停步，只问杰里米（Jeremy）在不在石坑那儿。

“他在呐，老爷，”她答道。“您几位歇歇吃口饭不？”

“不啦，杰里米会管我们饭的，”他喊道，便领路上那道小幽谷，谷中一条溪水在芦苇丛生的水潭里流淌。不多时传来斧声，转过一道弯，两人看见一个汉子正在幼树林里砍木杆。仲冬又打了一声唿哨，那樵夫撂下家什，咧嘴笑着转过身来，扯着自己的额发。

===Original===

Midwinter sat down on a tree-trunk.
